# QUESTIONS.md: open questions before Phoxium starts

This file reconciles three independent need analyses (Sonnet, Opus, Fable agents, high effort, 2026-11-18).
Each analyst read `CLAUDE.md` and `CRT.md` and listed questions, tensions and spikes.
About 306 raw questions were merged and deduplicated into the catalogue below.
Where the analysts proposed different defaults, the difference is kept visible (see "Where the analysts disagreed").

Nothing here is a design.
It lists what to decide, what to measure and what to prototype before committing to a design.

## Decisions recorded (2026-11-18)

Answers from the project owner to the blocking table. They override the defaults in the entries below, which carry a "DECISION" bullet where they changed.

| Id | Decision |
|---|---|
| HW-1 | Detected on the reference PC: laptop with Intel UHD Graphics plus NVIDIA RTX A2000 8GB Laptop GPU (driver 596.71). Two active reference monitors (24 in, 2560×1440, 60 Hz, about 53×30 cm, SDR IPS). |
| COL-1, CAP-3 | Desktop HDR is off: the only active monitors are the reference monitors, which have no HDR. The owner also says neither the Intel nor the NVIDIA GPU uses HDR. |
| PHY-4 | Mask type is a 3-way choice (aperture grille, slot mask, delta dot), all other look parameters are independent. Well-known CRT models ship as starting-point presets (data files, 3 to 5, user-editable), not code (see PHY-4). Extended to up to 10 on request. |
| PHY-14, SCP-6 | Ageing and burn-in are in scope, opt-in, with a persistent tube state (see PHY-14). |
| PHY-9, SRC-7 | Composite video and interlace are in scope as optional modules, off by default, after the first usable version (see PHY-9, SRC-7). |
| OUT-1, CAP-4 | Must work with one screen. SP-1 and SP-2 become blocking for the whole project. Two monitors is a bonus, not the guaranteed mode. |
| SRC-1, SRC-2 | Test inputs: any NES-style still snapshot, then Mina the Hollower in real conditions. Any input resolution and colour count must work. |
| COL-1 | Output is SDR only, no HDR output and no HDR colour (decided by the assistant, see COL-1). Internal computation is linear-light floating point. |
| PHY-2 | Default is option A (area-averaged mask with a live pixels-per-triad readout). Option D (offer a coarser tube preset that the host can resolve) must exist as an alternative. |
| SCP-1, SCP-2 | No real CRT and no instruments. Physics comes from literature and web research, tagged by confidence. More research passes are wanted on the weakest data. |
| PRJ-1, PRJ-2 | Personal project. No GPL shader code is reused, inspiration only. |
| SCP-5 | The wording "each source RGB pixel becomes RGB light sources" is shorthand for the beam-and-phosphor model. `CLAUDE.md` is updated. |
| TEC-1 | Vulkan, GLSL, Rust and ImGui are the owner's own guess, not hard constraints. Counter-proposals welcome, decided after the spikes. |
| OUT-3, OUT-5 (2026-11-18, planning) | No separate control window. F11 toggles fullscreen and a resizable windowed output. F12 (default changed to F10 on 2026-11-19, D-17) opens and closes the ImGui settings menu inside the output window; the menu takes the focus while open and gives it back to the source on close. Output otherwise stays non-activating. F11 and F12 are global hotkeys, configurable. |
| SRC-1, SRC-2 (2026-11-18, planning) | Mina the Hollower is not on the reference PC. A stand-in pixel-art game is used during development; Mina is the final acceptance test on the owner's other machine (PLAN.md T-83). First usable version keeps all 3 mask types. |

Still open:
- Which GPU drives the monitors: answered by a setting (default `auto` picks the adapter of the covered monitor, see the decision gate below and HW-3).
- Nothing else blocking. The owner's decisions of 2026-11-19 are in the "Owner decisions" block of the decision gate below.
- OUT-2, OUT-3 (no-focus window and menu placement): defaults stand, decided by SP-2.
- TIM-1 (latency), PHY-12 (temporal scope): defaults stand.

## Decision gate (the first-phase review; drafted and decided 2026-11-19)

### Owner decisions (2026-11-19)

The owner answered the draft proposals below. The draft text stays underneath as the record of the evidence; where an answer differs from a proposal, the answer wins.

| Topic | Decision | Entries |
|---|---|---|
| Stack | **Decided:** keep Rust, Vulkan (through `ash`) and GLSL, the "winner combo". No move to Direct3D 12. | TEC-1, TEC-2, A1, A4, A5 |
| Capture path | **Decided:** keep the simple CPU copy, because it is the most universal. The zero-copy path (a shared handle between the capture texture and Vulkan) is not cancelled: it becomes a later, optional performance setting, to study once the features work ("study later as an extra performance option, don't forget it"). What is known: the adapter check found that both Vulkan devices can import Direct3D 11 textures (extension queries only, untested), and the two monitors are split across the RTX and the Intel iGPU, so a cross-adapter case exists. **Built 2026-11-20 (owner request):** the zero-copy path exists (`[global.capture] path`), refused with a notice when capture and render are on different adapters; measured and checked headless, live check pending (CAP-2, A29). **Default changed 2026-11-20 (owner request "add this non-zero-copy option (CPU) as a parameter in the main menu (by default: zero-copy)"):** zero-copy is the default with an automatic fallback to the CPU copy; the CPU copy is a choice in the menu's Source section, switchable live both ways. | CAP-2, A3, A29 |
| Settings menu | **Decided:** Dear ImGui, inside the output window. It takes the focus while open (accepted) and gives it back on close. Default key F10, reassignable. The owner wants a modern custom look: done (`ui/theme.rs`, Inter font). | TEC-5, A17, OUT-3, D-7, D-17 |
| GPU | **Decided:** which GPU runs the whole chain is a setting, default `auto` = the adapter of the monitor the output covers. Implemented as `[global.gpu] adapter` (see CHANGELOG). | HW-3 |
| One screen | **Decided (conditional GO):** a single screen must work, at least when the third-party game is not in exclusive fullscreen. If it fails on a real game, debug then. | OUT-1, CAP-4, D-1 |
| Performance | **Decided:** 60 fps is a target, not a hard requirement. Features first; optimise after they work. Offer quality and performance settings. The 4.2 ms budget stays a goal, not a gate. | PRF-1, PRF-2 |
| First-version scope | **Decided:** all three mask types in; halation off in the first usable version; persistence, saturation and automatic beam limiting off by default. | PRJ-6, D-5, D-6 |
| Hotkeys | **Decided:** F11 (fullscreen) is a default binding and can be reassigned; the menu key defaults to F10; the quit chord stays fixed. | OUT-5, D-17 |
| Early-look renders | **Still open, non-blocking.** In plain words: look at `tv26_grille_2p9_montage.png` at 100 %; is the difference between the physical render (left) and the scanlines-only render (right) worth the project? | SCP-7, PHY-2 |

T-03 (adapter topology) and T-04 (shared-handle spike) were not run; T-26 depends on T-04's question.

### Proposal 1: stack, keep Rust + Vulkan (ash) + GLSL (TEC-1, TEC-2, A1, A4, A5)

- **Evidence:** the pipeline is built end to end (decode, amp, gun, abl, screen with grille, slot and delta masks, phosphor, encode; ARCHITECTURE 3.7), with a headless GPU harness whose validation count is 0 (T-09; synchronization validation on) and "535 tests" (brief, unverified here). No spike result needs D3D12: the one real finding of SP-2, the focus leak, reproduces with no Vulkan and no capture (OUT-2, `spikes/focus-leak`), so it belongs to Windows, not to the API. The main argument for a D3D present path, HDR output, left with COL-1 (SDR only).
- **Alternative rejected:** capture and present on D3D12 (TEC-1 trigger: SP-1 or SP-2 "painful"). It would rewrite T-20 onward (PLAN T-13 text) for no measured gain. Not weighed further: wgpu and vulkano (TEC-2 default stays ash).
- **Risks and unknown:** present mode and compositor latency of the Vulkan output are not measured (PresentMon not run; T-59 result not consolidated here, see TIM-1); behaviour on other vendors is untested (HW-3 default: the dev GPU is the only certified target). Revisit trigger: a measured missed-refresh or latency cost traced to the Vulkan present path.

### Proposal 2: capture path, keep the CPU copy; cancel T-26 unless a measurement says otherwise (CAP-2, A3)

- **Evidence:** the CPU round trip works: capture holds 56.8 / 57.0 / 57.2 fps (3 covered runs) against a source that itself makes about 57 distinct frames per second, and render holds 60.3 fps (CAP-4 hands-off rerun). With patterns as source, `upload` and `decode` are under 0.06 ms per frame (ARCHITECTURE 3.7 measured-cost bullet, 320x240; the T-47 table shows `upload` absent because patterns bypass capture). No CPU-copy cost at a real window size is recorded in the repo.
- **T-04 was not run**, so the shared handle is neither validated nor measured (extension support on both GPUs unverified). Proposal: do not spend T-04 and T-26 now; mark T-26 cancelled.
- **Reopen condition (the number to measure):** callback CPU time (copy to staging, map, row copy) plus Vulkan staging upload, and capture-to-submit latency, at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440 sources, read from `--stats` and `--log` (`diag.rs`, T-59 QPC points) with `pixel-demo` at its largest sizes (T-95) and later the real game. T-04's own fail rule is the budget: if the CPU path costs more than 3 ms at the real size, or the capture rate falls below the source rate, then T-04 runs (pass rule: saves at least 1 ms per frame or 30 % of capture-to-submit) and T-26 is reinstated; the alternative named there is a D3D11 decimation pass before readback.
- **Rejected:** shared handle now (unvalidated cross-API synchronisation, Opus xhigh task, for a gain not shown to be needed); GPU-to-GPU copy (same interop risk, same lack of a need). **Risk:** at a large source (a 4K window) the copy may exceed the budget; hybrid graphics (proposal 4) could make the shared handle unavailable anyway.

### Proposal 3: ImGui crate set A, focus hand-off, menu key F10 (TEC-5, A17, OUT-3, D-7, D-17)

- **Crates:** `dear-imgui-rs`, `dear-imgui-sys` `=0.22.0` and `dear-imgui-ash` `=0.22.0` (default features off, `dynamic-rendering`), our own Win32 `input.rs`, exact pins because the crate went 0.20 to 0.22 in three weeks. Evidence (TEC-5, `spikes/imgui-spike/RESULTS.md`): builds from crates.io with no submodule and no libclang (MSVC C++ needed), MIT OR Apache-2.0 and `cargo deny check licenses` clean, `ash 0.38` shared, headless validation 0 messages, overlay pass 0.15 ms windowed (about 1280x720) and 0.27 ms fullscreen 2560x1440, cold release build 54 s.
- **Hand-off design:** on the menu key drop `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`, remember the foreground, plain `SetForegroundWindow(output)`; on close restore the style and plain `SetForegroundWindow(remembered)`. Evidence: 10/10 opens, 10/10 text, 10/10 closes in each of two modes, a 28-cycle 94 s run 28/28, independent poll exactly 56 changes of 56, plain method every time (no synthetic Alt or input needed). The T-07 hand-back must be off while the menu is open (OUT-2).
- **Menu key:** F10 default (D-17; F12 is held system-wide by another program on the reference PC, `RegisterHotKey` error 1409; F10, F11 and Ctrl+Alt+F12 register), configurable, with a visible message on failure. PLAN T-94 and RESULTS section 7 still say "F12 default": superseded by D-17. Caveat (RESULTS 9.2): a global F10 swallows the menu-bar key of every application.
- **Rejected:** `imgui` 0.12 plus `imgui-rs-vulkan-renderer` 1.16.0 (builds, not run, not needed; tracks Dear ImGui 1.89); an own ash renderer (kept as the fail order); a non-ImGui toolkit (D-9).
- **Unknown:** real mouse and keyboard by a person, a real game as the source, and a hand-off not triggered by an input event were not tested; the missing LICENSE files in the crate packages mean the release zip must carry upstream notices.

### Proposal 4: render adapter policy (HW-3; T-03 not run)

- **Default proposed:** the Vulkan device is the adapter that drives the output monitor, matched by LUID (`VkPhysicalDeviceIDProperties::deviceLUID` against the DXGI adapter owning the output), with the D3D11 capture device created on the same adapter where possible; `--gpu` overrides; a visible warning on mismatch. Note the wording difference: HW-3 and T-03 say "the capture adapter's LUID", this draft says the output monitor's adapter; T-03 settles which matches reality.
- **Evidence for the need:** Windows lists the Intel UHD as the adapter with the 2560x1440 desktop and the RTX A2000 as a second adapter with no display mode (HW-3 finding); today the D3D device uses the default adapter and Vulkan prefers an NVIDIA discrete GPU, and nothing checks that they agree (HW-3 IMPLEMENTED, ARCHITECTURE 3.5). The runs so far (render 60.3 fps, capture 57 fps) were all on that unchecked combination.
- **Unverified:** the behaviour on a laptop where the iGPU drives the display. T-03 would measure: DXGI adapters with LUID and `EnumOutputs` desktop rectangles; Vulkan devices with `deviceLUID`; the capture device LUID; the printed line "capture and render on the same adapter" or the mismatch warning; and the per-application GPU preference in Windows graphics settings (UI path unverified). Fail case (monitors only on the Intel iGPU): a cross-adapter copy is unavoidable and T-04 would have to measure it.
- **Rejected:** always NVIDIA (silent wrong choice on other machines, D-18 says the software must adapt to any host); always the capture adapter without a warning.

### Proposal 5: one-screen go/no-go: GO with conditions (OUT-1, CAP-4, D-1)

- **Evidence:** covered on the same monitor, 3 runs of 117 s: capture 57.0 fps mean (49.8 minimum), no capture interval above 100 ms (max 66.3 ms), render 60.3 fps with no render gap above 50 ms; the capture never fell to 0; covering costs nothing measurable against the uncovered control (CAP-4). The Windows external activation at about 25 s (7 of 7 runs, also without capture or Vulkan) is mitigated by `output::hand_back_target`: one hand-back, foreground back on the source 0.10 s later (OUT-2, `check-focus.ps1` reports MITIGATED, not PASS). Antivirus clean on Defender (PRJ-4); captured pixels bit-exact (CAP-3).
- **Conditions:** (a) the verdict stays PROVISIONAL until a real game runs: the stand-in is pixel-demo (D-13), Mina the Hollower on the owner's other machine at the end (T-83), with D-1 as the answer if a real game stops delivering frames (fallbacks: output 1 px smaller, second monitor); (b) input reaching the game and cursor behaviour are untested; (c) a game that pauses on deactivation may stutter once at the blip; (d) minimised sources deliver no frames, exclusive fullscreen is out (CAP-5); (e) the initiator of the 25 s activation is unidentified (T-94 asks to find it).
- **Rejected:** NO-GO (nothing shows the covered source throttled); "GO" unconditional (the real-game case, the hard one, is untested).

### Proposal 6: early-look renders (T-16, SP-7): owner judgement pending

- **Files:** `target\early_look\` of the checkout (gitignored output of `cargo run --release -p crt-model --example early_look`; regenerate in any checkout): `pvm20_grille_1p54_montage.png`, `tv26_grille_2p9_montage.png`, `tv26_slot_2p9_montage.png` (left physical, right scanlines only; also `_physical.png`, `_scanlines.png`, `_2x` and `source_256x240.png`), and `metrics.txt`. View at 100 %.
- **Numbers (metrics.txt, host 1920x1440):** physical against scanlines-only, mean abs diff 30.84 codes at 1.542 px per triad (88.9 % of pixels differ by 5 codes or more), 67.86 at 2.909 (92.7 %), 67.93 for the slot mask at 2.909; flat-white horizontal luminance ripple (Michelson) 0.5338, 0.8162 and 0.8169 against the Barten threshold 0.014; vertical ripple 0.0255, 0.0185 and 0.0632 (slot, dominant period 5.69 px); the dominant horizontal period (2.84 and 2.91 px) equals the beat period of the host grid (2.85 and 2.91 px).
- **Reading the numbers (assistant's, for the owner to weigh):** the difference in these renders is largely that host-grid beat, which CRT.md calls a non-physical artefact, and the product's tier C (below 3 px per triad) prefilters it away: the same file records the tier C path differing from scanlines-only by 0.006 codes at 1.54 px per triad. So on a 2560x1440 panel the visible differentiator at most presets rests on the scanline and beam model, and the mask matters only where it is resolved (3 px per triad and more; the two tubes rendered give 1.54 and 2.9 on this panel, and PLAN P-1 lists presets at 1.0 to 1.7). The images cannot say whether that is enough.
- **Question for the owner:** do the montages show a difference worth the project as scoped? If not: PLAN T-16 fail path (a stronger option D, host-subpixel masks HW-6, or re-scoping). Verdict to be written into SCP-7 and PHY-2 after the owner's look.

### Proposal 7: performance position (PRF-1, T-47, T-91; PRF-2, T-48)

- **Measured (T-47, release build, RTX A2000, 1920x1200 monitor since no 2560x1440 monitor exists on the machine, pattern sources; pixel-bound times expected about 1.4 times higher at the design size, not measured):** budget 4.2 ms (25 % of 16.7 ms) is met by no configuration. Defaults 4.6 to 6.0 ms; preset 09 7.2 ms; preset 01 flat 7.8 ms; flat-tube `screen` 5.2 to 6.4 ms against the 2.5 ms target of the M3 chain; curved presets 28.6 ms (01) and 59.1 ms (05); 5 % tail 162.5 ms. Presets 09 and 01 flat still hold 60 fps because the budget is a share of the frame, not the frame.
- **Since T-47:** T-91 (warp path) brings a curved tube to 0.35 to 0.39 times the flat tile path (headless debug build with validation, so not comparable with the live table, and the flat tile path is now the slower one: input for T-48).
- **Work to meet the budget:** T-92 (beam tail on a coarse pass, 6x cost), T-93 (flat `screen` 5.2 to 6.4 ms to about 2.5, `encode` 1.8 to 6.1 ms on detailed content, `phosphor` about 1.0, `abl` 0.5 to 0.8, `amp_samples` cap), then T-48. A live re-measure after T-91 to T-93 on a quiet GPU is needed (the GPU hit its power cap and then 86 to 87 C in the heavy runs).
- **Fallback if the budget is still missed:** quality tiers (T-48): reduce beam reach and tail, lower-resolution beam, FP16 arithmetic, tiers that change only approximation (mean abs error against reference at most 1 % of white), with automatic drop on sustained overrun (OUT-6).
- **Decision needed from the owner:** is 60 fps at the 4.2 ms budget a hard requirement on the reference PC (and at which output size, since D-18 says there is no single target), or is 60 fps held with a documented higher share, falling to a faster tier, acceptable? The plan default is 4.2 ms at the standard tier with the stand-in game running (T-48).

### Proposal 8: first-version scope (PRJ-6, D-5, D-6)

All three mask types stay in (D-5, already implemented: T-39a to T-39c); halation off in the first usable version (D-6, "No", M4; optics is still a placeholder slot); persistence, saturation, ABL stay settings off by default as built.

### Not settled by this gate

- The real-game CAP-4 verdict, input reaching the game, cursor behaviour (T-83).
- Hybrid-graphics behaviour and the shared-handle path (T-03, T-04 not run).
- PresentMon present mode, missed refreshes, latency and click-through results (OUT-2, OUT-4, T-59).
- Performance at 2560x1440 and after T-91 to T-93 on a live GPU.
- Visible value of the mask on this panel (owner's look at the T-16 images).
- Identity of the 25 s activation initiator; Night Light and 150 % scaling in CAP-3; other antivirus and anti-cheat (PRJ-4).
- Anything not in the eight proposals: D-15, D-3, D-4, D-8, D-10 and the remaining defaults keep their status.

## How to read this file

Each question has an id, the question, why it matters, who can answer, options when useful, and a recommended default.

| Who | Meaning |
|---|---|
| USER | Product-owner decision: preference, scope, budget, or hardware you own |
| SPIKE | Needs a small prototype or experiment on your real machine (hours to 2 days) |
| RESEARCH | Needs desk research (docs, licences, crate status), no code |
| DATA | Needs measurement data or material that is not yet in `CRT.md` |

Windows capture, presentation, Vulkan and HDR facts are the analysts' own knowledge unless a source is listed in "Feasibility facts to verify".
Every such fact that drives a decision is tied to a spike.

If you want to move fast, answer the "Decisions needed from you first" table and say "accept the defaults" for the rest.

## Decisions needed from you first

These are the questions all three analysts ranked as blocking.
Each has a recommended default so you can reply with exceptions only.

| Id | Question | Recommended default |
|---|---|---|
| HW-1 (partial) | What GPU and which monitor(s) do you play on: resolution, refresh rate, panel type (LCD, mini-LED, WOLED, QD-OLED), HDR peak nits, subpixel layout, VRR, one or two screens? | None: this blocks most model decisions. Please provide it |
| SCP-1, SCP-2 (answered) | What makes the model "physically accurate" (principle by construction, numeric checks, match to a reference tube), and do you own a real CRT or instruments (colorimeter, macro camera, photodiode)? | Physical by construction plus numeric invariants from `CRT.md`. Assume no tube and no instruments, and design validation not to depend on them |
| PHY-2 (answered) | When the host cannot resolve the mask (a PVM mask gets 2.3 to 2.7 px per triad on 4K, anything on 1080p), what happens: area-filter, zoom, enlarge the pitch, or use host subpixels? | Area-integrate by default with a live px-per-triad readout. Enlarged pitch only as an explicitly labelled non-physical option |
| COL-1 (answered) | Is HDR a requirement, an enhancement, or later? How is the 75 to 85 % mask loss handled in SDR? | HDR is the reference, not required. SDR uses an honest exposure control, no hidden bloom compensation |
| OUT-1, CAP-4 (answered) | Is the reference setup one monitor (output covers the game) or two? Does a covered, off-screen or minimised source keep rendering and keep delivering captured frames? | Design for both. Guarantee two monitors first, let a spike decide whether one monitor works |
| OUT-2 | Can a Vulkan window that is topmost, borderless, non-activating (optionally click-through) present fullscreen at full rate, with HDR, without ever taking focus? | Borderless topmost non-activating window, exclusive fullscreen disallowed. Spike before committing |
| OUT-3 | What exactly does "does not steal focus" cover, and where does the ImGui menu live? | DECIDED 2026-11-18: no separate control window. F12 toggles the ImGui settings menu inside the output window (it takes the focus while open, returns it on close); F11 toggles fullscreen and a resizable windowed output. Output otherwise never activates. Implemented by T-52a (menu key F10 by D-17, hotkey T-54; live check pending). |
| SRC-1, SRC-2 (answered) | Which content must work (pixel art only, emulators, DOS, modern 3D, video), which 5 to 10 apps form the acceptance set, and what does "1:1" mean in practice? | Low-resolution raster content first. Per-profile crop and integer divisor, with a warning when the capture is not integer-scaled |
| TEC-1 (answered) | Are Vulkan, GLSL, Rust and Dear ImGui hard constraints or preferences? Is cross-platform a real future goal? | Keep them. Isolate capture and presentation behind interfaces so a D3D path stays possible |
| PHY-12 | Temporal scope of the first version: frame-integrated, 60 Hz afterglow, or rolling-scan sub-frames at 120/240 Hz? | Frame-integrated first. Model emission as a function of time so sub-frames can be added later |
| TIM-1 | What added latency (source Present to photons) is acceptable? | 1 to 2 frames at 60 Hz, measured and documented, not promised as "zero" |
| PRJ-1, PRJ-2 (answered) | Personal or public project, which licence, and may GPL shader code be reused? | Personal first, public-quality architecture. Clean-room from `CRT.md` and papers, no GPL code copied |
| SCP-5 (answered) | Is "each source RGB pixel becomes real RGB light sources" literal, or shorthand? | Shorthand. Reword as "source RGB drives three beams; each phosphor element emits in proportion to the beam energy it receives" |
| CAP-2 | Zero-copy D3D11 to Vulkan interop, or CPU copy first? | Measure both in a spike. A CPU copy is cheap for a 320×240 source but not for a 4K capture. **Settled 2026-11-20:** both built and measured (see CAP-2); zero-copy is the default with an automatic fallback, the CPU copy a menu choice |

## Scope, definitions and acceptance

### SCP-1 Accuracy contract
- **Q:** What does "physically accurate" mean as a contract, and at which plane: light leaving the virtual faceplate, or light reaching the eye from the host panel?
- **Why:** Without a testable criterion the differentiator cannot be verified and tuning falls back to "looks right", which `CRT.md` criticises in existing shaders.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Options:**
  - Principle: every stage is a physical mechanism in physical units.
  - Principle plus numeric checks of effects that have published references (scanline modulation versus σ/P, mask transmission, halation ring radius, BT.1886 curve, decay residuals).
  - Photometric match to a specific reference tube.
  - Full spectral or light-transport model (out of reach with current data).
- **Default:** Principle plus numeric checks. The honest claim is "physically structured, parameterised, partially measured", because most parameters in `CRT.md` are estimates.

### SCP-2 Ground truth available
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** no real CRT and no instruments. All parameters come from literature and web research, tagged by confidence. Action: extra research passes on the weakest data (spot size versus current, decay tails, halation amplitude, consumer pitch, Trinitron curvature) before presets are frozen.
- **Q:** Do you own or can you borrow a real CRT (which model), a colorimeter or spectro, a macro-lens camera, a photodiode and oscilloscope, a high-speed camera?
- **Why:** A colorimeter unlocks primaries, white, gamma and uniformity. A photodiode unlocks decay. A macro lens unlocks spot size versus current, the largest data gap.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Assume none. Validate against `CRT.md` invariants and published photos, and state it. If a single tube is available, one well-photographed tube beats ten estimated presets.

### SCP-3 Per-effect acceptance tests
- **Q:** For each modelled effect, what is the pass criterion and test pattern?
- **Why:** It turns the `CRT.md` tables into executable checks and ends endless tuning by eye.
- **Who:** RESEARCH drafts, USER approves.
- **Examples:** a flat field of 240p lines gives 57 % modulation at σ/P = 0.25; a 1-pixel vertical line is dimmer than a horizontal one by the configured ratio; the halation ring radius equals 1.74 t within one host pixel; brightness is conserved across sub-frames.
- **Default:** Derive about 15 to 20 analytic checks from `CRT.md` before any shader is written.

### SCP-4 Meaning of "universal"
- **Q:** Universal along which axes: any capturable window, any CRT family, any content type, any GPU vendor, any Windows version, any host display?
- **Why:** Each axis multiplies the test matrix, and "universal" collides with "physically accurate" on the output side (see tension T-1).
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Universal over capturable windows and over CRT families (one parameterised model, presets per family). Not universal over host displays: accuracy tiers instead (PHY-2).

### SCP-5 Wording of the core requirement
- **Q:** Is "each source RGB pixel is transformed as real RGB light source" literal or shorthand?
- **Why:** Read literally it describes a pixel-sprite model. `CRT.md` shows a tube has no pixel grid: the beam is a continuous signal sampled by the mask, and pixel boundaries fall at arbitrary mask phases.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Shorthand. Update `CLAUDE.md` to the beam-and-phosphor wording above.

### SCP-6 Non-goals
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** ageing and burn-in (see PHY-14), composite video (see PHY-9) and interlace (see SRC-7) are all in scope, removed from the non-goals below. All three are optional modules, off by default, after the first usable version.
- **Q:** Confirm out of scope for the first version, each as its own decision: composite/S-Video/NTSC, interlace field simulation, audio, light-gun and pointer mapping, bezel and cabinet art, room reflections, ageing and burn-in, purity/doming, monochrome and vector tubes, projection CRTs, rotated (TATE) screens, recording and streaming, Linux/macOS, multi-window capture, RetroArch preset import.
- **Why:** "Anything else physically relevant" is unbounded, and each item adds a model, UI and tests.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** All out, except TATE (cheap if planned early, decide with the user) and pointer mapping (OUT-4). `CRT.md` already treats composite as a separate pre-stage.
- **DONE (2026-11-23), TATE:** `source_rotation` (off, counter_clockwise, clockwise) turns the tube a quarter turn for an upright, portrait capture; the scanlines become the columns of the picture. The chain works in the tube's frame, the turn is applied by `decode` and `encode` only (ARCHITECTURE A33), so it is exact (whole pixels) and the rest of the model needed no change. Open: the host LCD layout is off while turned; not tried on a real vertical game or a portrait monitor.

### SCP-7 Why a new application at all
- **Q:** What concrete result must Phoxium show that ShaderGlass plus crt-royale, crt-guest-advanced, Sony Megatron or the Blur Busters beam simulator cannot? Could the physical model first be prototyped as a RetroArch shader?
- **Why:** ShaderGlass already covers capture-and-overlay. If the value is only the physical model, the capture shell is plumbing, and if the value is the shell, the model is secondary.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Build the application. Three testable differentiators: voltage-domain horizontal response (dim isolated vertical lines), brightness-dependent spot in mm with mask sampling in mm, halation as a ring at the right radius. Build a headless renderer from the start so the model can be validated without the capture shell, rather than prototyping inside RetroArch.

### SCP-8 Physics versus taste
- **Q:** May the product include non-physical controls (bloom to recover mask loss, mask strength, scanline darkness), and who wins when the physical result is dim or ugly on your display?
- **Why:** On SDR the physical result is dark. Every existing shader cheats on brightness, and `CRT.md` cites cgwg's warning that "looks right" corrections are untrustworthy.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Options:** forbid; allow behind a clearly labelled stage after the physical chain; allow freely.
- **Default:** Allow, in a separate, labelled "host compensation" stage, off in reference presets. Exposure is honest (it is the panel's brightness); bloom compensation is not.

### SCP-9 Ideal tube or aged unit
- **Q:** Do defaults represent a perfectly adjusted new tube or a typical aged unit (misconvergence, corner defocus, vignette, geometry residuals, drifted white)?
- **Why:** Faithful defects make the picture objectively worse, and you may reject an accurate result as "ugly". Memories of aged consumer sets differ from a factory-adjusted PVM.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Factory-adjusted tube within service tolerance, with defects as parameters at the low end of their documented range.

### SCP-10 Where the model stops
- **Q:** Does the model end at the outer glass in a dark room, or include ambient light, specular reflections and viewing geometry?
- **Why:** Raised blacks and reflections dominate real contrast (ANSI 100 to 150:1) but need a room model that is not tube physics.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Dark room. One ambient-illuminance scalar raises blacks by the diffuse reflectance. No specular reflections in the first version.

## Source content

### SRC-1 Content classes and acceptance set
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** test inputs are a NES-style still snapshot (any game) fed as an image or test pattern, then Mina the Hollower as the first real-condition target. Any input resolution and colour count must work. The model only sees the captured RGB frame, so colour count has no effect, but resolution does through native-raster recovery (SRC-2). An image-file source type is needed from the start (see UX-5).
- **Q:** Which content must work in the first version (1:1 pixel art, emulators and which output modes, DOSBox, modern pixel-art indies at arbitrary window size, modern 3D, video), and which 5 to 10 applications form the acceptance set?
- **Why:** The model maps source pixels to beam current, which is well defined only for low-resolution raster content. At 1080 source lines a real CRT shows no scanlines either, and window modes, scaling, throttling and cursor use differ per application.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** 240p/288p/480p pixel art, emulators and DOSBox first. 3D and video are not refused, but nothing is tuned for them. You must name the applications.

### SRC-2 Knowing the native raster
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, scaffold):** per-run options `--crop x,y,w,h` and `--divisor N|NxM` (1 = native), plus automatic detection by default (run lengths of identical pixels and rows, largest surviving factor, 16 px margin for Windows 11 rounded corners). Only simple integer nearest-neighbour upscales are handled, by decision of the owner. Tested at x1, x3, x4, x5 on `pixel-demo`. Not yet done: per-profile storage, a menu control, a visible warning for smoothed sources.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-20, owner requirement):** a menu control and per-profile storage. The Source section has "Scaling multiplier": Auto (default, the detected value shown), None (the capture is native) or x1 to x10, X and Y separately under Advanced; stored in the source profile, applied live (native size, placement and readouts follow). The detection keeps running under a forced value, so the section shows "detected: x3" and a hint "detected x3, forced x2" when they differ; the forced value always wins. Black bars around the picture (letterbox) are found by the Auto picture area (CAP-7; first by "Detect black bars"), and the detection of the scale runs again on the cropped area.
- **Q:** How does the tool learn native resolution, crop and scale factor when the window is scaled (3×, 4×, non-integer, letterboxed, with emulator UI)?
- **Why:** The whole model starts from source pixels on known lines. A smoothed or non-integer-scaled capture cannot be recovered. This is the largest practical correctness risk, and the need does not mention it.
- **Who:** USER for policy, SPIKE for detection reliability.
- **Options:** require native 1× client area; user enters integer divisor; automatic detection of pixel repetition; user enters native resolution and the tool resamples.
- **Default:** Per-profile crop rectangle and integer divisor, an auto-detect "suggest" button, and a visible warning when the capture is not integer nearest-neighbour. Client area is the default crop.

### SRC-3 Mapping source rows to scanlines
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-19, T-96, PLAN D-13, D-18):** the host scale modes keep one scanline per source row x `line_multiplier` in every mode; `native`, `integer` and `fit` put each source row on exactly k whole host pixels (k = 1, `source_scale` or the largest that fits the actual output), so the scanlines align with the host pixel grid; `fit_free` is documented as breaking that alignment. The line multiplier is stored in the source profile.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-30):** `line_multiplier` (1 or 2, `params/source.toml`): lines = source rows x multiplier, no row resampling; the scanline pitch `line_pitch_mm` of `FrameConstants` = raster height / lines (PVM-20M4U class, 240 lines: 1.208 mm; multiplier 2: 0.604 mm). Fixed modes and a free "lines" value are not offered; the multiplier is not yet part of a tube preset (T-50). The beam stage (T-36a) must draw each native row as `line_multiplier` scanlines.
- **Q:** What defines the raster: one scanline per source row, fixed modes (240, 480, 576), double-scanned VGA 200-line modes, or a free "lines" parameter?
- **Why:** σ/P at 240p and 480p differs by 2, which flips visible scanlines on or off. A 320×200 DOS game on a VGA monitor (paired fine lines, 70 Hz) and the same pixels on a 15 kHz TV are different pictures.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Raster mode is part of the tube preset, overridable per profile: lines = source rows × an integer line multiplier (1 for 240p, 2 for double-scan), no row resampling.

### SRC-4 Aspect and pixel aspect ratio
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-19, T-96):** per-profile PAR (`par`, `--par 8:7`), a forced display aspect (`source_display_aspect`, `--aspect 4:3`) and `raster_aspect` (`--aspect fill`) stored in the source profile with `eotf` and the line multiplier; command-line flags override the profile. In the whole-number scale modes the rows are whole host pixels, the columns too when the PAR is 1 (a PAR of 8:7 gives 4.571 px per column at x4, reported as not integer-aligned).
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-30):** `tube_aspect` (4:3 default, any value, 16:9 and 16:10 as later presets), `raster_aspect` (`par` default: picture aspect = width x PAR / height, shrunk to the tube width when wider; `fill_tube`: the raster stretched to the whole visible area as on a real tube) and `source_par` (NES 8:7 gives 1.219 displayed aspect). The default is `par` with PAR 1 so a square-pixel capture keeps its aspect and the pre-T-30 picture is unchanged; `fill_tube` is the real-tube behaviour. PAR does not yet feed the pixel clock of the amplifier model (T-33). No per-profile PAR storage or system presets yet (T-50, T-51 profiles).
- **Q:** How are display aspect and PAR chosen (presets per system: NES 8:7, Genesis H40 32:35, VGA 320×200 5:6), and what happens with 16:9 pixel art (320×180) that never existed on a 4:3 tube? Are 16:9 and 16:10 tubes (BVM-D24, FW900) in scope?
- **Why:** The tube stretches the raster to cabinet aspect regardless of pixel count, so a 1:1 capture is wrong for non-square-PAR systems. PAR also changes triads per source pixel and the pixel clock used by the bandwidth model. A 4:3 image uses only 2880 of 3840 columns on a 4K 16:9 host.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Selectable tube aspect (4:3 default, 16:9 and 16:10 as later presets), per-profile PAR override.

### SRC-5 Source colour and gamma interpretation
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-30):** `source_eotf` = `drive_voltage` (default, V = code / 255 into the gun law) or `srgb` (sRGB EOTF to linear light, then V = L^(1/tube gamma), so the tube shows the sRGB luminance: code 20 gives 0.006995). The tube gamma is still the `Settings::source` stand-in in `decode` until T-34 moves it into the gun stage; the V in [0, 1] contract is in `shaders/decode.comp`. Per-profile choice (PLAN D-3: Mina starts on `srgb`) comes with the profile storage (T-51 settings).
- **Q:** What is the default meaning of a captured 8-bit value: CRT drive voltage reinterpreted with tube gamma (consoles), sRGB-authored PC content, or already-processed emulator output?
- **Why:** `CRT.md` rule 9 and the sRGB-versus-2.4 example (level 20/255) show this changes every shadow and saturation. It is a policy choice, not physics.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Per-profile source EOTF. Default: captured values are gamma-encoded drive voltages, tube gamma 2.4 above cutoff with no linear toe. "Decode as sRGB" as an option.

### SRC-6 Source cadence, virtual field rate and static content
- **Q:** Is the virtual tube's field rate the source rate (59.94, 60.1, 50, 70.09, 30 fps), a fixed value, or the host refresh divided by an integer? How does the model tell "source repeated a frame" from "source did not render", given the capture API only delivers frames when content changes?
- **Why:** It decides flicker, afterglow, whether rolling scan is possible, and what happens when no new frame arrives.
- **Who:** USER, informed by SPIKE SP-3.
- **Default:** The virtual CRT runs its own clock at the configured rate and re-scans the last captured frame. Rolling scan only when the host rate is an integer multiple within tolerance.

### SRC-7 Interlaced sources
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** interlace is in scope as an optional, manual mode after composite video, off by default. The user declares the field order and that alternate frames are fields (or that a woven frame is split into fields). Fields are shown alternately at the output refresh with the phosphor decay applied between them, which gives line twitter and flicker. Until then, and for any source without a declared field order, 480i and 576i are treated as 480 and 576 progressive lines (σ/P about 0.5). It needs a 60 Hz output matched to the field rate (the dev monitors are 60 Hz). Low value for the first test sources (NES, Mina the Hollower).
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-20, T-32; ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Interlace", A21):** `params/interlace.toml`, off by default and bit-identical when off. `interlace_mode` = `alternate_frames_are_fields` (each captured frame is a field; the bottom field lands half a line pitch lower) or `split_woven_frame` (a woven frame; each refresh draws the rows of one parity at twice the pitch, at their own positions); `interlace_field_order` (top or bottom first); `interlace_field_select` (alternate every output refresh, or lock one field; a locked field of a woven frame is the 240p double-strike look). The field follows the parity of the output frame counter, not the capture sequence; the persistence stage gives the inter-field decay; a notice shows when the output refresh differs from the field rate (5 % tolerance). Estimates, not sourced: the bottom field is shifted while the top field keeps the progressive position (a real tube centres the interlaced raster, a quarter line higher for the top field), and the 5 % tolerance. Open for a live run: whether a real interlaced source (a 480i emulator or video player) shows clean twitter with the output-counter parity, or needs the parity from the capture sequence.
- **Q:** Is 480i/576i field simulation in scope, given a window capture only yields woven or deinterlaced progressive frames with no field parity?
- **Why:** Field order and parity cannot be recovered reliably, and faking them may look worse than progressive.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Out of the first version. Treat as 480 progressive lines (σ/P(480) about 0.5). A manual "alternate frames are fields" mode later.

### SRC-8 Mid-session changes
- **Q:** Must the pipeline adapt live when the source switches mode or resizes (SNES 256 to 512 hi-res, PS1 mode switches, emulator window recreation), and what happens to history buffers (afterglow, ABL state)?
- **Why:** Geometry and line count are properties of the source mode, and resource reallocation can glitch.
- **Who:** USER plus SPIKE.
- **Default:** Hot-rebuild resolution-dependent resources within a frame, clear history on mode change, re-run crop and scale detection.

## Hardware and OS baseline

### HW-1 Your exact setup
- **ANSWERED (2026-11-18, read from the machine):** laptop with Intel UHD Graphics (reports 2560×1440 at 59 Hz) and NVIDIA RTX A2000 8GB Laptop GPU. Two active reference monitors (EDID: 2017 and 2018 units, 53×30 cm image area, 24 in 16:9, pixel pitch about 0.233 mm). Panel type and subpixel layout were not read from the machine: the reference monitor is an SDR IPS panel, presumably RGB stripe (to verify with a subpixel test pattern, see HW-6).
- **Consequence for PHY-2:** a 4:3 tube image at full height is 1920 px wide on this host. A 20 in pro tube (about 1245 triads) gets about 1.5 px per triad, a 26 in consumer TV (about 660) gets about 2.9, a 19 in arcade slot tube (about 480) gets about 4. Only coarse tubes approach resolvable, so option A (area-average) is the default and option D (coarser preset) matters.
- **Q:** Which GPU(s), CPU, monitors (resolution, size, refresh, panel type, HDR capability and real peak and sustained nits, subpixel layout, VRR) and how many monitors?
- **Why:** This single answer fixes host pixels per triad, HDR headroom (3 to 7×), whether rolling scan is possible and the performance budget.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** None. Blocking.

### HW-2 Windows baseline
- **Q:** Minimum Windows version: Windows 10 22H2, Windows 11 22H2/23H2, or 24H2 and later? Pinned or tracking?
- **Why:** Borderless capture (`IsBorderRequired`, build 20348+), cursor toggle and `MinUpdateInterval` differ by build. The reference PC runs an Insider build (10.0.26300), which may hide regressions present on release builds or show fixes that release builds lack.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Windows 11 only, x64, exact minimum build fixed after SP-1. Test also on a non-Insider build and re-run the spikes as a smoke test after updates.

### HW-3 GPU vendors, floor and hybrid graphics
- **FINDING (2026-11-18):** the reference PC is a hybrid-graphics laptop. Windows reports the Intel UHD Graphics as the adapter with the 2560×1440 desktop, and the NVIDIA RTX A2000 Laptop GPU as a second adapter with no display mode. Which GPU physically drives each reference monitor is not known yet. If the Intel GPU drives the monitors, the desktop compositor runs on Intel, the game may render on NVIDIA, and our renderer on NVIDIA would need a cross-adapter copy of every captured frame (no shared-handle handoff). Running the renderer on the Intel iGPU may be too slow. This is a new top risk, to settle in SP-1 (detect which adapter composes, measure the cross-adapter cost) and before choosing the Vulkan device.
- **Q:** Which vendors must work (NVIDIA, AMD, Intel Arc/iGPU)? What is the weakest GPU? Must hybrid-graphics laptops and multi-GPU desktops work, where the captured window, the compositor and the output may sit on different adapters?
- **Why:** External-memory import, HDR swapchain colour spaces and present behaviour are driver-specific. Cross-adapter capture forces a copy and complicates interop (same-adapter requirement).
- **Who:** USER, plus SPIKE if yes.
- **Default:** Your own GPU is the only certified target, others best effort. Single adapter only: select the Vulkan device by the capture adapter's LUID and warn on mismatch.
- **IMPLEMENTED differently (2026-11-18):** the scaffold does not match LUIDs yet. The Direct3D capture device uses the default adapter (NVIDIA on the reference PC) and the Vulkan device is the requested `--gpu`, else an NVIDIA discrete GPU, else any discrete GPU. The two agree on the reference PC, but nothing checks it and nothing warns on a mismatch. Which GPU composes the desktop is still unmeasured.
- **RESULT of the adapter diagnostic (2026-11-19, T-03, one run of `phoxium --list-adapters`, no window, no capture session, about one second):**
  - Windows lists three adapters. The NVIDIA RTX A2000 (LUID 00000000:00017801) has an attached monitor output (`\\.\DISPLAY5`, "Generic PnP Monitor", 1920x1080 at (0, 0)). The Intel UHD Graphics (LUID 00000000:00017426) also has one (`\\.\DISPLAY1`, "Generic PnP Monitor", 1920x1200 at (1920, -116)). The Microsoft Basic Render Driver (software) has none. So the monitors are split across the two GPUs, not only on the Intel iGPU. This corrects the 2026-11-18 finding that Windows showed the RTX with no display mode, and the monitor sizes differ from the two 2560x1440 reference monitor recorded in the study phase (the diagnostic reports what Windows sees now; the setup may have changed).
  - Vulkan lists two devices with a valid deviceLUID: the RTX A2000 (LUID 00000000:00017801, Vulkan 1.4) and the Intel UHD Graphics 770 (LUID 00000000:00017426, Vulkan 1.4). Each Vulkan LUID equals the LUID of the DXGI adapter of the same GPU.
  - The Direct3D 11 capture device, created on the default adapter, is on the RTX A2000. The renderer, with no `--gpu`, also picks the RTX A2000. Verdict printed: capture and render on the same adapter.
  - Which monitor the output covers decides whether presenting crosses adapters: on the monitor driven by the RTX there is no cross-adapter step for capture, render and present; on the monitor driven by the Intel GPU, the finished frame must reach the Intel GPU (done by Windows). That crossing was not measured. Not determined: which adapter runs the captured game (Windows graphics settings, per application).
  - External memory, from queries only (nothing allocated): both Vulkan devices offer `VK_KHR_external_memory_win32`. For B8G8R8A8 images, the D3D11 texture handle type and the D3D11 texture KMT handle type are both import yes, export no on both devices. So a shared-handle import of a D3D11 texture into Vulkan looks possible on the same adapter; exporting a Vulkan image to D3D11 does not. Whether the import really works is not tested (T-04).
  - The monitor names are the generic driver string "Generic PnP Monitor"; the diagnostic does not read the EDID model name.
- **DECISION (owner, 2026-11-19):** the GPU that runs the whole chain is a setting. **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-19, unit tests only, no live run):** `[global.gpu] adapter` in `settings.toml`: `auto` (default), an adapter LUID or a name part (changed 2026-11-20, review D-02: the menu stores the adapter name with its PCI ids, the LUID only as a hint, because a LUID is valid only until Windows restarts; a configured adapter that is missing is shown in the title, the window list and the menu); `--gpu <index>` wins. `auto` takes the adapter owning the monitor the output window covers (largest overlap of a DXGI output with the window rectangle), matched to the Vulkan device by deviceLUID, else the previous default (NVIDIA discrete, else any discrete, else the first). The capture's Direct3D 11 device is created on the same adapter (`EnumAdapters1` by LUID), and restarted on it once the output window exists if the first guess differed. Warnings (console and `adapters::current()` for the menu): a configured adapter or index not found (fallback to `auto`), an ambiguous name, a chosen adapter that does not drive the covered monitor, a renderer or capture device that ended on another adapter. `--list-adapters` marks the chosen adapter and prints the warnings. ARCHITECTURE 3.5, README "GPU choice".
  - **Only a live run can tell:** that `auto` picks the Intel UHD when the output covers the Intel-driven monitor and the RTX on the RTX-driven one (with the real window rectangles); whether Windows.Graphics.Capture delivers frames to a D3D11 device on the Intel UHD when the game renders on the RTX, and at what capture rate and cost; whether the chain holds the frame rate on the Intel UHD (GPU ms per stage with `--stats`); the cost and latency of a forced cross-adapter present (RTX chosen, output on the Intel-driven monitor) against the same-adapter case; the capture restart when the first guess differs (windowed output placed by Windows); and the behaviour when the output is moved to the other monitor after start (no re-selection, no warning).
  - **Monitor changes (2026-11-20, A32; unit tests only):** a move of the output onto a monitor of another GPU (drag, F11, display change) is now noticed: once a second the app samples the output's monitor; on a move `adapters::select` runs again on the new rectangle and `adapters::moved_notice` puts "moved to a monitor driven by another GPU (<name>): frames cross adapters (slower); restart to use that GPU" (or "the GPU setting keeps the current one") in the title, the console and the menu's GPU and system section; back on a monitor of the GPU in use it clears. The devices are never re-created during a session (decision: too risky for a rare event; a restart re-chooses). The automatic quality restarts its streaks on a move, the persistence's automatic rate follows the measured frame period, the UI scale follows the new DPI. Only a live run can tell the cost of the crossing and that the notice appears on the reference PC.
- **Live run (2026-11-19, 18:54 to 18:58, unattended, pre-release build):**
  - **Setup.** Covered source pixel-demo `--scale 3 --content motion`, `phoxium --window pixel-demo --fullscreen --stats --log`, 24 s per case, medians of the per-second values after 5 s. Each case had its own settings file in a temporary `APPDATA`; the owner's `%APPDATA%` has no settings folder of the old name, before or after. Logs: `target/live-results/step6*`.
  - `--list-adapters` gives the same topology as T-03: the RTX owns `\\.\DISPLAY5` (1920x1080 at 0,0), the Intel UHD owns `\\.\DISPLAY1` (1920x1200 at 1920,-116); verdict "same adapter".
  - **(a) Output on the RTX monitor, `auto`:** RTX for capture and render (`auto: owns the monitor the output covers`), no warning.
    - First run: capture delivered 1 frame, then 0 fps for 23 s; render held 60.3 fps. The source title showed 0.0/s at pickup and the divisor was not detected.
    - Rerun: capture 57.4 fps, render 60.4 fps, GPU 2.82 ms.
    - The cause of the stall is not known (1 run of 2). **Found 2026-11-19 (focusfix):** the source stopped, not the capture; see CAP-4 "Capture stall of the GPU-selection run". No capture restart happened in that run (no `capture: restarted` line).
  - **(b) Source and output on the Intel monitor, `auto`:** Intel UHD 770 for capture and render, no warning. Frames flow: capture median 40.4 fps (log average 44.8, minimum 35.6, 3 capture gaps over 50 ms, maximum 79.1 ms), render 51.5 fps, GPU 15.45 ms (`screen` 14.39 ms) on the 1920x1200 output. On the Intel UHD the default chain does not hold 60 fps.
  - **(c) Intel monitor, setting `adapter = "RTX"`:** RTX (`gpu.adapter setting`), with `warning: gpu: the chain runs on NVIDIA RTX A2000 ... but the monitor the output covers is driven by Intel(R) UHD Graphics ...: every presented frame crosses adapters`. Capture 59.2 fps, render 60.6 fps, GPU 2.51 ms.
  - **(d) Intel monitor, `--gpu 0`:** RTX (`--gpu`), with the same warning. Capture 58.6 fps, render 60.6 fps, GPU 2.99 ms.
  - **(e) RTX monitor, setting `adapter = "Intel"`:** Intel, with the reverse warning. Capture 42.3 fps (7 gaps over 50 ms), render 60.1 fps, GPU 12.35 ms.
  - **Across the five cases:** no crash and no foreground change. The GPU timestamps of the cross-adapter cases (c, d: 2.5 to 3.0 ms) are not higher than the same-adapter RTX case (2.8 ms). They do not contain the copy Windows makes to the other adapter, and the latency was not measured.
- **Zero-copy capture and adapters (2026-11-20, A29, CAP-2):** the zero-copy path (the default) shares textures between the capture's Direct3D 11 device and the Vulkan device, which needs both on one adapter. Since HW-3 creates them on the same adapter, it normally holds; when they differ (the capture fell back to the default adapter, an adapter vanished) the path is refused (one console line, the reason in the menu) and the CPU copy, which crosses adapters, is used. Import support was confirmed on the RTX A2000 (NT handle, headless test); the Intel UHD 770 reports the same import support by query but was not exercised.

### HW-4 Host display classes
- **Note (2026-11-18):** with SDR output, HDR display classes drop out of scope. Rolling scan (120 Hz or more) is deferred with it.
- **Q:** Which host classes are first-class and which degraded: SDR LCD, HDR LCD (FALD, mini-LED), WOLED, QD-OLED; 1080p, 1440p, 4K, 8K; 60 to 480 Hz?
- **Why:** On 1080p even a consumer-TV mask gets about 2.2 px per triad. Rolling scan needs 120 Hz or more and fast pixels, and `CRT.md` warns against strobed, FALD and mini-LED panels.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** First-class is your display. Others run in a documented degraded but honest mode.

### HW-5 Host characterisation
- **Q:** Where do the host's physical width, pixel pitch, peak luminance, primaries and EOTF come from: EDID, Windows HDR metadata, ICC profile, user entry, colorimeter?
- **Why:** Millimetre units, absolute luminance and gamut conversion need them, and EDID is often wrong. Windows Auto Color Management may also transform the output.
- **Who:** RESEARCH, then USER.
- **Default:** Read EDID and Windows values as initial guesses, always editable. SDR assumes sRGB unless a profile is given. Document the colour-management interaction.

### HW-6 Host subpixel use
- **Q:** Should the mask be aligned to the host panel's own subpixels (Megatron approach), and is rotated or portrait (TATE) output in scope?
- **Why:** It triples effective horizontal mask resolution for aperture grilles, and is the only way to resolve PVM-class pitch on 4K. But it is panel-specific (RGB, BGR, WOLED, QD-OLED triangle), needs 100 % scaling and 1:1 pixel mapping, is not a physical model of the tube, and breaks in screenshots and streaming. There is no reliable OS API for the layout.
- **Who:** USER plus SPIKE.
- **Default:** Panel-independent area-integrated mask first. Reserve a per-display "subpixel layout" profile field. Revisit once the panel is known.
- **DONE (2026-11-23):** `mask_host_subpixel` = off (default), rgb, bgr: each channel's mask value is the area mean over its own host subpixel (a third of a pixel wide, offset a third of a pixel), narrowing linearly through the blend band from the whole-period box; the tier thresholds are unchanged. A machine setting (kept by preset changes, like the quality tier). Oracle `crt_model::mask::HostSubpixel` and `host_value_subpixel`, GPU tests in `stage_mask_grille.rs` and `stage_mask_slot_delta.rs`. Open: a look on a real RGB and a BGR panel, lowering the 3 px limit for a subpixel panel (needs that look first), WOLED and QD-OLED layouts, a subpixel test pattern.

### HW-7 OLED wear and panel protection
- **Q:** Is the output an OLED, and are burn-in risk from static high-luminance mask and borders, and the panel's own ABL (which changes brightness with picture level and corrupts the CRT ABL and rolling-scan models), acceptable?
- **Why:** A fixed periodic mask at HDR peak is the worst case for OLED wear.
- **Who:** USER plus RESEARCH.
- **Default:** Document the risk. Offer a slow sub-pixel orbit of the mask. Never show a static UI by default.

## Capture

### CAP-1 Capture API
- **Q:** Windows.Graphics.Capture (per window, GPU texture, works occluded), DXGI Desktop Duplication (per monitor), or both? Can the capture border and indicator be disabled, and is the output window reliably excluded from capture (feedback loop)?
- **Why:** It defines latency, what can be captured, cursor handling, border, cost and the supported OS builds. Desktop Duplication cannot isolate one window and captures our own output.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-1).
- **Default:** Windows.Graphics.Capture only for the first version. Require a build where the border can be disabled.

### CAP-2 Getting frames into Vulkan
- **Clarification (2026-11-18):** there is always an intermediate copy of the source pixels. Windows.Graphics.Capture hands our process a GPU texture holding the composed pixels of the other application's window, made by the OS. "Zero-copy" and "CPU copy" only describe how that texture reaches our Vulkan renderer:
  - **Shared handle (zero extra copy):** the capture texture lives in a Direct3D 11 device (the capture API requires one in our process), and Vulkan reads the same GPU memory through a shared NT handle. No further copy.
  - **GPU-to-GPU copy:** copy the shared texture into a Vulkan-owned texture. One cheap GPU copy, simpler synchronisation.
  - **CPU round trip:** read back to RAM, then upload. Cheap for a small source, slow at 4K.
  - The captured frame is the window's size in screen pixels. If the game upscales its 1× image (for example 4×) before presenting, the capture is the upscaled image, and the native raster must be recovered by detection (SRC-2).
- **Q:** How does the capture texture (Direct3D 11) reach Vulkan: shared NT handle with fence or keyed mutex, or CPU readback and upload? Is zero-copy reliable on your GPU and worth it?
- **Why:** A CPU copy costs several ms at 4K and defeats "fully GPU". Unreliable interop on one vendor changes the architecture. For a small source the CPU copy is trivial.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-1).
- **Default:** Measure both. Sonnet and Opus favour zero-copy with CPU as a diagnostic fallback, Fable favours CPU copy first and shared textures only if latency demands. Pick after measuring the capture size you actually use.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18):** the CPU round trip (staging texture, mapped copy, Vulkan staging buffer), as the first working path. Windows.Graphics.Capture only delivers a frame when the window content changes. The shared-handle path is not measured yet.
- **SETTLED (2026-11-20, owner request "zero-copy capture addition"; ARCHITECTURE A29):** both paths exist; the CPU copy stays the default (the most universal: any adapter pair), the zero-copy path is an optional, experimental setting (`[global.capture] path = "zero_copy"`, the menu's Performance section, `--capture-path zero-copy`). **Changed the same day (owner request "add this non-zero-copy option (CPU) as a parameter in the main menu (by default: zero-copy)"):** zero-copy is the default; when the machine cannot run it (no extension, no import, capture on another adapter) the CPU copy is used without a title notice, and any runtime failure still falls back to the CPU copy with a notice; a stored `path = "cpu"` is respected, a file without the key gets the default (settings of v0.2.0); the choice moved to a "Capture" combo in the Source section, applied at once in both directions: the Vulkan device now enables the sharing extension whenever it can import, so no restart is needed (the CPU path is bit-identical with or without it, tested). Going back to the CPU copy: the menu, `path = "cpu"`, or `--capture-path cpu`. The headless tests add the live switch: 60 frames in lockstep over zero-copy, CPU copy and zero-copy again (no frame lost or repeated, including a frame kept for want of a free slot at the switch), and 300 frames with a concurrent writer and a switch every 20 frames (every frame whole and newer than the previous). It is a GPU-to-GPU copy into a pool of 3 shared textures (the "GPU-to-GPU copy" variant above, the capture texture itself is never shared: WGC recycles it), imported into Vulkan once per pool with `VK_KHR_external_memory_win32` (NT handle, KMT as a fallback) and sampled in place by `decode`. Synchronisation: an event query waited on the capture thread after the copy (option A of A29); no fence or keyed mutex was needed.
  - **Correctness, headless (`tests/zero_copy.rs`, RTX A2000, validation and synchronization validation on, 0 messages):** the decode output and the whole default chain's output are bit-identical to the CPU path for the same pixels (the same `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` texels and `texelFetch`); the crop equals the CPU path's (the real staging read-back compared); 300 frames read back one by one while a writer thread published new frames into the 3 slots as fast as it could (11 585 frames written, every one copied with the real capture code), some frames two in flight: every frame complete (no tearing), the frame taken (never a stale slot), the crop intact; a new pool size is imported and the old one released; a device without the extension and a bogus handle are refused cleanly and the CPU path goes on.
  - **Cost per frame (measured 2026-11-20, `tests/zero_copy_bench.rs`, release build, headless, NVIDIA RTX A2000 Laptop GPU, NT handles; median [10th-90th percentile] of 240 frames, 120 at 4K, ms; three runs, the first shown, the medians of the others within the ranges given below; the laptop GPU is shared with the desktop and throttles, so the numbers are relative).** Capture side = the capture thread's work per frame (wall time). GPU upload = the buffer-to-image copy of the CPU path (GPU timestamps); zero-copy has none. Render frame = one whole headless frame with the chain off and a 64 x 64 target (staging memcpy, submission, the fence wait): its difference between the paths is the render thread's share of the copy. Decode = the `decode` segment, both paths (the zero-copy acquire and release barriers are in it).

    | Source | CPU path: capture copy + map + rows | Zero-copy: GPU copy + event wait | CPU path: GPU upload | CPU path: render frame | Zero-copy: render frame | Decode, CPU / zero-copy |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1280 x 720 | 0.511 [0.508-0.572] | 0.063 [0.058-0.086] | 0.287 [0.286-0.289] | 1.955 [1.583-2.358] | 1.499 [1.365-1.764] | 0.060 / 0.062 |
    | 1920 x 1080 | 1.166 [1.143-1.236] | 0.109 [0.107-0.135] | 0.642 [0.640-0.646] | 3.100 [2.351-4.107] | 1.738 [1.484-2.392] | 0.120 / 0.111 |
    | 2560 x 1440 | 3.147 [2.597-4.637] | 0.208 [0.178-0.275] | 1.138 [1.135-1.146] | 5.692 [4.457-7.356] | 2.910 [2.394-4.013] | 0.222 / 0.190 |
    | 3840 x 2160 | 6.439 [5.764-9.511] | 0.417 [0.357-0.508] | 2.564 [2.554-2.584] | 11.182 [8.664-17.027] | 3.897 [3.485-4.793] | 0.475 / 0.370 |

    Medians over the three runs: CPU-path capture side 0.51 to 0.55 (720p), 1.17 to 1.66 (1080p), 3.15 to 3.77 (1440p), 6.44 to 7.36 (4K); zero-copy capture side 0.063 to 0.064, 0.105 to 0.112, 0.21 to 0.28, 0.42 to 0.50; render frame CPU path 1.7 to 2.0, 2.9 to 3.1, 5.7 to 6.8, 11.2 to 12.8; zero-copy 1.3 to 1.5, 1.7 to 1.8, 2.8 to 2.9, 3.9 to 4.3.
  - **The old question (PLAN T-04: does the CPU copy exceed 3 ms at the real size?):** at 1280 x 720 and below, no (about 0.5 ms on the capture thread plus about 0.4 ms on the render thread). At 1920 x 1080 it stays under (1.2 to 1.7 ms plus 1.1 to 1.4 ms). At 2560 x 1440, the size of the development monitors, the capture thread alone takes 3.1 to 3.8 ms and the render thread about 3 ms more; at 4K about 7 ms each. Zero-copy brings the capture thread to 0.06 to 0.5 ms and removes the upload (the render frame of the measurement drops by 0.4 ms at 720p, 2.8 to 3.9 ms at 1440p, 7 to 8.6 ms at 4K). A pixel-art game in a window rarely needs it; a game upscaled to a 1440p or 4K window does.
  - **Still to verify live** (no window or capture session was used): a real WGC capture on a real window in zero-copy (frame pacing and the capture fps against the CPU path; the yellow border; a window resize, which makes a new pool; minimise and restore; a driver reset or device removal during the session; the Intel UHD as the chosen adapter, which reports the same import support; a covered source, CAP-4); the analyses fed by samples (integer-scale detection and the Auto picture area lock as on the CPU path); the fallback notice when capture and render end up on different adapters; the CPU cost of the samples (about ten read-backs per second); the menu's live switch both ways on a real window (no hitch, no black or stale frame at the switch); a first start with a v0.2.0 settings file (zero-copy chosen by itself); the window validation layer silent with the extension now always enabled (`--validation`).
- **Live finding (2026-11-20, evening review, see "Live review on the real desktop"):** zero-copy crashed intermittently at attach (10 of 35 starts on the RTX, 4 of 5 on the Intel UHD, once at a source resize): a race between the capture thread's sample read-back and the main loop taking the previous frame left that frame with an empty pixel buffer marked as its own. Fixed the same day (the capture thread no longer empties a sample not taken; the CPU analyses read only a sample consistent with the frame; the detectors validate their input). The zero-copy default stands; 30 starts per GPU are still to be repeated live.

### CAP-3 Bit-exactness of the captured pixels
- **Note (2026-11-18):** output is SDR only. Desktop HDR is off on the reference PC: the owner says so and both active monitors (reference monitor) have no HDR. The registry showed no HDR flag either way, so this rests on the hardware. If HDR were on, the capture would be scRGB with an SDR white level, and original code values would have to be recovered.
- **Q:** Are captured pixels identical to what the application rendered under DPI virtualisation, DWM scaling, HDR desktop mode, Auto HDR, Night Light and ICC colour management? With the desktop in HDR, how are the original SDR code values recovered (FP16 scRGB, SDR white-level slider)?
- **Why:** Any scaling or colour transform before the model silently breaks the 1:1 premise.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-5).
- **Default:** Assume the native composed surface and verify with a test-pattern application. Ship a "capture integrity" check in the tool.

- **Result (T-12 / SP-5, 2026-11-19, release build of the pre-release `main`, Windows 11 26300, RTX A2000, desktop SDR, `scripts/check-capture.ps1`):** a known 256x240 PNG (grey, R, G, B ramps of all 256 codes, a 1-pixel checkerboard, seeded random noise; shown by `pixel-demo --image --scene image`) was captured with `phoxium --save-capture` and compared pixel by pixel after removing the scale (every scale x scale block must equal the source pixel).

  | Condition | Capture size | Max diff (codes) | Differing pixels |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | windowed, scale 1, 2, 3, 4, 100 % DPI (primary monitor, 1920x1080) | exactly source x scale | 0 | 0 |
  | same, left half covered by a topmost window | exactly source x scale | 0 | 0 |
  | scale 1, 3, 4 on the second monitor (window DPI 120 = 125 % scaling, client resized to exact size) | exactly source x scale | 0 | 0 |

  Findings: (1) the capture is bit-exact in every tested condition, a partly covered window included (the captured surface is the window's own composition, not the screen); (2) the 36 pixels of the two bottom window corners (54 at 125 %) are darkened by the Windows 11 rounded window corners (up to 250 codes off): a 10x10 pixel corner zone is excluded from the compare and reported apart, T-56 should crop or mask it (this also touches the client-area crop: the source's last rows near the corners are not usable); (3) at 125 % scaling the title bar and borders grow, so a 1040x999 window opened on a 100 % monitor and dragged to a 125 % monitor has a smaller client area (254x232 for 256x240 at scale 1) until it is resized: the capture then simply has that size (pixel-demo does not handle `WM_DPICHANGED`, a real game may); (4) a window taller than the monitor (scale 5, 1200 px on a 1080 px screen) is captured clipped at 1280x1181, so source windows must fit on a monitor; (5) off-screen parts of a window are not captured (confirmed with scale 4 placed partly below the monitor: 14136 differing pixels).
  Not tested: Night Light on, 150 % scaling, a DPI-unaware source under DWM virtualisation, HDR/Auto HDR (HDR is off), colour management profiles. System display settings were deliberately not changed; the 125 % second monitor was the only way to see a non-100 % DPI. Owner to repeat with Night Light on (pending). CAP-3 verdict for the default configuration: pass (bit-exact, bottom corners excepted).

### CAP-4 Covered, off-screen, minimised or throttled sources
- **Q:** When the fullscreen output covers the source on the same monitor, or the source is off-screen, minimised or on another virtual desktop, does the source keep rendering and does the capture keep delivering frames?
- **Why:** This is the natural one-monitor scenario. Engines and browsers may throttle or pause when occluded or unfocused, Windows may drop a flip-model game out of independent flip, and some capture implementations refuse minimised windows.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-1, blocking), on several sources (emulators on D3D11/Vulkan/OpenGL, a Unity or Godot game, DOSBox, a browser).
- **Default:** None until measured. Fallbacks to evaluate if it fails: keep 1 px of the source visible, move the source to a virtual display, or require a second monitor.

- **Result (T-06 / SP-1c, 2026-11-19, PROVISIONAL: synthetic source, pixel-demo scene at scale 4 = 1040x999 window, 1024x960 client, native 256x240, divisor 4x4 detected; no real game, D-13; release build, `--fullscreen --stats --log`, primary monitor 1920x1080, output covering it; the PC was in interactive use during the runs, see below):**
  - **Covered on the same monitor:** 100 s analysed (the run ended at 101 s of the 120 s asked, see below): capture fps avg 57.2, min 49.7, p5 54.5, median 57.3, sd 2.6; render fps avg 60.1 (58.3 to 61.4); one capture interval above 50 ms in 100 s (62.2 ms max), zero render gaps above 50 ms (max 47.2 ms); the source kept animating and the capture kept coming for the whole run (the capture never fell to 0). Windowed non-covered control (`--no-activate`, 29 s): capture avg 56.2, one 66.1 ms gap. So covering the source costs nothing measurable: the below-60 capture rate is the source plus the compositor (pixel-demo paints from an 8 ms `WM_TIMER`, so about 56 to 58 distinct frames per second reach the capture), not the cover. Nothing in the numbers shows the source being throttled or paused when occluded (pixel-demo is a plain GDI window, the easy case).
  - **Foreground:** the output did not take the foreground when it appeared. 27.4 s after the start of the covered run the foreground window became the phoxium output (poll-foreground and phoxium's own log agree) and stayed so to the end. The same event at the same offset (27.4 s) appeared in a windowed `--no-activate` run (50 s: foreground moved to the output at 27.4 s, then to another application at 30.4 s). A 45 s solo run without the poll script and the second-monitor run (`--monitor 0`, ended at 26 s) showed no such event. The poll log also shows foreground changes made by other applications in use during every run, so a foreground change cannot be attributed to the tool. **Open: the zero-foreground-change criterion is not settled; rerun hands-off.**
  - **Early exits:** the two fullscreen runs ended at 101 s (of 120) and 26 s (of 60) while other work was going on on the same screens: most likely quit with Ctrl+Alt+Q (the quit chord then) because the output covered it. Interference from interactive use of the PC, not a tool failure.
  - **Cursor:** the cursor stayed visible (flags 1, arrow) over the output in every run; its position moved between runs (owner). Not tested: input reaching the source (no game, no input synthesis allowed).
  - **Second monitor (`--monitor 0`, bonus mode, 26 s analysed):** capture avg 56.3, min 49.4, no capture gap above 50 ms, one render interval of 62.4 ms.
  - **Source minimised (during a windowed run):** capture fell to 0 fps within about 1 s and stayed 0 for the 9 s of minimisation, the output kept showing the last frame at 60 fps, and capture resumed (15 fps, then 52 fps) as soon as the window was restored. As expected, a minimised source delivers no frames. Started with the source already minimised, `--window pixel-demo` did not find it and fell back to a substring match on another window (the terminal whose tab title held the exe path): window selection should ignore terminals or require the process, for T-56.
  - **Verdict: PROVISIONAL, synthetic source.** Throughput criterion (avg >= 58 fps): 57.2 fps average, 0.8 fps under, equal to the uncovered control and limited by the source; gap criterion (no gap above 100 ms): pass (max 62 ms); foreground criterion: undetermined, see above; input reaching the game: not tested. A real game on Vulkan or D3D (T-83) is still required for the final CAP-4 verdict.
- **Hands-off rerun (T-06, live-B, 2026-11-19 09:49 to 10:02, unattended run; synthetic source: pixel-demo scene at scale 4, 1024x960 client, native 256x240, divisor 4x4; release build of the integration head of that day; poll-foreground at 100 ms, `--stats --log`; output on the primary monitor 1920x1080 (monitor 1), second monitor = monitor 0):**
  - **Covered, same monitor, 3 runs of 120 s** (117 s logged each; the first log row, a startup burst, is excluded): capture fps avg 56.8 / 57.0 / 57.2 (mean 57.0), min 52.9 / 50.7 / 49.8, p5 53.9 / 53.8 / 53.7; capture intervals above 50 ms: 5 / 3 / 3, max 66.3 / 65.9 / 60.9 ms, none above 100 ms; render fps avg 60.3 in all three (min 59.3), no render gap above 50 ms (max interval 47.8 / 47.1 / 48.2 ms); capture never fell to 0. Run-to-run variance is small (0.4 fps on the mean). GPU frame about 4.7 to 5.2 ms.
  - **Foreground: FAIL, reproducible, caused by phoxium, not by the owner.** In every one of the 7 runs the foreground window changed from the source to the phoxium output exactly once, 24.4 / 25.5 / 25.6 s after the start of the run in the phoxium log (poll CSV: 28.3 / 28.0 / 28.4 s after the poll start), and stayed there until exit, when it went back to pixel-demo. Same event windowed with `--no-activate` (25.4 s), with the output on the second monitor (25.3 s), with the source minimised (25.4 s) and with `--pattern ramp` (no capture at all, 26.4 s): so it is independent of the mode, the monitor and the capture. Control with the poll script and pixel-demo only, no phoxium, 45 s, unattended: no change (1 baseline row). Idle seconds never dropped below 297 in any run, so no keyboard or mouse input reached the PC. This explains the 27.4 s event of the live-A runs. Cause not found: `output.rs` calls no `SetForegroundWindow` (windows created `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`, `ShowWindow` with `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` when not activating), so a Windows-side activation of the topmost output about 25 s after creation is suspected; to investigate (window messages `WM_ACTIVATE`, `WM_MOUSEACTIVATE`, `WM_NCACTIVATE` and the thread that triggers them) before T-07.
  - **Windowed `--no-activate` on top of the source, 60 s:** capture avg 52.0 (min 45.8, p5 47.7), 2 intervals above 50 ms (max 65.9 ms), render avg 60.1; lower than the covered runs (about 57) and than the live-A windowed control (56.2): not explained, maybe the source window partly occluded or the output on top throttling it; to repeat before concluding.
  - **Second monitor (`--monitor 0`), 60 s:** capture avg 53.7 (min 48.9, p5 49.8), 1 interval above 50 ms (max 64.9 ms), render avg 60.1. Same foreground event at 25.3 s.
  - **Source minimised (windowed `--no-activate`, 45 s, minimised without activation at 15 s, restored without activation at 30 s):** capture 52 fps until 14 s, 19 fps in the second of the minimise, then 0 fps for about 14 s (one capture interval of 14.87 s), then 56 fps again in the first second after the restore; the output kept rendering at 60 fps (avg 60.1, no render gap) showing the last frame. Expected failure mode, documented.
  - **Render control (`--pattern ramp`, 60 s, pixel-demo running underneath, no capture):** render avg 60.1 fps (min 59.4), max interval 22.8 ms, zero gaps above 50 ms. The render side paces at the display rate by itself, so the 57 fps capture average is source plus capture pacing, not render pacing.
  - **Pass/fail against the T-06 criteria:** covered average >= 58 fps: FAIL by 1.0 fps (57.0), the limit is the source (pixel-demo paints from an 8 ms `WM_TIMER`, about 57 distinct frames per second), so the criterion cannot be met with this stand-in; no gap above 100 ms: PASS (max 66.3 ms); zero foreground changes: FAIL (one per run, at about 25 s); input reaching the game: not tested. Cursor not re-measured.
  - **Idle seconds (before / after run, minimum during), seconds since the last keyboard or mouse input:** aborted first start (argument bug) 263.9 / 277.7; r1 297.8 / 433.4 (304.7); r2 462.2 / 596.9 (469.0); r3 598.8 / 733.3 (605.6); no-activate 737.9 / 812.5 (744.8); monitor 0 814.4 / 888.5 (821.2); minimised 890.3 / 949.8 (897.1); ramp 951.5 / 1025.6 (958.3); no-phoxium control 1044.8 / 1098.1. No process left running after any run; Ctrl+Alt+Q (the quit chord then) was never needed or sent.
