- **DONE (2026-11-24, ARCHITECTURE A38), the safe part of the remaining REV-2 items:** the video device opens on its worker and its drop is bounded (the status is `Opening`, `Running(size)` or `Failed(why)`; the drop shuts the Media Foundation source down from the dropping thread and waits at most 1.5 s; unit tests with a worker that never returns); only a `gpu::GpuError` ends the session (`render_error_is_fatal`), any other render error skips the frame and prints once; a panic in the `FrameArrived` callback is caught and recorded for the loop (D-05); a single-instance guard (named mutex `Local\\Phoxium-single-instance`, `--allow-multiple`, runs with `--exit-after`, `--snapshot` or `--save-capture` exempt); `scripts/release.ps1` compares asset SHA-256 and the draft's target commit before publishing (checked against fakes and the public v0.7.0 `.sha256` asset). **PHY-12, FP16 history with stochastic rounding, accuracy study on the CPU** (binary16 emulated with numpy, 20000 independent pixels, `h = rho h + (1 - rho) e`, 600 static frames then 400 black, rho 0.5 to 0.99, drive 1 and 0.01): round to nearest is biased (mean error 0.1 % at rho 0.8, 2 % to 3.6 % at rho 0.99) and stalls in the black tail (a residue of 1e-7 to 6e-7 where f64 reaches 1e-39 to 1e-9, a visible afterglow that never ends); stochastic rounding is unbiased (mean error at most 1.4e-5 at rho 0.99, the black tail follows f64 to three digits) with a per-pixel spread of at most 4e-3 of the value at rho 0.99, about one 8-bit level. Verdict: stochastic rounding is usable, round to nearest is not; at rho 0.99 the noise is at the edge of visibility, so the `fast` and `low` tiers only, as the TODO says. The study is now a CPU unit test, `crt_model::fp16` (binary16 rounding in f64, nearest with ties to even, and stochastic from `noise::cell`, with the claims above as assertions); the shader change and its GPU test against it are not done. Still open, needing a live capture: a static source blank after a zero-copy import failure, the `UNDEFINED` first transition of an imported slot, the video stride, the no-signal card.
- **MEASURED (2026-11-24, GPU session), larger sources and the frame budget; this replaces the design note above, whose claim that `screen` does not depend on K was wrong:** RTX A2000 Laptop, headless, 2560x1440 output, game content, total frame ms (`screen` in brackets). **standard**: 640x480: defaults 2.7 (2.3), 01 flat 8.9 (5.9), 01 9.8 (6.7), 05 13.7 (10.1); 1280x720: defaults 7.2 (6.2), 01 flat 30.3 (23.0), 01 32.4 (24.9), 05 48.4 (39.5). **fast**: 640x480: 2.8, 4.3, 5.1, 6.7; 1280x720: 7.3, 11.6, 13.4, 18.8. **low**: 640x480: 2.3, 3.9, 4.4, 5.9; 1280x720: 6.0, 9.9, 11.2, 15.8 (configurations in the same order). So the `fast` tier already cuts `screen` by 2.2 to 2.8 times at these sizes (the K rule shrinks the amp image that pass 1 of `screen` walks), and `fast` keeps 640x480 near 5 ms and 1280x720 near 13 ms (18.8 for the slot TV). **Where the time goes (a temporary patch that skipped one dispatch, reverted, not committed):** at the standard tier 1280x720, preset 01 flat, `screen` 23.6 ms is pass 1 (`screen_lines`) about 19.5 ms and pass 2 (`screen_warp`) about 2.2 ms (01 curved: 19.4 and 4.2); at 640x480 pass 1 is 4.2 and pass 2 1.0 to 1.6; at `fast` 1280x720 pass 1 is 4.7 to 4.9 and pass 2 2.0 to 3.9. Pass 1 is about 85 % of `screen` at the standard tier and is linear in K (19.5 at K 16, 4.7 at K 4): each entry loops over the amp samples within its reach and runs `beam_run_x` for every run of unequal samples, so its cost is source pixels x K x reach. Pass 2 is a few ms and not the target. **Error of the fast K rule at these sizes (`tests/quality_tiers.rs` with `PHOXIUM_TIER_NATIVE` and `PHOXIUM_TIER_TARGET`, 2560x1440 output):** the standard tier is within 0.011 % MAE and 0.2 % max of exact at every size. `fast` on game content: MAE 0.10 % to 0.12 %, max 1.1 % to 1.2 % of white (640x480 and 1280x720, every configuration); on noise 0.16 % to 0.20 %; on a ramp 0.004 % to 0.006 %. The worst case is the 1-px checker, the Nyquist stress pattern: MAE 0.88 % to 1.17 %, max 1.3 %, so it exceeds the 1 % MAE contract of the tier on the slot TV (1.17 %) at these sizes, and the contract test stays at 320x240 on 1920x1080 (worst fast 0.66 %, max 2.0 %). **What follows.** (1) At the standard tier a source above about 800x600 (the 16.7 ms frame is reached at about 500 kpx on 01) misses 60 fps by itself, and 1280x720 misses 30 fps on the slot TV (48 ms); the automatic drop (`quality_auto`) reacts to overruns, so a user with it on gets `fast` there. (2) The cheap lever, already built, is the fast K rule: applying it by default above a source size would move a standard-tier picture by about 0.1 % MAE on game content and up to 1.2 % on the worst stress pattern, which breaks the standard contract (MAE 0.5 %) on that pattern only. (3) **DECIDED (2026-11-24, owner): `quality_auto` is on by default** (the picture changes only when the frames are late; a fixed rule by source size would break the standard contract on a 1-px checker and tie the rule to one GPU). Still to check live (not possible headless): the reaction time (3 s of overrun before the first drop, so a large source hitches for about 3 s), no flapping between tiers, the way back up, and that the title and the overlay show the tier in force. A user who selected `exact` is also dropped when the frames are late; the title and overlay say so. (5) **FP16 history, the gain measured** (a temporary swap of the three history images and their GLSL layouts to rgba16f, timing only, the picture was not checked, reverted): `phosphor` at 2560x1440, standard tier, game content, 0.93 ms to 0.43 ms (01 flat), 1.00 to 0.45 ms (01), 0.59 ms for 05 after the swap; about 0.5 ms saved, as the TODO assumed, i.e. 13 % of a 3.9 ms frame at 320x240. Together with the CPU study (stochastic rounding is unbiased, round to nearest is not) the shader change is justified for the `fast` and `low` tiers; it also needs a format switch on a tier change (the images are reallocated, the history restarts). (4) An exact speed-up of pass 1 (merging near-equal amp samples is not exact; shared-memory staging of the sigma terms would not touch the sample loop) is not an easy bit-exact win, so no change was made.
- **LIVE CHECKS (2026-11-24, unattended session, the user away; every run with `APPDATA` pointed at a scratch folder, so the settings of the PC were untouched):** `check-capture` EXACT at x1 to x4 on the zero-copy path and again with `--capture-path cpu`; `check-pixel-demo` PASS (layouts, resize, corners, rates 30 to 120 Hz); `check-menu` PASS windowed and fullscreen (10 of 10 opens to the output and closes to the source); `check-hotkeys` PASS; `check-focus` MITIGATED (one foreground blip handed back in 0.12 s, no focus event on the output). **Automatic quality drop, default on:** pixel-demo 1280x720 on the slot TV preset, output 1904x1011: the first run flapped (drop to `fast` after 3 s at 33.7 ms, back to `standard` 10 s later because `fast` had the headroom, dropped again at 36.3 ms, each time 3 s at about 31 fps). Fix: a raise also needs the tier above predicted to fit, from the cost ratio of the two tiers measured at the drop (`quality::Probe`), else it waits for 160 s of headroom in a row; second run: one drop after 3 s, no other change in 70 s, 60 fps. **Default window:** a first start filled the work area (0,30 to 1920,1080 on the reference PC). **Validation layer:** a windowed run with `--validation`, zero-copy, a tier drop: no message. **Native dialogs on the multithreaded-COM thread:** a probe opened and closed both dialogs without a hang (1.3 s and 3.5 s until a helper thread closed them). **Second unattended hour (screen allowed):** the zip of v0.9.0 unzipped into a scratch folder runs (fresh settings, 640x480 slot TV, 11 ms per frame). Fullscreen, 1280x720, slot TV: drop to `fast` after 3 s (35.0 ms), 11.6 ms afterwards; the source switched to 256x240 with the N key of pixel-demo: `quality back to standard` about 10 s later, 4 to 6 ms, no flapping. The DPI check of `check-pixel-demo -MoveTo` passes on the 125 % monitor (window dpi 120). The overlay (`Ctrl+Alt+F9`) shows `fast (auto, selected standard)` and the menu (`F10`) opens, both seen on a cropped screenshot of the output window only. **Intel UHD 770, live (the monitor it drives, `--fullscreen --monitor 0`, 1920x1200, slot TV preset, 640x480 source):** the chain runs, the drop goes `standard` to `fast` (91 ms) to `low` (43 ms) and stops at `low` at 38.5 ms, about 24 fps, with the capture down to 45 to 60 fps; no flapping. **Capture check:** `check-capture -Occlude` and `-MoveTo` (the 125 % monitor, dpi 120) EXACT. **Window picker:** the output opened with no source, the search text `pixel-demo` was typed and Enter pressed (keyboard only, no screenshot because the list shows the open windows): the first match, a terminal whose title held the path, was attached, the source profile was saved and the foreground went to the source. **F11 from a fullscreen first start** (nothing remembered) gives a centred framed window of 1296 x 972 (the older default size), not the work area; the first-start window of the picker and of a source is the work area. Decision for the owner: use the work area there too. **Defect found:** with no source (the picker) the first-start window opened on the first monitor of the enumeration, here the non-primary one; fixed after the release (primary monitor first, `output::default_work_area`), see the CHANGELOG. **CPU-copy capture:** the read-back now runs into a buffer of the capture thread and is swapped under the frame lock, so the render thread's `take_frame` no longer waits for it (the zero_copy tests, 11, and the capture check above pass). **Intel UHD 770 (finding):** the smoke run of the gate chose the Intel adapter (the monitor layout of the PC had changed: one monitor on each GPU) and printed `max per-stage storage image bindings count (22) exceeds device maxPerStageDescriptorStorageImages limit (16)` at `vkCreatePipelineLayout` (the optics layout, see the TODO); `--pattern ramp --snapshot` with `--gpu 0` and `--gpu 1` differs by 1 of 255 at most, so the picture is right on the Intel GPU.
- **DECIDED (2026-11-24, owner), the two open design questions of the performance work:** (1) the FP16 phosphor history with stochastic rounding is **dropped**: the gain is 0.5 ms at 1440p (3 % on large sources, 13 % at 320 x 240, which already fits the budget), and the history format switch would reset the phosphor trails at every automatic quality drop; the CPU study (PHY-12) and the timing stay as the record, and it can be reopened for a 144 Hz screen or a weaker GPU. (2) Weak GPUs get a **documented limit, no new tier**: a coarser internal resolution would change the look, which the tiers never do; the levers are a smaller output window or a lighter preset (README, REFERENCE known limits). Not measured yet, and not planned: which stage dominates on the Intel UHD 770 (it would show whether a cheaper mask method could help). (3) The **prefix reuse** (skip the decode-to-`screen` chain on refreshes with no new source frame) was a **go, after the optics layout fix**; it was built and measured on 2026-11-25 and dropped (see the TRIED AND DROPPED note).
- **DONE (2026-11-24, the owner's unattended evening), capture robustness:** a static source stayed blank after a zero-copy failure on the render side, because the CPU path had no frame to copy until the window redrew. The capture thread now keeps the newest captured frame while it runs zero-copy and copies it through the CPU path at once when the path switches (`Worker::last`, `retry_pending`); the frame pool has 3 buffers instead of 2 so that holding one never starves the capture. Test `a_static_source_is_copied_at_once_when_the_path_switches_to_the_cpu_copy` (12 zero_copy tests pass); live: `check-capture` EXACT, `check-pixel-demo` rates 30 to 120 Hz PASS, capture 59 fps and render 60 fps in a phoxium run. The other suspicion of the TODO item, the `UNDEFINED` first transition of an imported slot, is closed: the headless tests read the right pixels and the validation layer printed nothing in the live runs. **Not done in that evening, still on the TODO:** the optics layout (its composite pass alone reads 18 storage images, so it needs the pyramid levels packed into one atlas image or a similar restructuring, about half a day with byte-comparison against the presets), the prefix reuse (starts with a written plan), the permanent GPU timestamp (marks are chained per pipeline segment and the segment list feeds the stats line, the CSV columns and the profile tool, so it touches all of them) and the PRF-2 candidates.
- **DONE (2026-11-25), the optics layout (Intel UHD 770, `maxPerStageDescriptorStorageImages` 16):** the composite pass reads its 18 images (the diffusion and halo images, the top, the 12 pyramid levels, the three bloom lobes) as **sampled images** (`texture2D`, `texelFetch`, `GL_EXT_samplerless_texture_functions`; the exact texel, no filtering) in a set 1 layout of its own, and the passes that write images keep a set 1 layout trimmed to the storage images 0 to 4 and the weights buffer; so a pipeline layout has 6 storage images at most (the composite 1, plus 19 sampled images against the 200 of the Intel driver; its `maxPerStageResources` is 200 too). The images got the `SAMPLED` usage and the barriers `SHADER_SAMPLED_READ`. Checked: 28 offline float renders (nine optics configurations on two patterns, all three halo methods, bloom, the low tier, diffusion, glare and vignette, and the ten presets with bloom) are **byte-identical** to those of the 0.9.2 binary, and each configuration gives a distinct picture; the optics tests pass; `--gpu 1 --validation` printed the error with the 0.9.2 binary and prints nothing with the new one (and nothing on the NVIDIA card).
- **TRIED AND DROPPED (2026-11-25), the prefix reuse (PRF-2; the owner had said go, after the optics layout fix):** it skipped `decode` and the slots `signal` to `screen` on a refresh with no new source frame, when the parameters, the settings, the view, the geometry of the frame constants (not the frame index and the times) and the routing were unchanged and every prefix stage that runs was static (`amp`, `gun` and `screen` with interlace off; `abl`, composite, impair, tape and tv never, so the consumer TV presets 03, 05 and 06, which have ABL on, never reused); the cache was committed at submit. **It worked as designed:** three headless tests (a refresh; parameter and frame changes with persistence on; bursts of frames in flight under the validation layer; the time-dependent stages never reused) showed every picture **byte-identical** with and without it. **Measured live on the RTX A2000 (AC power), PVM preset, a 1280 x 720 source:** at a 5 fps source the mean GPU time fell from 7.2 to 4.4 ms; at a **30 fps source (the typical game on a 60 Hz screen) it rose from 7.3 to 11.5 ms**: the frames that still recompute take about three times longer (`screen` 16 ms against 5, `amp` 4.4 against 1.2) because the GPU powers down between the bursts; `nvidia-smi`: utilisation 72 to 80 % against 50 %, graphics clock 1.6 to 1.76 GHz against 1.88, power draw not lower (59 to 65 W against 60 to 64 W); render stayed at 60 fps in both. The first frame after an idle stretch (a static menu, then action) also took about 40 ms against 7 ms. At a 640 x 480 source (GDM preset, 30 fps) the mean GPU time fell by about 10 %. So on this hardware the reuse saves nothing and delays the frames that matter; the code and its tests were reverted (nothing of it is in the repository). Reopen only for a GPU that keeps its clocks (a desktop card), or as a power-saving mode measured at the wall.
- **DONE (2026-11-23), decoder realism and analogue impairments (TODO.md Medium):** the composite module already followed the carrier phase per pixel, per line and per frame (dot crawl); it gained six neutral-by-default decoder controls (hue slope along the line, burst jitter per line, second-axis error, red gain, green matrix) and a new slot `impair` (hum bar, line jitter and crawl, ghost, noise and snow, line tracking) with a shared integer hash for its random numbers (ARCHITECTURE A34, `crt_model::impair`, `crt_model::noise`). All amounts are estimates (no measured values in `CRT.md`); GPU against oracle to the FP16 step. Open: not checked by eye on a real source in motion; the impairments act on the decoded RGB, after the composite module.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-20), composite part (T-31):** the `signal` slot (before `amp`, voltage domain) carries the optional composite / S-Video module, off by default (`params/composite.toml`, oracle `crt_model::composite`, ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Composite video", A22): NTSC (I/Q, 1.3 / 0.5 MHz) or PAL (U/V with the V switch), subcarrier phase per pixel (f_sc / f_px, the clock of `amp_pixel_clock_mhz`), per line (frac(f_sc / f_line): 180 degrees NTSC, 120 degrees NES timing) and per frame (`composite_frame_advance_deg`, 180 or 0), an optional Gaussian channel with burst-referenced colour gain, notch or band-split 1-line comb (PAL: delay line), tint and colour. It gives chroma bleed, cross-colour (artifact colours of fine luma patterns near f_sc) and dot crawl (the rainbow on fine diagonal detail follows from the same cross-colour); S-Video is a parameter choice and RGB is the module off. Open: the pixel alignment still relies on the native-raster recovery (SRC-2); the subcarrier phase of each machine at the start of a line (`composite_phase_deg`) is not documented in `CRT.md` and is a user setting; adaptive comb filters and the encoder's I/Q filters are not modelled; only a live run with a real 8-bit-era source can confirm the look.
- **Q:** Is the video-amplifier stage in the first version: horizontal low-pass in the voltage domain, asymmetric rise/fall slew (adjacent-pixel nonlinearity), peaking, cable reflection? How is the source pixel clock defined for a captured window, since bandwidth in MHz needs one? Is composite/NTSC ever in scope?
- **Why:** `CRT.md` rule 1: blurring in linear light is wrong, and one-pixel vertical lines come out 5 to over 50 % dimmer than horizontal ones. The pixel clock is not provided by the capture and must come from the video-mode preset.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Asymmetric horizontal filter in the gamma domain in the first version, with a per-profile "bandwidth in source pixels" parameter. Composite excluded, with a defined insertion point before the tube model.

### PHY-10 Optical stage
- **Q:** Which optical effects come first: halation ring (radius 1.74 × glass thickness, 17 to 25 mm, hundreds of host pixels), veiling glare, phosphor-layer diffusion, tint and thickness uniformity, vignette, reflections? Which default amplitude within the 0.5 to 5 % uncertainty?
- **Why:** The ring is non-separable and dominates GPU cost, and the amplitude range changes the look.
- **Who:** USER for default and priority, SPIKE for cost (SP-8: FFT versus mip pyramid versus ring sampling).
- **Default:** Halation (low-resolution ring kernel, about 1.5 %), short-range diffusion and vignette in the first optical milestone. Reflections and ambient out.
- **SETTLED (2026-11-20, T-44 cost study and T-45 implementation; off by default per D-6 and D-24):** the `optics` slot implements, in physical units (`params/optics.toml`, oracle `crt_model::optics`): phosphor-layer diffusion (fraction and Gaussian sigma in mm, energy conserving), the halation halo (shape derived from the glass: Lambertian emission, Fresnel reflectance of the glass side rising to total internal reflection at asin(1 / n), return at r = 2 t tan(theta), absorption T^(2 / cos(theta)); ring edge 1.747 t, a Fresnel haze inside holding about 9 % of the halo for n 1.52 and T 0.85, the tail truncated at 99 % of its energy; first return only; the amplitude `optics_halation_weight` is the final fraction of the emitted energy, default 1.5 %, because no source separates the ring amplitude), veiling glare (uniform, `optics_glare_weight` x APL, default 1 %), vignette (v^(rho^2), corner / centre 0.85), per-channel glass tint (neutral by default: the tube white is measured through the glass) and diffuse ambient reflection (lux, T^2 r_s, tube white in cd/m^2; specular reflection out). Every effect redistributes light, so a full white field keeps its level away from the edges. Presets 01 to 09 set the faceplate thickness, halation weight and (05) transmission from the CRT.md starting presets; none enables the stage.
- **LIVE FINDING AND RESOLUTION (2026-11-20, real desktop review):** switching `optics_enable` on lifted the blacks by 20 to 27 codes even with the glow at 0, because the defaults of the halation (0.015) and the glare (0.01) were on. **Resolved:** `optics_halation_weight` and `optics_glare_weight` default to 0 (opt-in; the physical values 1.5 % and 1 % stay in the tooltips with the warning that a glass veil lifts the blacks). Measured through the whole chain (`look_recipes_whole_chain`, a 320 x 240 source at 960 x 720, 8-bit display codes of the green channel; far = 240 px from a white window on black, beside = 15 px, inside = a black window in a white picture): Optics on at the new defaults 0, 0, 0 (off: 0, 0, 0); old defaults 13, 28, 30; glare 0.01 alone 13, 13, 29; halation 0.01 alone 2, 21, 9; 0.015 alone 2, 25, 11; 0.03 alone 3, 34, 15. The presets keep their explicit halation (1 to 3 %; none sets a glare weight; presets 06 and 10 set none), so enabling Optics with a preset gives that preset's halation veil and no glare. The glow itself lifts blacks only through its wide lobes: weight 0.12, radius 6 mm, threshold 0.6 gives 0, 16, 0 at falloff 0, 2, 24, 9 at falloff 0.5 (the default), and 3, 33, 14 for weight 0.2 at falloff 0.7; the README now recommends falloff 0 for a glow that keeps the blacks black. The README vignette recipes were revalidated on the GPU against the model (`vignette_recipes_keep_the_sides`): the old black-corners recipe left the middle of the left edge at 27 % (4:3) and 13 % (16:9) of the centre; corner 0, start 0.6, exponent 4, softness 0.2, roundness 0 keeps 99 % on both with a black corner; roundness 0 is needed on wide pictures (round puts the middle of the left edge of a 16:9 picture at 87 % of the way to the corner, 0 at 71 %). Chromatic aberration lateral 3 mm gives colour moire at the sides: the README and tooltips advise 0.5 to 1 mm (monitor), 1.5 to 2 mm (TV), axis 0.2 to 0.5 mm.
- **Cost study (T-44), headless (`tests/stage_optics.rs`, `optics_cost_study`, ignored, run by hand).** Tube images 1920 x 1080 and 1920 x 1200 at 4.966 px/mm (PVM-20M4U tube across the width), glass 12 mm, n 1.52, T 0.85 (ring edge 104.1 px, halo support 619 px), every effect on with a 5 % halo and a 0.4 mm (2 px) diffusion, a game-like frame. GPU time of the whole `optics` slot, bursts of 40 back-to-back frames (single frames with readbacks left the GPU idle and read 2 to 10 times higher), each paired with a burst of a calibration pass (one full-resolution read and write); the RTX A2000 Laptop was clock- and power-limited and shared, so the calibration pass itself ranged from 0.105 to 0.6 ms between bursts: treat every number as +/- a factor 2 to 3, the ranking and the orders of magnitude as the result. Accuracy against the f64 oracle (FFT convolution with the pixel-integrated kernel): mean and max absolute error in % of white on the frame; ring profile: point source, radial profile error relative to the oracle ring peak, max / mean, and the half-peak radius of the ring's inner edge (oracle 103 px).

  | Method (1920 x 1080) | Slot median / min ms | MAE % white | Max % white | Ring max / mean % of peak | Edge half-peak px |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | kernel, 16 taps (64 px cells) | 1.22 / 1.05 | 0.022 | 0.25 | 58.9 / 4.7 | 51 |
  | **kernel, 24 taps (32 px cells, chosen)** | **1.14 / 1.08** | **0.010** | **0.13** | **48.6 / 2.8** | **80** |
  | kernel, 32 taps (same level here) | 2.81 / 2.52 | 0.010 | 0.13 | 48.6 / 2.8 | 80 |
  | kernel, 48 taps (16 px cells) | 4.12 / 3.00 | 0.007 | 0.13 | 37.6 / 1.1 | 95 |
  | pyramid (least-squares mix of 11 levels) | 4.53 / 3.86 | 0.24 | 1.90 | 72.3 / 9.7 | 83 |
  | gaussians (4 separable, full resolution) | 65.5 / 63.4 | 0.033 | 0.23 | 46.8 / 5.0 | 3 (no ring) |
  | no halo (diffusion, glare, vignette, tint, ambient) | 2.59 / 2.37 | 0.005 | 0.11 | - | - |
  | composite alone (vignette, tint, ambient) | 0.52 / 0.46 | - | - | - | - |
  | composite with the 2 px diffusion | 2.30 / 0.93 | - | - | - | - |
  | pyramid and composite (glare) | 0.33 / 0.28 | - | - | - | - |
  | calibration pass | 0.48 / 0.38 (best burst 0.105) | - | - | - | - |

  At 1920 x 1200 (same px per mm) the errors are the same within 0.001 point (kernel 24 taps: MAE 0.010 %, max 0.135 %; pyramid 0.245 % / 1.80 %; gaussians 0.033 % / 0.22 %), the times 1.5 to 3 times higher in that run (calibration median 0.48 ms; kernel 24 taps 3.03 / 1.49 ms, gaussians 80 ms). Earlier runs of the same study gave the kernel at 24 taps 1.51 to 1.68 ms minimum with the calibration at 0.12 ms, and showed the diffusion as the largest single term (0.7 to 0.9 ms with a shared-memory implementation, replaced by two 1D passes).
- **Choice:** the `kernel` method at 24 taps (`optics_halo_method` = `kernel`, `optics_halo_taps` = 24): the exact physical kernel as cell masses on one level of a zero-padded box pyramid (the finest level whose texels keep the kernel within 24 texels; 32 px cells for a 619 px support), bilinear upsample. It is the only candidate that keeps a ring (the pyramid mix and the Gaussian mix are centre-peaked glows: half-peak at 3 px for the Gaussians), it meets the error budget by a wide margin (MAE 0.010 % against 0.5 %, max 0.13 % against 4 %), the halo energy is the configured weight +/- 0.0001 (tests), and its halo part costs about as much as the glare's pyramid (0.3 ms measured with the composite): the stage at 1920 x 1080 measured 1.05 to 1.14 ms with every effect on and a 2 px diffusion, about 0.4 ms scaled to the best calibration burst; the <= 1.0 ms target holds for the halo, and for the whole stage when the clocks are up or the diffusion is small (the default 0.15 mm is a 3 px kernel radius instead of 9). Its known limit: a single-pixel source has its ring's inner edge smeared over one cell (half-peak at 80 px against 103 px, profile error up to 49 % of the ring peak at the edge, 2.8 % on average); larger content is not affected (frame errors above). 48 taps halves the cells (95 px) at about 3 to 4 ms. The `pyramid` and `gaussians` methods stay selectable (advanced) for comparison.
- **Open:** the ring amplitude (no source), a live measurement of the stage on the owner's machine with a game (the headless numbers above are noisy), a sharper edge at the same cost (two levels: the ring core on a finer level, the tail on a coarser one), the specular reflection of the room, the second halo ring.
- **Owner request (2026-11-20) and resolution:** "is there a way or parameters to add some GLOW?" and "need also more control, be more pushy around the VIGNETTE". The halation ring is thin and far (1.75 times the glass thickness); the glow meant is a smooth bloom around bright areas. Resolved (ARCHITECTURE A31, oracle `crt_model::optics::{Bloom, Vignette}`): (1) **glow**: `optics_bloom_weight` (0 = off, slider to 0.3, hard 0.6), `optics_bloom_radius_mm` (1/e radius of the main lobe, 0.5 to 60 mm, default 6), `optics_bloom_falloff` (0 = one Gaussian, 1 = lobes at 3 and 9 times the radius for a long tail; default 0.5), `optics_bloom_threshold` (soft knee, 0 = all light, default), `optics_bloom_tint_rgb` (per channel, advanced); energy conserving, the weight joins the halation and glare sum (at most 1); (2) **vignette**: the physical law v^(rho^2) stays the default bit for bit, plus `optics_vignette_enable`, `_start`, `_exponent`, `_softness`, `_roundness`, `_centre_xy`, `_floor`, and `optics_vignette_corner` down to 0 (black corners). The menu's Optics panel shows the master switch first and says in its first line that every effect needs it (`optics_enable` stays off by default so the 0.2.0 look and the presets are unchanged). Physical part: the glass thickness falloff and the scatter that the glow stands for; beyond them (low corners, late start, high exponents, thresholds) it is an artistic grade. Open: how it looks on a game (live run), the right default amount once seen, and whether the threshold's feed pass (about 4 full-resolution read-and-write units headless) is worth a cheaper form.

### PHY-11 Geometry and viewing model
- **Q:** Which geometry is in scope: spherical and cylindrical curvature, flat outer face with curved inner face (refraction, concave look), pincushion and S-linearity residuals, overscan, rounded corners, tilt? Is the mask laid out in tube-surface coordinates with the beam distortion separate? Is curvature a perspective view from a fixed eye point?
- **Why:** Physically the mask is fixed to the tube and geometry errors move the beam, so warping everything with one UV transform gives wrong moiré. Curvature breaks input alignment (OUT-4), worsens host pixels per triad at the edges, and has no sourced radius for cylindrical Trinitrons.
- **Who:** USER plus RESEARCH.
- **Default:** Flat or simple spherical/cylindrical mapping, overscan and rounded corners first. Mask in tube coordinates. Ray-cast curvature through the glass, convergence polynomials and distortion terms later. Fixed centred eye at a stated distance. Radii as tunables.
- **Implemented (T-37 and its review, 2026-11-19):** flat, spherical and cylindrical faceplates seen from a centred eye at a stated distance (ray cast to the phosphor surface, raster uniform in arc length), overscan per axis, residual pincushion/barrel, trapezoid and rotation on the beam only (mask in tube coordinates), per-channel convergence (static plus radial), ABL raster breathing, and the rounded-corner visible opening (`geometry_opening_corner_radius_mm`, light exactly 0 outside). Overscan and `raster_scale` keep the spot's size in mm and dim the picture by the raster's area. **Deferred:** (1) S-linearity residuals: CRT.md gives the cause (beam position proportional to tan theta, corrected by S capacitors) and the residual size (0.5 to 2 % of the height, 2 to 5 % horizontally on TVs, BVM-D20 under 0.5 % centrally) but not the residual's profile across the picture, so a one-parameter remap would be invented; needs a measured or published linearity curve (RESEARCH). (2) Refraction through the faceplate: CRT.md states the method (ray cast through the outer surface, n about 1.53) but gives outer radii and thicknesses only for a few flat-face tubes, not per curved preset; needs per-tube outer radius and glass thickness (RESEARCH). (3) The local magnification of pincushion, trapezoid and rotation on the spot footprint (anisotropic, up to 1 + 3|k| radially in the corner) is not applied. **Cost (T-91, 2026-11-19):** the non-separable geometries no longer force the per-pixel reference beam (T-47: `screen` 4.7 to 6.1 times the flat cost live): the standard quality evaluates the beam per raster line into stage-owned line images and resamples them per host pixel through the geometry (exact vertical integral, Catmull-Rom along the line at >= 1.2 grid steps per spot sigma, mask applied after in tube coordinates; ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Warp path"). Against the reference: worst MAE 0.055 %, worst max 3.71 % of white over spherical (eye at infinity, 600 and 300 mm), cylindrical, pincushion +/- 0.02, trapezoid, rotation and a convergence-plus-overscan case; a flat field keeps its luminance. Curved `screen` costs 0.35 to 0.39 times the flat tile path on presets 01 and 05 (1536 and 1920 x 1440); the footprint semantics (Lambertian, local magnification not applied) are unchanged, so (3) stays as stated.

### PHY-12 Temporal scope and phosphor decay
- **Q:** Which temporal model in the first version: none (frame-integrated), 60 Hz frame-feedback afterglow, or rolling sub-frame scan at 120/240/480 Hz? Which decay model and numbers: the `CRT.md` two-term fit to the Sony patent residuals, the fast or slow literature tables, or measured values? Is flicker at 50 to 60 Hz acceptable?
- **Why:** Faithful rolling scan needs N times the peak luminance on top of mask loss (HDR), FP16 linear history buffers, exact cadence, fast pixels, panel ABL off and no strobe or FALD. At 240 Hz only 4 sub-frames per field exist. Published decay times differ by about 100×, so only measurement settles them, and the green and blue tail drives the ghost trail: at 60 Hz about 5 % of a frame's green and blue energy reaches the next frame, then about 3 % and 2 % the frames after; red is negligible (T-63, Kuhn 2002 P22 fit, CRT.md "Decay and persistence"). Real impulse behaviour is visible flicker and a photosensitivity concern.
- **Who:** USER plus DATA.
- **Default:** Frame-integrated first with an optional colour-dependent afterglow feedback term, `CRT.md` fitted decay (tagged [D]) exposed as sliders. Rolling scan as a later milestone, only on displays that pass a capability check, off by default with a warning.
- **Frame-feedback part implemented (T-41, 2026-11-18):** `phosphor` slot, off by default (`phosphor_persistence`). Decay numbers: Kuhn's P22 fit (T-63), not the 0.5 to 4 % range (T-63 showed that range was a residual luminance, not a per-frame energy ratio); per-frame ratios exact at 1 to 3 frames (green 5.36 / 2.97 / 2.03 %, blue 4.72 / 2.60 / 1.78 % at 60 Hz, red none), the power-law tail fitted by four exponentials over 1 s (within about 1 %), static picture brightness kept exactly in steady state (a cleared history is primed to the steady state of the first frame, so a static picture has no fade-in; after a picture change the trail shows for about 1 s, by design). Exposed: frame rate (the output refresh) and a per-channel tail strength 0.07 to 1. Rolling scan and flicker remain open.

### PHY-13 Whole-frame dynamics
- **Q:** Are ABL, raster breathing (EHT sag) and DC-restoration errors in the first version?
- **Why:** They need a frame-wide average (a reduction pass, a frame of latency or an estimate), a time constant, and temporal state, with constants from memory, and they fight the host panel's own ABL.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Not in the first version. The architecture keeps a per-frame global state for them.
- **SETTLED (2026-11-18, T-35) for ABL and raster breathing:** implemented, off by default (`abl_enable`, `abl_breathing` = 0). GPU reduction of the frame mean beam current, low-pass on real frame times, gain applied with one frame of latency, state kept on the GPU (no readback). Constants from CRT.md (T-63): threshold 1.4 mA [S], tau = 0.5 s / 3 [D] (settling 0.5 s), full-white current 2.8 mA [E] chosen so the onset of a black-to-white step is tau ln 2 = 0.116 s (CRT.md: about 0.1 s), full white 50 % of a small window [E]. DC-restoration errors remain open.

### PHY-14 Second-order effects
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** ageing and burn-in are in scope, opt-in and off by default:
  - **Ageing (tube-wide):** phosphor efficiency loss per colour (blue wears fastest, so the white point drifts warm), lower maximum beam current and cutoff drift from cathode wear, slightly larger spot. Parameters, not a simulation of hours.
  - **Burn-in (local):** a per-colour wear map on the tube surface, accumulating beam energy over time. Each element's efficiency is a decreasing function of its accumulated charge, applied in the phosphor emission stage. A persistent HUD or letterbox bar leaves a ghost, tinted by the colour that wore.
  - **Time:** wear takes thousands of real hours, so the model needs a time-acceleration control, a "burn in now" tool and a reset ("new tube"). The tube state is saved to disk between sessions and is tied to tube coordinates, not to the output window.
  - **Data:** only one sourced figure (15 % loss after 20,000 h at 350 cd/m², one datasheet). The colour ratios and wear curve are estimates [E].
  - **Implemented (2026-11-20, T-42, T-43; ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Ageing and burn-in", A20):** wear law 1 / (1 + c Q) (Pfahnl's coulombic ageing law [K], decelerating, so burn-in saturates), Q in hours of full white at 350 cd/m², calibrated so the tube white keeps 85 % after 20,000 h [S]; colour rates red 0.5, green 1, blue 2 [E] (`ageing_rates_rgb`). Ageing: hours of use x mean level x white luminance (100 cd/m² default [S]) as a uniform charge; cathode emission loss and cutoff drift per gun and a spot growth factor as direct parameters [E] (CRT.md has no cathode wear curve). Burn-in: a 640 x 480 per-colour map over the visible area, accumulated from the beam charge with `burnin_acceleration` (1 = real time), "Burn in N h now" and "New tube" menu buttons, `--reset-burnin`, saved per tube preset in `%APPDATA%\Phoxium\tubes\<preset>.state`. Still open: the colour ratios, the curve shape beyond the one calibration point and the cathode wear sizes need data (a measured ageing curve per P22 phosphor); whether the efficiency should act before or after the phosphor saturation (it acts before, on the fresh light, today).
- **Q:** Which of these are in the first version: phosphor saturation (blue and green sulfides), backscatter desaturation, convergence offsets, damper wires, doming, purity patches, black-matrix diffuse reflection?
- **Why:** Each is cheap to add but invites a parameter without data.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Damper wires (cheap, iconic) and polynomial convergence in the second phase, saturation as a per-channel soft clip behind a toggle, doming and purity out.

### PHY-15 Imperfections and determinism
- **Q:** Are random terms (dot size variation, mask irregularity, jitter, line swim) included, and must they be seeded and reproducible?
- **Why:** Reproducibility for tests and screenshots, and noise affects moiré.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** None in the first version. Any added noise is seeded.

### PHY-16 Independent sliders or coupled physics
- **Q:** Are parameters independent controls, or coupled through the operating point (raising contrast raises beam current, spot size, ABL activity and raster size together)?
- **Why:** "All parameters tunable live" and "physical" pull apart: free independent sliders allow tubes that cannot exist.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Two layers. Physical tube parameters (preset, rarely touched) and the tube's own user controls (brightness, contrast, focus, colour temperature) that act through the physics. An expert mode unlocks everything.
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** the owner chose independent parameters (mask type is a 3-way choice, every other look parameter is independent, "impossible tubes" are allowed, possibly with warnings). The two-layer default above is superseded.

### PHY-17 Chromatic aberration (colour fringes)
- **Q (owner, 2026-11-20):** "Same for chromatic aberrations? Is there a way, parameters?" The only colour-fringing controls were the per-colour convergence offsets of the geometry stage (static dx, dy per beam, radial terms marked advanced): nothing discoverable called chromatic aberration.
- **Why:** On a real tube the coloured fringes come from (1) static and dynamic misconvergence: the three beams land a little apart, a constant offset plus a term growing toward the edges, red and blue roughly mirror images about green in an in-line gun (CRT.md "Convergence, purity and landing": 0.2 to 0.6 mm centre and 0.3 to 0.8 mm corners on monitors, up to 2 mm in TV corners, 2 to 3 mm on cheap sets); (2) per-gun focus differences (a different spot size per colour); (3) dispersion of the faceplate glass, negligible (the light leaves the phosphor at the glass surface, no lens: a lateral colour of the eye-to-phosphor path through a few mm of glass is far below a triad, and CRT.md has no figure for it). CRT.md has no separate note on the deflection yoke's lateral colour or per-gun focus: (1) is the convergence table above.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Resolution (2026-11-20, ARCHITECTURE A30):** (1) as a first-class group "Chromatic aberration" in the Geometry panel, not advanced, with a one-sentence note: `geometry_chroma_lateral_mm` (red and blue apart at the picture corner along the radius, red outward by half, blue inward by half, green fixed; slider -10 to 10 mm, files -20 to 20: each beam then moves at most the 10 mm hard limit of one beam's convergence), `geometry_chroma_axis_mm` and `geometry_chroma_angle_deg` (the same separation everywhere along an angle, clockwise on the screen like the rotation; 0 to 5 mm, files up to 20), `geometry_chroma_edge_exponent` (growth (r / rc)^e of the lateral term, 1 to 3, files 1 to 4, default 2 = the quadratic polynomial of CRT.md and of the radial convergence term). They add to the convergence controls (kept, as the per-beam "advanced convergence" controls), through the same hook evaluated where the light lands (`geometry_convergence_px`), so every beam path and quality tier has them: tile path, line-image path, reference form and curved warp path (on a curved faceplate the separation is a distance on the phosphor, seen foreshortened like the raster, about 3 % at the corner of a 300 mm sphere). The tile and line-image paths take the offset at the column and line centre, as for the radial convergence: worst error against the oracle 0.04 px (5 mm lateral, exponent 3, in a corner), 0.006 px in the reference form. The skirt margin and the warp path's line-image pad include half of each amount. Both amounts at 0 (defaults): bit-identical to the build before (17 scenes recorded, every tier, the ten presets as shipped, convergence set flat and curved). Oracle `crt_model::geometry::Chroma`; tests `tests/stage_chroma.rs`, `tests/stage_chroma_reference.rs`.
- **Follow-up (open):** (2) per-gun focus (`beam_focus_chroma`, the red and blue spot widths as ratios to green, 0.8 to 1.5) is not implemented: the spot law has one set of widths for the three guns (`beam_fwhm_mid_mm`, `beam_fwhm_peak_mm`), and its per-channel value is per-channel only through the current; `beam_sigma_max_px` takes the red channel as the widest, and the reach of the sums, the skirt margin, the warp plan's sampling rule and the headroom readout all use one sigma. A per-channel ratio needs those five places changed together (bit-exact at ratio 1 through a uniform branch), plus a source for the size of the difference (CRT.md has none). A live look at the fringes (sizes that read as "a real tube" at normal viewing distance) is pending too.

### PHY-18 Visible phosphor trail
- **Owner feedback (live manual test, 2026-11-20):** "phosphor persistence: the trail is too short / there is no trail at all".
- **Finding, physics and not a bug.** The persistence stage, its real-time time base and the whole chain do what PHY-12 specifies:
  - Headless through every slot (`tests/persistence_chain.rs`; a white bar for one frame, then black; the defaults with persistence on and all ten presets, ABL off; 60 and 144 Hz frame periods, automatic rate): the summed trail of frames 1 to 3 equals the oracle to the 4th decimal, green 5.36 / 2.97 / 2.03 %, blue 4.72 / 2.60 / 1.78 % at 60 Hz (6.31 / 3.49 / 2.39 % and 5.56 / 3.06 / 2.09 % at 144 Hz), red exactly 0.
  - A white bar moving 40 source pixels per 60 Hz frame on black, display output, green: the bar 212 (8-bit), 57, 44, 37, 33 one to four frames behind it (pvm-20m4u the same, kv-trinitron-tv 188 / 49, 37, 32, 28). The trail is there and falls like the measured P22 power law, but it lasts a few frames: at 60 fps, with the eye tracking the object and the host LCD's hold blur, it is easy to miss, and a bright object over a bright background shows next to nothing. Red never trails (the oxysulfide red decays within 2 ms).
  - Reviewed for a path that could hide the trail live: the bool is on in every preset (`presets.rs` `m4_values_follow_the_recorded_policies`) and in the owner's settings (preset delta-17in), no quality tier, ageing or A/B path touches it (the A/B bypass turns the whole chain off on purpose), `is_identity` is true only with persistence and saturation both off, the window loop renders every vertical blank whatever the content rate (the afterglow decays between output frames, so a 30 or 60 fps game shows it at any refresh), and the history is cleared only on a source size change, a new capture session or a skipped frame. The automatic rate restarts the history while it settles: a 60 to 144 Hz change restarts it at 11 of the first 22 frames of the new rate, then never; a steady 60 Hz with pacing jitter and a dropped frame every 50 restarts it once (the 31st frame, the first estimate moving from 61 to 60 Hz), none in 10 s after (stage unit test `rate_change_restarts_the_trail_only_while_it_settles`). A last rate move under 5 % refits only after a full window, so for half a second the fit can be for about 63 Hz on a 60 Hz output: trail ratios 2.9 % high (measured), harmless.
  - The owner's settings file sets `phosphor_frame_rate_hz` = 397 (the manual override, probably a slider moved by accident): each 60 Hz output frame then decays the trail by 2.5 ms of phosphor time instead of 16.7 ms. It makes the trail slightly brighter and much longer (moving bar 63, 48, 41, 35), not shorter; reset it to 0 (real time).
  - The tail strength cannot help: at 60 Hz the power-law tail already carries 98 % of a frame's own light, so 10 times the tail gives 5.44 % instead of 5.36 %. A time-constant multiplier helps little on the green and blue tail either: a power law is nearly scale free (30 times slower: green 5.4 to 11 % in the first frame).
- **Resolution (2026-11-20):** artistic controls beyond the measured phosphor, identities at their defaults (the default picture is unchanged bit for bit), the presets unchanged: `phosphor_extra_afterglow_rgb` (0 to 2, hard 10) and `phosphor_extra_afterglow_time_ms` (5 to 1000 ms), an exponential glow mixed into each channel's response, (phosphor + g e^(-t / tau)) / (1 + g), real time, red included: the control for a long visible trail (1 at 200 ms: 51 four frames behind instead of 33; 2 at 300 ms: 52, the bar 141); `phosphor_decay_scale` (1 to 30, hard 0.1 to 100), every time constant multiplied (20: 40 four frames behind, red 13 % in the next frame); `phosphor_tail_strength_rgb` up to 10 (non-physical above 1). The menu's Phosphor panel opens with a plain explanation; README "Phosphor persistence: getting a visible trail" gives starting values. Oracle `crt_model::phosphor::DecayControls`; tests in crt-model, `stage_phosphor`, `persistence_chain`.
- **Live review 2026-11-20, two findings resolved (2026-11-20):**
  - **Bug, no trail under uneven pacing (the trail lost after the A/B bypass).** Live, after Ctrl+Alt+F7 twice, the bar showed 217 in R, G and B (the picture without persistence) and a trail of 0 for 10 s while the phosphor pass still ran. Cause: the window loop's measured frame periods (`frame_time_ms`, CPU time between two recorded frames) are uneven at 60 Hz (the `--log` files of the same session: render intervals from 0.3 to 2 ms up to about 33 ms in every second); with strictly alternating 1 / 32.3 ms the old automatic rate (trimmed mean of the middle 15 of 31 sorted periods) moves between 55 and 63 Hz with every new period, beyond the 5 % hysteresis, so it refitted and restarted the history (primed to the picture: y = x) at 599 of 600 frames. The bypass only changed the loop's pacing; a session that starts with that pacing has no trail at all, which may be what the owner saw. Fix (`gpu/stages/phosphor.rs`): the automatic period is the mean of the last 64 periods (a stall capped at 2.5 times the mean once the window is full), a new fit rate is taken only after it settled (16 frames within 2.5 % when more than 5 % away, a full window when closer, never before the window is full), and each frame steps by the mean period (a period above three times the mean steps by itself, clamped to 250 ms). Measured: alternating 1 / 32.3 ms and phase-locked random intervals give at most one restart in 10 s (at the end of the first window) and a step within 1.6 % of 16.67 ms; through the whole chain (preset `delta-17in`, uneven pacing) the trail ratios equal the 60 Hz oracle within 1 % before the bypass, after it, and after `phosphor_persistence` off then on (0 with the old estimate). The other stages that keep state were checked the same way (`tests/stage_state_resume.rs`): ABL restarts cold after the bypass or `abl_enable` off then on (the first frame unlimited, twice the settled light of a white field) and settles back within 120 frames, by design; the burn-in map is kept and receives no charge while its slot is off (the map bit-identical to a run without those frames); optics keeps no state (the picture bit-identical after the bypass and after `optics_enable` off then on); composite and interlace depend on the frame index only.
  - **Discrepancy, shorter trail live (57, 39, 29, 24 against 57, 44, 37, 33): a measurement effect.** The headless numbers above are for the bare defaults with persistence on, a 12 source-pixel bar stepping 40 px and a 960 x 720 output. With the live conditions headlessly (`--pattern bar:8:24`, preset `delta-17in`, 1264 x 1181 output, 8-bit display output with the preset's noise dither, 60 Hz +/- 0.3 ms, the bar running for 200 frames) the chain gives the bar 203 and 58, 45, 38, 34 at the places the bar really was. The live script took its step from the bar's half-peak width (33 px for 8 source px, so round(24 x 33 / 8) = 99 px instead of 94.8 px) and its window drifted 4 px per frame off the old positions; the same reading on the headless output gives 56, 38, 29, 23, the live values. Extra afterglow 0.5 at 150 ms: true places 67, 59, 54, 51 (the script reading 66, 50, 39, 31; live 67, 50, 41, 32); decay scale 20: 72, 55, 47, 42 (script 70, 47, 36, 28; live 70, 48, 36, 29). The live trail agrees with the model; README "Phosphor persistence" and the CHANGELOG give both sets. The red trail (16 one frame behind live, 16.2 headless) is the colour transform: the preset's SMPTE-C green and blue light has a small red component in the panel's sRGB; with sRGB tube primaries it is 0.0 (the physical red phosphor itself still has no trail). Test `tests/persistence_chain.rs` `live_bar_measurement_explained`.
- **Follow-up (2026-11-21), the trail is too strong:** the owner: "the persistence is too much: maybe just add a global `blend` parameter (between 0 and 1) to fine-tune the whole effect balance". **Resolved:** `phosphor_persistence_blend`, out = mix(x, y, blend) in linear light between the frame without any trail (x) and with the full trail (y, the measured decay plus the artistic controls), applied to the output of the recurrence only (the history runs unchanged: a change is instant, no restart; a static picture keeps its brightness). Default 0.5 in the parameter table and in all ten presets (owner choice: about half the trail), 1 reachable and bit for bit the previous output (same output hash on the released v0.4.0 and on the branch for a 40-frame sequence with jittered periods, with and without extra afterglow). Moving bar through the whole chain (`tests/persistence_chain.rs`, green, 8-bit): blend 1: bar 211.8, 57.3, 43.8, 36.9, 32.5; blend 0.5: bar 222.3, 41.8, 32.0, 26.9, 23.7; blend 0: bar 230.5, 0, 0, 0, 0. A single excitation's ratio becomes b w0 R_k / (1 - b (1 - w0)) (oracle `blended_trail_ratio`), checked against the GPU in `tests/stage_phosphor.rs` `blend_follows_the_oracle` and through every slot for the defaults and the ten presets.
- **Not done then, done since (PHY-19):** a phosphor-type selector (P1, P7, P31, P39): CRT.md had decay constants for P22 only at the time (the "P31 residual of 4 % at 20 ms, Wolf and Deubel" was a misquote, corrected in PHY-19). **Open (USER):** whether the trail now matches the owner's expectation live, and which extra-afterglow values to recommend; a measured long-persistence phosphor would turn the artistic control into a physical one (RESEARCH).

### PHY-19 Phosphor-type selector
- **Request (2026-11-21):** a phosphor-type selector built only on sourced data (research pass of the same day, condensed in CRT.md "Phosphor types").
- **DECISION (2026-11-21), shipped:** `phosphor_type` (Phosphor panel, first row), values `p22` (default, the colour tube, unchanged), `p4` (white, B&W TV sulfide), `p31` (green), `p1` (green), `p39` (long-persistence green), `philips_yellow_data_display_blend` (US 4,405,880; never called P3 or P134). One data row per type in `crt_model::phosphor_types` (xy, `DecayFit`, registered points, source). P31 xy: RCA 1961 0.245, 0.523 (Phosphor Technology's 0.287, 0.521 disagrees by 0.04 in x; the registration-era value, as the research recommends). Decays [D]: P4 and P31 a pure power law through the 10 % and 1 % points (t0 10.29 µs n 1.198; t0 8.30 µs n 1.339); P1 an exponential, tau 10.4 ms (both points on it); P39 an exponential through its one point, tau 65.1 ms (**one-point approximation**: the registered curve is not exponential, the 1 % time unknown); yellow blend an exponential through the patent's lower bound, tau 14.8 ms. **Approximation in every row:** the registered step-off times are used as the impulse response (exact for an exponential only).
- **Engine (minimal change):** a monochrome type is resolved on the CPU (`src/phosphor_type.rs`, applied with the ageing spot growth where the pipeline records a frame): `decode.comp` drives all three channels with the BT.601 luma Y' of the gamma-encoded code values (the `composite::LUMA` weights, before the drive voltage and gun law); the mask is off through `mask_enabled` (continuous coating); every per-gun and per-phosphor `_rgb` vector (gun, ageing, phosphor stages) and the convergence take the green value, so the channels stay identical; the phosphor stage uses the type's fit on every channel (decay scale, extra afterglow, tail strength and persistence blend act on top; P1, P39 and the yellow blend have no power-law tail, so the tail strength does nothing there); the colour matrix is the rank-one matrix that maps (L, L, L) to light of the type's xy at Y = L (`output_match_srgb` does not apply); white balance and the colour-temperature retune are off (one phosphor, nothing to retune: gains (1, 1, 1)); the wear luminance shares are a third each. The optics tints keep their values (with the rank-one matrix they change luminance only, not colour).
- **Gamut:** P1, P39, P31 (RCA xy) and the yellow blend lie outside sRGB. The existing output handling applies: a negative display channel is clipped to 0 and counted as out of gamut. The chromaticity is tested before that step (CPU, exact to 1e-9); the GPU test checks the clip and the ratios of the remaining channels.
- **P22 bit-identical:** 40-frame moving-bar sequence through the whole chain (defaults, persistence on, 60 Hz), output hash `dd3a781343fcf649` on this branch and on the base `db6f7da` (`tests/phosphor_types.rs` `p22_sequence_hash`, the same test run on both).
- **Measured (single excitation through every slot, 60 Hz, blend 1, green; equal to the oracle to the 4th decimal):** P4 3.86 / 2.02 / 1.34 %, P31 1.71 / 0.83 / 0.52 %, P1 20.2 / 4.08 / 0.83 %, P39 77.4 / 60.0 / 46.4 %, yellow blend 32.3 / 10.5 / 3.4 %. P1 and P39 smear strongly at 60 Hz: authentic (made for 50 Hz and slower refresh), said in the menu.
- **Held back:** P7 and P38 (3 s to 1 % and 1 s to 10 %: beyond the 1 s window of the frame-feedback fit; they need a longer horizon first), P3 and P134 (no chromaticity; no data at all for P134), P43 and P45 (only a vendor decay without conditions, B), the Philips white blend (a lower bound only, not requested). Presets unchanged (P22).
- **Correction:** CRT.md credited Wolf and Deubel (1997) with "about 4 % after 20 ms"; the full text gives, for a repetitively excited P31 bar, below 5 % at 10 ms, below 2.5 % at 30 ms and 1.4 % at 50 ms. Corrected in CRT.md "Decay and persistence" and the T-63 notes.
- **Open (USER):** a live look at the types; an "IBM 5151" style preset (P39) if wanted.

## Colour and HDR

### COL-1 HDR requirement and SDR behaviour
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-46):** exposure, soft shoulder (output_knee, 1 = hard clip) and the clip counters (ClipCounts: over white, out of gamut) are in the encode stage; no hidden normalisation of a non-D65 tube white (its blue can exceed panel white, the counter shows it). See ARCHITECTURE 3.7.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-19, T-84, owner decision D-16):** the "mask loss in SDR" answer below was incomplete: with a resolved mask, and with the scanlines at any density, a full-white field has elements above panel white (audit B1/M1), and the 'no clip' preset notes covered the unmasked white only. Now: a CPU headroom readout (mask peak x scanline peak x white matrix maximum x exposure, `src/headroom.rs`, `Renderer::headroom` for the UI) and `output_headroom_mode` with per-mode parameters: `shoulder` (default, knee `output_knee` 0.6, roll-off to the white point `output_shoulder_white`, automatic = readout x 1.02; non-physical above the knee), `physical` (exposure / max(1, readout x (1 + `output_headroom_margin`)): nothing clips, dimmer), `mask_depth` (`mask_depth` 0..1 scales the mask contrast, mean kept, non-physical; exposure then derived as physical) and `clip` (T-46). Measured at 2560 x 1440 (tests/presets.rs flat-white test, ABL off): readouts 1.41 (preset 01) to 3.87 (06); hard clip loses 12 to 54 % of the white mean, the default shoulder 16 to 55 % (1 to 4 points more: the knee 0.6 also compresses the mean; it removes the clip bands and tints), physical keeps the mean exactly at 25 to 69 % of the intended white. Open for the owner: whether the shoulder default and its knee 0.6 stay, given that measured trade-off.
- **DECISION (2026-11-19, owner: "default to C"; T-84b):** the default headroom mode is no longer the shoulder. The new value `auto` is the parameter default: mask depth (option C) where the readout has a resolved mask (physical mask peak above 1), the shoulder (knee 0.6) where the peak comes from the scanlines only (a depth limit has nothing to lower there; D-18 adaptivity: the choice follows the host and the source lines every frame, `headroom::effective_mode`). `mask_depth` keeps its default 0.5 as the parameter default, but presets with a resolved mask on the reference host set their own depth 0.25 (E: user-tunable). The derived exposure keeps the white mean at the intended white times 1 / (readout x 1.02), so the stripes are softer and nothing clips; measured at 2560 x 1440 (flat-white test of `tests/presets.rs`, share of the intended white kept): presets 01, 02, 04, 07, 09, 10 (shoulder, no resolved mask) 83.5, 78.2, 62.9, 80.3, 81.5 and 71.8 %; presets 03, 05, 06, 08 (mask depth 0.25) 62.8, 58.3, 56.9 and 58.5 % (47.3, 42.1, 40.2, 41.6 % at depth 0.5). None reaches the 85 % target: the mask presets would need a nearly flat mask (depth about 0.05) and for the scanline-only presets every mode that avoids the hard clip gives up at least 1 - 1 / readout of the white. The test lists each as a documented exception with its measured floor. The three modes and `clip` stay selectable.
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** the owner does not want HDR output or HDR colour, and left the internal precision to the assistant. Chosen:
  - **Output:** SDR only (sRGB host, 8-bit, or 10-bit when available, with final dither). HDR output is out of scope. The present stage is kept behind an interface so an HDR path could be added later.
  - **Internal computation:** linear-light FP16 (FP32 where banding shows), with the tube's white at 1.0 and no clamp inside the pipeline. Reason: the gun power law, mask sampling (a lit element is 4 to 6 times brighter than the area average), halation and glow sums, and afterglow accumulation all need unclamped linear values, and 8-bit intermediates band in the dark tones.
  - **Mask loss in SDR:** with the area-averaged mask (PHY-2 option A) the mask loss is averaged out, so there is nothing to compensate. A single exposure control maps tube white to panel white. When the mask is resolved (tier A or B), element peaks above panel white are soft-clipped and a clip indicator is shown. No hidden bloom compensation (SCP-8 stage stays optional and labelled).
  - **Colour:** tube primaries and white converted to sRGB primaries by a 3×3 matrix in linear light, clipped in linear light with a warning (COL-2 default). No wide gamut.
  - **Consequences:** SP-4 (HDR path) is deferred. Rolling scan (PHY-12, M4) is effectively out for SDR, since it needs peak luminance headroom, and stays a later option. HW-7 OLED ABL concerns shrink but static-mask burn-in remains.
- **Q:** Is HDR required for the "reference" experience (as Sony Megatron assumes), at what minimum peak (600 or 1000 nits)? On SDR, where the physical model needs 3 to 7× headroom, do we render physically and accept a dark image (CRT white about 15 to 35 % of panel white), normalise so the mask-averaged white equals panel white (elements clip), or apply a non-physical compensation? Which HDR format: HDR10 PQ or scRGB FP16?
- **Why:** `CRT.md` says a pixel-level mask with visible scanlines needs about 400 to 700 cd/m² for a 100 cd/m² CRT white, and rolling scan multiplies that again. OLEDs only reach peak on small windows (panel ABL), which conflicts with full-field mask patterns. The answer defines whether SDR is a supported target at all.
- **Who:** USER plus SPIKE (SP-4).
- **Default:** HDR recommended, not required. Reference CRT white configurable (default 100 cd/m²). SDR renders physically with a global exposure slider the user sets knowingly, clipping shown. Format chosen after SP-4. HDR is the first major milestone after the first usable version.

### COL-2 Colour fidelity target
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-46):** the 3x3 matrix from the tube primaries and white to sRGB with D65 (crt_model::output::tube_to_display) and the output_match_srgb toggle (identity). Open for the owner or T-50: the toggle defaults to on (identity) so that the default chain stays an exact identity; COL-2's tube-faithful default is reached by presets that turn it off.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-19, T-84):** the white through the matrix counts in the SDR headroom readout (COL-1): the largest display channel of the tube white, `crt_model::output::white_matrix_max` (row sums for a flat field; sums of the positive entries when the mask or the scanlines separate the channels, since a negative entry is not offset where its channel is dark: EBU preset 02 1.044, D93 presets 1.347). The preset exposures stay the white-balance limit of the unmasked white (largest linear sRGB channel of the tube white x exposure <= 1); they are no longer described as 'no clip'. The headroom default is `auto` since T-84b (COL-1).
- **Q:** Does the output reproduce the tube's own primaries and white (so game reds look less saturated and D93 looks bluish) or colour-manage to what the artist saw on sRGB? Which primaries and white points ship (SMPTE-C, EBU, measured Trinitron, P22 average, custom xy; D65 or D93 default)? What happens to out-of-gamut colours?
- **Why:** A faithful 9300 K picture looks blue next to a D65 desktop. Console art was authored on real tubes, PC art on sRGB monitors. A real CRT does not colour-convert, and a white-point change is a per-channel gain, not a chromatic adaptation.
- **Who:** USER plus DATA (do you calibrate your display).
- **Default:** Tube-faithful and absolute, tube white selectable per preset (D65 for professional presets, 9300 K for consumer and PC presets), one-click "match sRGB colours" toggle. Linear 3×3 conversion from tube to host primaries, clip in linear light with a warning, proper gamut mapping later.
- **IMPLEMENTED in part (T-34, 2026-11-18), corrected by the T-34 review (gate 11):** white as a per-channel gain, as a RELATIVE retune like a service-menu colour-temperature adjustment. The T-34 first version applied the absolute solution P g = W on top of the tube white that the T-46 matrix already assumes for tube RGB (1, 1, 1), so the white was applied twice (SMPTE-C D65 ended at xy (0.334, 0.511)). Now the CPU computes g = s(target) / s(tube white), with s(W) = P^-1 W (columns of P = the unit-luminance lights of the primaries), scaled to a maximum of 1 (`src/white.rs`, oracle `crt_model::gun::white_retune_gains`); the `gun` stage multiplies the beam current by them. The target is the new parameter `gun_retune_white_xy` (default D65, the tube white of `colour.toml`); a target equal to the tube white gives gains (1, 1, 1) exactly and a bit-identical output. A D65 SMPTE-C tube retuned to D93 (0.2831, 0.2971) gets gains (0.621, 0.753, 1) and the display white is that chromaticity within 0.001 through the real chain with the T-46 matrix (cross-stage test `tests/stage_gun.rs`). Switch `gun_white_balance`, default off: the tube white is used as it is (this is a user choice, not a dependency on T-46, which exists). Still open: gamut clipping policy beyond the clip counters and the "match sRGB colours" default (T-46, T-50).

### COL-3 Absolute or relative luminance and host EOTF
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-46):** host EOTF inverse in encode: pure power (default 2.2) or piecewise sRGB, from Settings.host; checked against the oracle for all 256 codes (20/255: pure 2.2 host gives code 20 for gamma 2.2 light and 16 for gamma 2.4 light; sRGB host 12 and 7). Still open: moving the host EOTF into the parameter table (settings tasks) and the calibration pattern.
- **Q:** Does the model work in cd/m² (a 100 cd/m² tube white shown at 100 cd/m²) or relative units? Which EOTF is assumed for the host in SDR (piecewise sRGB, pure 2.2, 2.4, calibrated), and is there a calibration step?
- **Why:** Absolute units make ABL, glare and ambient parameters physical but need a characterised host. A host-EOTF mismatch (2.2 versus sRGB) shifts dark levels exactly where the CRT model is sensitive.
- **Who:** USER plus DATA.
- **Default:** Internal absolute units with an output exposure factor so an uncharacterised SDR host still works. A host-gamma parameter (default pure 2.2) and a calibration pattern.

### COL-4 Precision, banding and dithering
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18, T-46):** final dither of 1 LSB in encode (off, ordered 8x8 Bayer, interleaved gradient noise seeded by the frame index), off by default, never on float targets. Blue noise proper (a texture) is not implemented; the noise mode is blue-noise-like. The presenter records whether a 10-bit SDR surface format is offered (Presenter::ten_bit_offered); the swapchain stays 8-bit, and the dither already scales to 1023 levels.
- **Q:** Which working precision (FP16, FP32 for accumulation) and which output bit depth (8-bit SDR, 10-bit SDR, 10-bit PQ, FP16 scRGB)? Is dithering applied?
- **Why:** Gamma 2.4, a mask that dims 4 to 6×, and a decay tail at 0.5 % (about code 16 of 255) band in 8 bits. Dithering can create moiré, none creates banding. Rolling scan needs exact linear sums across sub-frames. Cross-vendor bit-exactness is not achievable.
- **Who:** SPIKE.
- **Default:** FP16 intermediates (FP32 where tests show banding), 10-bit or FP16 swapchain when available, blue-noise dither only in the final encode, off in test mode. Reproducibility defined as within a tolerance.

## Performance and resources

### PRF-1 Frame-time target
- **Q:** At which output resolution and refresh, on which GPU, in how many ms must it run, with what share left to the game? What is the weakest supported GPU?
- **Why:** "As fast as possible" is not a spec. 4K at 240 Hz is a 4.2 ms budget, shared with the game on the same GPU. The mask integration, ring convolution and multi-scanline beam integration have real cost, and the budget decides the algorithms.
- **Who:** USER (hardware), SPIKE (measure, SP-8).
- **Default:** The full model at the host's native resolution and refresh, using at most 25 % of the frame time (at most half a refresh interval) on your GPU at the default preset.
- **Measured (T-47, 2026-11-19, live, first part of PRF-1; budget 4.2 ms = 25 % of 16.7 ms):**
  - **Setup.** Release build of `task/T-47` (from the pre-release base commit), RTX A2000 8GB Laptop GPU (Vulkan device 0), `--fullscreen --monitor 0 --stats --log`, generated 320x240 pattern as the source (`--pattern`, no capture), nothing else running. **Deviations from the T-47 text:** (1) the machine exposes two monitors, 1920x1200 at (1920, -116) (monitor 0) and 1920x1080 (monitor 1, primary); there is no 2560x1440 monitor today, so the runs are on the 1920x1200 monitor (2.3 Mpx instead of 3.7 Mpx; the tube is the 4:3 raster fitted to the output, not read back from the app, so about 1600x1200 is presumed, 1.9 Mpx against 2.8 Mpx for the 1920x1440 design tube). Pixel-bound stage times at the design size are therefore expected to be about 1.4 times higher than below (not measured). (2) Each run lasted 24 s (not 40 s): the first 5 per-second rows were dropped, the median and the minimum of the 18 remaining per-second means are given. One 10 s warm-up run came first. (3) This build has no `--set`: variants are temporary copies of the preset file with one key changed (deleted afterwards). (4) Not measured: pixel-demo as capture source, a detailed pixel-art image, `window:50` and `crosshatch:16` on the presets, presets 05 and 09 with persistence off or reference quality, the 2560x1440 case.
  - **Table (GPU ms, median of per-second means; min of the total in brackets; "-" = stage absent or skipped).**

| Config | Pattern | Total | upload | decode | amp | gun | abl | screen | phosphor | encode | Render fps | 60 held | SM clock / reason (sampled at 14 s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Defaults (no preset) | ramp | 4.94 (4.61) | - | 0.02 | 0.04 | 0.04 | - | 3.98 | - | 0.73 | 60 | yes | 982 MHz, idle clocks (0x1), 34 % |
| Defaults | checker:1 | 6.02 (5.72) | - | 0.01 | 0.02 | 0.02 | - | 2.36 | - | 3.57 | 60 | yes | 1920 MHz, no limit, 39 % |
| Defaults | window:50 | 4.56 (3.91) | - | 0.03 | 0.06 | 0.06 | - | 2.57 | - | 1.76 | 60 | yes | 825 MHz, idle clocks, 41 % |

| Preset vga-15in (09) | ramp | 7.17 (7.06) | - | 0.01 | 0.02 | 0.02 | - | 5.23 | 0.96 | 0.88 | 60 | yes | 1897 MHz, power cap, 80 C |

| 01, `beam_tail_weight=0.05` | ramp | 162.54 (160.40) | - | 0.02 | 0.53 | 0.11 | - | 160.17 | 1.03 | 0.64 | 6 | no | 1650 MHz, thermal, 86 C |
| 01, `geometry_curvature=flat` | ramp | 7.83 (7.74) | - | 0.01 | 0.36 | 0.06 | - | 5.55 | 0.98 | 0.82 | 61 | yes | 1890 MHz, no limit, 85 C |
| 01, `phosphor_persistence=false` | ramp | 25.33 (25.05) | - | 0.02 | 0.53 | 0.12 | - | 23.73 | - | 0.92 | 39 | no | 1687 MHz, thermal, 86 C |
| 05, `geometry_curvature=flat` | ramp | 14.97 (14.15) | - | 0.01 | 0.37 | 0.07 | 0.53 | 8.71 | 0.87 | 4.27 | 60 | yes | 1867 MHz, thermal, 87 C |

  - **Reading the table.**
    - **Budget.** No configuration meets 4.2 ms on the median. The defaults are at 4.6 to 6.0 ms (best minimum 3.91 ms on `window:50`, only under idle-ramp clocks that inflate it); every preset run is far over. Presets 09 (7.2 to 11.0 ms) and 01 flat (7.8 ms) still hold 60 fps because the budget is a share of the frame, not the frame. The M3 target of 2.5 ms for the stage chain is not met by any run.
    - **Dominant stage.** `screen` (beam and mask) is 39 to 92 % of the total in every run: 2.4 to 4.0 ms at the defaults, 5.2 to 6.4 ms on preset 09 and on preset 01 flat, 23 to 36 ms on preset 01 curved, 53 to 82 ms on preset 05 curved. The next costs are `phosphor` 0.9 to 1.1 ms (fixed, three RGBA32F history images), `encode` 0.7 ms on smooth content but 1.8 to 6.1 ms on high-frequency content (checker, window:50), `amp` 0.45 to 0.53 ms with `amp_samples = 16` (0.02 to 0.06 ms at the default K = 4), `abl` 0.5 to 0.8 ms, `gun` 0.1 ms, `decode` and `upload` negligible. The `encode` figure is pattern-dependent for no reason found in the code; it is the last segment before present, so a wait on the compositor or flip may be inside it (hypothesis, not verified).
    - **Geometry fallback.** A curved faceplate forces the non-separable form of `beam_eval`: preset 01 curved 26.2 ms against 5.6 ms flat (4.7 times), preset 05 spherical 53.0 against 8.7 ms flat (6.1 times). This is why `beam_quality=reference` on preset 01 (23.3 ms) is no slower than standard (26.2 ms): under cylindrical curvature both run the exact 2D sum, so the reference cost of a flat tube is not measured here. Persistence costs 1.0 ms (preset 01 with and without persistence: 28.55 and 25.33 ms total; the `phosphor` segment is 1.0 ms, the rest of the difference is run-to-run variation in `screen` under the changing clocks). Preset 09 is spherical yet cheap (5.2 ms); a small spot is the probable reason (not verified).
    - **Beam tail.** `beam_tail_weight=0.05` multiplies the `screen` cost of preset 01 by 6.1 (26.2 to 160.2 ms): the tail widens the line and sample reach the sum must cover.
    - **GPU state.** Run order: default patterns, presets 01/05/09 (ramp, then checker), then the variants. Low-load runs (defaults) ran at 660 to 1050 MHz idle-ramp clocks (reason 0x1, GPU idle), so their ms are inflated by the clock governor (the checker default at 1920 MHz shows `screen` 2.36 ms against 3.98 ms at 982 MHz). Heavy runs sat at 1815 to 1897 MHz under the power cap (0x4, 44 to 83 W; max 2100 MHz) and, from 86 to 87 C onwards, were thermally limited (0x20, 1650 to 1687 MHz, about 10 % below the earlier heavy runs). The later variant rows (reference, tail, persistence off) are therefore slightly pessimistic against the first ones. No GPU setting was changed.
    - **Idle log.** The seconds since the last input rose monotonically from 242 s (before the build) to 974 s (after the last run); no input appeared during any run, and no output window was left up (Get-Process checked after each run; the `fg_changes` column was 0 in the CSVs checked).
  - **Conclusion for T-47 and T-48.** The first usable model is 1.1 to 1.4 times over budget at the defaults and 2 to 22 times over on the presets (curved geometry), on a 1.9 Mpx tube; the 4.2 ms budget will not be met at 2560x1440 without cutting `screen`. T-48 (standard tier <= 4.2 ms with preset 01 and 05 style curvature) must remove the non-separable fallback for curved faceplates, cap the beam reach (tail), cut `screen` on flat tubes by about 2 times (to about 2.5 ms), and also take back about 1.5 ms from `phosphor`, `encode`, `amp` and `abl` (preset 01 non-screen chain: 2.3 ms, 56 % of the budget alone).
  - **Optimisation levers, ranked by expected gain from the measured stage costs (not implemented).**
    1. Curved geometry on the separable path (`screen`): evaluate the separable beam and mask on the undistorted raster, then map to the tube with the geometry warp (or bound the warp per tile and keep the tile-separable form with a locally linearised mapping). Expected: preset 01 26 to about 6 ms (gain about 20 ms), preset 05 53 to about 9 ms (gain about 44 ms), the biggest item by an order of magnitude.
    2. Cap the beam reach and put the tail on a coarser pass: the 5 % tail costs 134 ms on top of preset 01; a tail at half or quarter resolution (separable, low-pass) or a reach limit of 3 sigma for the tail term. Expected: back to about 26 ms (tail case), or to the flat cost with lever 1.
    3. Flat-tube `screen` (5.2 to 6.4 ms, 2 to 3 ms over the share): early-out of black tiles and rows (pixel art has large black areas), fewer mask quadrature points per host pixel at low zoom, shorter beam line range, FP16 maths in the inner loops. Expected: 1.5 to 3 ms.
    4. `encode` on high-frequency content (up to 6 ms with preset 05 checker): find why it scales with the pattern (dither noise, texel cache pressure, compositor wait inside the segment) and fix or fuse it with the previous pass. Expected: 0 to 3 ms on detailed content, nothing on smooth content.
    5. `phosphor` (1.0 ms): store the exponential history terms in FP16 or fuse the pass into `screen` (the tube image round trip) when persistence is on. Expected: 0.4 to 0.6 ms.
    6. `amp` at `amp_samples = 16` (0.45 to 0.53 ms, about 10 times the K = 4 cost): clamp K to what the host pixel can resolve at the tube scale or share the box integral across rows. Expected: 0.2 to 0.3 ms.
    7. `abl` (0.5 to 0.8 ms): reduce the APL on a quarter-resolution image or fuse the apply pass into `screen`. Expected: 0.3 to 0.5 ms.
    8. Cooling and power: the heavy runs hit the power cap and then 86 C; every lever above also lowers the throttle, worth about 10 % in the later runs.
- **Re-measure (2026-11-19, 18:49 to 18:53, pre-release build, menu closed, GPU otherwise idle; logs `target/live-results/step5`):**
  - **Setup.** RTX A2000 on the monitor it drives (monitor 1, 1920x1080). `--pattern ramp` (320x240), `--stats --log`, 24 s runs, median of 19 per-second values after 5 s. Windowed runs: `--no-activate`, 768x720 client, picture 768x576. Fullscreen runs: `--monitor 1`, picture 1440x1080. Preset 01 flat is a temporary copy of the preset with `geometry_curvature = "flat"`.
  - **Table** (GPU ms; SM clock, power and temperature sampled by nvidia-smi at 14 s; decode 0.01 to 0.11 ms everywhere, no upload with a pattern):

| Config | Total (min) | amp | gun | abl | screen | phosphor | encode | Render fps | GPU at 14 s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Windowed, defaults | 3.84 (3.47) | 0.21 | 0.12 | - | 2.95 | - | 0.43 | 60.3 | 435 MHz, 5.8 W, 59 C, idle clocks |
| Windowed, 01 | 4.84 (4.16) | 0.74 | 0.13 | - | 3.23 | 0.29 | 0.14 | 60.2 | 1155 MHz, 25.6 W, 61 C |
| Windowed, 01 flat | 4.57 (3.81) | 0.71 | 0.14 | - | 2.97 | 0.42 | 0.16 | 60.2 | 1170 MHz, 25.6 W, 62 C |
