  - **Reading.**
    - Every run holds 60 fps. Only the windowed defaults are under the 4.2 ms budget.
    - Fullscreen, the curved presets 01 and 05 now take 6.0 and 6.2 ms, against 28.6 and 59.1 ms in the T-47 table. The output is not the same: T-47 had a 1920x1200 output with a tube of about 1600x1200; this run has a 1440x1080 picture, about 0.8 times the pixels. Most of the difference is therefore not the size. It is consistent with the T-91 warp path: curved and flat cost about the same now.
    - 01 flat (6.72 ms) was slower than 01 curved (6.03 ms) in this run; its clock was higher, so the clock does not explain it. Not investigated.
    - Throttle reason 0x1 (GPU idle) in every run except fullscreen 01 flat: the clocks stayed between 427 and 1402 MHz because the GPU was not fully loaded, so these ms are not at the maximum clock.
- **Performance work (2026-11-20, headless, `tests/perf_profile.rs`; ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Performance work 2026-11-20" has the full table):**
  - **Method.** Release builds of the base (`exec/integration`) and of `task/perf`, the whole chain headless into a `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` target like the live path, 320 x 240 sources (game-like pixel art, 1-px checker, per-pixel noise, ramp, flat grey), outputs 1280 x 720, 1920 x 1080 and 2560 x 1440, the defaults and presets 01 (flat and cylindrical) and 05; per scenario the median of 5 rounds of 6-frame bursts, every scenario once per round; base and branch run four times alternately (base, new, base, new, then new, base, new, base) and averaged. RTX A2000 Laptop, clocks 1567 to 1897 MHz under load, 79 to 87 C, power cap (0x4) and thermal (0x20) limits seen in some runs: numbers are relative, +-5 to 10 % run to run.
  - **`encode` (the T-47 mystery): found and fixed.** The clip counters were `atomicAdd` on a host-visible storage buffer (system memory): every clipping pixel sent an atomic over the bus. Isolation: without the atomics the pass took 0.13 ms on the checker instead of 3.1 ms at 1920 x 1080; a per-subgroup ballot reduction (one atomic per 32 pixels) changed nothing (the driver already merges a warp's atomics); the counters in device-local memory, cleared on the GPU and copied to the host after the pass, give 0.04 to 0.25 ms on every content (were 1.2 to 4.1 ms on detailed content, which clips the most: the 1-px checker at full drive, noise, the shoulder region of a resolved mask). Dither, uniform branching and the readback were not involved.
  - **Before -> after (total GPU ms, standard tier, the new default):** 1920 x 1080: defaults game 2.06 -> 1.08, checker 5.27 -> 1.28; 01 flat game 5.57 -> 2.78, checker 10.00 -> 3.79; 01 game 3.17 -> 3.00, checker 6.42 -> 3.89; 05 game 4.11 -> 3.67. 2560 x 1440: defaults game 3.19 -> 1.63, checker 7.37 -> 1.96; 01 flat game 8.98 -> 4.02, checker 15.39 -> 5.66; 01 game 4.78 -> 4.35, checker 9.23 -> 5.72; 05 game 5.79 -> 5.27. The defaults now fit the 4.2 ms goal at every size and content; preset 01 flat at 1440p checker is still 5.7 ms (`screen` 3.4, `phosphor` 1.5).
  - **Residual hot spots.** `screen` on curved tubes (unchanged in `standard`: 1.5 to 5.2 ms; the `fast` tier takes 30 to 45 % off with the amplifier sub-sample cap), `phosphor` 0.35 / 0.8 / 1.45 ms at the three sizes (bandwidth: three RGBA32F history images read and written per pixel; FP16 history needs an accuracy study, PHY-12), `amp` 0.36 ms at K = 16 (0.11 in `fast`).
- **Re-measure (2026-11-23, headless, release, `perf_profile::chain_profile`, 2560 x 1440 = the real monitors, standard tier, RTX A2000 Laptop; total GPU ms, p10 to p90):** defaults game 1.61, checker 1.96; 01 flat game 4.07 (`screen` 2.08, `phosphor` 1.32), checker 5.54; 01 curved game 4.44 (`screen` 2.22, `phosphor` 1.44, `amp` 0.37), checker 5.83; 05 game 5.41 (`screen` 3.19, `phosphor` 1.38, `abl` 0.11), checker 7.57 (`screen` 5.19). Same as 2026-11-20: nothing regressed, nothing new.
  - **Curved-tube `screen`: closed.** The curved presets cost the same as flat ones (01 curved 2.22 ms against 2.08 ms flat on a game frame; the 26 and 53 ms of T-47 were the non-separable fallback, gone since the warp path, T-91). What remains on the slot TV (05) is the spherical mask and beam on detailed content (3.2 to 5.2 ms), and the `fast` and `low` tiers already take 30 to 45 % off.
  - **Phosphor history: measured upper bound, not changed.** The history is three RGBA32F images read and written per pixel (112 bytes per pixel with the light in and out). A throwaway prototype with the three images in RGBA16F (shader and format only, reverted) takes `phosphor` from 1.44 to 0.54 ms on 01 at 2560 x 1440 (total 4.44 to 3.35 ms: 0.9 ms, 25 %). That is the most any history format change can give. FP16 everywhere is not acceptable as the physical default (the reason written in `gpu/stages/phosphor.rs`): an FP16 history stalls within 2^-11 / (1 - rho) of the steady state of a term, 2.4 % of the slow term at 60 Hz (rho 0.98), 9.8 % at 240 Hz (rho 0.995), and any term can be the slow one once `phosphor_decay_scale` or the extra afterglow are used, so a split by term index (fast terms FP16, slow term FP32) is not safe either. The one defensible place is an approximate tier: FP16 history in `low` only (a trail error of a few percent of the trail, below one 8-bit code on most content, to be measured with the tier error table), which needs a second shader variant (the storage format is a compile-time qualifier), the format chosen by tier in `alloc_history` (a tier change restarts the history) and a re-measure of the `low` error line. Every configuration holds 60 fps with at least a factor 2 of margin (worst 7.6 ms of 16.7), so this stays on the list, not done.

### PRF-2 Quality tiers
- **Q:** Are quality tiers wanted, what degrades first, and may a lower tier change the look or only the approximation error? Manual, automatic or both?
- **Why:** If "physically accurate" is the differentiator, tiers should change error, not physics.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Named tiers (reference, standard, fast) that change low-frequency pass resolution, kernel truncation, sub-frame count and mask filtering only. Manual selection with a displayed GPU-time readout. Auto-drop on overrun only per OUT-6.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-20, T-48; ARCHITECTURE 3.7 "Quality tiers", A27):** `quality_tier` = `exact`, `standard` (default), `fast`, `low` (menu panel Performance; kept across preset changes), changing evaluation choices only, never a look parameter. `exact` is the build before the tiers bit for bit (33 full-chain renders of the defaults and the ten presets compared byte for byte). `standard`: the flat-tube beam on the line-image path on an aligned grid and a parallel ABL sum, within one FP16 step of exact (MAE 0.012 %, max 0.195 % of white), `screen` 2.4 to 3 times cheaper on a flat tube. `fast`: also at most ceil(host px per source px) amplifier sub-samples (at least 4) and the interpolated line path on scaled flat rasters: MAE 0.81 %, max 2.64 % of white (1-px checker on a K = 16 preset; game art about 0.1 %), 25 to 40 % cheaper than standard on the curved presets. `low`: also a 3-sigma beam reach and a coarser warp grid: MAE 0.86 %, max 2.64 %, about 5 to 15 % cheaper than fast. The error is measured against `exact` per tier (`tests/quality_tiers.rs`: MAE <= 1 % of white as PLAN T-48 asks, max <= 3 %); the exact tier against the f64 oracle is the stage tests' contract as before. Owner choice still open: whether `standard` (nearly exact, the default) or `fast` should be the default on weak GPUs, and whether a `low` tier with FP16 phosphor history or lower-resolution optics is wanted (not built: their error is not measured yet).
### PRF-3 Sharing the GPU, power and idle
- **Q:** How much GPU time may the tool take from a game on the same GPU, should it back off, does it render continuously or only on new frames, and do power, heat and battery matter?
- **Why:** A slower game changes the source cadence and corrupts temporal effects. A pixel-art game should not turn the PC into a heater.
- **Who:** USER plus SPIKE.
- **Default:** No automatic back-off in the first version, measure and document the combined load. Re-present the last output when the source is unchanged and no temporal state is evolving. No laptop optimisation.

### PRF-4 Live tunability versus rebuilds
- **Q:** Must every parameter respond within one frame, or may structural parameters (mask geometry, tube size, internal resolution, line multiplier, precomputed kernels) take a short rebuild? Undo, reset to preset and "diff against preset"?
- **Why:** Strict per-frame tunability forbids precomputed tables that would otherwise buy speed.
- **Who:** RESEARCH.
- **Default:** Scalars apply in one frame, structural changes rebuild within about 100 ms with a visible "applying" state, per-parameter reset.

## Tuning UX

### UX-1 Units and derived readouts
- **Q:** Does the UI show physical units with live derived readouts (px per triad, triads per source pixel, σ over line pitch, FWHM over pitch, moiré beat period, headroom used, GPU time per pass), or shader units, or both?
- **Why:** Physical units are the visible proof of the differentiator, and the readouts explain why something looks wrong (budget, moiré).
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Physical units everywhere with tooltips and the confidence tag, derived readouts always visible, shader-unit view behind an advanced toggle.

### UX-2 Presets and profiles
- **Q:** What is the structure: tube presets, host-display profiles, per-source profiles (crop, PAR, video mode, EOTF), global settings, per-executable auto-attach, human-editable files, preset versioning, and sharing?
- **Why:** Mixing tube, host and source settings makes presets non-portable, and display calibration must not live inside a tube preset. Breaking the format later invalidates your work.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Three layered, versioned, human-editable text files (tube, host, source profile) with schema version, source and confidence fields. Manual attach first, auto-attach by executable and window class later. No RetroArch preset import (compare against ShaderGlass side by side).

### UX-3 Test patterns, A/B and calibration
- **Q:** Which built-in aids are in the first version: test patterns (grey ramp, PLUGE, 1-pixel lines, windows at 10/25/50/100 % APL, checkerboard, flat colour fields, convergence crosshatch, moving bar for decay, moiré grid, subpixel-layout check, HDR peak check), per-stage enable flags, split-screen A/B, full bypass, host calibration (px per triad, banding and gamma check, peak estimate)?
- **Why:** Test patterns double as validation material for SCP-3, remove capture from the loop when tuning, and are required for host calibration.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Built-in pattern generator as a first-class source type, per-stage flags, split-screen A/B and bypass in the first version. Photo overlay and measurement wizards later.

### UX-4 Parameter count and audience
- **Q:** How is a parameter space of 80 or more values kept usable, and is the UI aimed at a physicist-level tuner or a casual "pick a TV" user?
- **Why:** `CRT.md` criticises crt-guest-advanced for its huge empirical parameter space.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Preset-first basic view with the tube's own user controls, an expert view grouped by physical stage (signal, gun, beam, mask, phosphor, optics, geometry), bypass and A/B split.

### UX-5 Headless, command line and export
- **Q:** Are a command-line launch (profile and target arguments, no menu), a headless render mode (image or video in, floating-point image out), image and test-pattern inputs, stage-by-stage debug views, and screenshots required?
- **Why:** A launcher workflow and the automated test path both need them, and validation and benchmarking need a repeatable input without a live game.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Yes: CLI with profile and target arguments, headless render command, image and pattern input from the start, capture recording later.

## Tech stack decisions

### TEC-1 Hard constraints or preferences
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** all four are the owner's own guess, not constraints. With HDR output dropped (COL-1), the main argument for a Direct3D present path is gone. Assistant recommendation: keep Rust and ImGui. Start with Vulkan on NVIDIA, and switch capture and present to Direct3D 12 only if SP-1 (interop) or SP-2 (no-focus present) is painful. Shaders in GLSL, or in Slang or HLSL compiled to both SPIR-V and DXIL, if a backend switch must stay cheap.
- **Q:** Are Vulkan, GLSL, Rust and Dear ImGui firm requirements or preferences, and why (portability to Linux later, learning, existing skills, shader reuse, performance)? Is a D3D12 or D3D11 path acceptable if Windows-only is permanent?
- **Why:** On Windows the capture API, compositor and present chain are Direct3D and DXGI native. Vulkan adds an interop layer, vendor-dependent present behaviour and less-travelled HDR and VRR paths. If cross-platform is a real goal it must shape the architecture now. The Rust ecosystem's centre of gravity for graphics is elsewhere (WGSL, native GUIs).
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Keep the mandate. Isolate capture and presentation behind interfaces, and allow a D3D11 capture stage and, if SP-4 fails, a DXGI present stage. Confirm interop is acceptable with SP-1 before committing.

### TEC-2 Rust Vulkan binding
- **Q:** ash (raw, explicit), vulkano (safe wrapper), or wgpu (portable, limited extension and present control, prefers WGSL)?
- **Why:** External memory import, HDR swapchain colour spaces, exact present control and exclusive-fullscreen avoidance are extension-level features that an abstraction may hide.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-1, SP-4).
- **Default:** ash with a thin in-house layer and a GPU allocator crate.

### TEC-3 Shader toolchain and parameter source of truth
- **Q:** How is GLSL compiled to SPIR-V: build-time glslang or shaderc, runtime shaderc (C++ dependency), or naga (limited GLSL)? How are about 80 parameter definitions (units, ranges, defaults, confidence tags) shared between Rust, GLSL and the menu without three-way drift? Is hot reload required?
- **Why:** It affects build complexity for a single developer, hot-reload productivity on a physics-heavy project, and which GLSL features are usable.
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-9) plus RESEARCH.
- **Default:** glslang or glslc at build time, runtime compilation with hot reload in development builds, precompiled SPIR-V in release, parameters generated from one declarative table.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18), first part:** `glslc` at build time from a fixed shader list in `build.rs`, SPIR-V embedded in the binary. No hot reload and no parameter table yet.

### TEC-4 Windowing library
- **Q:** winit or raw Win32 via the `windows` crate for the output window?
- **Why:** Non-activating, topmost, click-through, capture-excluded windows, mouse-activate handling, per-monitor DPI v2 and two windows need Win32 details a portable library may fight (it may activate the window on show).
- **Who:** SPIKE (SP-2).
- **Default:** Raw Win32 for the output window, winit only if shown to cover all needed behaviour. Per-monitor DPI v2, swapchain at physical pixel size, no OS scaling on the output.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-18):** raw Win32 through the `windows` crate (non-activating, topmost, tool window, `MA_NOACTIVATE`, per-monitor DPI v2). Click-through and capture exclusion are not done yet.
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-11-20, A32):** click-through (`WS_EX_TRANSPARENT | WS_EX_LAYERED`, full alpha) as a setting, a hotkey and a menu row, set by the same one-place style rule as `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`. Capture exclusion is still not done.

### TEC-5 ImGui binding
- **Q:** Which Rust binding and Vulkan renderer for Dear ImGui (imgui-rs, dear-imgui-rs, cimgui-based), given maintenance status, and can input be fed manually from a custom Win32 window without focus? Is "ImGui" Dear ImGui specifically, with a native Rust GUI as a fallback only with your agreement?
- **Why:** Bindings lag upstream (imgui-rs 0.12 tracks Dear ImGui 1.89, dear-imgui-rs 0.22.0 tracks 1.93.1 WIP with docking). Binding maintenance is a long-term single-developer risk, and docking or multi-viewport would help the control-window case.
- **Who:** RESEARCH plus SPIKE (SP-2).
- **Default:** Dear ImGui through whichever binding passes a one-day spike with custom input feeding, evaluating dear-imgui-rs first. Own Vulkan renderer if its backend does not fit ash.
- **SPIKE T-08, build part (2026-11-19, headless only; details in `spikes/imgui-spike/RESULTS.md` on branch `spike/T-08`; decision at T-13):**
  - `dear-imgui-rs`, `dear-imgui-sys`, `dear-imgui-ash` pinned `=0.22.0`: build from crates.io with no git submodule, no network and no libclang (vendored cimgui and Dear ImGui, pregenerated bindings); they need the MSVC C++ compiler. Licences MIT OR Apache-2.0; the whole dependency tree passes `cargo deny check licenses` with `deny.toml`. The crate packages ship no LICENSE file: the release zip must take the notices from upstream (Dear ImGui MIT, cimgui MIT).
  - `dear-imgui-ash` 0.22.0 uses our `ash 0.38`, and its `dynamic-rendering` feature (default `render-pass` off) works: headless test on the RTX A2000, no `VkRenderPass`, offscreen RGBA8, validation and synchronization validation on, 0 messages. Checked: window colour exact at a known pixel, text drawn, slider grab moves with the value, 50 % window blended over a background image exactly (error 0, tolerance 1 code), overlay pass about 0.01 ms at 320x240.
  - Win32 input fed by hand works: pure `WM_*` -> ImGui IO translation, unit tested (mouse, wheel, keys with left/right modifiers, UTF-16 `WM_CHAR`, focus, DPI); fed into ImGui headless it drags the slider, types into the text field, clicks the button and selects a combo entry with the keyboard (needs `NavEnableKeyboard`).
  - Build cost: `dear-imgui-sys` C++ rebuild 16 s (release), cold release build of the spike 54 s, `live.exe` 2.3 MB. API churn is high (0.20 to 0.22 in three weeks, renderer constructor changed): keep exact pins.
  - Fallback `imgui` 0.12 + `imgui-rs-vulkan-renderer` 1.16.0 (MIT, ash 0.38, has `dynamic-rendering`) builds; not run, not needed.
  - Not yet verified (live harness `src/bin/live.rs` and `scripts/run-t08-live.ps1` written, not run): F12 focus hand-off to the output window and back to the source (10 cycles, windowed and fullscreen), F11 toggle with the source focused, whether `RegisterHotKey(VK_F12)` succeeds (Microsoft documents F12 as reserved for the debugger), and whether `SetForegroundWindow` needs a workaround.
- **SPIKE T-08, live part (2026-11-19, branch `task/T-08live`, `spikes/imgui-spike/RESULTS.md` section 9):**
  - F12: `RegisterHotKey(F12)` fails with "Hot key is already registered" (1409) on the reference PC, also from a bare PowerShell process: another program holds F12 system-wide (not the debugger reservation; holder not identified). F11, F10 and Ctrl+Alt+F12 register. The menu key must be configurable, with a visible fallback when registration fails.
  - Hand-off: with Ctrl+Alt+F12 or F10, windowed and fullscreen, 10 cycles each: 10/10 opens activated the menu window, 10/10 typed "t08" reached the ImGui text field, 10/10 closes returned the foreground to the source, independent poll exactly 20 changes, 0 validation messages. A 28-cycle (94 s) run: 28/28, 56 changes of 56. Plain `SetForegroundWindow` from the `WM_HOTKEY` handler was enough every time (`auto` never fell to Alt or AttachThreadInput; forced `plain` identical): no synthetic Alt and no input synthesis needed in the product (a hotkey press is an input event; hand-offs not triggered by input were not tested).
  - External activation of T-07 (about 25 s after start): not reproduced in the spike window (shown `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE`, `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` while closed); the menu was open across the 25 s mark and the log shows no extra foreground change. Cause of the difference not isolated.
  - Overlay GPU cost: 0.15 ms windowed (about 1280x720), 0.27 ms fullscreen 2560x1440.
  - Not tested: real mouse and keyboard by a person, a real game as the source, hand-off not triggered by input. One unexplained transient (empty foreground title for one live poll sample during an open in run 2 fullscreen; the independent poll saw nothing).
  - Recommendation for T-52a: crate set A pinned (`dear-imgui-rs`, `dear-imgui-sys`, `dear-imgui-ash` `=0.22.0`, dynamic rendering) with our own `input.rs`. Hand-off design: on the menu key, drop `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`, remember the foreground window, `SetForegroundWindow(output)`; on close restore `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` and `SetForegroundWindow(remembered)`. Plain method only; leave the Alt and attach fallbacks out of the product unless the game test (T-06 game) shows plain failing. Configurable menu key, F12 default, with a fallback (for example Ctrl+Alt+F12) when registration fails.
- **DECIDED (T-52a, 2026-11-19): crate set A, integrated in the product.** `dear-imgui-rs`, `dear-imgui-sys`, `dear-imgui-ash` pinned `=0.22.0` (renderer `default-features = false`, `dynamic-rendering`), no platform backend; the spike's `input.rs` copied into `src/ui/input.rs`; menu key default F10 (D-17, the hotkey is T-54). Integration (ARCHITECTURE 3.8): `Ui` owns the context and the renderer (created after the device, `Drop` shuts the renderer down before the context and the device), the UI pass is the last step of `Pipeline::record_ui` after `encode` (display space, only while shown, bit-exact when hidden), in-flight count = `FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT` (2). Headless tests `tests/ui_menu.rs` pass with validation and synchronization validation silent; UI GPU cost at 1920 x 1080 headless: overlay 0.007 ms, menu and overlay 0.074 ms. Licence: notices of Dear ImGui and cimgui in `THIRD-PARTY.md`, appended to the release zip; `cargo deny check licenses` passes. Still to check live: `scripts/check-menu.ps1` (focus hand-off of the product, 10 cycles windowed and fullscreen, 10 min closed), with the T-54 hotkey.

### TEC-6 Pass architecture
- **Q:** Fixed ordered pipeline in code, or a data-driven multi-pass graph like slangp? Compute or fragment? Intermediate formats?
- **Why:** Flexibility versus simplicity. A physical pipeline has an order (the seven stages in `CRT.md`) and is not meant to be rearranged. Mask integration, convolution and accumulation suit compute, final present suits fragment.
- **Who:** RESEARCH plus SPIKE.
- **Default:** Fixed ordered stages with compute for model passes and one fragment pass for output encoding and UI composition. No user-defined graphs.

### TEC-7 Shader testing and reference renderer
- **Q:** How are shaders tested and is a high-precision offline reference renderer a first-class deliverable (headless, deterministic, arbitrary supersampling, image in and floating-point out, golden-image diffs with tolerance, analytic tests from `CRT.md`, invariants like energy conservation and symmetry, software Vulkan in CI)? Should it be a slow CPU renderer in double precision, or a headless compute path at high supersampling?
- **Why:** There is no practical way to validate a physical model by eye. Floating-point results differ across GPUs, so pure golden images are flaky. A reference quantifies approximation error of prefiltered masks and truncated tails, and enables the sensitivity analysis in PHY-5.
- **Who:** USER (scope and cost), RESEARCH.
- **Default:** Yes. Headless render command sharing the same shader source, analytic test vectors from SCP-3 as the primary tests, a few golden images per preset with tolerance and only per GPU class, CI on a software Vulkan device if stable. A CPU double-precision crate for the analytic parts (beam, mask integration, halation) is optional, recommended by Sonnet and answered by the headless compute path in Opus and Fable.

### TEC-8 Robustness
- **Q:** What happens on device lost, TDR, monitor hot-plug, HDR toggle, resolution change or capture session loss, and which diagnostics are required (GPU timestamps per stage, frame-time and capture-jitter overlay, logging, RenderDoc compatibility with interop)?
- **Why:** These are frequent in a long-running background tool, and PRF-1 and TIM-2 cannot be settled without measurements.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Recreate swapchain and capture automatically, full device recreate on device lost, log everything. GPU timestamp queries and an optional stats overlay from day one.

### TEC-9 Build and CI
- **Q:** What build system, pinned toolchain and Vulkan SDK version, and is CI wanted given hosted Windows runners have no GPU and cannot run window capture?
- **Why:** GPU-dependent tests cannot run on hosted runners, and SDK drift changes shader compilation.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Local-first build with a pinned Rust toolchain and SDK, a local test command, CI limited to build, unit tests and offline-renderer tests on a software rasteriser, cargo-deny for licences.

## Project, legal and process

### PRJ-1 Audience, distribution and licence
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** personal project. Public-quality architecture stays a goal, licence is not needed now.
- **Q:** Is this strictly personal, shared as binaries, or open source, and under which licence? If binaries are shared, is code signing planned?
- **Why:** It decides the hardware matrix, installer, signing, support, documentation, licensing and how much generality is worth paying for. An unsigned program that captures windows, draws a topmost overlay and registers global hotkeys attracts SmartScreen and antivirus warnings.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Personal first, public-quality architecture, nothing that prevents release. Source build for personal use, signed release later if ever. MIT/Apache-2.0 dual licence if released.

### PRJ-2 Reuse of third-party code
- **DECISION (2026-11-18):** no GPL shader code is copied. Reading existing shaders for ideas is allowed, implementation is written from physics, papers and permissive sources.
- **Q:** May code be derived from GPL projects (crt-royale, crt-guest-advanced, crt-geom, ShaderGlass, Magpie), or must the implementation be written from physics, papers and permissively licensed sources (the Blur Busters beam simulator is MIT)? Check each source's actual licence.
- **Why:** Deriving from GPL code makes the combined work GPL if distributed. Reading for ideas differs from copying, and formulas and published data are free.
- **Who:** USER plus RESEARCH (licence verification).
- **Default:** Clean-room from `CRT.md` and papers. Adapt permissive code only with attribution. Record every external source consulted and the licence of every dependency.

### PRJ-3 Data licensing and brand names
- **Q:** May pitch tables, datasheet values, hobbyist measurements and photos be embedded in presets and docs, and may presets name vendors and models (Sony, Trinitron, PVM, Diamondtron, CromaClear, Megatron) and mention competing tools?
- **Why:** Facts are generally free, but compiled databases and photos can carry rights, notably in the EU. Naming a preset after a model claims fidelity the data cannot support, and trademark exposure is higher in distribution.
- **Who:** USER, RESEARCH if public.
- **Default:** Store values with citations, do not copy tables wholesale or redistribute third-party photos. Generic class names in the UI, model names only in descriptive citations.

### PRJ-4 Anti-cheat, antivirus and privacy
- **Q:** Do the games you run use anti-cheat (EAC, BattlEye, Vanguard), and is a topmost overlay plus global hotkeys acceptable there? What privacy guarantees apply (captures only the chosen window, offline, no telemetry, frames never leave the GPU unless a screenshot is requested)?
- **Why:** External capture without injection is the best stance, but overlays and low-level hooks are what anti-cheat and antivirus heuristics look for. Capture tools are sensitive.
- **Who:** USER plus SPIKE (SP-11).
- **Default:** Anti-cheat titles are out of scope. No hooks, no injection, no input synthesis. Offline, no telemetry.

- **Result (T-11 / SP-11, 2026-11-19, PROVISIONAL: one machine, no anti-cheat title tested):** Windows Defender active (antimalware client 4.18.26100.4, real-time protection and behaviour monitoring on, tamper protection on, signatures of 2026-11-19 00:42, nothing changed). `Get-MpThreatDetection` held 0 entries and `Get-MpThreat` 0 before and after about 20 live runs of the unsigned release build (`Get-AuthenticodeSignature`: NotSigned) over about 20 minutes, each with the global hotkey Ctrl+Alt+Q (the quit chord then) registered, a topmost non-activating fullscreen or windowed output, Windows.Graphics.Capture of the pixel-demo window beside it, and PNG writes. The Defender operational log held no detection or remediation event (ids 1006 to 1008, 1015, 1116 to 1119) in that period. Pass for Defender. Not tested: a debug build, other antivirus products, SmartScreen on a downloaded zip, any anti-cheat title.

### PRJ-5 Time budget and maintenance
- **Q:** How many hours per week, for how long, and who maintains it as Windows, drivers and ImGui bindings change? How much will be AI-written and user-reviewed, and how are AI-sourced facts verified (several `CRT.md` values are tagged [E])?
- **Why:** The physics scope in `CRT.md` can absorb years. The tool sits on OS behaviours that change between builds. Unverified numbers can silently become "physical constants" in presets.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Timeboxed phases of 2 to 4 weeks with a usable tool after each. Every [E] value stays tagged in the preset until replaced by a sourced value. Pin supported Windows builds and re-run the spikes after updates.

### PRJ-6 First usable version and milestones
- **Q:** What is the smallest version you would use daily, and are the proposed milestones acceptable?
- **Why:** It orders the work and keeps physics from starting before the capture and presentation path is proven.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default first version:** capture one window, native-resolution recovery, no-focus borderless fullscreen output, one aperture-grille tube with gun law, amplifier stage, current-dependent beam, area-filtered mask and phosphor colour, SDR output, menu with save and load. No HDR, no temporal model, no halation in the first cut.
- **Default milestones:** M0 spikes. M1 first usable version. M2 optical stage (halation, vignette) (HDR output dropped, see COL-1). M3 slot and delta masks, geometry. M4 temporal rolling scan. M5 validation against a reference tube and polish.

### PRJ-7 Documentation as a deliverable
- **Q:** Is a model specification (equations, units, parameter list, provenance) kept separate from `CRT.md` and in sync with the code, and is a user guide needed?
- **Why:** "Physically accurate" is only credible with traceability from each shader parameter to an equation and a source.
- **Who:** USER.
- **Default:** Yes. One model document per stage with provenance tags, one parameter table with units, range, default and tag, and a short user guide.

### REN-1 Project name
- **DECISION (2026-11-21, owner):** the project is renamed to **Phoxium**, everywhere (README, code, GitHub), and the README is simplified for users.
- **Done in the repository:** product name `Phoxium` in every user-visible text and in the version resources; crate, folder, package and binary `phoxium` (`crates/phoxium`, `phoxium.exe`); test knobs and GLSL macros `PHOXIUM_*` (no compatibility layer); settings folder `%APPDATA%\Phoxium`, with a one-time copy of the settings folder of the earlier versions (`%APPDATA%\ultimateCRT`) at the start of a live session when the new folder does not exist (`migrate.rs`: copy into a temporary sibling then rename, never overwrite, never delete the old folder, a failure is a warning and is retried next start); zip `phoxium-vX.Y.Z-windows-x64.zip`; `release.ps1` default repository `arnaud-sintes/Phoxium`. The README keeps the user guide only; the detail moved to `docs/REFERENCE.md`, which the zip carries.
- **Kept on purpose:** the burn-in file magic `UCRTWEAR` (saved wear maps stay readable); the released CHANGELOG sections and their links; the published v0.2.0 and v0.3.0 assets on GitHub (`ultimatecrt-...zip`).
- **Follow-up (2026-11-23, owner request):** the old name is removed from `PLAN.md`, this file, the documentation, the scripts and the code comments, except the migration of the old settings folder, the burn-in magic and the released changelog sections above. The GitHub repository is renamed and `origin` points at it; the local folder and worktrees already carry the new name.

## Tensions inside the need

These contradictions or conflicts were raised by at least two analysts.
Resolving them is part of answering the questions above.

| Id | Tension | Related questions |
|---|---|---|
| T-1 | "Physically accurate" against the host display. PVM-class masks need 3 to 8 host px per triad and 3 to 7× HDR headroom, which 1080p and SDR outputs cannot give. "Universal" and "physically accurate" collide on the output side | PHY-2, COL-1, HW-4 |
| T-2 | "Each source RGB pixel becomes real RGB light sources" against the physics: the beam is continuous, the mask samples it, and a pixel is a voltage sample after amplifier filtering | SCP-5 |
| T-3 | "Interacting with the output must not steal focus" against an ImGui menu that needs keyboard and mouse focus, a fullscreen window that is normally foreground, and games that hide or clip the cursor | OUT-3, OUT-4 |
| T-4 | "Universal" against "generally 1:1 pixel art". The scanline model needs a known low line count, and a scaled or smoothed capture breaks it | SRC-1, SRC-2 |
| T-5 | "As fast as possible" against a heavy physical model (non-separable halation ring, area-integrated masks, up to 4 sub-frames at 240 Hz) sharing the GPU with the game, with no budget or priority order | PRF-1, PRF-2, PHY-10 |
| T-6 | "As fast as possible" and low latency against "no injection": external capture has a latency floor, and rolling scan adds intentional delay | TIM-1, CAP-8 |
| T-7 | "Physical, not emulated" against the data: the structure can be physical, but most parameters are estimates, and named vendor presets imply precision that does not exist | PHY-4, PHY-5, SCP-1 |
| T-8 | Temporal accuracy against brightness and panel behaviour: sub-frame scanning multiplies peak luminance on top of mask loss, while panels have ABL, local dimming and response time. Temporal accuracy also needs a perfectly paced output from a never-foreground process | PHY-12, COL-1, OUT-2 |
| T-9 | "All parameters tunable live" against "physical": independent sliders allow impossible tubes, and live tunability conflicts with precomputation and parameters that change resource sizes | PHY-16, PRF-4 |
| T-10 | Vulkan against the D3D-native Windows capture and present chain: an interop layer is mandatory, and HDR and pacing control are less direct. GLSL and Dear ImGui on a Rust core add binding-maintenance risk | TEC-1, TEC-2, TEC-5 |
| T-11 | "Runs externally, no injection" against exclusive-fullscreen games, protected content, mouse alignment with curvature, and input forwarding to a covered window (which resembles injection) | CAP-5, OUT-4, PRJ-4 |
| T-12 | Fullscreen output on the same monitor as the captured window: the output covers its own source, and the pointer lives in the source's coordinates, not the picture's | OUT-1, CAP-4 |
| T-13 | Source fidelity against compositor reality: the capture is already a composited 8-bit frame, possibly scaled or colour-managed, and an HDR desktop changes the encoding. The model wants raw DAC code values. Interlace, composite artefacts and sub-frame timing are lost before the model starts | CAP-3, SRC-7 |
| T-14 | Physical colour fidelity against art intent: tube primaries and D93 make colours differ from what an sRGB-authored game shows | COL-2 |
| T-15 | OLED as the ideal output (fast, HDR, true black) against static high-contrast mask patterns (burn-in) and panel ABL fighting the CRT ABL model | HW-7 |
| T-16 | A 4:3 tube against 16:9 content and hosts: much modern pixel art is 16:9, and a 4:3 image uses only 2880 of 3840 columns on 4K | SRC-4 |

## Risks that could invalidate the project

Each risk is retired or confirmed by the listed spike.

| Risk | What would happen | Retired by |
|---|---|---|
| The one-monitor scenario is infeasible: covered sources throttle or stop delivering frames | A second monitor becomes the only supported setup | SP-1 |
| The no-focus window is always compositor-composed (no independent flip), adding a frame and no VRR | Total latency of 2 to 3 frames, unpleasant for action games | SP-2, SP-3 |
| The differentiator is not visible: with an area-filtered mask and a frame-integrated temporal model, much of the physics collapses to blur, gamma and glare | The project loses its reason to exist beyond learning | SP-7 |
| The faithful model looks dimmer, softer or more moiré-prone than tuned-by-eye shaders on your display | Fidelity versus appeal must be decided (SCP-8) | SP-6, SP-7 |
| Key constants stay estimates (spot growth, decay tails, consumer pitch, halation amplitude) | "Physical" claim must be worded honestly | SP-13 |
| (Retired 2026-11-18: output is SDR only) HDR on the host is worse than SDR (aggressive ABL, FALD blooming on fine patterns, unstable EOTF) | HDR cannot be the reference | SP-4 |
| Windows capture or present behaviour changes across builds (the reference PC is an Insider build) | Breakage after updates | HW-2 policy, re-run spikes |
| Hybrid graphics: the Intel iGPU composes the desktop while the game and our renderer run on the NVIDIA GPU | Cross-adapter copy of every captured frame, extra latency, no shared-handle handoff, or a renderer too slow on the iGPU | SP-1 (new, see HW-3) |
| Game plus tool exceed the GPU budget, dropping the source cadence | Temporal effects and pacing corrupted | SP-8 |

## Spikes and prototypes

Each spike is small (hours to 2 days), throw-away, and retires named risks and questions.
Recommended order: SP-1 and SP-2 first (they can kill the capture and no-focus premises), then SP-3 and SP-4, with SP-7 in parallel with your answers to the decisions table.

| Id | Spike | Retires | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| SP-1 | Capture one window with Windows.Graphics.Capture from Rust and show it in a Vulkan window, with both CPU-copy and shared-texture (NT handle plus fence) handoff. Test windowed, borderless, covered, off-screen, minimised, exclusive-fullscreen, DPI-scaled and HDR-desktop targets, the cursor option, border removal and self-exclusion, on your top 5 sources | CAP-1, CAP-2, CAP-4, CAP-5, CAP-6, CAP-7, CAP-9, HW-2, TEC-2 | Frames arrive from a fully covered window at source rate. Handoff cost measured at 1080p and 4K. Minimum Windows build known |
| SP-2 | Raw Win32 borderless topmost non-activating window (and a click-through variant) with a Vulkan swapchain over a focused target on one monitor. Log the foreground window, check game pause on deactivation, raw-input and cursor-lock games, gamepad, Alt-Tab, flip mode (PresentMon), missed presents under game load. Include a menu window and an in-output overlay with manual ImGui input feeding, hotkeys and the panic exit | OUT-2, OUT-3, OUT-4, OUT-5, TEC-4, TEC-5 | The game never loses focus or throttles. Present mode identified. No missed refresh over 10 minutes |
| SP-3 | End-to-end latency and cadence: a frame-counter source filmed with a high-speed camera or photodiode next to the output. Log capture timestamps for 60, 60.1, 50 and 70 Hz sources against 60, 120, 240 Hz and VRR outputs. Count duplicates, drops and jitter. Try frame-locked versus free-running field phase | CAP-8, TIM-1, TIM-2, SRC-6 | Latency floor in ms. Jitter histogram. Pacing policy chosen |
| SP-4 (deferred: HDR output out of scope, replace by an SDR output precision and banding test) | HDR path: HDR10 and scRGB Vulkan swapchains in the borderless window on your panel. Measure luminance of known patches and fine 1-pixel stripes at peak, panel ABL at small window sizes, and test brightness compensation for mask loss. Render a full-field mask pattern at 100 cd/m² CRT white in SDR and HDR | COL-1, COL-3, HW-4, HW-7, TEC-1 | A known code value gives a known cd/m² within tolerance. Panel ABL and dimming characterised |
| SP-5 | Source-fidelity test: a test-pattern application with known pixels captured with scaling, DPI virtualisation, Auto HDR, Night Light and colour management on and off, diffed bit for bit. Native-raster and integer-scale auto-detection on emulator frames at 3×/4× with bilinear filtering | CAP-3, SRC-2 | Captured values proven bit-exact, or the conditions that break it listed. Detection reliability known |
| SP-6 | Mask budget visual test: analytic aperture grille, slot and delta masks viewed at the real tube-to-pixel scale on your display, comparing area-integrated, enlarged-cell, zoomed and host-subpixel variants. Photograph the result | PHY-1, PHY-2, PHY-8, HW-6, OUT-7 | You pick a mask policy after seeing it on your own panel |
| SP-7 | Offline reference renderer for one aperture-grille tube (gun law, amplifier, beam, mask, halation) with heavy supersampling, plus the analytic test table from `CRT.md` as unit tests (scanline modulation, mask pitch, ring radius). Side-by-side with ShaderGlass plus an existing shader. Sensitivity analysis of the uncertain parameters | SCP-1, SCP-3, SCP-7, PHY-5, PHY-8, TEC-7 | Analytic checks pass. You confirm a visible difference and know which unknowns matter most |
| SP-8 | Cost study at 4K on your GPU: halation ring (FFT versus mip pyramid versus sparse ring sampling), multi-scanline beam integration and mask integration, FP16 history buffers, and rolling scan at 240 Hz with 4 sub-frames while a game runs | PRF-1, PRF-2, PRF-3, PHY-10 | Frame time and headroom known |
| SP-9 | Toolchain: GLSL to SPIR-V in Rust with reflection-driven parameter metadata, hot reload, and a headless software-Vulkan render for tests | TEC-3, TEC-6, TEC-9 | A parameter defined once appears in shader, Rust and menu. A headless test runs without a GPU |
| SP-10 | VRR behaviour of the unfocused output window and its interaction with a fixed-refresh rolling scan | OUT-6, TIM-2 | VRR engages or is ruled out |
| SP-11 | Antivirus and anti-cheat smoke test: an unsigned build capturing and overlaying with a registered hotkey, run beside your real games | PRJ-4, OUT-5 | No flags, or the offenders identified |
| SP-12 | Rolling-scan feasibility: port the MIT Blur Busters beam-simulator logic into the spike pipeline at your maximum refresh and check banding, flicker and brightness loss with a 60 Hz test source | PHY-12 | Feasible or ruled out on your display |
| SP-13 | Reference data feasibility: photograph one real CRT (or use published photos) and fit a scanline profile and pitch. Test whether a home measurement of primaries or phosphor decay is practical | SCP-2, PHY-5, PHY-7 | You know what you can measure yourself |

## Where the analysts disagreed

| Topic | Positions | How it is handled here |
|---|---|---|
| Reference topology | Sonnet: two-monitor as the supported primary mode, one-monitor only if the spike works. Opus and Fable: one-monitor is the design case because it is the hard one | OUT-1: two-monitor guaranteed, one-monitor as the design target pending SP-1 and SP-2. Needs your answer |
| Frame handoff to Vulkan | Sonnet and Opus: zero-copy interop by default. Fable: CPU copy first because small sources make it cheap | CAP-2: decide after measuring the capture size you actually use |
| Minimum Windows build | Sonnet: Windows 11 only. Opus: Windows 11 24H2+. Fable: Windows 11 22H2+ | HW-2: Windows 11 only, exact build after SP-1 |
| Temporal scope in the first version | Sonnet: frame-integrated plus optional afterglow. Opus: frame afterglow and ABL in the first version. Fable: frame-integrated, ABL later | PHY-12 and PHY-13: frame-integrated first, ABL and rolling scan later |
| Compensating mask loss in SDR | Sonnet: separate "host compensation" group. Opus: allow in a separate non-physical stage. Fable: honest exposure control, no hidden bloom | SCP-8 and COL-1: exposure slider plus an optional labelled compensation stage |
| Reference renderer implementation | Sonnet: slow double-precision CPU crate. Opus and Fable: headless compute path at high supersampling | TEC-7: headless compute path first, CPU crate optional |
| Vulkan binding for ImGui | Opus: evaluate dear-imgui-rs first. Sonnet and Fable: whichever passes a spike | TEC-5: spike first, dear-imgui-rs evaluated first |
| TATE (rotated) output | Opus: in scope early because rotation is cheap if planned. Sonnet: decide explicitly | SCP-6: user decision |
| Tube quality default | Fable: factory-adjusted at the low end of defect ranges. Opus: factory-adjusted reference with ageing sliders | SCP-9: factory-adjusted, defects as parameters |
| Same data, different spot sizes | Opus used much smaller spot sizes than Sonnet and Fable in the physical tables | Already resolved in `CRT.md` (see its "Resolved conflicts") |

## Feasibility facts to verify

These came from the Opus analyst's web checks or the analysts' own knowledge.
None of them is verified on your machine, which is why they are tied to spikes.

- Borderless capture (`IsBorderRequired = false`) needs Windows build 20348 or later and an access request. [Microsoft Learn: GraphicsCaptureSession.IsBorderRequired]
- `MinUpdateInterval` and cursor-capture toggles arrived in later builds, and a frame-rate cap near 50 fps was reported on some builds even with `MinUpdateInterval` = 0. [Win32CaptureSample issue 82]
- OBS skips minimised (iconic) windows when capturing with the WinRT API, and libwebrtc's capture session drops frames for minimised windows. [OBS winrt-capture source, libwebrtc wgc_capture_session]
- `imgui` 0.12 (imgui-rs) tracks Dear ImGui 1.89. `dear-imgui-rs` 0.22.0 tracks 1.93.1 WIP with docking (crates.io, checked by T-08 on 2026-11-19).
- The reference PC reports Windows build 10.0.26300, which Opus identified as an Insider build. No analyst checked which capture features that build has.
- Not yet checked by anyone: behaviour of Vulkan swapchains on Windows when a non-activating topmost window is presented (layered on DXGI or not), VRR for an unfocused window, HDR swapchain colour spaces per vendor, D3D11 to Vulkan external memory support per vendor, and whether hosted CI has any usable software Vulkan implementation.

## Live review on the real desktop (2026-11-20, evening, owner away)

Binaries: release build of `main` dd1b272 (version 0.2.0 strings). Every run used a temporary settings folder (`APPDATA`), the owner's real `settings.toml` was not touched (same SHA-256 before and after). Machine: RTX A2000 drives the 1920x1080 monitor at (0, 0), Intel UHD drives the 1920x1200 monitor at (1920, -116); both 60 Hz. Source: pixel-demo `--content motion --native-preset snes --present integer --scale 3` (768x672 client) unless stated. Numbers are from `--stats` and `--log`.

### Zero-copy capture on real windows

- **Accepted on both adapters.** RTX (default auto): "capture path: zero-copy asked (Vulkan import of shared textures: NT handle)", the path in force stays zero-copy for the whole run (298 of 298 log seconds in a 5-minute run). Intel UHD (`--gpu 1`, fullscreen on its own monitor): also accepted and kept.
- **Cost, RTX, windowed, 30 s each, same scene:** zero-copy copy 1.13 ms per frame on average (0.3 to 1.9 ms per second), GPU frame 2.16 ms, no upload segment; CPU copy 2.43 ms per frame plus a 0.62 ms upload, GPU frame 2.55 ms. Capture rate 56.8 fps (zero-copy) and 57.2 fps (CPU), render 60 fps in both.
- **Intel UHD, fullscreen 1920x1200:** capture 40 to 52 fps, render 60 to 61 fps, GPU frame about 10 ms (the screen stage alone about 9 ms), zero-copy copy 1.4 to 3.9 ms per frame (10 ms in the first second).
- **5-minute stability (RTX, windowed, zero-copy):** no stop, 291 output screenshots one second apart, never two identical in a row; capture 52.4 fps average (41.9 minimum per second), render 60.2 fps (55.4 minimum); 13 capture intervals and 32 render intervals above 50 ms over 298 s; quit by Ctrl+Alt+End within 8 s.
- **Capture border:** no yellow border around the captured window (0 yellow pixels in the 7 rows above the frame), zero-copy and CPU alike.
- **Minimise and restore the source (zero-copy):** the capture pauses (longest capture interval 1.5 s, 0 fps in that second) and resumes by itself; no crash.
- **Resize the source while running:** CPU copy: 4 resizes (900x760, 600x520, 1100x900, back), no problem, the new native size is detected each time. Zero-copy: the same 4 resizes passed in one run, but another run crashed at the first resize with the start-up crash below.
- **Live switch through the menu (F10, Source, Capture combo, by mouse):** zero-copy to CPU and back, twice each, under `--validation`: the console reports each change ("capture: path set to cpu", "... zero-copy"), the static corner marker of the output keeps its level in 60 screenshots over 1 s after each click (no black frame), no capture or render interval above 50 ms in the seconds of the switches, **0 validation messages** in 78 s.
- **BUG, crash at attach with zero-copy (intermittent, severe):** `panicked at crates\phoxium\src\scaling.rs:288:44: range start index 49216 out of range for slice of length 0` in `scaling::Detector::feed`, called from `app::run` (app.rs line 1180, `detector.feed(&frame.data, ...)`), a few hundred ms after the first placement line. With zero-copy the frame's CPU pixel buffer is empty until the first CPU sample, but the scale detector is fed with it. Count: 10 crashes in 35 zero-copy starts on the RTX (27 direct runs plus 8 starts inside the regression scripts), 4 of 5 on the Intel UHD, 1 crash at a source resize after a good start; 0 crashes in 12 CPU-copy starts. The crash handler works (crash file written, settings saved). Repro: `pixel-demo --content motion --native-preset snes --present integer --scale 3`, then `phoxium --window pixel-demo` (default zero-copy), repeated 5 times. Workaround: `--capture-path cpu`.
- **FIXED (2026-11-20, after the review; root cause confirmed headless):** the buffer was not "empty until the first sample". While a detection runs (the integer scale at attach, again after a resize) every zero-copy frame is read back for the CPU analyses. When the capture thread started the read-back of frame N+1 before the main loop had taken frame N, it took frame N's sample buffer out of the shared slot to refill it; the main loop could then take frame N during that read-back, marked as carrying its own pixels, with an empty buffer, and the detector indexed it. The window is a read-back long (about 1 ms), at every frame of a detection, hence intermittent and more frequent on the slower Intel GPU; the CPU copy holds the lock during its copy and never had the window. Reproduced deterministically in `tests/zero_copy.rs` (`a_frame_taken_while_the_next_sample_is_read_back_keeps_its_pixels`: a test hook holds the capture thread inside that window; before the fix the frame came out with 0 bytes for 160x100 and the app's detector call panicked at the same line). Fix: the capture thread leaves a sample not taken yet in place (a new buffer is used instead); every frame records the size of the picture its buffer holds; the analyses read pixels only through a checked sample (`Frame::sample`: this frame's own pixels, `Frame::last_sample`: the newest picture of this size; module `analysis`), and every detector refuses a buffer that does not hold its region (fuzz test with random lengths and sizes, 0 and huge included). Detection quality under zero-copy, headless: x3 locked in 0.5 s with the loop's rule (every frame sampled while detecting) and in 3.3 s from the 10-per-second samples alone; the Auto picture area found 144 x 108 at 24, 18 inside black bars. To verify live: 30 zero-copy starts on each GPU, and source resizes.
- **White flash at start (seen in the same review):** a white client area for about 0.3 s when the output window opened, before the first frame. Fixed with the crash: the window class background is black, the client area is filled black until the first frame, and the window is shown only once its first frame is presented (or after 1 s without one). To verify live.
- **Note:** the console prints "capture path: zero-copy" every second also for `--pattern` sources, which capture nothing.

### Phosphor persistence through the real output window

Method: `phoxium --pattern bar:8:24` (a white bar 8 source pixels wide moving 24 source pixels per frame on black, 320x240), windowed output 1264x1181 on the RTX monitor (4 host px per source px), screenshots of the output window (System.Drawing), green and blue column means over the middle 20 % of the height, at the places the bar occupied 1 to 6 frames earlier (the same method as `tests/persistence_chain.rs`). Settings in a temporary `settings.toml`, preset `delta-17in` (persistence on); medians over 25 to 60 screenshots. Expected (README, headless test, 60 Hz): bar 212, green 57, 44, 37, 33 one to four frames behind.

| Case | Bar G | Green 1 to 6 frames behind | Red 1 to 4 behind |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bare defaults (persistence off) | 235 | 0 0 0 0 0 0 | 0 |
| Preset, persistence on | 203 | 57 39 29 24 20 16 | 16 11 8 6 |
| Same, window moved to the Intel UHD monitor | 201 | 57 38 28 23 18 15 | - |
| Same, back on the RTX monitor, smaller window | 205 | 57 39 33 27 23 20 | - |
| Extra afterglow 0.5, 0.5, 0.5 at 150 ms | 182 | 67 50 41 32 25 18 | 52 42 34 26 |
| Decay scale 20 | 192 | 70 48 36 29 24 19 | 89 34 15 9 |
| A/B bypass on (Ctrl+Alt+F7) | 255 | 0 0 0 0 0 0 | 0 |

- The first trail frame matches the headless value (57); frames 2 to 4 are lower than the headless test (39, 29, 24 against 44, 37, 33). **Explained 2026-11-20 (PHY-18):** the measurement stepped back 99 px per frame instead of 94.8 px; at the true places the model gives 58, 45, 38, 34 under these conditions, and the same reading of the headless output gives the live values. The trail keeps the same age profile on the other adapter's monitor (time constant follows real time). Red shows a small trail (16 one frame behind) in the output, from the colour matrix, although the red phosphor itself has none.
- Extra afterglow 0.5 at 150 ms raises the trail four frames behind from 24 to 32 (+35 %) and dims the moving bar from 203 to 182; decay scale 20 gives 29 four frames behind (README states 40). Both remain subtle at 60 Hz: the owner's "trail too short" matches the physics plus these values.
- **BUG, persistence lost after the A/B bypass:** with persistence on, Ctrl+Alt+F7 (bypass on) then Ctrl+Alt+F7 again (console "bypass: off (CRT chain on)"): the trail is 0 in every slot and the bar is 217 in R, G and B (persistence off look), and it stays so for at least 10 s; the phosphor segment still runs (0.10 ms). Reproduced twice. Repro: settings `preset = "delta-17in"`, `phoxium --pattern bar:8:24`, press Ctrl+Alt+F7 twice, compare the trail behind the bar before and after. Restarting brings it back. This may be what the owner saw as "no trail". **Resolved 2026-11-20 (PHY-18):** the uneven frame pacing after the bypass made the automatic trail rate refit, so restart the history, at nearly every frame; fixed in the phosphor stage, to be confirmed live.
- The owner's real settings carry `phosphor_frame_rate_hz = 397` (the manual trail rate the README warns about); not changed here.
- Further persistence checks (preset `delta-17in`): a static picture keeps its level with persistence and extra afterglow 0.5 at 150 ms (centre of `--pattern window:30`: 215.9 in all 171 samples over 10 s, no brightening or darkening). The trail one frame behind the moving bar stays between 50 and 62 over 135 screenshots in 5.6 s: no reset or flash every second. At start the output shows a full white area (255) for about 0.3 s before the first rendered frame (seen in the first 5 samples of the static run): a white flash at window creation.

### Look controls (glow, vignette, chromatic aberration), screenshots

pixel-demo `--content card --native-preset snes --scale 2`, CPU copy (to avoid the zero-copy start-up crash), bare defaults plus the overrides below, output window 1264x1000 client on the RTX monitor. Codes are 8-bit green means of the output. GPU times from `--stats` (whole frame about 2 to 4.5 ms in every case, on a busy desktop; optics segment 0.6 to 1.4 ms, screen 1 to 2.4 ms).

| Case | Overrides | Black areas (centre, corner) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default | none | 0, 0 | Reference. |
| Glow | optics on, bloom 0.2, 6 mm, falloff 0.7, threshold 0.6 | 27, 20 | Visible soft halo around white squares and lines; also a general grey veil over every black area. |
| Gentle glow | optics on, bloom 0.08, 4 mm | 26, 21 | Halo hard to see; the veil is the same as the strong glow. |
| Strong vignette | optics on, corner 0.15, start 0.3, exponent 3, softness 0.3 | 24, 15 | Clear, smooth darkening toward the corners (white bar near the top-left corner 231 to 161), no banding, no visible start ring. Good. |
| Black corners | optics on, corner 0 | 18, 1 | Not "black corners": the whole picture becomes a spotlight (a white area at mid-left edge 165 to 24), because the default start is 0 (falloff from the centre). |
| Chromatic aberration, lateral 1.5 mm | none else | 0, 0 | Thin red and blue fringes at the picture edges, centre clean; colour fringes on 16 % of pixels (5 % default). Good "cheap TV" look. |
| Chromatic aberration, lateral 3 mm | none else | 0, 0 | Strong fringes at the edges and colour moire on the fine patterns near the sides (23 %). Strong but believable for a bad set. |
| Chromatic aberration, axis 0.5 mm, angle 0 | none else | 0, 0 | Uniform red-left / blue-right fringe on every vertical edge (13 %). Works. |
| Extra afterglow 0.5 | persistence on | 0, 6 behind moving parts | Static picture unchanged; trails as measured above. |

- **Finding: switching the optics stage on lifts the blacks by 20 to 27 codes on this bright test card even with the glow at 0** (vignette case). The cause is the optics defaults that are not glow: veiling glare 0.01 of the mean picture level and halation 0.015 (physically motivated, but in sRGB a linear 0.01 is about code 25). A user who only wants a vignette or a glow gets grey blacks. Proposal: when the user enables optics for glow or vignette, keep glare and halation at 0 unless chosen (or lower their defaults), and say in the Optics panel that glare lifts blacks.
- Recommended values: glow 0.12 to 0.15 at 6 mm, falloff 0.5, threshold 0.6 (visible halo, little veil; to verify once the glare default is settled); vignette 0.3 corner with start 0.3, exponent 3, softness 0.3 for a strong look, 0.85 (default) for the physical one; "black corners" needs start 0.6 to 0.7, exponent 4 to 6, softness 0.2 (the README and tooltip say corner 0 to 0.05 gives black corners, which with the default start darkens the whole picture); chromatic aberration lateral 0.5 to 1 mm (monitor) and 1.5 to 2 mm (TV), axis 0.2 to 0.5 mm.

The trigger is not identified (no ETW trace taken).

Double-click on the title bar maximises (frame 0,30 1920x1050 on the 1920x1080 monitor, the picture re-placed 1369x1027). The minimise button minimises; restore brings back the maximised window and the next click on the same button restores 900x700. The app keeps running in every state.

## Live check of the released v0.3.0 (2026-11-21, owner away)

The exact v0.3.0 zip binaries, an isolated settings folder (`APPDATA` pointed elsewhere; the owner's files unchanged, hashes before and after), the RTX A2000 monitor (1920x1080) and a 1536x960 second monitor on the Intel UHD.

- **Start without arguments:** the window appears 0.47 s after it is created, already showing the picker (42 samples in its first 1.5 s, client mean 27 of 255 throughout): no white flash. Typing `pixel` and Enter attaches pixel-demo; `--log` shows `capture_path` = `zero-copy` in every row.
- **Persistence** (`--preset delta-17in`, `phosphor_frame_rate_hz` 0, pixel-demo `--content motion --native 960x240 --present 1to1`, a 4 px bar moving 8 source px per frame, output 1.9625 px per source px): green 59, 45, 38, 34, 31, 28 one to six frames behind a bar of 225 (median of 37 shots; README 57, 44, 37, 33). Bypass on: trail 0, bar 255. No reset after the bypass, an output minimise and restore, or a source minimise and restore (no shot of a 6 s series below 40 % of the median trail energy).
- **Finding, fixed the same day (OUT-6, frame pacing): uneven present pacing of the framed output.** In the framed (windowed) output the loop's frame intervals alternate between about 1 to 3 ms and 31 to 53 ms (per-second min and max of `--log`) after a few seconds, with or without a bypass; borderless fullscreen stays at 15 to 18 ms. With a captured source the pairs of frames 1 ms apart cannot both have a new capture, and the screenshots then show the trail entries 16 source px apart instead of 8 (the trail is sampled every other source frame: 59.7 at 16 px, 43.7 at 32 px, 36.6 at 48 px), so moving content steps like 30 fps. Not caused by the bypass (a pattern source without any key shows it from about 5 s on). Capture ran at 44 to 58 fps for a 60 fps pixel-demo throughout.
- **Click-through** (fullscreen over pixel-demo, another window in the foreground): off, the hit test finds the output and a click leaves the foreground alone; on (`WS_EX_TRANSPARENT | WS_EX_LAYERED`), the hit test finds pixel-demo and the click activates it; off again restores the first case.
- **Minimised source:** title and console notice "source minimised: no frames (restore it to resume)", capture 0 fps, the last picture stays; restored: capture resumes (50 fps), same picture means. The notice is at the end of the title, so a long source title pushes it out of sight, and fullscreen has no title (the menu's System panel lists it).
- **Snap, F11, F10:** release at the left edge: left half of the work area (0,30 960x1050); top edge: the whole work area; the edge shared with the second monitor does not snap (by design). After a snap the window keeps the snapped size when dragged again (no restore of the earlier size, also by design). F11 twice: borderless topmost 1920x1080, then back to the framed rectangle, the source keeps the foreground. F10: the output takes the foreground, F10 again gives it back to the source.
- **Check scripts** on the zip binaries: check-capture PASS (x1 to x4 bit-exact), check-pixel-demo PASS (the freshness check accepted the zip binaries, built after the last source change), check-focus MITIGATED (one hand-back blip at 24.7 s, the known 25 s blip), check-hotkeys PASS, check-menu PASS windowed and fullscreen (3 cycles each).
- **Picture area Auto:** pixel-demo 1:1 in a 900x720 window: "Picture found inside the black bars: 256 x 240 at 322, 240". **BUG, fixed:** then pixel-demo key 3 (integer x3, window 768x720): "source scaling: native (x1), 768x720" in 5 of 9 tries with v0.3.0, each restarting both programs (the first frame after the resize still showed the 1:1 picture and locked x1 for the session). Fixed in `scaling::Detector` (x1 early only after 6 native frames, otherwise the frames vote); the same sequence with the fixed release build, interleaved with the v0.3.0 tries: x3 in 8 of 8. With an exact-size pixel-demo window (x3, x1, x3 again) v0.3.0 found x3 every time (3 of 3).
- **Optional modules from the settings file** (`--image`, `--validation`, flat grey 128, a black picture with a white square, colour bars; picture 1025x961): no crash, no validation message. Bare defaults: grey centre 107.9, corners 108.6 (no vignette). Optics on: centre 109.5, corners 103.0 (the vignette is on by default inside Optics, corner 0.85), black far from the white square 0.0 (glare and halation default 0). Ageing at 20 000 h: grey 107.9 / 107.5 / 106.7 (blue down 1.5 codes). Burn-in on a new tube: identical to the defaults (no wear yet). Composite: means unchanged, local maxima up to 194 on grey (artefacts). Interlace (alternate frames are fields) on a still image: means 108.0 to 108.2 over 6 shots, no visible change on a static picture. Blacks 0.0 in every case.

## Comparison with ShaderGlass (2026-11-25)

Asked by the owner (a TODO item). **Sources and limits:** the project's public pages only, [ShaderGlass on GitHub](https://github.com/mausimus/ShaderGlass) (the README, the releases page, the first page of the issues list) and what a web search returned (the itch.io and Steam pages, the v1.2 manual quoted by a search result); the FAQ and the online manual were not read, the releases page shows no year, and nothing here is measured: the input lag, the GPU cost and the image quality of ShaderGlass were not tested, and Phoxium's own input latency is not measured either. No head-to-head evidence was found. What is not stated on those pages is marked *unverified*.

**What it is.** ShaderGlass (mausimus, GPL-3.0, C++20, DirectX 11, v1.3.2 of 1 November, about 4.4k stars, a Steam page rated Very Positive with 57 reviews) is an overlay that runs GPU shaders on top of the Windows desktop, mostly the libretro/RetroArch slang library, in a floating window or fullscreen.

| | ShaderGlass | Phoxium |
|---|---|---|
| Idea | A shader player: a large library of ready-made effects (CRT, upscalers, TV and VHS looks) | One physical model of a CRT tube: each source pixel drives three beams, parameters in physical units, presets from published tube data |
| Looks | 1200+ precompiled libretro shaders; external `.slang` and `.slangp` files can be imported (a limited RetroArch back-end); shader parameters; profiles | One fixed chain with its parameters and 10 tube presets; no import of external shaders |
| Capture | The Windows capture API with DirectX 11; Desktop Glass (a transparent window shades whatever is behind it), Window Clone (one window), USB capture card or webcam, OBS through Game Capture | Windows.Graphics.Capture of a window or a whole monitor, zero-copy by default with an automatic CPU fallback, still images, video devices (Media Foundation), test patterns; nothing injected into the target, the focus never taken |
| GPU API | DirectX 11, any DirectX 11 GPU; a dedicated-GPU selection issue is open (#309) | Vulkan 1.3; NVIDIA is the target, an Intel iGPU works but is slow, AMD untested; the GPU is a setting (auto: the adapter of the monitor the output covers) |
| System | Windows 10 2004 or later and Windows 11; Linux through Wine or Proton (window clone only) | Windows 11 x64 only |
| Accuracy | What each shader does; no validation is claimed (*unverified*) | Each stage tested against an f64 oracle; quality tiers with a measured error (fast: about 0.1 % mean on game content) |
| Cost | Depends on the shader (*unverified*); the manual lists latency options (a flip presentation mode) | Measured: 9 to 14 ms for a 640 x 480 source at 2560 x 1440 on an RTX A2000 (standard tier), an automatic drop to a cheaper tier; about 24 fps on an Intel UHD 770 for the slot-mask preset |
| Source handling | Crop, scale, aspect (a Stretch option, output scales below 100 % since 1.3.2) | Integer-upscale and picture-area detection, scale modes, per-game profiles |
| Extras | Shader search, command line (`-input`, `-autoclose`, `-display N`), keep the PC awake; a separate app, ShaderBeam, for black-frame insertion and a beam simulator | Search, keep awake, global hotkeys, command line, burn-in and ageing, composite, tape and interlace modules, stats overlay |
| HDR | Users report an over-exposed output over Windows AutoHDR content (itch.io comments) | SDR only; HDR is out of scope |
| Licence and distribution | GPL-3.0; a zip and Steam | MIT; a zip on GitHub, unsigned |
| Maturity | Many releases, 175 open issues, a large user base | 0.9.x, tested on one reference PC |

**Where Phoxium is ahead:** a coherent physical model with validated stages and parameters in real units (the differentiator of `CLAUDE.md`); a permissive licence, so the code can be reused; zero-copy capture and a GPU choice that follows the monitor; measured quality and cost tiers with an automatic drop; ageing, burn-in and the optional signal modules in one chain.

**Where ShaderGlass is ahead:** far more looks and a community behind them, the import of any slang shader; the overlay modes (Desktop Glass, USB input, OBS) and Linux; a lower hardware bar (DirectX 11, Windows 10); distribution on Steam; maturity and a user base that finds and reports the problems.

**What to take from it** (candidates, none decided): the overlay use (shade whatever is behind the window, without picking a window first; Phoxium has click-through and a "Whole screen N" source, not the transparent overlay); Steam as a way to reach users and to get past the SmartScreen warning of an unsigned zip; the points their issue list raises that Phoxium should be checked against: a dedicated GPU not recognised (#309; test the `auto` choice and the setting on the hybrid laptops), Windows AutoHDR content over-exposed (not tested here; Phoxium captures SDR), the pointer stuck on the screen (#299), a frame-rate cap (#319; the output has a frame cap setting) and bilinear downscaling (#302, #330; the scale modes). **Not to take:** code or shaders (ShaderGlass is GPL-3.0 and its shaders carry their own licences; the project policy is inspiration only).

## Study: source colour targets (2026-11-25)

For the TODO item "a GBC target: a new input step" (owner decisions of the same day: its own slot; every target, Nintendo and other makers; a frame that is not 5-bit quantised is transformed too). First pass: a survey of the targets, what colour data exists for each and how good it is, which kind of transform each needs, the sources. **Method and limits:** web searches and the pages below; **read in full:** the near.sh colour emulation article (archived by ares), the GBCC tech notes, the nesdev NTSC video page, the pagetable C64 colour page. **Seen only as search snippets:** everything else. **Blocked or empty:** Sega Retro (a bot-protection page) and CPCWiki (HTTP 403) refuse automated reads, the pagetable C64 page builds its palette in the browser (no RGB value in its text), `pepto.de` and `colodore.com` were not fetched. Every number below is as a source states it; none was measured here, and nothing is a data file yet.

### What the study found

1. **Three kinds of transform, not one.** (a) **Colour correction of a small RGB code** (Game Boy Color, Game Boy Advance, and probably Game Gear, Neo Geo Pocket Color, WonderSwan Color, Lynx): the console sends 4 or 5 bits per channel to an LCD that mixes the channels; the correction is a channel mix plus transfer curves, or a 3D lookup table. (b) **Monochrome mapping** (original Game Boy, Pocket, Virtual Boy, green monitors): the luminance of the source picks one of a few shades. (c) **Fixed palettes made by a video chip** (NES, Atari 2600 and 8-bit, C64, ZX Spectrum, Amstrad CPC, Genesis, Amiga, Atari ST, MSX, Apple II, CGA): the accuracy is in the palette, so the transform is a **palette remap** from the palette the emulator uses (it must be known, and pixels must match its entries) to the hardware palette; for the chips that generate composite colour (NES, 2600, C64) the hardware palette can instead come from the signal itself, which is what the `composite` stage of Phoxium already models.
2. **No instrument-measured colorimetry was found for any LCD target.** The best data for the Game Boy Color is a lookup table photographed and matched by eye; the circulating formulas have no stated origin; for the Game Boy Advance the matrices are tuned by eye against a real console. Measured data exists for composite signal levels (the NES) and as community palettes of unstated provenance. **So every target file must say how its values were obtained** (measured, photographed, matched by eye, a formula of unknown origin, an estimate), and a target should ship only when its source can be named.
3. **The 5-bit expansion is invertible.** The source of a handheld emulator is the 5-bit code expanded to 8 bits; near.sh states the rule (the source bits repeat to fill the target bits), so the native 5-bit code can be recovered exactly and a target table can work on the 32 levels. This also settles the owner's third decision: a source that was not expanded this way is transformed on its colours directly (no recovery).
4. **Licence.** The correction formulas and tables mostly live in GPL emulators (the project policy is inspiration only): take published numbers and measurements, never code or tables copied from a repository. The near.sh formulas carry no licence statement and the Game Boy Color one has an unknown author; the GBCC table is a single author's measurement of a hobby project (its repository licence was not read).
5. **Reflective LCDs depend on ambient light** (no backlight on the Game Boy Color, the frontlit Game Boy Advance): the transform should not claim one colour for all rooms; the optics stage already has the ambient light and screen reflectance parameters.

### Survey of the targets

| Target | Kind | Data found | How good |
|---|---|---|---|
| Game Boy Color | LCD correction | (1) an unattributed popular formula: `R = (r*26 + g*4 + b*2)`, `G = (g*24 + b*8)`, `B = (r*6 + g*4 + b*22)` on 5-bit codes, clamp at 960, shift by 2, no gamma or white point stated (near.sh); (2) GBCC: an 8 x 8 x 8 lookup table sampled from a real console using Harvest Moon 3 colours, matched by eye on a PC, with subpixel colours `#FF7145`, `#C1D650`, `#3BCEFF` (gbcc.dev); (3) BGB "reality" from photographs of the LCD, the blue primary is outside sRGB (BGB documentation); (4) Gambatte "accurate" mode, attributed to Pokefan531 | photographed or eye-matched; no gamma, white point or method stated |
| Game Boy Advance (AGS-001 frontlit, AGS-101 backlit) | LCD correction | Talarubi's formula: LCD gamma 4.0 in, 2.2 out, matrix rows (0, 50, 255), (30, 230, 10), (220, 10, 50) over 255 (near.sh); the AGS-101 treated as about gamma 2.2, the AGS-001 as bluish and low in contrast (libretro forum) | tuned by eye |
| Game Gear, Neo Geo Pocket Color, WonderSwan Color, Lynx | LCD correction | none found; near.sh says there is no good filter for the WonderSwan and the Neo Geo Pocket, and that the WonderSwan Color has a software contrast flag nobody can emulate precisely; Lynx 4096 colours, Neo Geo Pocket Color 4096 | no data |
| Game Boy (DMG), Pocket, Super Game Boy | monochrome | named shades and community hex values (a DMG entry listed as `B8F878` and `80B050`, two of four legible); Super Game Boy palettes: none | community values; an Aseprite thread says nobody is known to have measured the Game Boy's signal output as was done for Sega consoles |
| Virtual Boy | monochrome (red) | none found | no data |
| NES / Famicom | composite-generated | nesdev: voltage table measured on a terminated 75 ohm TV by lidnariq (about 10 mV noise, 4 mV quantisation), e.g. sync 48 mV, colour burst low 148 mV, levels 228, 312, 552, 880, 616, 840, 1100 mV, and the emphasis variants; a YUV decode; differential phase of about -2.5 degrees (2C02E) or -5 degrees (2C02G) per palette row (sources: a forum capture for the 2C02G, none stated for the 2C02E); "no single correct palette" (decoders differ) | measured signal levels; the best data of the survey; decode constants are the page's, not calibrated |
| SNES | CRT gamma | a 32-entry gamma ramp for the 5-bit channels (starts 0x00, 0x01, 0x03, 0x06, ends 0xe8, 0xf0, 0xf8, 0xff), from Overload of Super Sleuth / Kindred (near.sh) | a table of known origin, no method |
| Commodore 64 | composite-generated | 16 colours from luma and chroma, not an RGB table; Pepto (2001) is the de facto standard, Colodore (2017) the PAL refinement used by VICE; the first VIC-II revision (6569R1) has 5 luma levels, later ones 9 (pagetable); no values read | published measurement and calculation exist (pepto.de, colodore.com), not yet read |
| Amstrad CPC | fixed palette | 27 colours, 3 levels per channel (off, half, full); the half level is 127 or 128 on a modern display (CPCWiki, snippet) | exact by construction up to the level choice |
| ZX Spectrum | fixed palette | normal level about 85 % of the bright level (0.55 V against 0.65 V in one source), differs per model; emulators use 0xD7 or 0xD8 for normal; Wikipedia gives approximate values | model-dependent |
| Atari 2600 / 8-bit | composite-generated | NTSC 16 hues x 8 luminances, PAL 13 x 8 (snippets), GTIA 256 colours; community palettes of unstated provenance; a Gopher2600 blog post, "the NTSC colour problem", is a lead | no measured table found |
| Sega SG-1000, Master System, Mega Drive/Genesis | fixed palette (DAC) | Genesis 9-bit, Master System 6-bit, Game Gear 12-bit; a community reference lists measured Genesis DAC levels 0, 52, 87, 116, 144, 172, 206, 255 against a linear alternative 0, 36, 72, 108, 144, 180, 216, 252 (a secondary, forum-level source) | nonlinear DAC; to verify against the original measurement |
| Amiga OCS, Atari ST/STE, PC Engine, MSX (TMS9918), Apple II, CGA | fixed palette or composite-generated | bit depths only (Amiga 12-bit, ST 9-bit with 3-bit DAC, STE 12-bit); nothing on the PC Engine, the TMS9918, the Apple II, or the CGA brown | to study |

### Built, first target (2026-11-25)

The Game Boy Color matrix of the survey is built as the first target (slot `target`, `presets/targets/game_boy_color.toml`, ARCHITECTURE A39). Two findings while building: (1) the 5-bit expansion read straight (`code / 255`) differs from the device code over 31 by up to **0.0027**, not 0.0009 as first estimated here (code 28 expands to 231, 0.9059 against 0.9032), which the matrix turns into up to 0.54 of an output code, so the target **recovers the 5-bit codes exactly** when all three channels of a pixel are expansions and transforms any other colour on its value (the owner's decision 3); (2) the GBC formula puts white at **240 of 255** (the clip 960 shifted by two bits), a darker white than the source, which is part of what the real panel did. Provenance of the numbers is still the unattributed near.sh formula (kind of data: a formula of unknown origin, an estimate).

The same day the near.sh article was read in full a second time for the other two of its targets (complete code, no truncation, no licence statement on the page): the **Game Boy Advance** (Talarubi: `lcdGamma = 4.0`, `outGamma = 2.2`, the sums written on (b, g, r) as `0 lb + 50 lg + 255 lr`, `30 lb + 230 lg + 10 lr`, `220 lb + 10 lg + 50 lr`, each over 255, raised to 1 / 2.2 and scaled by `0xffff x 255 / 280`, no clip: the data file writes the same numbers on (R, G, B), and white comes out at 0.988, 0.935, 0.950) and the **SNES** (the 32-entry `gammaRamp`, credited to Overload of Super Sleuth / Kindred, indexed by the 5-bit channel). Both are built as targets (`game_boy_advance`, `super_nintendo_black_boost`; the SNES as a second kind, `curve`). The study's row of the survey for the Game Boy Advance was right on the numbers and left the channel order open; it is settled by the code. Everything else of the survey (the monochrome and palette targets, the composite-generated colours, the targets with no data) is still unbuilt: no source for the numbers has been read yet, or the transform kind does not exist (palette remap, monochrome mapping).

### What this means for the design note (after the study is complete)
