- **The slot cannot be one matrix.** A profile has a **kind**: `matrix` (the input transfer, a 3 x 3 matrix, the output transfer: Game Boy Advance, probably the other LCDs), `lut3d` (a 3D table sampled with interpolation: the Game Boy Color of GBCC, and a general format that covers the matrix as a special case), `mono_palette` (luminance to N shades), `palette_remap` (a table from a named source palette to the hardware palette). Start with `matrix` and the per-channel curves, then `lut3d`, then the palettes.
- **Palette remap needs a named source palette** (the emulator's), a selection in the menu next to the target; a frame whose colours match no entry is left alone or reported.
- **Composite-generated targets could reuse the `composite` model** (the NES voltage table is the model's input) instead of a stored palette: to be decided once the NES and C64 data are read in full.
- **Provenance is part of the data.** Each profile file lists the source of every value and a quality tag; the first profile shipped should be the one whose source is best (the NES levels are measured; the Game Boy Color's best is a photographed table).

### Sources

Read in full: [near.sh, Color emulation (ares archive)](https://archive.ares-emu.net/near.sh/articles/video/color-emulation.html); [GBCC tech notes](https://gbcc.dev/technology) (moved from gbcc.github.io); [nesdev, NTSC video](https://www.nesdev.org/wiki/NTSC_video); [pagetable, C64 colours](https://pagetable.com/c64ref/colors/). Seen as search snippets: [BGB, reality](https://bgb.bircd.org/reality/index.html), [libretro Gambatte documentation](https://docs.libretro.com/library/gambatte/), [libretro forum, real GBA and DS colours](https://forums.libretro.com/t/real-gba-and-ds-phat-colors/1540/142), [GB Studio Central, colour reproduction on GBC devices](https://gbstudiocentral.com/?p=2287), [Game Bub, colour correction](https://www.crowdsupply.com/second-bedroom/game-bub/updates/color-correction-on-the-game-bub), [nesdev, PPU palettes](https://www.nesdev.org/wiki/Palette), [Wikipedia, video game console palettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_video_game_console_palettes), [CPCWiki, CPC palette](https://cpcwiki.eu/index.php/CPC_Palette), [Wikipedia, ZX Spectrum graphic modes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZX_Spectrum_graphic_modes), [Gopher2600, the NTSC colour problem](https://jetsetilly.github.io/Gopher2600-Blog/posts/the-ntsc-colour-problem), [Sega Retro, palettes and CRAM](https://www.segaretro.org/CRAM_dots), [Aseprite forum, GameBoy palette survey](https://community.aseprite.org/t/gameboy-palette-survey/8568). Named but not fetched: pepto.de (colorvic), colodore.com.

### Still to do before the design note

The primary pages: `pepto.de/projects/colorvic` and `colodore.com` for the C64 values; the NES palette and phase pages of nesdev in full and lidnariq's measurement thread; the Sega levels from a source that allows reading (or pasted by the owner); the CPC table; the Atari TIA and GTIA colour documents and the Gopher2600 post; the targets with no data at all (Game Gear, Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, WonderSwan Color, Virtual Boy, MSX, Apple II, PC Engine, CGA, Amiga and Atari ST monitors). If the owner has a Game Boy Color, a Game Boy Advance or a colorimeter, one reading would replace an eye-matched table.

## Audit of the colour targets (2026-11-25)

Seven independent audits (one per target for the Game Boy Color, the Game Boy Advance and the SNES; four surveys for the other platforms), each by a researcher who was given the numbers in use or the question and not the conclusions, reading emulator source, shaders, forums, hardware notes and measurement projects on the web. Nothing was copied from any repository (policy: published numbers and measurements with attribution, no code or tables from GPL, LGPL or AGPL repositories). Limits: Sega Retro, CPCWiki, Bulbapedia and the Gametech wiki refused automated reads (Cloudflare or a bot wall), the Grimware CPC oscilloscope pages are gone (the domain now redirects to spam), and the Hatari and WinUAE colour code was unreadable; those facts are marked unverified. Every number below is as the cited page states it; a few are the auditors' own arithmetic and are labelled so.

### The three shipped targets

| Target | Verdict | What the audit found |
|---|---|---|
| Game Boy Color (`game_boy_color`) | **Amended** (labels, notes, provenance; numbers kept) | The weights are the Gambatte "fast" correction (13, 2, 1 over 16, then (3g + b) / 4, then (3r + 2g + 11b) / 16), a lineage that copies itself (near.sh, higan, ares, tech-in-japan: the author is unknown to all). The clip at 960 exists only in near.sh, ares and higan; Gambatte has none, no physical basis was found, and it costs white 6 percent. Emulators disagree about white: SameBoy and Gambatte-fast 255, Gambatte-accurate and the libretro gbc-color shader about 248, mGBA 0.91 to 1.0 by profile, GBCC a warm (228, 219, 196), ares and near.sh about 240. No instrument measurement backs the formula, and it applies no gamma. The Pokefan531 matrices (libretro gbc-color, mGBA, Gambatte accurate) are eyeballed from photographs, one lineage: R = 0.82 R + 0.24 G - 0.06 B, G = 0.125 R + 0.665 G + 0.21 B, B = 0.195 R + 0.075 G + 0.73 B (luminance 0.94), with a gamma step. Instrument readings exist (Pokefan531's ColorMunki Photo files in the Handheld-Color-Space-Project: contrast about 3.2 to 3.8 to 1, white near x 0.335, y 0.362, strongly dependent on the viewing angle) but carry no licence and are partly marked invalid by their author. The GBCC 8 x 8 x 8 table (MIT) is the best-licensed photographed data. |
| Game Boy Advance (`game_boy_advance`) | **Amended** (notes, provenance; numbers kept at the article's 255) | The formula, the gamma 4.0 and 2.2 and the (b, g, r) orientation were verified against the article; "courtesy of Talarubi". Current ares has used **240** for the first weight of the red row since 6 April 2022 (commit a6d3a57, "slightly reduce red tint, to bring the display closer to my GBA SP": eye-tuned on one console), the other seven weights unchanged. The independent shader family (libretro and mGBA, Pokefan531 and hunterk) keeps a **neutral white**: rows that sum to 1 and one factor of 0.91 to 0.94, with gammas 2.5 / 2.0 (mGBA), 3.2 / 2.2 (old libretro), 2.2 / 2.2 (sp101). Only near.sh and ares leave a tinted white (0.988, 0.935, 0.950 here; red 0.965 with 240). Measured data: a backlit AGS-101 read with a ColorMunki Display (user-contributed, uncalibrated, protector on): white XYZ (121.8, 123.4, 149.2), black (0.43, 0.39, 0.65), so the primaries are about R (0.619, 0.348), G (0.330, 0.606), B (0.144, 0.084) (the auditor's arithmetic), contrast about 315 to 1, an effective gamma of **2.3 to 2.75**, close to sRGB; the 4.0 is supported by no instrument data and is plausible only for the reflective AGS-001 at one angle (a reflective unit's gamma swings from 1.3 to 3.5 with the angle). No licence on that data. |
| Super Nintendo (`super_nintendo_black_boost`) | **Renamed and relabelled; not the console's output** | The 32 entries are exact: i (i + 1) / 2 for codes 0 to 15, min(255, 8 (i + 1)) from 16 (the auditor's derivation, all 32 verified; it is the deepBlackBoost option of ares and of bsnes). It came from the Super Sleuth emulator (Overload), is not measured and not a power law (effective exponent 1.6 at code 4, 1.04 at code 15), and near.sh says it only partly offsets an LCD's higher black level. The console's DAC is close to linear in the code (an oscilloscope run on one 3-chip SNES revision 1 PPU2, nesdev forum, lidnariq; the full-scale span against INIDISP 0 to 15 is 4 to 748 mV with a 0.99983 quadratic fit). The bsnes author called the related display gamma an "unprincipled kludge". Stacked in front of a CRT gun law of power 2.4 it gives an effective exponent of about 3.5 at code 8. **For a real SNES on a tube the right target is none**; the table stays only as an emulator look, with the warning in its notes. |

### The other platforms: what could be built, and with which transform

| Platform | Data found | Verdict | Transform kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sega Mega Drive / Genesis | **Measured**: TmEE (SpritesMind) read the divider outputs of MD1, several MD2 and the Genny3 ASIC on a scope, 15 levels in mV (0, 108, 192, 261, 324, 378, 432, 483, 537, 588, 642, 699, 768, 849, 951); the normal levels, even entries scaled to 255: **0, 52, 87, 116, 144, 172, 206, 255** (shadow 0, 29, 52, 70, 87, 101, 116, 130; highlight 130, 144, 158, 172, 187, 206, 228, 255). BlastEm uses a table derived from the same voltages (49, 87, 119, 146, 174, 206: up to 3 apart). Emulator inputs differ: Genesis Plus GX outputs a linear step of 34 (0 to 238), Kega Fusion step 33, Regen 32. | Yes, with numbers; the white scale is open (4.7 V is about 0.9 V at the encoder, so 238 or 255) | 8-entry per-channel curve, after identifying the emulator's step |
| Sega Master System, SG-1000 | MD and SMS levels by TmEE: SMS1 0, 90, 173, 255; SMS2 0, 89, 174, 255; the first VDP (315-5124) has a blue offset of about 22 to 23 percent (bfbiii, a capture card plus scope; furrtek's die taps), derived blue 0, 104, 170, 255; the rule of most emulators is linear 85 per step. TMS9929A Y and colour-difference levels in mV exist for the SC-3000. | Needs more (red and green levels disagree between a scope reading and the "R and G are linear" statements) | 4-entry curves, a separate blue curve for one chip |
| NES / Famicom | **Measured** signal levels (nesdev, lidnariq, 75 ohm terminated, about 10 mV noise): sync 48, burst 148 to 524, blank 312, low levels 228, 312, 552, 880, high 616, 840, 1100, 1100 mV, emphasis about 0.816, 12 phases of 30 degrees, phase distortion about -2.5 (2C02E) or -5 degrees (2C02G) per luma row. Decode constants are choices (SMPTE 170M or others), and the nesdev page itself says there is no single correct palette. Emulator palettes differ (Mesen2 a fixed 64-entry table, FCEUX another, Nestopia generated). | Yes as a signal model; the emulator palette must be identified first | Composite-generated: the project's `composite` model with these levels as input |
| Commodore 64 | Mäkelä's 40 MHz scope table of the luma pin per VIC-II revision (6569R3: black 700, white 1850, red 1090, cyan 1480, green 1340, blue 1020, yellow 1620 mV; 6569R4: 500, 1875, 840, 1300, 1100, 760, 1500); Pepto's chroma angles of 22.5-degree multiples (blue 0, purple 45, red 112.5, orange 135, brown 157.5, yellow 180, green 225, cyan 292.5) from a vectoscope and a test program, peak saturation, gamma 2.8 to 2.2 or 2.5 / 2.2; Colodore refines it with unpublished remeasurements. The angles are disputed on the Lemon64 forum (unverified). VICE defaults to the Pepto PAL palette, and its Colodore red differs from Lospec's. | Yes, with numbers; chroma amplitude per colour and 8565 data are missing | Composite decode (luma, angle, amplitude, PAL delay line) |
| Atari 2600 (NTSC, PAL, SECAM) | Structure only: 15 hue phases, 8 luma steps, hue 1 in phase with the burst; no voltage measured. The phase step is disputed (23 to 28 degrees; MAME and Stella 26.2 or 26.7). SECAM is an exact 8-colour table (black, blue, red, magenta, green, cyan, yellow, white). Stella's PAL is a generated model. | NTSC partly, PAL needs more, SECAM yes | Composite with user parameters; SECAM a fixed table |
| Atari 8-bit, 5200 | Altirra's reference manual: delay about 24 degrees (trimpot), 16 luma steps, a PAL phase table; "no single authoritative answer". Atari800 holds a 16-entry luma table cited to Atari documentation, read only in GPL code. | Partly | Composite with parameters |
| Atari 7800 | A derived formula (tint 180, phase 25.714, saturation 0.2162), "best guess". | Needs more | Composite |
| Amstrad CPC | 27 colours from three levels per channel; 127 or 128 for the half level, about 40 percent measured on one scope according to a search snippet; the CPCWiki pages could not be read, the Grimware scope data is gone. Analog RGB, so not the composite model. | Needs more | 27-entry remap with a level parameter |
| ZX Spectrum | Nominal: normal at 85 percent of bright (0.55 V against 0.65 V), computed at maximum saturation; "real colours currently unknown"; emulators use 0xC0, 0xCD, 0xD7 or 0xD8, 0xEE for normal and disagree. | Needs more | 15-entry remap, or PAL decode from the ULA's Y and colour differences |
| MSX (TMS9918A) | Datasheet Y, R-Y, B-Y percentages, confirmed against a scope reading on the SC-3000 (white 964 mV, dark blue Y 392, B-Y 516); openMSX derives its palette from the data book. V9938 palette values from the data book (3-bit GRB) but no DAC levels. | TMS yes, V9938 needs more | Decode from Y and colour differences |
| Apple II | Derived only: Y, I, Q per lo-res colour (Apple's manual), 14.318 MHz clock; no instrument measurement. The emulator frame already holds the artefacts, so it must be decoded from the pixel bit pattern, not remapped. | A model, not a palette | Composite decode of the bit stream |
| IBM CGA / EGA | RGBI palette **measured** on an IBM 5153 by Hugo Holden (via VileR, int10h.org): 00 000000, 06 C47E00 (brown), 07 C4C4C4, 08 4E4E4E, 09 4E4EDC, 11 4EF3F3, 15 FFFFFF; the brown circuit cuts green by about a third; the canonical formula is 0x55, 0xAA, 0xFF with AA5500 for brown. DOSBox defaults to the canonical palette. Composite colours have no table. | RGBI yes, composite needs more | 16-entry remap; composite decode |
| NEC PC Engine / TurboGrafx-16 | A silicon dump of the RGB-to-YUV table exists (furrtek) but no values could be read; Mednafen's palette does not match composite. | Needs more | 512-entry table |
| SNK Neo Geo | A computed DAC model from the resistors (3.9k, 2.2k, 1k, 470, 220, dark 8.2k, shadow 150 ohms); the weights (3, 7, 13, 26, 51 percent) are not measured and the dark-bit weight is disputed. | Needs more | 32-level curve plus a dark term |
| Amiga OCS, ECS, AGA; Atari ST, STE | Nothing measured. AGA is a plain 8-bit DAC; the STE register has a bit-order quirk (the extra bit is the least significant: level index ((n and 7) << 1) or (n >> 3)). | No data | A reorder only; no curve known |
| Game Boy (DMG), Pocket | Only photograph-based: BGB's "reality" schemes (calibrated photographs, values unpublished); SameBoy (Expat) DMG `081810 396139 84A563 C6DE8C`, Pocket `07100E 3A4C3A 818D66 C2CE93`; ares DMG `AED927 58A028 206229 1A452A`, Pocket `E0DBCD A89F94 706B66 2B2B26` (neutral grey: the three sources disagree on the Pocket's hue); Gambatte `578200 317400 005121 00420C`. The 0F380F, 306230, 8BAC0F, 9BBC0F set has no known origin. The hardware has no fixed RGB, only 0, 33, 66 and 100 percent "on", and the shades depend on light, contrast dial and unit. | Needs more | 4-shade monochrome palette |
| Super Game Boy | **Hardware data**: the 32 built-in palettes, RGB555 words claimed to be lifted verbatim from the SGB ROM (rustyboi, MIT; not checked against a ROM). 1-A (the power-on palette) is 67BF 265B 10B5 2866, that is FFEECD, DE944A, AC2920, 311852; 1-B 637B 3AD9 0956 0000; 1-C 7F1F 2A7D 30F3 4CE7; 1-D 57FF 2618 001F 006A. | Yes, with numbers (verify against a ROM); the SNES output chain's effect on them is unknown | 4-colour palette per selection |
| Virtual Boy | Four levels (black and three red brightness registers; on-time 25, 50, 100 percent of 6.4 microseconds, furrtek); Mednafen's emulator choice is a gamma 1 / 2.2 times a colour, ledonscale 1.75. The LED's hue is unmeasured. | Needs more | 4-level red curve |
| Atari Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, Game Gear | Emulators expand 4 bits linearly (Lynx 15 x nibble + 30 in Mednafen); no measured data and no published correction. | No data | Matrix and gamma if ever measured |
| WonderSwan, WonderSwan Color | ares has a formula for each, "similar to the GBP" and "similar to the GBC": the mono endpoints equal the Pocket's, the colour matrix is the GBC's with a 480 clip on 4-bit inputs, so both are borrowed, not measured. near.sh says no good filter exists. | Needs more | Curve; matrix |

### What the audit changes

1. **Shipped targets**: the notes, provenance and labels of the three are amended as above; the SNES one is renamed `super_nintendo_black_boost` and warns against stacking it on a gun law. No number changed. Each file now says what is an estimate and what the independent sources disagree on.
2. **Order of what to build next** (step 2, the Mega Drive DAC curve, built the same day: the measured levels as the published table has them (the first step is 51.5 from the mV, written 52), the emulator inputs by step 34, 33 and 32, not read in the emulators' code; step 1, the Super Game Boy palettes, built the same day: the 32 palettes cross-checked word for word against Bulbapedia and Gambatte, zero disagreement; ROM provenance unverified), by quality of the data and the kind of transform it needs (nothing before its data is on this page): (1) the **Super Game Boy palettes** (hardware data, a 4-colour mono palette kind; verify against a ROM); (2) the **Mega Drive** DAC curve (measured, but the emulator's input step must be selectable: 8-entry curve kind); (3) **C64** and **NES** (the best measured signal data; both need the `composite` model driven by per-colour levels, phases and amplitudes, a design note first, since the emulator's palette must be recognised to give a colour index); (4) **CGA RGBI** (measured palette; a 16-entry remap kind); (5) the **Game Boy Color** colorimetric alternatives (the Pokefan531 matrices need the gamma handling read from the shader files; the GBCC 3D table is MIT but needs a LUT kind) and a measured **AGS-101** profile (ask the data's author for permission, the repository has no licence; it would be a colorimetric kind: gamma, primaries, white).
3. **Licence findings** (not legal advice): a 16, 27 or 32-entry table that follows from a formula or from voltages is a plain fact; the sources to avoid copying from are the GPL ones (VICE, openMSX, Caprice32, AppleWin, Gambatte, Mesen2, the Beetle cores, VBA-M, FCEUX, Stella), bsnes (GPLv3) and the repositories without a licence (Handheld-Color-Space-Project, Kitrinx TG16_Palette); the permissive ones (ares ISC, SameBoy Expat, GBCC MIT, mGBA MPL-2.0 at file level, MAME BSD-3, pally MIT-0, rustyboi MIT) allow reuse with notice. Codebase64 is CC BY-NC-SA and conflicts with attribution-only use. Forum posts (SpritesMind, nesdev, SMS Power, libretro) state no licence: cite the author, quote numbers only.
4. **Still unknown** (no source found): the earliest appearance and author of the Game Boy Color formula; the origin of SameBoy's per-channel curve; any measurement of the GBA frontlight case or of a unit-to-unit spread; the C64 chroma amplitude per colour and the 8565 chip; the Atari 2600 voltages; the CPC levels in volts; the real Spectrum voltages; the Virtual Boy LED hue; any measured data at all for the Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color and Game Gear.

## Design note: signal-driven colour targets, the C64 and the NES (2026-11-25)

For step 3 of the order of the audit ("Audit of the colour targets"). No code yet; this note settles the shape, lists the numbers each target needs and what is open, and ends with the decisions that are the owner's.

### The problem

Four kinds of target exist (`matrix`, `curve`, `palette`, `dac`; ARCHITECTURE A39). All four turn a colour into a colour with a table the sources give. The Commodore 64 and the NES are different: their hardware does not make RGB colours, it makes a **composite signal** (a luma level and a chroma wave of a given phase and amplitude), and the colour on the screen is what a television's decoder made of it. The accurate data is signal data (Mäkelä's oscilloscope table of the VIC-II luma pin, Pepto's chroma angles; nesdev's measured NES voltage levels and 12-phase waveform), and no RGB palette is authoritative (the nesdev page says so for the NES, and the two best C64 palettes, Pepto and Colodore, differ). An emulator shows one of those palettes as plain RGB. The target has to go back from the emulator's colour to the signal that the hardware made, then forward to the colour a decoder gives.

### What exists that the target can use

The `composite` model (`crt_model::composite`, slot `signal`, parameters `composite_*`) already encodes drive voltages R'G'B' to Y, U, V (BT.601 luma, BT.470 colour differences), modulates the chroma on a subcarrier with the line and frame phase, optionally goes through a channel, separates luma and chroma (notch, 1-line comb, PAL delay line), demodulates with chosen bandwidths (I 1.3 MHz, Q 0.5 MHz, PAL U and V 1.3 MHz), and returns R'G'B'. It has the PAL V switch, the NTSC 33-degree axis, a hue and saturation control, the decoder's imperfections, and is off by default. Its **input is RGB'**: it cannot be given a luma level and a chroma phase directly. A flat colour field goes through it unchanged (tested), so it adds the artefacts (bleeding, dot crawl, cross-colour, the PAL delay line's saturation loss) and no colour error of its own.

### The proposal: a hardware palette computed from the signal, then the existing composite model

Three parts, built in this order, each useful alone:

1. **A new target kind, `signal`: a hardware palette computed from signal levels.** The data file holds, per colour index, the **luma level**, the **chroma phase** and the **chroma amplitude** the hardware makes (with the numbers' sources), the reference levels (blank, black, white), and the **decoder constants** the television is assumed to use. The kind turns each entry into the drive voltages R'G'B' a decoder produces (Y = (level - black) / (white - black); U and V from the amplitude and phase; R' = Y + V / 0.877283, B' = Y + U / 0.492111, G' from the luma equation: the same matrix the composite model decodes with, so the two agree). This is the colour a monitor's decoder drives the tube with, and the tube's own gun law, primaries and white then take over: **no PC gamma or sRGB conversion** (Pepto's model converts through a source gamma 2.8 to a PC gamma 2.2 because it targets a PC display; Phoxium's tube is the display). The result is a **computed palette**; it is exact for a flat colour.
2. **Recognising the emulator's colours.** The target has to know which colour index each captured pixel is. Two options: (a) **named emulator palettes** (VICE Pepto PAL, VICE Colodore, Mesen2, FCEUX, Nestopia ...) stored in the data file and matched exactly, which needs the numbers of those palettes, and the big ones are in GPL repositories (not to be copied, the project policy) or are generated by a program; (b) **the nearest hardware colour**: classify each pixel to the nearest entry of the target's own computed palette (distance in R'G'B' or in Y, U, V) within a threshold, and pass a pixel with no close entry through unchanged. (b) needs no emulator table, works for any emulator whose palette is near the physical one (the 16 C64 colours and the 54 distinct NES colours are well separated: neighbouring hues are 22.5 or 30 degrees apart, the grey and luma steps are large), and fails soft. **Recommendation: (b) first**, a named palette later only where (b) mis-classifies (a test against the published palettes of the audit, Pepto's and Colodore's 16 hex values and the pally generator's NES output, measures how often).
3. **Composite artefacts as an option.** With `composite_enable` on, the existing `signal` slot runs after the target: it encodes the computed palette's R'G'B' (which round-trips through BT.601 exactly, so the luma and chroma are the hardware's) and shows the bleeding, the cross-colour of the 8-bit era, dot crawl and, for the C64, the PAL delay line. The C64's even and odd line hues are mirror images about the U axis (Pepto's palette is their average), which is the PAL V switch the model already has, so the C64 needs nothing else. The pixel clock, the subcarrier and the standard are the existing parameters (a C64 PAL: 0.985 MHz dot clock x 8 = 7.88 MHz, subcarrier 4.43361875 MHz).

### The numbers each target needs (from the audit; none measured here)

- **C64** (PAL 6569, NTSC 6567): the **luma** per colour from Mäkelä's 1996 scope table, per VIC-II revision (6569R3 black 700 mV, white 1850, red 1090, cyan 1480, green 1340, blue 1020, yellow 1620; 6569R4 black 500, white 1875, red 840, cyan 1300, green 1100, blue 760, yellow 1500; the file has all 16 per revision, to be read in full); the later chips' nine luma levels in the units of 1/32 of white (black 0; blue and brown 8; red and dark grey 10; purple and orange 12; grey and light blue 15; green and light red 16; cyan and light grey 20; yellow and light green 24; white 32); the **chroma angles** of Pepto (blue and light blue 0, purple 45, red and light red 112.5, orange 135, brown 157.5, yellow 180, green and light green 225, cyan 292.5 degrees, contested on the Lemon64 forum), the **amplitude** the same for every colour (Pepto's peak saturation: unknown to better than a model, the 8565 is not measured), a **revision** choice (R1 has four luma levels, R3 and later nine).
- **NES** (2C02E, 2C02G; NTSC-U with a 7.5 IRE setup, NTSC-J without): the **voltage levels** of nesdev (lidnariq, a 75 ohm terminated TV, about 10 mV noise): sync 48, burst 148 to 524, blank 312, the four low levels 228, 312, 552, 880 and high levels 616, 840, 1100, 1100 mV, the emphasis-attenuated levels (192, 256, 448, 712 and 500, 676, 896, 896; about 0.816), the **12-phase square wave** of a colour (hue 0 a constant high, hue 13 a constant low, hues 14 and 15 black; the colour phase in 30-degree steps), the phase distortion per luma row (about -2.5 degrees on the 2C02E, -5 on the 2C02G), and the decoder constants (SMPTE 170M: R = Y + 1.139883 V, G = Y - 0.394642 U - 0.580622 V, B = Y + 2.032062 U, **a choice, not a fact**). The black and white references are parameters: black at the blank level (312 mV, Japan) or 7.5 IRE above it (about 361 mV, USA), white at the top level (1100 mV, "119 IRE"), so the television's contrast decides the rest.

### Data format (sketch)

`kind = "signal"`: `[signal]` holds the reference levels, the decoder constants (matrix, axis angle, standard) and the `colours` (index, name, luma level, phase, amplitude), a `[[signal.revisions]]` list for the chip revisions (luma sets, phase distortion), a `colour_count`, the recognition `threshold`; the provenance carries a tag per key as for the other kinds. The oracle is `crt_model::target::Signal` (computes the palette, classifies a colour), checked against the published hex palettes (Pepto's and Colodore's 16 values for the C64, pally's MIT-0 generator for the NES: plain numbers, tolerance given), and by hand-derived entries (a grey, the colour burst phase, the black reference).

### The variant selection has to grow (built 2026-11-25, first step: `source_colour_variant` and the named combo)

The C64 needs a chip revision, the NES a chip and a region; the Mega Drive an emulator, the Super Game Boy a palette (a slider of 1 to 32). Two parameters, `source_colour_palette` (a number) and `source_colour_input` (an enum with the names of the emulators), do not scale: each new target would add values to a shared enum. **Proposal: one parameter, `source_colour_variant` (a number, 1 based), and a combo in the menu that shows the names from the target's data file** (the variants are already named in the files: `1-A`, `Genesis Plus GX`, `6569R3`), the profile field `colour_variant` replacing the two, the per-target default named in the file. A migration is cheap (nothing is released: v0.9.4 has none of these parameters). This is a separate small step, before the C64.

### Limits to state (not to hide)

- **The NES chroma is a square wave of 12 phases, and the composite model's is a sinusoid.** Part 1 (the hardware palette) takes the **fundamental** of the square wave (the amplitude and phase a decoder's filters pass), which is what a television's chroma filter keeps; the harmonics and the exact waveform of the artefacts (the NES's 8-pixel dot crawl, the narrow colour fringes of the 4-sample pixel) are **not** modelled in part 3, whose phase advances continuously over a pixel. A dedicated 12-sample encoder (a new `signal` encode path, as Bisqwit's and Blargg's NES filters do) is a **later, separate** step if the artefacts matter.
- **The classification fails on** an emphasis pixel (the emulators fake it as a fixed factor, the hardware attenuates the levels: classify to the base colour, accept the small error), a picture scaled with a smoothing filter (no entry is close: the pixel passes through; the integer upscale and the nearest-neighbour prefilter are the default), a hand-edited emulator palette.
- **No colour is better than its data**: the C64 chroma amplitude per colour and per chip, and the 8565, are unknown; the NES decoder is a choice; both stay **estimates** in the files, with the provenance tags.
- **Phoxium shows the television's decode, not the monitor's.** A C64 on its 1084 monitor and on a television differ; the decoder constants and the saturation are the existing controls.

### Tests

The oracle against the published palettes (tolerance per entry, the worst entry reported), hand-derived entries, the classification (every hardware colour is its own nearest, the distance between the two closest entries is larger than the threshold, a published emulator palette classifies with its misses counted), the GPU test against the oracle (as `tests/stage_target.rs`), the round trip through the existing composite model (a flat colour field unchanged), and the `signal` kind's file validation.

### Steps and decisions

1. **The variant selection** (one parameter and a named combo, the migration): small, first (built 2026-11-25).
2. **3a, the C64** (built 2026-11-25; the recognition reference ended up independent of the chip revision, see below) as a `signal` kind and the nearest-colour classification (the simpler of the two: sinusoidal chroma, a PAL line mirror the model already has, 16 colours). Needs Mäkelä's full table read.
3. **3b, the NES** (built 2026-11-25, see below) with the fundamental of the 12-phase wave, the revisions and the regions; the same kind. Needs the nesdev wave tables read in full.
4. **3c, composite artefacts** checked with both (done 2026-11-25, `tests/target_composite.rs`: a flat field of every hardware colour comes back within 0.0002 through the composite model on the C64 PAL settings with the delay line and on the NES NTSC settings; the edge bleeds; the dot crawl repeats every third line with the NES line rate of 15.746 kHz and every second with the standard one; no code change was needed). The NES square-wave encoder is **not planned** (owner, 2026-11-25).

**Decisions for the owner:** (1) the classification: the nearest hardware colour (recommended), or named emulator palettes (needs their numbers, or the user's own files); (2) the variant parameter and combo replacing the two parameters now (recommended, before more targets); (3) the NES reference levels (black at the blank level, white at 1100 mV, recommended) and the decoder (SMPTE 170M, recommended); (4) whether the NES square-wave encoder is wanted at all or the sinusoidal fundamental is enough; (5) the C64 default revision (6569R3, the common late PAL chip, recommended).

**Decided 2026-11-25 (owner):** (1) the classification is the **nearest hardware colour**; (2) the variant parameter and the named combo replace the two parameters, first; (3) the NES reference levels and the SMPTE 170M decoder as proposed; (5) the C64 default revision is the 6569R3; (4) the NES square-wave encoder is **skipped** (the advice that follows is kept for the record).

**Built, step 3a (the C64), 2026-11-25.** The `signal` kind and the C64 file (`presets/targets/commodore_64.toml`, four PAL revisions, Makela's luma, Colodore's angles, one amplitude). **A finding that changed the design:** the first version compared each pixel with the hardware colours of the chosen revision raised to a gamma; against the published palettes it failed (the revision 3 colours recognised Pepto's palette, but revisions 4, 5 and 1, whose measured luma differs from the quantised levels both palettes use, mis-classified up to 9 of the 16 colours of Pepto's palette), because the emulator palettes assume nominal levels and a television setting, not a chip. The recognition therefore uses a **reference palette that is independent of the revision** (the late luma levels over 32, Colodore's angles and amplitude, a contrast of 1.1 between Pepto's 1.0 and Colodore's 1.2, a gamma of 1.136), found by an exhaustive search over contrast 1.0 to 1.2, amplitude 0.133 to 0.1875 and gamma 1.136 or 1.273: with contrast 1.1 every colour of Pepto's palette, of the Colodore defaults and of VICE's Colodore file is recognised (the farthest distance to its own reference is 0.146, 0.122 and 0.207), the two closest references are 0.125 apart, and the threshold 0.25 only decides what is left alone. The output colours stay the revision's own. The classification therefore asks no emulator table (those of the big emulators are in GPL repositories); the cost is a threshold wider than the separation of the closest colours, so a picture that is not C64 content is posterised to the 16 colours when the target is on. The hardware colours reach the shader in the frame uniform buffer (a 2 KiB table), not in push constants (too small for 64 colours).

**Built, step 3b (the NES), 2026-11-25.** The `signal` kind grew per-colour levels and swings and per-revision black, white and phase turn, and the NES file (`presets/targets/nintendo_entertainment_system.toml`) has 64 colours and four variants (2C02G and 2C02E, black at the blank level or with the 7.5 IRE setup). Findings: (1) **the model against pally's 2C02G palettes** (MIT-0, made with its IIR phase distortion filter, which also lowers the chroma): a mean distance of 0.07 in drive voltage, so the output keeps the nesdev numbers (a chroma of 1.0) and the difference is stated; (2) **recognition**: the palettes of the emulators that decode the signal (pally J and U, the Mesen2 default, which equals Nestopia's canonical palette to one level) are recognised with a reference model at a chroma of 0.8 and a black of 335 mV (between the Japanese blank level 312 and the American setup 365.6, since the American palettes are darker), found by a search over the black level, the phase, the chroma and the contrast; at most 3 of the 64 colours of a palette are confused with a neighbour (dark greens and blues of row 0); (3) **the FCEUX default palette cannot be recognised**: it is a hand-tuned palette and no setting of the decode model (the hue turn from -40 to 30 degrees, the chroma 0.8 to 1.4, the contrast 1.0 to 1.3, the black level, the phase a row) gets under 15 confused colours of 64; the only fix would be a named palette (its table is in a GPL repository), so the file says it is not supported; (4) the greens of the decode: the colour is decoded from the unclamped red and blue, so the luma is kept when a channel clamps (the hand-derived test of `$18` says so). Emphasis is not modelled (a pixel is recognised as its base colour); the square-wave encoder is not planned.

**Built, step 4 (the IBM CGA), 2026-11-25.** The palette remap needed no new shader: the `remap` kind builds the same `signal` transform from two tables, the reference colours emulators show (the canonical palette and PCem's dark yellow) and the monitor's colours (Holden's measured voltages over 1.30 V). Findings: the voltages computed from Holden's table give VileR's hex values exactly (0x4E, 0xC4, 0xDC, 0xF3, 0x7E); the DOSBox Staging `ibm5153` preset agrees for colours 8 to 15 and gives much darker normal colours (0xA6 against 0xC4), for a reason no source states; the monitor's gamma is unpublished, so the drive voltage is the voltage fraction. Holden's PDF could not be read (the numbers are through VileR's tabulation).

**Built, the colorimetric Game Boy targets, 2026-11-25.** (1) **Game Boy Color, Pokefan531**: the libretro gbc-color matrix (public domain header) read with its gamma handling (the input is raised to 2.2 minus a lighten value, default 1.0 so 1.2; Gambatte's frontlight position gives 1.7 at the centre and 2.2 below; lum 0.94 before the matrix; 1 / 2.2 after; no clamp in the GLSL and mGBA, a clamp at 0 in Gambatte), equal to Gambatte's accurate mode; mGBA's sRGB, DCI and Rec2020 profiles are different matrices of the same lineage (the sRGB one is shipped). The matrix kind got variants. (2) **AGS-101, measured**: the readings of two sessions of one unit (2023 ColorMunki Photo with HCFR, 2026 ColorMunki Display uncalibrated, both through a protector), the XYZ of the primaries and white, and 32 grey steps each; the matrix is recomputed in a test from the XYZ (the normalised primary matrix and the Bradford adaptation), the exponent from the ramp with a logarithmic least-squares fit over the steps 4 to 28 (2.30 and 2.40; a fit on the linear values gives 2.45 and 2.58 and a fit over all steps 2.2 and 2.3, the method matters and is stated); the Brankale project's own shader (a per-channel exponent curve, 32 entries, or a polynomial) is not used and not copied. Brankale's repository has no licence file and no reuse statement (its README credits Pokefan531 for the measurements and welcomes corrections); Pokefan531's libretro shaders and LUTs are public domain by his own statement. The data is used as numbers with attribution and **permission has not been asked**: ask Brankale, or drop the target.

**Built, the Atari 8-bit and 5200 (GTIA, NTSC), 2026-11-26.** `atari_8bit_gtia`, the `signal` kind with a **grid** form (16 hues x 16 luma = 256 colours, index = the colour register, hue x 16 + luma; one luma list of 16 levels for every hue; a per-revision chroma `amplitude`; the phase of hue h is `angle + phase_row_deg x (h - 1)`, with the angle 180 and the row turn -delay). The numbers were read from the Altirra Hardware Reference Manual itself (Appendix D, fetched as a PDF and read as text): Table 116 gives 16 luma levels in volts on the 800XL, the 130XE, the 800 and (by a probe on R203, no composite output) the 400, +/- 8 mV; Table 117 gives the saturation 23.9, 23.3, 35.0 and 30.6 percent (checked here: the sync-to-brightness-0 distance of 0.532, 0.424, 0.560 and 0.132 V is 40 IRE, so the luma range of 1.112, 0.912, 0.800 and 0.216 V is 83.6, 86.0, 57.1 and 65.4 IRE, and 20 IRE of chroma over it gives those four ratios); the hue formula is the manual's (U = sat cos(180 - delay (hue - 1)), V = sat sin; hue 1 and the burst at 180 degrees, -57 degrees behind I). The ladder (36k, 18k, 9.1k, 4.7k ohm, the conductance of the set bits over the total) is recomputed in a test and follows the measured 800XL levels within 0.012 of the range (brightness 8 at 0.5241 against 0.518 measured). Design choices: (1) brightness 0 and 15 of each machine are the tube's black and white (the **pedestal is not modelled**: the manual says the GTIA's brightness 0 is at the blanking level, about -8 percent below the NTSC black, and that no machine puts brightness 15 at 100 IRE, 57 to 86 IRE after the television's gain control; a variant with the pedestal and the AGC white is possible with the same file format, `black_mv` and `white_mv` per revision); (2) the machines take a hue delay of **25.5 degrees**, the middle of the manual's 23.5 to 27.5 (no machine's trimmer is known), and two variants of the 800XL take the ends; (3) the decoder is the composite model's, which equals the matrix the manual gives (1.140, -0.395, -0.581, 2.032); (4) the recognition reference is a linear luma, a chroma of **0.27** (between the XL and XE at 0.233 and 0.239 and the 400 at 0.306; the 800 at 0.35 is the outlier), a delay of 25.5 and no gamma, chosen with a short search over the computed palettes of the machines (0.25, 0.27, 0.28 and 0.29 tried: the 800XL and 130XE are found best near 0.25, the 800 near 0.29), threshold **0.12** (the farthest machine colour from the reference colour found is 0.119). **What stays unmeasured and unchecked:** no emulator palette was available to test the recognition (Altirra's palette comes from GPL code, Atari800's too; no licensed list of 256 colours was found), so the claim is only that computed palettes of the four machines and of both ends of the delay range land within 0.1 of the intended colour for 248 to 256 of the 256 colours (the 800: 169), with the exact index right for 104 to 228; hue 1 and hue 15 are 3 degrees apart at the middle delay (the two closest reference colours, `$C1` and `$E1`, are 0.005 apart), so a confusion between them changes the output by almost nothing. The chroma-to-burst ratio (the television's gain control making the chroma equal to the 40 IRE burst) and the decode matrix are assumptions; the 5200 has the same chip and no measurement of its board; PAL is not built (the hue chain spacing differs, 18 to 19 degrees real against 22.5 ideal, and Table 14 has no voltages). **Shared code extended:** `SIGNAL_MAX` 64 to 256 (`crt_model::target`, with the `Signal` struct, now 12 KiB), the frame uniform buffer table 128 to 512 `vec4` (the references now start at entry 256, `TABLE_REFERENCES` in `target.comp`; the frame buffer is 8288 bytes, within the 16384 that Vulkan guarantees), the `grid` and `amplitude` keys of the file parser; no push-constant change; every older target gave the same result in the GPU test and the composite test. The Atari 2600 and 7800 (third pass: luma network, `COLU and 15`, hue = 180 - (hue - 1) x step) fit the same grid with 8 or 16 lumas and 16 hues.

**Built, the TMS9928A / TMS9929A (SG-1000, SC-3000, MSX1, ColecoVision, TI-99/4A), 2026-11-26.** `texas_instruments_tms9928`, the `signal` kind with colour differences: a colour may give `r_y` and `b_y` (R' - Y and B' - Y as the chip's outputs carry them, around a `neutral` level) in place of a phase and an amplitude, per colour or as per-revision lists. **Data:** the Texas Instruments data manual (November 1982) Table 2-3, 15 colours of Y, R-Y and B-Y in percent of white with the differences around 47 (fetched as the Wikipedia article's reproduction of the table; the manual PDF has no text layer and no renderer is installed here, the two researchers of the second pass had read it from the scan), and TmEE's oscilloscope readings of a TMS9929ANL in an SC-3000 (SMS Power forum, 30 January 2017, mV, white 964, 0 V the black level of the chroma outputs, the load not stated), which agree with the manual within 0.019 of white in every decoded channel (the magenta is the worst). **The decode matrix, settled as far as the data allow:** the scope readings cannot choose a matrix (they agree with the manual under every linear decode); what chooses is the gamut. (1) The differences at unit scale, R' = Y + (R-Y), B' = Y + (B-Y), G' from the BT.601 luma equation: every channel of the 14 colours is between 0.13 and 1.0 except the red of light red (1.13 on the manual, 1.14 on the scope; clamped, G' from the unclamped red keeps the luma) and the blue of light blue on the scope (1.01); dark yellow is (0.83, 0.76, 0.33). Kept. (2) The same numbers as BT.601 Pb and Pr (1.402 and 1.772 times): five channels between 1.14 and 1.35 (dark blue's blue 1.34), impossible colours: the 'wrong scale', ruled out; the NTSC YIQ matrix with the differences read as I and Q is worse. (3) honestbob's answer in the forum thread (B-Y x 0.564, R-Y x 0.713, then BT.601) is (1) to 0.1 percent (1.402 x 0.713 = 0.9996, 1.772 x 0.564 = 0.9994), and the G-Y coefficients 0.509 and 0.194 that TmEE tried are (1)'s own: **the wrong yellows of the first research pass were not reproduced**; their cause (the SC-3000 RGB board's own scaling, the extra pulses of the 9929A on the back porch that shift the neutral, the display gamma, all named in the thread) is open. The 1953 NTSC luma weights (0.30, 0.59, 0.11) differ from BT.601's by at most 0.005 in a channel (0.004 in the green of dark blue). Output: no gamma (the tube's gun law takes over, as for the C64), channels clamped to the rails. **Recognition:** emulators show the chip at display gammas from 1.0 to 1.6 (the Wikipedia article's palette is the decode raised to 1.6; I also recalled that the widely copied palette beginning 21C842, 5EDC78, 5455ED equals the decode at an exponent of 1.0 to the rounding of a byte, but could not fetch a source, so nothing relies on it), and no single exponent holds both ends (a search over single exponents: the best, 1.25, still takes 3 of 15 colours for a neighbour at each end, and 1.2 or 1.3 take 4 at one end), so the reference palette is the 15 colours **at two exponents, 1.1 and 1.5**, each pixel taken for the nearest of the 30 entries: the Wikipedia palette is recognised in full (farthest 0.052), the decode at any exponent from 1.0 to 1.6 in steps of 0.025 loses at most one colour of 15 to a neighbour (medium and dark green, or medium and light red), the closest two entries of different colours are 0.04 apart (medium red at 1.1, light red at 1.5), the threshold is 0.12. Not checked against any emulator's own palette. **Shared code extended:** (a) the colour differences (`r_y`, `b_y`, `neutral`, in the colours and as per-revision lists; `crt_model::target::U_PER_B_Y` and `V_PER_R_Y` are the constants `decode_yuv` already used); (b) `[signal.reference] extra_gammas`: further exponents, each making a set of the reference colours, the hardware colours repeated per set (entry k x n + i, so no change to the oracle's `Signal`, the shader, the push constants or the frame table; `count` is n x sets, at most 256); (c) a revision's `luma_mv` may be 0 (the scope variant's black). No existing file changed in result. **Not built:** the TMS9918A's composite colours (its luminance column differs from the 9928A's in cyan, 67 against 73 percent, and dark green, 46 against 47, and the chroma phases are not in the sources), so the TMS9928A (NTSC) and TMS9929A (PAL) share one table here; the machine encoders (MSX, ColecoVision, TI-99/4A, SG-1000 each their own) and the television; the overshoot and chroma bleed between pixels.

**Built, the SNK Neo Geo palette DAC, 2026-11-26.** `snk_neo_geo`, the `curve` kind (32 entries) extended with `[[curve.variants]]`, each a `scale` on the whole output (`Curve::scale`, pushed as `out_scale`: no shader or push change), and `crt_model::target::ladder_output` (the divider `G_on / (G_all + G_pull)`). **Data:** the resistors 3900, 2200, 1000, 470, 220 ohm on bits 0 to 4 and the pulldowns 8200 (dark bit) and 150 ohm (shadow), confirmed in the sources of MAME (`src/mame/snk/neogeo_v.cpp`, BSD-3-Clause: `resistances[] = {3900, 2200, 1000, 470, 220}`, `weights_dark` with 8200, `weights_shadow` with 150, a shared scaler from the unpulled ladder, a second palette bank at +0x2000 for the shadow) and of Geolith (`src/geo_lspc.c`, BSD-3-Clause, Rupert Carmichael: the same values, the divider as r_to_vcc and r_to_gnd, dark and shadow factors per code); only the numbers are used. The table is my own computation (the unit tests recompute it from the resistors; 7.799, 13.825, 21.624 ... 138.249 (code 16) ... 255, rounded to whole codes) and agrees with the research pass (7.8, 13.8; weights 3.1, 5.4, 11.9, 25.4, 54.2). The factors are 0.985663, 0.557054 and 0.552576, the same for every code, as in the research pass (0.9857, 0.557, 0.553). **Findings from reading Geolith:** its comment lists the resistors from bit 0 = 220 to bit 4 = 3900 but its code indexes `{3900, ..., 220}` by bit, which is the physical order the weights need (bit 0 the smallest weight); its 'resnet' palette smooths every level with `(v[i-1] x 1.6 + v[i] + v[i+1] x 1.6) / 4.2` before normalising, so it is not the pure ladder; its 'Raw' palette is 6 bits (`raw = i ^ 1`, level `(raw x 259 + 33) >> 6`, the shadow `val >> 1`), i.e. levels `(2 c + 1) x 255 / 63` with black at 4 and the dark bit recoverable from the parity (not implemented). **Honest limits:** the pixel carries neither the dark bit nor the shadow state, so variants 2 to 4 are user-chosen states of the whole picture; MAME and Geolith render both themselves, so they need variant 1 at most and in practice no target; no emulator that shows plain `pal5bit` levels was verified (the input is E); the ladder's node is unloaded; the dark-bit weight stays disputed (FBNeo's opposite sign, the 95 percent claim unsupported); the table is integer (8-bit), the largest rounding 0.5 of 255.

**Built, the Sega Master System / Mark III colour DAC, 2026-11-26.** `sega_master_system`, the `dac` kind extended (fewer than 8 codes with the last point repeated: no shader loop change; `Dac::output_green` and `output_blue`; `[[dac.inputs]]` may carry its own `output`, `output_green`, `output_blue`; the shader's push table now holds input, red, green and blue rows of eight bytes, the Mega Drive repeating red three times). **Data re-read at the sources (2026-11-26, through a summarising fetch tool, not verbatim; licences: forum posts, numbers and attribution only, nothing copied, no GPL code read):** TmEE, SpritesMind forum 'SMS and SMS mode output levels' (22 Oct 2015): SMS1 0, 324, 620, 916 mV and SMS2 0, 364, 720, 1052 mV, the post's ratios 0.354, 0.677 and 0.350, 0.684 and its levels 90, 173 and 89, 174 (364 / 1052 is 0.346, 88.2: the post's 89 is kept); **one RGB value per level and no channel named** (the research notes said 'the channel of the 90 / 173 values is not stated': confirmed); SMS2 read at the VDP output because of a DC offset at the usual divider point, the SMS1 at another point than the Mega Drive; the 2022 follow-ups (Eke, TmEE) say the DAC is roughly linear unloaded and the dividers' loading compresses the levels, and TmEE says he meant 'CXA output' for the 1 Vpp white; the Mega Drive VDP in SMS mode 0, 99, 162, 255. bfbiii, SMS Power forum thread 18235 (19 Dec 2020; Maxim, sverx, TmEE, furrtek, Eke follow up): red and green 'pretty much linear', SMS1 blue first step 22 to 23 percent above where it should be, confirmed with an oscilloscope, SMS2 blue normal and its range somewhat higher, the 315-5246 always linear, the cause the VDP (315-5124) and not the encoder; TmEE (30 Dec 2020) found his own readings more or less linear and did not see the blue; furrtek's decap (8 Mar 2021): red and green taps 0, 1, 2, 3 tenths, the first blue tap about 1.2 tenths (Eke: the Mega Drive VDP's SMS-mode B1 tap is shifted too); the SMS Power palette page says the Mark III and SMS1 VDP slightly boost the first blue level (its table empty) and gives the %00BBGGRR format and no RGB values. **Reconciling the numbers:** the research notes' 'derived blue 0, 104, 170, 255' is 85 x 1.225 (22 to 23 percent on the linear step) and 'blue 0, 102, 170, 255' is the die tap (1.2 / 3 x 255 = 102); neither follows from TmEE's 90 and 173, which belong to a loaded measurement of an unnamed channel; so the readings are two families, not one table: variant 2 (bfbiii: red and green 85 and 170, blue 104) and variants 1 and 3 (TmEE's levels, on the three channels for the SMS2; variant 1 adds the blue push as 90 x 1.225 = 110, an estimate, 107 if the push is carried through TmEE's piecewise curve). **Honest limits:** the scope reading of red and green (linear) conflicts with TmEE's compressed numbers by up to 5 of 255; which one a console and television give depends on the load; only the first blue step is raised (nothing published about the others); the input 0, 85, 170, 255 is the usual emulator rule, not read in any emulator; bfbiii's Google document and TmEE's own thread 15700 were not read; the SMS Power thread and the SpritesMind thread were read through a summariser, so the per-post details may be imprecise. **Left out:** the CRAM substitution table of the TMS modes 0 to 3 ($00 $00 $08 $0C $10 $30 $01 $3C $02 $03 $05 $0F $04 $33 $15 $3F; the SMS Power palette page's 'approximate' and 'incomplete' list, its first entries confirmed there: $08 medium green, $3F white): an emulator shows the CRAM entry's colour, not the TMS colour, so a `remap` would have nothing to recognise; and the Game Gear, the Mega Drive in Master System mode.

**Built, the IBM CGA composite colours, 2026-11-26.** `ibm_cga_composite`, the `signal` kind with a reference given as RGB colours (`[[signal.reference.rgb_references]]`: the canonical RGBI palette and PCem's dark yellow, each standing for a colour index; `Signal` itself and the shader are unchanged, the table holds 17 entries). **Data read here (web fetch through a summarising tool, 2026-11-26; the pages state no licence, numbers and attribution only, no code used):** reenigne, 'CRTC emulation for MESS' (2006): the eight chroma waves over eight steps (black 00000000, blue 00001111, green 01111000, cyan 00111100, red 11000011, magenta 10000111, yellow 11110000 'also used for colour burst', white 11111111; two more phases, 00011110 and 11100001, cannot be made in the chroma bit), the four levels of his circuit simulation with a 75 ohm load (Y=0 C=0 0.416 V, Y=1 C=0 0.709 V, Y=0 C=1 1.160 V, Y=1 C=1 1.460 V; a 2010 comment said the first numbers were high by two and the author corrected them; the post does not say which card revision); The PC Emulation Book, 'Composite Video': the multiplexer selects the clock from B, G, R, intensity adds to luma only, the burst is the yellow clock at 180 degrees on a wheel with blue at 0, red at 90, yellow at 180; Wikipedia: the hues lack purity, the early cards have equal luma for 1 to 6 and 9 to 14; int10h.org and the reenigne DOSBox post: old cards differ only in phase, the new cards feed R, G, B, I into the luma. **What was not found:** the new card's weights (0.29, 0.10, 0.22, 0.07, 0.32) and the resistor values are from the second research pass and could not be re-read (the Vogons posts answer 403; the assignment of 680, 1k, 2k, 3k ohm to I, G, R, B is by order and matches the weights within 0.007); any burst amplitude in volts, any sync level, the string's order in time. **Derivations:** (1) the six waves are one wave rotated left by 4, 2, 3, 6, 7 and 0 steps (blue, red, magenta, cyan, green, yellow), so their fundamentals have equal amplitude and phases 45 degrees apart; (2) **the direction of the phase turn is a choice**: read with the first character first in time, the wave that is earlier leads, which on the U axis (burst at 180) puts red at 270 and the colours in the wrong hues; the other direction (hue = 180 - 45 x steps) gives blue 0, magenta 45, red 90, yellow 180, green 225, cyan 270, within 13 to 16 degrees (alternating) of the NTSC hues of the pure colours (347, 61, 103, 167, 241, 283) and equal to The PC Emulation Book's wheel; (3) the old card's luma is the mean of the two intensity levels at the mean chroma (0.5 for the six hues), which reproduces reenigne's 0.72 / 0.28 within 0.008; (4) **saturation:** the burst and the colours are the same wave, so a fixed-gain decoder shows chroma at the card's own ratio, the fundamental 2 p / pi of the swing p over the span: 0.4558 (old, swing 0.7475 V of 1.044 V) and 0.1846 (new, swing 0.29), which is the 'saturations differ' of reenigne and the 'signal level reduced by about 2.5' of a Vogons snippet (0.29 / 0.72 = 0.40); a decoder with automatic chroma control and a sync-based luma gain shows 20 IRE over 100 IRE for both (0.2), which needs a sync tip at 0 V (0.416 V = 40 IRE, 1.044 V = 100.4 IRE: a coincidence that supports it, not a source). Defaults: the old card at its own ratio. **Honest limits:** no composite monitor or television was measured; the 4-variant split is the part of the design that depends on the receiver and is an estimate; the recognition uses the RGBI colours, not the composite palettes of DOSBox, PCem or 86Box (they run their own decoders over the 640 x 200 patterns); no emulator was checked.

**Built, the Atari 7800 (MARIA, NTSC), 2026-11-26.** `atari_7800_maria`, the `signal` kind on the grid of the Atari 8-bit target with `rgb_references` of the CGA target (no shared code change). **Data re-read here (web fetch through a summarising tool, 2026-11-26, plus MAME's file read in full):** the Atari 7800 Programming wiki 'RGB' (sites.google.com/site/atari7800wiki/rgb, anonymous, no licence stated): resistors SYNC 8.2k, LUM3 10k, LUM2 20k, LUM1 39k, LUM0 82k; NTSC sync -40 IRE, blank 0, black 7.5, white 100; contrast 0.0526 with brightness 0 (sync at 47.5 IRE, black-referenced; the blank-referenced try was 0.0444 and -0.081, 'very dark'); colour 40 / 185 = 0.2162 (the page cites 'some calculations' for 185 IRE as 100 percent U or V); tint 180; phase 360 / 14 = 25.714; PAL contrast 0.044, sync -43, burst 42.86 over 200 = 0.2143, no PAL phase. A Bannister forum post derives the 0.0526 as (40 + 7.5) x 8.2 x ((1/10 + 1/20 + 1/40 + 1/80) / 15) / (100 - 7.5), with 40k and 80k for 39k and 82k: that is exactly linear, while the listed 39k and 82k give a ladder at most 0.0032 of the range from linear (luma 8 at 0.5324), hence the ladder variant (the amplitude follows from the same formula, 0.2734). The 7800 development wiki 'Atari 7800 Color Documentation' (observations, no licence stated): hues about 25.7 or 26.7 degrees apart (two historic pot calibrations), 24.7 cold up to 27.7 warm, most of the drift in the first 20 minutes. MAME `src/mame/atari/a7800.cpp` (header `license:BSD-3-Clause`; MAME as a whole is GPL, only numbers of this file are used): changelog 'Contrast = 0.0526 --> 0.05, Colour 0.2162 --> 0.22, Phase 25.714 --> 25.7, Colorburst/Hue 180', tables 24.7 to 27.7 in half-degree steps (the source holds the 25.7 one active and the others in comments, its changelog says 26.2 was made active), notes that hue 1 is 'gold' (about -33 degrees) on a CRT and 'green' on a flat panel, and that at 27.7 hues 14 and 15 match hues 1 and 2. **The finding that changed the design:** MAME's active palette is on hand under a licence we may use, so the Atari 8-bit design (a reference computed by the model at its default calibration) was tested against it and failed: the model's colours are 0.120 from MAME's on average (0.207 at `$3F`), MAME's greys are linear (0x11 steps, which supports the linear luma) but its hue 1 sits near 157 degrees on the U axis (the model: 180), and a model fitted to the palette with its own tint, amplitude and delay (tint 166, amplitude 0.21, delay 25.9) is still 0.044 away, so MAME's palette is an observation and not the wiki's formula. As a reference the model recognised 57 of MAME's 256 colours (178 taken for another, 21 for none). The reference is therefore MAME's table (a 0.05 threshold), the shown colours are the model's. Open: the tint (a per-revision grid angle would make a 'CRT gold' variant, a shared code change), no other emulator palette (Stella, ProSystem and a7800 palettes are GPL or unread), PAL (no phase), the 2600's TIA (not part of this item), the resistor values unchecked against a schematic, the 185 IRE base. Licence: the table is kept with a BSD-3 notice in `THIRD-PARTY.md` (the file header only names the licence; the standard text and the header's holders are used, MAME's `docs/legal` was not read): the owner should confirm that shipping the numbers in a data file is acceptable.

**Built, the NEC PC Engine / TurboGrafx-16 composite colours, 2026-11-26.** `nec_pc_engine_composite`, a new `lut` kind (`Lut` in `crt-model`, shader kind 6, the 512 colours in the frame block's existing 512-entry `target_table`, so no uniform growth and every other target unchanged). **Data read here (raw files fetched from github.com/Kitrinx/TG16_Palette with curl, parsed with awk, 2026-11-26):** `bfbii.h` holds three tables of 512 x 3, `bfbii_lut` (8-bit colours in hex, unnamed palette, used by nothing in the repository and not by this target), `pbpry_int_lut` (the silicon table: columns Cb, Cr, Y as `tg16color.c` calls its decode, U and V 1 to 30, Y 0 to 31) and `index_lut` (checked: entry x is exactly (x >> 6, (x >> 3) and 7, x and 7), a plain count of GGGRRRBBB, so the table is already in palette-word order). The samples of the third research pass all match (black {16, 15, 0}, blue steps {18, 15, 0} {20, 15, 1} ... {30, 15, 3}, red 1 {14, 18, 1}, white {16, 15, 31}); the column order U, V, Y, which that pass called the agent's reading, is the converter's own. **The decode was checked against the converter's output:** the repository also ships `palette.pal` (the Mednafen palette, 512 x 3 bytes, the converter's rounded 8-bit result); the decode of `tg16color.c` and `color_lib.h` read in full (1's complement `c_table` over 15, Umax 0.436, Vmax 0.615 x 0.96, the rotation `pb c - pr s`, `pr c + old pb s` at -10.4 degrees, `yuv_to_rgb` with the exact BT.601 coefficients, `desaturate_rgb_linear` 192/255, clip and round) was recomputed in awk from the table and gives the same bytes as `palette.pal` for all 512 entries (0 mismatches); the same decode in Rust (`decode_pbpr`) reproduces 12 of them in a unit test. Two oddities of the source: `pop_rec_const` falls through its switch, so every Rec. is the 601 one (only 601 is used), and the comment says 14/15 where the code has 0.96 (the second variant). **The table against a plain encode:** a BT.601 encode of the 3-bit levels (levels over 7, Y over 31, U and V over 15) is on average 0.25 codes of Y, 1.0 of U and 0.8 of V from the table, at most 0.55, 2.9 and 2.4; the grey diagonal is exactly linear (Y 0, 4, 9, 13, 18, 22, 27, 31, U 16, V 15), so the differences are in the chroma (part of the U difference is the console's phase turn that the decode then applies again). **Recognition:** exact index recovery from the three levels (the oracle's `lut_index`, shader `lut_index`: n = (code + 18) / 36, then the code must be n spread by repeating its bits, 255 n / 7 rounded, or 36 n; both conventions kept because 3-bit levels are expanded either way in emulators and the usual one was not looked up: the first rule is the bit repetition that every other 3-bit target here assumes, the second the n x 36 that the Atari ST note mentions; no emulator's source was read for the PC Engine and no emulator picture was run through the target). **Licence:** the table is NEC's data as dumped by furrtek; the file has no licence header, GitHub reports none for the repository (the converter is MIT, `color_lib.h` a copyright line): shipped as hardware facts with attribution, said so in the data file, `docs/THIRD-PARTY.md` (with the converter's MIT text) and the changelog; no code copied, the GPL MiSTer palette not used. Open, as in the TODO line: the RGB output has no data; the constants are a fit to one Duo-R by eye; the phase varies per console; the SuperGrafx and the later revisions have no recorded difference; whether the owner keeps a table with no licence.

**Built, the Mega Drive shadow and highlight levels, 2026-11-26.** `sega_mega_drive` variant 1 (Genesis Plus GX) now maps 15 points (`Dac` arrays of 15, `Dac::len`; a variant's own `output` sets its number of codes; shader kind 5 reads 15 inputs and 15 outputs from the same 32-byte push table, kind 3 and the push block of 100 bytes unchanged; Kega Fusion and Regen are the 8-point kind 3 as before, bit for bit). **Arithmetic checked here:** TmEE's 15 levels scaled so that 951 mV is 255 are 0, 28.96, 51.48, 69.98, 86.88, 101.35, 115.84, 129.51, 143.99, 157.67, 172.14, 187.40, 205.9, 227.6, 255, written 0, 29, 52 (the published table's normal level; 51.5 is half a level either side), 70, 87, 101, 116, 130, 144, 158, 172, 187, 206, 228, 255; the even entries are the 8 normal levels, the first 8 the shadow levels and the last 8 the highlight levels, as the repository had them. **Genesis Plus GX's rule** (the file's notes did not state it; read on 2026-11-26 through a summarising fetch, as a description of the pixel macro of `core/vdp_render.c` in the GPL repository, numbers only, no code or table copied, the source not read line by line; and ekeeke's statement of the maximum 238 = 0xEE in GPGX issue 345): each channel is a 4-bit value copied into both halves of the byte, which is 17 times it, with the value n for shadow, 2 n for normal and 7 + n for highlight (shadow 0, 17 ... 119; normal 0, 34 ... 238; highlight 119, 136 ... 238). So a Genesis Plus GX picture sits on the 15 points 17 k (k = 0 to 14) and the three modes agree wherever they meet (119 = shadow 7 = highlight 0, level 7, 130; 136 = normal 4 = highlight 1, 144; 170 = normal 5 = highlight 3, 172; 204 = normal 6 = highlight 5, 206; 238 = normal 7 = highlight 7, 255; and 34 = shadow 2, 68 = shadow 4, 102 = shadow 6): each is a function of the level only, so there is no ambiguity and a 15-point map replaces the 8-point map plus line (first shadow step 29, not 26). Only the 32-bit (8 bits per channel) picture was described; the 15 and 16-bit builds were not modelled. **Not done:** Kega Fusion and Regen (steps 33 and 32 per code, white 231 and 224) are not documented anywhere in the repository for the shadow and highlight half steps, and no page was read for them, so they keep the 8 normal levels (a shadow pixel is a few levels off on them); half their own step would be a guess. Open as before: the white scale (255 against 238), the black level, the encoder's loading.

**Built, the Atari 2600 (TIA, NTSC), 2026-11-26.** `atari_2600_tia`, the `signal` kind on the grid (16 hues x 8 luma = 128 colours, index = register / 2) with `rgb_references`, the 7800's design; no shared code change. **Data read here:** MAME's `src/mame/atari/tia.cpp` in full (raw file by curl; header `license:BSD-3-Clause`, `copyright-holders:Wilbert Pol,Stefan Jokisch`): the NTSC palette is computed in code from a 16-pair (I, Q) table labelled 'Phase Shift 26.2' (hue 1 at 0.192, -0.127), luma j / 7, the YIQ matrix and an exponent of 0.9, with its notes (hue 1 gold on a CRT, 23 degrees at power-on to 28 warm, 25.7 to 27.7 preferred, other tables 24.7 to 27.7 not active); the 128 RGB values were computed once from those numbers (awk), shipped as the reference with a BSD-3 notice, and a unit test recomputes them from the typed table. **Not re-read:** Eric Ball's Stella message, the TIA manual, Gopher2600's blog and jigo2600 (three web searches for the Ball message returned nothing, so every Ball, TIA-manual, Gopher2600 and jigo2600 figure comes from the research pass of 2026-11-25 and is marked second hand in the data file). **Choices:** the default step 25.7 (the Field Service Manual's hue 1 = hue 15 calibration, as for the 7800), Ball's 24, Gopher2600's 26.7 as variants; the amplitude is Ball's 20 / 92.5 = 0.2162 over the luma of his top step 0.63 = 0.3432 (the earlier pass's '0.343' is that ratio); Gopher2600's assumed 0.30 and MAME's hue 1 magnitude 0.2303 as chroma variants; burst at 180 degrees on the U axis (the TIA manual), the Gopher2600 57 or 32.72 degrees recorded as a conflict. No ladder variant (the LM0 and LM1 resistors are in no source), no 2600 against 2600A variant (the numbers do not separate them). **Measured by the unit tests:** the model against MAME's palette is 0.192 on average at the default (worst 0.368), 0.238 at 24, 0.171 at 26.7, 0.158 at 0.30 and 0.114 at 0.23; as a reference the model would have taken 66 of MAME's 128 colours for another colour and 45 for none (threshold 0.12); MAME's two nearest colours are one level apart (hue 1 and hue 14 at the top luma, 0.0039); hue 15 is 0.0015 from hue 1 at 25.7, 0.17 at 24 and 0.11 at 26.7. PAL (a fitted 30-degree model) and SECAM (8 colours) are not built. Owner to confirm, as for the 7800, that shipping MAME's numbers in a data file under the BSD-3 notice is acceptable.

**Built, the MSX2+ / turbo R (V9958) palette, 2026-11-26.** `msx2_v9958`, a `dac` of eight points (`output = [0, 33, 74, 107, 148, 181, 222, 255]` = round(255 x code / 31) for the codes floor(4.5 n)), two input variants (bit repetition 0, 36, 73, ...; blueMSX's truncated 255 n / 7); no shared code change. **Read here:** Grauw's YJK article (table and formula, 'applies to all screen modes', no derivation); openMSX's `SDLRasterizer.cc` through a text summary (GPL, numbers only): on a V9958 machine its V9938 palette colours are built from its 5-bit YJK table through `(n << 2) | (n >> 1)`, with a comment that it is based on comparing red and green gradients, palette against YJK, in SCREEN11 on a real turbo R; on a V9938 machine it uses n / 7 directly; MAME's `v9938.cpp` through a summary (the palette through `pal3bit`, YJK through `pal5bit`, helpers not read); libretro blueMSX `VDP.c` through a summary (palette v x 255 / 7, YJK 255 x (Y + J) / 31). **Decisions:** (1) **openMSX on a V9958 machine already shows the 5-bit levels, so the target would be an identity for it and no variant exists**; the target is for the emulators that expand the 3 bits straight to 8 (MAME, blueMSX), the honest note being that it is for them only. (2) The V9938 is an identity (no documented mapping, linear output): not a variant, not a target. (3) The mapping is still one claim: the table equals round(31 n / 7), so it says nothing about the analogue side, and openMSX's turbo R comparison (as relayed, no numbers) is the only corroboration; the DAC is assumed linear, and the effect is at most 3 of 255. (4) The YJK and YAE decode is not built: an emulator has already decoded those pixels, so there is nothing for a pixel-level target to decode; MAME shows YJK pixels from 5-bit codes expanded to 8, which are already the 5-bit levels, so this target also moves them a little (left off for screens 10 to 12). (5) fMSX was not checked. **Open:** the data books' 'R.G.B. Output Level' pages (readable copy or a measurement), the expansion fMSX and other emulators show, whether openMSX's comparison with the turbo R can be read at its source (the comment is the only trace).

**Built, the Amstrad CPC and its green monitor, 2026-11-26.** `amstrad_cpc` (a `dac` of three points, `output = [0, 102, 255]`, three input variants: half level 128, 127, MAME's 96) and `amstrad_cpc_green_screen` (a `matrix`, three equal rows 9, 3, 1 over 13, 8-bit input, four variants by exponent); no shared code change. **Read here:** nothing new: CPCWiki (Gate Array and CPC Palette pages) answered HTTP 403 again, so the 0.40 (Grim, nocash, the Plus ASIC's 6/15), the 127 or 128 and MAME's 0x60 are the third research pass's figures, marked second-hand in the data files; no MAME source was read. **Choices:** no variant for the literal 0.5 (an emulator already shows 127 or 128, mapping it to itself is an identity, so none was built); no per-channel variant (green's half is 'significantly higher' in the notes, with no number: not invented); the 127 variant is only 0.8 of a code from the 128 one (kept because the input level is stated by the source). **Found while building:** (1) the green monitor's '27 linear steps' hold only with a half level of 0.5; the resistors sum voltages and the Gate Array's half is 0.40, which makes the steps uneven, out of the order of the index (red at half 3.6 below green and blue at full 4) and puts two colours (red and blue at half, 3.6 + 0.4, and green and blue at full) on the same grey, 26 greys instead of 27 (checked by a unit test and by hand); the target ships both (variant 1 the wiki's linear steps, the default, variants 2 and 4 the voltage sum). (2) The matrix kind cannot map an emulator's levels to 0, 0.5, 1 as such; an input exponent that puts the emulator's half level on 0.5 or 0.40 (1.3294 for 128 and 0.40, 0.9379 and 0.7095 for MAME's 96) does it on the three levels exactly and on a power law between, an estimate. (3) The decode stage takes the BT.601 luma of the target's grey, which is the grey itself (weights add to 1), so the green target needs only a monochrome phosphor type to look green; the GT64's tube phosphor is not identified in the sources. **Open:** which half level each emulator really shows (WinAPE, Caprice32, ACE, CPCEC, MAME and its version), Grim's per-channel readings, the monitor input stage and gamma, the modulator.

**Built, the Game Boy family (DMG, Pocket, Light), 2026-11-26.** `game_boy_lcd`, the `palette` kind (four colours by the luma of a grey source), five variants: BGB reality DMG and Pocket (photo-derived, read from the palette chunk of BGB's swatch images; the slot order is an inference), SameBoy DMG, Pocket and Light. **Shared code:** `colour_bits = 8` accepted for the palette kind (0xRRGGBB words, output over 255), a few lines; the Super Game Boy file is unchanged. **Read here again:** SameBoy `Core/display.c` (raw file): the three palettes and the LCD-off fifth entry match the values of the fourth pass (DMG 081810, 396139, 84A563, C6DE8C, off D2E6A6; MGB 07100E, 3A4C3A, 818D66, C2CE93, off CFDAAC; Light 0A1C15, 357862, 56B495, 7FE2C3, off 91EAD0), stored darkest to lightest; its LICENSE is Expat, Copyright (c) 2015-2026 Lior Halphon, with an extra condition only for the iOS directory (not used). The BGB numbers were not re-read (the images were not fetched here). **Decisions:** (1) the LCD-off colour is not shown: the palette kind has no background colour (a black source pixel is the darkest shade) and on a real screen the lightest shade is the LCD-off colour per BGB, though BGB's own swatches give them different hues (948A04 against 759833); extending the kind for it was not trivial and was not done. (2) The default variant is BGB reality DMG (a photo-derived set); SameBoy is offered as an emulator convention. (3) BGB's licence is unknown (closed freeware): the numbers are used with attribution in the data file, the notes, the changelog and THIRD-PARTY.md, and **the owner should ask BGB's author**. (4) **No instrument measurement** of a DMG, Pocket or Light screen exists anywhere the research could reach; the contrast wheel and the Light's backlight spectrum are not modelled. Checks: unit tests (colours written again from the hex of the sources, the LCD-off colour in none, a grey ramp on the four colours exactly, no grey-to-grey identity, the validation), an 8-bit packing test, and four GPU cases against the oracle (max 2.4e-4).

**Built, the Virtual Boy, 2026-11-26.** `nintendo_virtual_boy`, a `matrix` (red row only, input and output exponent 2.2, 8-bit input), two input variants (a grey picture by the BT.601 luma, Mednafen's default tint 0xF0F0F0; a red picture by the red channel); no shared code change. **Model:** the LED's light is proportional to its on-time (design statements of the manual, second-hand, and of the patent, not a measurement), the games' registers typically give 0, A, 2A, 3A, so light 0, 1/3, 2/3, 1; the emulator's 1 / 2.2 encode is undone, red kept, the light encoded again with 2.2 for the chain. **Decisions:** (1) on the red channel the two exponents cancel, so the red-ramp variant is an identity on a pure red picture (documented in the data file, the changelog and the reference; it only drops a green or blue part of a tint) and the target's effect is grey to red; the exponents stay so that the model is in linear light and the luma of a tinted picture is taken there. (2) The colour (1, 0, 0) is a stand-in for the Private Eye P4's 660 +- 20 nm red (sibling product, unofficial archive copy with no licence, three figures quoted: 660 nm, 2 fL, 70:1); the sRGB gamut cannot show 660 nm; the P4's contrast is not turned into a black lift. (3) Mednafen's `ledonscale` 1.75 is not a variant: it is the emulator's coefficient, it may clip (so cannot be undone), and a gain is the chain's job; the tint is not rescaled. (4) Red Viper's (v / 255 x 1.75)^0.9 is not the 2.2 encode, so variant 2 is only approximate for it; Beetle VB's 'black and red' option was not checked (no note on it). No new reading was done for this target. Checks: unit tests (hand derivation of the four levels at light 0 : 1 : 2 : 3, no green or blue for any colour, the validation), a shader push test, GPU cases against the oracle for both variants and a dedicated level test (ratios 1.0000, 1.9991, 3.0007).

**Built, the Apple II lo-res colours, 2026-11-26.** `apple_ii_lores`, the `signal` kind with 16 colours (index = the Apple lo-res colour number), four variants (the ratio of the dark and light chroma to the mid chroma, 0.707 of the ideal waves or Munafo's 0.60, times the decoder: chroma gain following the burst, or fixed). Derived, not measured: the level set is Gayler's (sync 0 V, black 0.5 V, white 2.0 V, burst 0.7 V p-p), the rest is the logic of the pixel stream. A lo-res colour is a four-bit pattern on the 14.318 MHz clock; each lit bit is a quarter-cycle pulse whose fundamental is 2 sin(45 degrees) / pi = 0.4502 of the span at a phase that turns by 90 degrees a bit (90 degrees for bit 0, from Munafo's 1, 2, 4, 8 at 90, 0, 270, 180), and a colour is the phasor sum: this gives, with no further assumption, all 15 phases of Munafo's table (3 at 45, 6 at 315, 9 at 135, 12 at 225, 7 at 0, 11 at 90, 13 at 180, 14 at 270), the greys cancelling, the mid colours at 2 / pi = 0.6366 and the dark and light ones at 0.4502 (Ticmanis' 0.707), and Gayler's violet, blue and green at 135, 225 and 315 degrees relative to the burst are purple 45, medium blue 315 and light green 225 here (an independent check of the grid's direction). The unit test recomputes the 16 colours from the bits. Chroma over burst: 0.6366 / 0.2333 = 2.73 (Munafo's table: 100 / 40 = 2.5), so a burst-following decoder (burst to 20 IRE) shows the mid colours at 0.5457 of the span, a fixed-gain decoder at 0.6366. **The 0.75 V black was tried as a variant and not built**: the manuals give it for the auxiliary pin with no burst level; a span of 1.25 V with the same burst would give 0.455 for the mid chroma; the guess is in the notes only. **Reference and a surprise:** MAME's lo-res palette (BSD-3, R. Belmont, from the 'Apple II Video Display Theory' sheet; `src/mame/apple/apple2video.cpp`, read in full, the 16 colours in lo-res order, both greys 808080) is the reference (exact recognition, independent of the variant); the model's colours on the two burst-following variants are 0.025 from it on average, 0.05 at worst (purple and dark blue), and a model reference would have recognised all 16, which the 7800 and the 2600 could not (their emulator palettes are tuned by eye); the fixed-gain variants are 0.063 and 0.042 away (purple 0.137) and a model reference would have lost 4 and 2 colours. So the default is **close to an identity on a MAME picture** (a visible but small change, at most 13 of 255 levels); the value of the target is that two independent derivations (the circuit logic, MAME's sheet) agree, the variants, and the recognition for the other palettes' pixels only if they are within 0.1 of MAME's. Not read: the 'Apple II Video Display Theory' sheet itself (the 'Actual' I and Q magnitudes 0.104, 0.193, 0.297, 0.489 of the fifth pass remain unexplained), the IIe network's resistor roles. Shared code extended: `amplitudes` on a `signal` revision (one chroma amplitude per colour).

**Built, the ZX Spectrum 128K, +2, +2A and +3 RGB output, 2026-11-26.** `sinclair_zx_spectrum_rgb`, the `dac` kind with three points per channel (0, normal, bright; no shared code change), six variants: the emulator's normal level (205 = 0xCD, the default; 192 = 0xC0, FUSE's; 216 = 0xD8, 85 percent) times the ratio of the analogue levels (0.68, the +2A and +3 schematic computation through 330 ohm, 173 of 255, the default; 0.63, one author's 128K SCART cable calculations, 161). **A documented choice, not a measurement:** the 128K socket is TTL, the analogue levels belong to the cable and the load, no measurement of a real Spectrum exists in the sources, and the emulators' values are conventions read from the notes only (205 has no name in the notes; Spectaculator comes from the request for this target; no emulator code was read).
