Not variants: 0.85 (the 48K composite figure of Wikipedia, citing a clone-design blog, 'indicative only': an identity for the 215 and 216 emulators, 216.75 of 255, and a different circuit) and the Harlequin readings (0.670, 0.729, 0.738, a snippet, a different board; 0.67 is 171 and 0.74 is 189 of 255, against 173 of the default). No variant is an identity (no emulator in the notes shows 161 or 173 as its normal level). **Not built:** the 48K PAL encode and the 128K's composite through the TEA2000 (the datasheet: 'equally spaced intensity levels', no Y, U, V table): no per-colour levels exist in anything readable, and the documents that would hold them are image-only or unread (TODO).

**Built, the ZX Spectrum 16K, 48K and Plus PAL composite colours, 2026-11-26.** `sinclair_zx_spectrum_48k_pal`, the `signal` kind with 15 colours (black, 7 normal, 7 bright; bright black is black), per-colour `level_mv` (the /Y drops of page 7: 0.30, 0.61, 0.15 V, the bright colours that times 1.40 / 1.06) and `angle` with a per-revision `amplitudes` list, `rgb_references` with 29 entries (black, the seven colours at 192, 205 and 216, and at 255; several entries per colour are allowed by the parser), two variants (**fixed_gain**, the default after the owner's decision: the standard PAL weights over the normal white; **burst_gain**: the burst, 1.294 V, brought to the PAL 3/14 of the span, the alternative for a television whose gain follows the burst; both rest on the probe-test levels). No shared code change. Arithmetic checked (tests): U drops 0.98 + 0.66 + 0.33 = 1.97 V against the measured all-on 1.96 V; V drops 1.40, 1.17, 0.24 V; black and white agree to 0.01 V on both axes; Y drops 0.30 + 0.61 + 0.15 = 1.06 V, normal white predicted 1.34 V, bright white 1.00 V measured, normal luma 1.06 / 1.40 = 0.757 of the bright (the Y bias network 7 kohm, 2 kohm, 5 kohm in parallel gives 0.763 but does not reproduce the absolute levels); the U and V drops are 1.10 (B-Y) and 2.0 (R-Y) with the NTSC luma weights (PAL ratio within 2 percent); the six colour angles come out within 0.7 degree of the standard PAL colour bars; the burst (U burst branch off +0.89 V, V BURST* branch on -0.94 V, not the burst rows taken as levels: those rows hold other colour pins) is (-0.89, +0.94) V, 1.294 V at 133.4 degrees against the +V line's 135 degrees, U and V the same size as the 4.4 kohm and 3 kohm branches make them. The struck-out V row is the yellow state (the model gives 1.63 V with the burst branch on, the struck number reads like 1.62). Findings: the burst is large against the colours (red and cyan 1.44 V against 1.29 V, a standard PAL burst is a third of the strongest colour), so a burst-following television desaturates (normal blue about 16, 17, 112), while the fixed-gain reading gives normal colours at 187 to 199 (red 192, 0, 0), close to the emulators' 192 and 205; which one a real Spectrum shows is open (the trimmers VR1 and VR2 and the clamp proposal move it). Not distinguished: the issues, the 6C001 (assumed equal), the loads R47, R56 and R63, the clamp. A one-channel 238 is taken for the bright colour, a two or three channel 238 passes through unchanged (threshold 0.08).

**Advice on the NES square-wave encoder (an engineering judgement, not measured: nothing was compared here).** The flat colours do not depend on it: a decoder passes the fundamental of the square wave, and part 1 computes exactly that. What the sinusoidal model of part 3 already reproduces is the structure of the NES artefacts: the colour clock is 2/3 of the pixel clock, a pixel advances the subcarrier by 240 degrees, and the 341-pixel line holds 227.33 colour cycles, a 120-degree advance per line (the `composite_line_rate_khz` and `composite_frame_advance_deg` parameters take these numbers), which is the diagonal dot crawl and the three-pixel hue edges. What it does not reproduce is the **harmonics** of the 12-phase wave (the third lies near 10.7 MHz, well above the 1.3 MHz chroma bandwidth and the 4 MHz luma bandwidth of a television, so a decoder with ordinary filters removes most of it) and the exact luma-chroma crosstalk of the square edges; both would show on a very wide-band decoder (an S-Video or RGB-bandwidth path) or in a filter-free emulator view, and hardly on a composite television. The encoder costs a new encode path in the shader, an oracle and tests (a few days of work) and a second composite code path to keep in step. **Recommendation: do not build it now.** Build 3b with the fundamental, compare the artefacts on a real NES capture or an emulator picture with the nesdev-described dot crawl, and decide then; if the difference is visible the encoder is a separate, self-contained step.
## Second research pass: the partial-data and no-data platforms (2026-11-25)

Five researchers (one hour, in parallel) dug the platforms the first audit left as "partial data" or "no data", with the Internet, GitHub, datasheets, schematics, forums and FPGA/emulator sources, each told to compute levels from schematics where the numbers were not published. What follows is what they brought back; every figure is as relayed and has to be re-read at its source before it goes into a data file. Limits: AtariAge, CPCWiki, sinclairzxworld, consolemods and Sega Retro refused automated reads (403 or a bot wall), the Wayback Machine is blocked to the tool, and the Grimware domain (CPC Gate Array scope data) redirects to a spam site and is not to be followed.

### What became buildable (data found, named, usable with attribution)

| Platform | What was found | Quality | Transform |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Atari 8-bit, 5200 (GTIA)** | Altirra Hardware Reference Manual (Avery Lee; redistributable verbatim): the **measured luma** of 16 levels on four machines (800XL: 1.112 to 2.224 V, 130XE: 0.468 to 1.380 V, 800: 0.640 to 1.440 V, 400 by probe on R203), oscilloscope +/-0.008 V; the luma ladder 36k, 18k, 9.1k and 4.7k ohm, from which the researcher reproduced the measured levels (luma 8 at 0.5241 of the range against 0.518 measured, and the "luma 7 and 8 nearly identical" quirk); hue delay 23.5 to 27.5 degrees a hue, hue 1 and the burst at -57 degrees behind I; chroma as a share of the luma range per machine (800XL 23.9, 130XE 23.3, 800 35.0, 400 30.6 percent); PAL phase table (Table 14, 22.5 degrees ideal, real 18 to 19) without voltages | Measured (scope) | `signal` kind, NTSC; PAL needs a decision on the tap spacing |
| **Atari 7800** | Atari 7800 Programming wiki formula (unnamed author, "established formulas"): luma network 8.2k, 10k, 20k, 39k, 82k ohm (almost linear, luma 8 at 0.5324), IRE levels, tint 180, phase 360 / 14 = 25.714 degrees, saturation 40 / 185 = 0.2162 (PAL 0.2143); two independent pots (TIA and MARIA) | Derived from a schematic, the original Atari documents unread | `signal` kind, labelled unverified |
| **TMS9928A / 9929A (SG-1000, SC-3000, MSX1, ColecoVision)** | The TI data manual (Nov 1982) Table 2-3, read by two researchers from the scan: Y, R-Y and B-Y of the 15 colours (white 1.00, neutral offset 0.47); an independent oscilloscope measurement of a TMS9929ANL in an SC-3000 by TmEE (SMS Power forum, 30 Jan 2017, mV, white 964) agrees to about 1 percent; the Rec.601 decode matrix gave wrong yellows (the chip's colour differences are not at Rec.601 scale), so the YPbPr-to-RGB matrix is an open point; the 9918A composite chroma angles are not given | Datasheet plus independent scope | `signal`-style per-colour Y, U, V with its own decode |
| **Neo Geo AES/MVS** | Resistor ladder from the schematics: colour bits 3.9k, 2.2k, 1k, 470, 220 ohm, dark bit 8.2k, shadow 150 ohm (neogeo-diag-bios docs, neogeodev wiki, MAME BSD-3 and Geolith BSD-3 agree); the researcher computed the 32 levels (0, 7.8, 13.8 ... 255 of 255, weights 3.1, 5.4, 11.9, 25.4, 54.2 percent, not binary), the shadow factor 0.557, the dark factor 0.9857 (8.2k pulldown) and 0.553 for both; d3m1ck's 95 percent dark claim and his shadow levels disagree with the divider model and are not supported; FBNeo adds the dark bit as a ladder input (opposite sign, same 1.4 percent) | Schematic-derived, no measured voltage per code | `curve` kind (32 entries) with variants for the dark and shadow bits |
| **Apple IIgs** | The 16-colour RGB palette (12-bit, 4 bits a channel, n x 17) from the IIgs technical note via Munafo | Authoritative for the RGB output | `remap` kind (what emulators show of it is not sourced) |
| **IBM CGA composite** | reenigne's formulas: old CGA (1804472, 1501486) composite = 0.72 chroma + 0.28 I, new CGA (1504910, 1501981) = 0.29 chroma + 0.1 R + 0.22 G + 0.07 B + 0.32 I (resistors 2k, 1k, 3k, 680 ohm agree), chroma 0 for colours 0 and 8, 1 for 7 and 15, 0.5 otherwise | Schematic-derived; the burst amplitude and the phases per colour are not given | composite `signal` with the NTSC decode |
| **Master System (VDP, Mode 4)** | TmEE's bench levels (SpritesMind, 22 Oct 2015): SMS1 0, 324, 620, 916 mV (0, 90, 173, 255), SMS2 0, 364, 720, 1052 mV (0, 89, 174, 255), the MD VDP in SMS mode 0, 99, 162, 255; the thread says the unloaded DAC is roughly linear and the dividers compress it, bfbiii (capture card plus scope) says red and green are linear and the SMS1 blue is 22 to 23 percent high, furrtek's die imaging gives the blue tap 1.2 / 10 (blue 0, 102, 170, 255, the researcher's arithmetic); the channel of the 90 / 173 values is not stated | Community bench plus die imaging, load-dependent | per-channel curve, blue for the first VDP |
| **SMS modes 0 to 3** | The CRAM value the VDP substitutes for each TMS colour (SMS Power Palette page; incomplete): $00 $00 $08 $0C $10 $30 $01 $3C $02 $03 $05 $0F $04 $33 $15 $3F | Hardware table, the page's own caveat | `remap` |
| **Mega Drive, shadow and highlight** | The 15-point ramp confirmed (0, 108, 192, 261, 324, 378, 432, 483, 537, 588, 642, 699, 768, 849, 951 mV): normal = the even entries, shadow = entries 0 to 7 (0, 29, 52, 70, 87, 101, 116, 130), highlight = entries 7 to 14 (130, 144, 158, 172, 187, 206, 228, 255); Eke's and Mask of Destiny's values (BlastEm) differ by up to 3; no newer measurement; the "0.9 Vpp" white is not stated anywhere (the divider output is 951 mV, the VDP output about 1.33 Vpp by Eke's schematic arithmetic), treat the table as relative | Bench, one forum member | extend the `dac` target with the shadow and highlight levels |

### Data found, but not enough, or not ready to ship

- **Game Boy Color, Brankale's 2026 sessions:** the repository has the instrument readings of the screen **with the protector removed** (2026-11-24, spectrophotometer calibrated, 30 minutes of warm-up) at three meter orientations (top, middle, bottom: never defined; the gamma changes with them): black-subtracted primaries stable across orientations, R (0.5055, 0.3283), G (0.3053, 0.4493), B (0.1670, 0.2589), white (0.3276, 0.3632) (the researcher's arithmetic), contrast 3.0 to 4.6, effective gamma 1.00 (top), 1.17 (middle) and 1.63 (bottom); no licence; the author's status is "validation in progress, data have issues". A declared orientation would make a target; the exponent is a choice between 1.0 and 1.6.
- **Game Boy Advance SP AGS-001 (frontlit, reflective):** 2026-11-20 with the protector removed, three orientations, gamma 2.32, 1.91, 1.44, white xy (0.342, 0.369), (0.365, 0.378), (0.350, 0.375); **whether the frontlight was on is recorded nowhere**; Game Boy Advance AGB-001 2026-11-16: contrast 9.27, gamma 1.63, primaries R (0.4944, 0.3269), G (0.3166, 0.4679), B (0.1646, 0.2640).
- **DS Lite and 3DS (transmissive, firm numbers):** DS Lite 2026-05-03 (Display colorimeter): primaries R (0.6065, 0.3381), G (0.3331, 0.5992), B (0.1523, 0.0867), white (0.3055, 0.3084), contrast 617; gamma disputed (1.85 DisplayMate, whose figures may not be reproduced without its permission, 2.06 by a researcher's arithmetic). Not a platform of this project's list, but a firm example.
- **Game Boy (DMG) and Pocket:** the Brankale repository has no DMG, Pocket, Light, Virtual Boy, Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket, WonderSwan or Game Gear folder (status "no screen data"). The only measured-by-photograph source is BGB's "reality" colour schemes (calibrated photographs under accurate light, a colour checker, gamma 2.2; freeware, closed source), whose values exist only as images and were read from the pixels by a researcher: DMG #948a04 #759833 #588f51 #3b7560 #2e615a, MGB #c2cfa8 #adbf92 #96a67c #727e64 #5a635c (five swatches left to right: four shades and the LCD-off colour, the order the researcher's inference). The popular 0F380F, 306230, 8BAC0F, 9BBC0F set has no primary source (credits to two different people on two sites, both unverified); SameBoy's are hand-tuned.
- **PC Engine:** furrtek's gist has no table (a 6502 utility that dumps the HuC6260 ROM); the palette is Kitrinx's TG16_Palette generator (tg16color.c, header MIT, 2020; the other files carry no licence) built on that silicon dump, tuned to Artemio's vectorscope plots and adopted by MiSTer; model constants Y = value / 31, Pb and Pr scales 0.436 and 0.615 x 0.96, phase -10.4 degrees ("varies per system"), desaturation 192 / 255; the 512-entry table is not usable until the licence is settled; no RGB-output DAC levels found.
- **Amstrad CPC:** three levels per channel; the half level is unresolved: 0.5 (nocash, about 1.0 V and 0.5 V with a monitor, about 4.0 V for both without), 0.40 (CPCWiki "measured closer to 40 percent", the Plus 4-bit mapping 0, 6, 15 = 40 percent) or 0.376 (MAME 0x60, GPL cross-check); the Grimware scope table was not read; the Retronik MM12 / MM14 service manual (too large for the tool) holds the driver schematics.
- **ZX Spectrum:** the service manual (read in full) gives resistors (R47 / R49 / R56 / R63 by issue and ULA) and the chroma bias, **no Y, U, V levels**; normal 0.55 V and bright 0.65 V (85 percent) is Wikipedia's nominal figure citing a blog that could not be fetched; "real colours currently unknown".
- **MSX V9938 / V9958:** the claimed mapping 0, 4, 9, 13, 18, 22, 27, 31 was **not found anywhere** and is dropped (it is round(31 n / 7), a linear 3-bit palette in a 5-bit DAC); the electrical levels are in a data book PDF that could not be read.
- **Apple II (composite):** Munafo's table (CC BY-NC 4.0, non-commercial: not usable in an MIT project without care) gives phase, amplitude and luma of the 14 lo-res and the 4 hi-res colours, all derived from NTSC parameters (gamma Y ^ -0.3), none measured.
- **Atari 2600:** luma L / 7 (unverified; a "Ball 2003" derivation Y = 0.09 L, 0.63 at L = 7, could not be checked), the hue step 24, 25.7 or 26.7 degrees (a community dispute; the Gopher2600 author concludes between 25.7 and 26.7, burst rotation 57 degrees), observed saturation of hue 1 per luma 0.126, 0.158, 0.184, 0.210, 0.235, 0.256, 0.277, 0.295; PAL and SECAM: no schematic or measurement found.
- **Atari ST and STE, Commodore Amiga:** nothing measured. ST: 3 bits a channel, 27 ohm output resistors on the ST and 75 on the STE, 1 Vpp RGB; **STE bit order** "0 3 2 1" for the values 1, 8, 4, 2 (bit 3 is the least significant: level index ((n and 7) << 1) or (n >> 3)); Amiga: the original hybrid is a binary-weighted DAC 1k, 2k, 4k, 8k per colour into an NPN stage and a 75 ohm termination (OpenAmigaVideoHybrid), black level "about 500 mV" in one SCART guide (context unread); the 1084S and SC1224 transfer characteristics not found.
- **Virtual Boy:** no LED wavelength, luminance or gamma measurement; the Mednafen model (GPL) is a duty fraction and a 1 / 2.2 power. **WonderSwan, Atari Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color:** no measurement; ares formulas (ISC) are borrowed from the Pocket and the GBC. **Sega Game Gear:** no measured screen data of any kind (four searches). **Super Game Boy output chain:** no measurement. The Codeberg ngpc_capture_demo project could not be found (the name is not indexed).

### Licence and reuse notes from this pass

- **Brankale's Handheld-Color-Space-Project: no LICENSE file**, no reuse statement; the only licence is GPL v2 for one parser (`chc.py`); its README credits Pokefan531 (almost all screen measurements) and pica200 (3DS); the data are ODS spreadsheets and spotread outputs; **ask Brankale and Pokefan531 before shipping any of it** (the AGS-101 target of 0.10.0 already uses the AGS-101 numbers without having asked; see TODO).
- **DisplayMate** reserves its figures ("may not be copied ... without prior written permission"): not usable.
- Munafo's Apple II page is CC BY-NC 4.0; Wikipedia text CC BY-SA; the Altirra manual is redistributable verbatim; forum posts state no licence (cite the author, quote numbers only); MAME (BSD-3) and Geolith (BSD-3) palettes and resistor models are citable; GPL, LGPL and non-commercial sources (Mednafen, BlastEm, FBNeo, Genesis Plus GX, DOSBox, MiSTer) are cross-checks only.

### What this changes in the order of work

1. Buildable with data of named quality, in the order of the data: **TMS9928/9929** (datasheet plus scope; the decode matrix to settle first), **Atari 8-bit GTIA NTSC** (measured luma, the best of the new ones), **Neo Geo** (schematic resistors, a 32-entry curve with dark and shadow variants), **Apple IIgs** (a remap of an authoritative palette), the **Master System** per-channel curve, the **CGA composite** old and new, the **Mega Drive** shadow and highlight levels, the **Atari 7800** (labelled unverified).
2. Waiting for data that exists but cannot be read from here: the CPC (Grimware, the Retronik service manual), the V9938 (the Yamaha data book), the ZX Spectrum (the schematic networks, Chris Smith's ULA book), the Atari 2600 stock luma network (the Field Service Manual schematics, images only), the PC Engine table (a licence-clear data file from Kitrinx or Furrtek).
3. Still no data at all: Game Gear, Virtual Boy, WonderSwan, Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, Amiga, Atari ST colour levels, the SGB output chain.

## Results of a second-PC session (2026-11-25)

The owner ran the hardware session of `docs/ENV.md` section 9 on a second laptop; the raw files (the hardware inventory, the adapter reports, seven stats runs, empty error files) were deleted after the analysis, and the numbers are in this section. The owner reports that every other test (file dialogs, cameras, the clean first start) **worked fine**, with no details; no data came back for the HDR step. **Update (owner, later the same day):** everything that was still to be tried on that laptop also worked well, HDR (Windows HDR on, whole-screen capture, Auto HDR content) and the 240 Hz mode included, again with no numbers or details; the newer Intel driver and the live check scripts (for which the owner found a workaround for the `AllSigned` policy) also worked. The manual menu checklist of `scripts/check-menu.ps1` (hotkey rebinding with a chord another program owns, the Save and Open dialogs, the clipboard, the AltGr warning) and a session on real games (SP-1 and SP-2) were also done on that laptop and worked well. Those items are closed on that word, not on measurements. Not run: the live check scripts (the laptop's `LocalMachine` execution policy is `AllSigned`, which refuses every unsigned `.ps1` file, `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` included).

### The second laptop, as measured

- **Windows 11 Pro, build 26200**: not a Windows 10 PC, so the Windows 10 question needs another PC. The owner is confident Phoxium also works on Windows 10 (no test; 2026-11-25), so the README and the reference say "expected to work, not tested" instead of requiring Windows 11.
- An 8-core 10th-generation Intel Core i7 and 32 GB of memory.
- **NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 Laptop GPU**, 6 GB, 95 W maximum, GeForce driver 32.0.16.2101 (621.01), **Vulkan 1.4**. **Intel UHD Graphics 630** (a 2020 driver, 27.20.100.8935), **Vulkan 1.2**.
- **Panel:** 1920 x 1080, 15.6 inch, offers **60, 240 and 300 Hz**. An RGB and an IR camera.

### Findings

1. **The Intel UHD 630 is not usable, and Phoxium says so.** `--list-gpus` shows it with Vulkan 1.2 and `--list-adapters` reports "not usable (needs Vulkan 1.3 and a graphics queue)"; the NVIDIA GPU is chosen in both modes. A newer Intel driver might offer 1.3 (not tried).
2. **Another GPU and driver work.** A GeForce driver on an RTX 3060 (the reference PC has a workstation driver on an RTX A2000): zero-copy capture active (copy cost 0.42 to 0.51 ms per frame, no fallback), the quality tiers and the automatic drop as on the reference PC, no warning and no error (the error output of every run was empty).
3. **The mixed-GPU case was not produced by this laptop.** In the "hybrid" run Phoxium's DXGI view already lists the **NVIDIA** adapter as the owner of the panel (`output \\.\DISPLAY1` under the NVIDIA adapter, "no outputs" under the Intel one) while Windows' own device list names the Intel GPU for `\\.\DISPLAY1`; in Discrete mode the Intel adapter disappears altogether. `auto` therefore picked NVIDIA in both modes ("capture and render on the same adapter"). The GPU time of the same run in both modes is the same within the window-size difference (below), the render rate is 60 in both, and the capture copy costs the same: **no cross-adapter cost is visible in Phoxium's own numbers**. What the GPU timestamps cannot show is a copy made by Windows to present, the power draw and the latency. The cross-adapter case of `QUESTIONS.md` HW-3 (the chain on an adapter that does not own the monitor) stays measurable only where the topology gives it, as on the reference PC.
4. **The GeForce RTX 3060 is faster than the RTX A2000** on the same scene (a 1280 x 720 source on the slot-mask preset in a 1904 x 1011 output): the first second at the standard tier costs 27.5 to 28.8 ms against 33.7 to 36.3 ms on the A2000 (`screen` 21 ms of it), and the `fast` tier 7.4 ms against about 11.6 ms.
5. **The automatic drop at 300 Hz behaves as designed.** At 60 Hz one drop to `fast` and the picture then fits (60 fps). At 300 Hz (a 3.3 ms period) the drop goes to `fast` then `low` and **stops there, with no flapping** (no "back to" line in any run); even `low` cannot meet the refresh, so the render rate follows the GPU time: slot preset 8.7 ms, **115 frames per second**; PVM preset 6.1 ms, **154 frames per second**. The panel's 240 Hz mode (4.2 ms): no stats run; the owner reports it worked well.

### Numbers

| Run (1280 x 720 source, output 1904 x 1011 unless noted) | First second | After the drop | Steady GPU ms | Render fps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hybrid, 60 Hz, slot-mask preset | 27.5 ms | `fast` | 7.37 | 60 |
| Hybrid, 60 Hz, slot-mask preset (second run) | 27.8 ms | `fast` | 7.43 | 60 |
| Hybrid, 60 Hz, PVM preset | 24.3 ms | `fast` | 5.23 | 60 |
| Hybrid, 300 Hz, slot-mask preset | 27.9 ms | `fast`, then `low` | 8.72 | 115 |
| Hybrid, 300 Hz, PVM preset | 24.3 ms | `fast`, then `low` | 6.09 | 154 |
| Discrete, 60 Hz, slot-mask preset (output 1920 x 1027) | 28.9 ms | `fast` | 7.74 | 60 |
| Discrete, 300 Hz, slot-mask preset (output 1920 x 1027) | 29.8 ms | `fast`, then `low` | 8.53 | 117 |

The Discrete output was 2.4 % larger (the window had a remembered rectangle), which accounts for its 5 % higher cost; at 60 Hz both modes render at 60 fps.

### What to do with it

The README now says the program was tried on a second laptop, that an Intel UHD 630 with that driver is not usable, and that on a 240 or 300 Hz panel the render rate follows the GPU time. The TODO keeps only what is left (HDR, a newer Intel driver, 240 Hz, the live check scripts); the Windows 10 question is under the hardware that is not available.

## Third research pass (2026-11-26): one agent per platform

Method (owner request): the "Partial data" and "No data at all" platforms of the TODO, in groups of five, one agent per platform, 15 minutes each, web only (search and fetch, no file written). Each agent reports values, source, quality and licence; what follows is what they read, not what we verified. Blocked everywhere: the Wayback Machine, the CPCWiki main site, AtariAge, zxdesign.info, Grimware (domain lost). Scanned PDFs (no text layer) could not be read, and no PDF renderer is installed here: the V9938 and V9958 data books are the open case (the electrical pages are still to be read by eye).

### Group 1: Atari 2600, Amstrad CPC, ZX Spectrum, MSX2 V9938, Apple II

**Atari 2600 (TIA): partial, parametric model only.** No instrument measurement anywhere. Eric Ball (Stella list, December 2003, schematic-computed, self-described as "still off"): CX2600 SYNC 10k and LM2 12k with BLK connected (7.5 IRE setup), CX2600A SYNC and LM2 27k, BLK open; luma 0.045 x (COLU and 15) per step (0.09 a step, 0.63 at the top, over a 92.5 IRE span); chroma 20/92.5; hue 180 - (hue - 1) x 24 degrees. TIA technical manual (TIA_1A, Atari copyright, OCR on archive.org): 15 phases, code 1 in phase with the burst, 228 counts a line, burst 16 counts, resistor values not given. Gopher2600 blog (2025, all rights reserved): 25.7 to 26.7 degrees a step, burst rotation 57 degrees (elsewhere 32.72), saturation 0.3 assumed, luma linear assumed; the Field Service Manual says to trim the delay pot R211 (R213 on the 2600A) "within one shade". jigo2600 doc (BSD-2 as reported, not checked): 14 stages of 24 degrees, burst on colour clocks 36 to 51. Conflicts: phase step 24, 25.7 or 26.7 degrees (reported drift 23 to 28 with temperature and pot), saturation 0.21 to 0.343, luma 0.63 against a linear 0 to 1, two board networks. PAL: only a fitted 30-degree model and emulator palettes; SECAM: the 8-colour table. Missing: LM0, LM1 and COL resistors, the output stage, any scope voltage, the PAL mechanism. Verdict: a `signal` target with the phase step exposed as a parameter (24 to 27 degrees, like the real pot) and unmeasured constants, labelled; PAL and SECAM as remaps only.

**Amstrad CPC: partial.** The half level is about 0.40 of full, not 0.5: CPCWiki Gate Array page (credited to Grim, confirmed by nocash; an instrument claim reported second-hand, no voltages), and the Plus ASIC maps its 4-bit DAC to #0, #6, #F (6/15 = 0.40) "to better match the real CPC palette". nocash scope on a CPC6128 (100 mV a division, loaded by a Philips TV): unloaded max and half both about 4.0 V, loaded about 1.0 V and 0.5 V (rounded, not an Amstrad monitor). Resistors (forum snippet, page 403): luminance-mixing 1k, 3.3k, 10k; output 220 or 270 ohm; R, G, B pull each other, min and max barely change. Green monitor GT64 and GT65: weights 9 : 3 : 1 (10k, 3.3k, 1k), 27 linear steps (well founded). Plus monitors: CM14 100 ohm, 0 to 10 mA a gun, linear; MM12 0.8 V black to 1.75 V white. Qualitative: on the CPC green half is "significantly higher" than red and blue half; Amstrad monitors respond weakly in red. MAME bug 8506 (fixed in 0.252) used capture and CTM644 photographs, no numbers. Conflicts: half 0.5 against 0.40; amplitude under load 0.85 to 1.1 V. Missing: Grim's per-channel half voltages, the output stage from the service manual or the KiCad project pelrun/cpc-schematics (licence unknown, not opened), the CTM644 input stage and gamma, the modulator. Verdict: a per-channel 3-level remap (0, about 0.40, 1.0) with a cross-channel term from the resistor network once the schematic is read; the green screen luminance index is buildable now.

**ZX Spectrum: partial, weak.** No per-colour Y, U, V levels. Wikipedia (CC BY-SA, quoting a zxdesign.info blog, not verified): the real colours are "currently unknown"; non-bright 0.55 V, bright 0.65 V (84.6 percent), "indicative only". 48K ULA load resistors (retrotechwiki, licence unknown): R47 on Y 220 ohm (Issue 2, 5C112) or 1k (6C001); R56 and R63 on U and V 220, 470 or 1k; LM1889 test points: pin 3 at 4.8 V, 500 mV peak to peak sine, subcarrier 4.433619 MHz. 128K RGB socket: composite 1.2 V peak to peak, 68 ohm in series on each line; one author's cable measured normal 0.44 V against a 0.7 V design target (0.63), cable-dependent. +2A and +3: BRIGHT mixed by 150 ohm and diodes, 330 ohm cable: bright 0.68 V, normal 0.46 V (ratio 0.68, schematic-computed). Emulator values 192 (FUSE), 205, 215 or 216 (85 percent), 224; none measured. Conflict: normal to bright 0.85 (48K) against 0.63 to 0.68 (128K and +3 through cables), different circuits. Missing: Chris Smith's book "The ZX Spectrum ULA" (the primary source, unread), per-colour U and V, burst, any vectorscope or colorimeter reading. Verdict: the RGB models (128K, +2A, +3) are computable from the schematic with the load as a parameter; the 48K PAL decode cannot be defended.

**MSX2 V9938 and V9958: partial; the digital side is documented.** The mapping "0, 4, 9, 13, 18, 22, 27, 31" does exist: floor(4.5 n) = (n << 2) | (n >> 1), 3-bit palette to 5-bit DAC code on the V9958 (Grauw, map.grauw.nl "The YJK screen modes", no licence stated, no derivation given; at most 1.4 percent from n/7). YJK equations (Yamaha V9958 data book, 1988, transcription on unpkg): R = Y + J, G = Y + K, B = 5/4 Y - J/2 - K/4; Y 5 bits, J and K 6-bit two's complement; YAE: attribute bit, Y 4 bits, a palette colour when A = 1. Rounding and clamp are Grauw's inference: B = floor((5Y - 2J - K + 2)/4), each channel clamped to 0..31 (19268 YJK colours, 12499 with YAE). V9938 data book (ASCII, 1985): "linear RGB video output", colour on composite needs an external encoder; burst registers R#20 to R#22 reset to 00h, 3Bh, 05h. Missing: the analogue output levels (the "R.G.B. Output Level" tables are on scanned pages), measurements on real machines, the per-machine encoder, composite constants. Verdict: a linear DAC (n/7 for the V9938, code/31 for the V9958) and the YJK decode are buildable, labelled datasheet plus inference; no voltages.

**Apple II: partial, logic-derived only.** Robert Munafo (mrob.com xapple2, CC BY-NC 4.0, so numbers with attribution only): burst at 180 degrees, 228 subcarrier cycles a line (no line-to-line alternation), pixels as square waves on the 14.31818 MHz clock so phases lie on a 45-degree grid; lo-res (phase, chroma, luma): dark 1, 2, 4, 8 at 90, 0, 270, 180 degrees, 60, 25; mid 3, 6, 9, 12 at 45, 315, 135, 225, 100, 50; light 7, 11, 13, 14 at 0, 90, 180, 270, 60, 75; greys 5 and 10 chroma 0 luma 50; hi-res violet, blue, orange, green equal 3, 6, 9, 12, about 12 degrees off the NTSC I and Q axes. Linards Ticmanis (comp.sys.apple2, 2005 and 2007): Fourier derivation, luma 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, chroma mid 1/(2 pi), dark and light 1/(2 sqrt2 pi), ratio 0.707; he later said he was "no longer convinced" (the real output is not an ideal square wave, hue is trimmed by a capacitor). Scope readings (AppleFritter 2024, hobbyist, text only): level set by a 200 ohm pot, 0.9 V on one II+ and 1.5 to 1.64 V on a rev 3 board; sync, black, white and burst only in unread images. Sams Computer Facts IIe: 2.5 V at the mixer transistor base. Conflicts: dark and light chroma 0.60 against 0.707; the hue grid against exact I and Q axes; output 0.9 to 1.64 V (a pot). Missing: output-stage resistors (Gayler's circuit description, over 10 MB, unread), measured levels into 75 ohm, rise times, colorimeter data on any Apple monitor. Verdict: signal-driven with a bit-pattern decode (rebuild the 14.318 MHz waveform, demodulate with stated filters); a palette remap is defensible only for lo-res flat areas; logic-derived, not measured.

### Group 2: Game Boy (DMG), Game Boy Pocket, Virtual Boy, WonderSwan, WonderSwan Color

None of the five has an instrument measurement (colorimeter, spectrophotometer, reflectance) that the agents could find, in English or Japanese. All are convention-grade; a palette target would be labelled "emulator convention", never "measured".

**Game Boy (DMG-01): partial, convention only.** BGB "reality" is the only documented method (bgb.bircd.org/reality; photographs under colour-accurate light, ColorChecker correction to sRGB gamma 2.2, 1/60 s shutter, defocused so patches average, several photos averaged, LCD-off colour included; camera, unit, revision and contrast setting not stated), but its numbers exist only as PNG swatches (`dmg-reality-colorscheme.png`, `mgb-reality-colorscheme.png`) that the agent could not read; licence none stated. Palettes, darkest to lightest: BGB default (via Lospec, origin not given) 081820, 346856, 88C070, E0F8D0; SameBoy (`Core/display.c`, MIT as recalled, no origin comment) 081810, 396139, 84A563, C6DE8C with LCD-off D2E6A6; Gambatte-libretro (GPLv2, so numbers cited only, table not copied; no method) lightest to darkest 578200, 317400, 005121, 00420C; folk palette 0F380F, 306230, 8BAC0F, 9BBC0F (no primary source); Dwedit on NESdev AFCB46, 79AA6D, 226F5F, 082955 (lightest to darkest, no derivation). Contrast circuit (qualitative, chipmusic forum): a rail of about -19 V divided into V1 to V5 by an IR3E02 and shifted by the contrast wheel; anecdotal multimeter readings -11 to -14 V on the wheel; no published voltage-to-luminance curve; BGB adds that the lightest shade equals the LCD-off colour and that more contrast pushes the darkest shade below its normal value. gbhwdb lists LCD boards DMG-LCD-01, 02, 03, 05, 06 and panel types A1, A2, A3, B, C, D (1989 to 1997), no optical data. Conflicts: hue from olive-yellow (Gambatte, folk) to blue-green (BGB default, Dwedit); lightest-shade luminance from very dark (Gambatte) to near white (BGB default). Missing: the BGB reality numbers (read the PNGs, or ask the author), any instrument reading, the contrast response, per-revision panels. Verdict: a 4-shade palette plus an LCD-off colour with a contrast parameter; SameBoy is the licence-safest source.

**Game Boy Pocket (MGB-001) and Light (MGB-101): partial, convention only.** Lightest to darkest. SameBoy (MIT) Pocket C2CE93, 818D66, 3A4C3A, 07100E, LCD-off CFDAAC; Light 7FE2C3, 56B495, 357862, 0A1C15, off 91EAD0. Gambatte-libretro (GPLv2, numbers only) Pocket A7B19A, 86927C, 535F49, 2A3325; Light 01CBDF, 01B6D5, 269BAD, 00778D. mGBA (MPL 2.0, `overrides.c`, 15-bit) Pocket 0x52D4, 0x4270, 0x2989, 0x10A3 (decoded by the agent as BGR555, channel order unconfirmed: (20,22,20), (16,19,16), (9,12,10), (3,5,4)); Light 0x7FCF, 0x738B, 0x56C3, 0x39E0. Nintendo Switch Online look (Lospec, Retronika, extraction method unstated) B5C69C, 8D9C7B, 637251, 303820. Lospec "PocketGB" (photographs of a working unit, no camera or lighting detail) AEA691, 887B6A, 605444, 4E3F2A. Idealised greys without source: FFFFFF, A9A9A9, 545454, 000000. No numeric response time, contrast ratio or reflectance; forum claims (unsourced) that the Pocket is FSTN, film-compensated, and the DMG plain STN, with faster response. Light backlight: described as blue-green or teal, no spectrum or chromaticity. Conflicts: Pocket white from olive-green (SameBoy, NSO) through grey-green (Gambatte, mGBA) to warm beige (the photo) and neutral grey; Pocket black from 07100E to 4E3F2A; Light mint (SameBoy) against saturated cyan (Gambatte, mGBA). Verdict: a 4-shade palette plus an optional LCD-off colour, "emulator convention (SameBoy, MIT)" or "NSO look"; nothing measured.

**Virtual Boy: partial; the level arithmetic is documented, the light is not.** LED chromaticity or wavelength: nothing found (no nm, part number, spectrum or calibrated photograph); sources say only "monochromatic red", Reflection Technology, 1 x 224 LED array. Level arithmetic (forum posts quoting Nintendo's development manual; virtual-boy.com, Guy Perfect, licence unknown): brightness registers are LED emission periods in units of 50 ns, the column table in units of 200 ns, brightness "directly proportional to how long the LED stays active"; the three reds are BRTA, BRTB and BRTA+BRTB+BRTC, so the ratios are each game's choice (one homebrew sets BRTA=2, BRTB=4, BRTC=2, repeat 16: ratios 2 : 4 : 8, which is 25, 50, 100 percent, a programme's choice, not a hardware constant); constraint (REPEAT+1) x (BRTA+BRTB+BRTC+REST+5) x 50 ns below the column period, shortest standard period 12.2 us (the forum's own numbers do not reconcile: 13 us worst case); effective maximum around 0x80 on hardware (two users). Luminance: nothing for the Virtual Boy; the related Private Eye is "2 fL nominal" (about 6.9 cd/m2, a search snippet of a PDF that could not be read; not the Virtual Boy). Mednafen (GPL-2, numbers only): `vb.ledonscale` 1.0 to 2.0, default 1.75, "LED on duration to linear RGB conversion coefficient", 1.0 "close to ideal" but dark, higher crushes white; default colour 0xF0F0F0, anaglyph 0xFFBA00 and 0x00BAFF; no origin. Not read: Sacred Tech Scroll (404), the Nintendo development manual, the Red Viper source, patent US5682171. Verdict: a per-level linear-light scale from the register values (on-time proportional to light, clamped near 0x80, times the column repeat) on one red primary whose chromaticity would be an assumption, flagged; absolute nits and the real game register values missing.

**WonderSwan (mono, WS-001): partial.** WSdev wiki (CC0, ws.nesdev.org/wiki/Display/Palette): mono palettes at ports $20 to $3F, four 3-bit entries pointing into an 8-entry shade LUT at ports $1C to $1F (two 4-bit nibbles a port); "shade 0 is the brightest, shade 15 the darkest"; mono model FSTN, "approximately linear gamma (~1.0)", "very similar to the Game Boy Pocket"; WonderSwan Color CSTN, not linear in mono mode (shade 0 = #FFF, 1 = #EEE, 15 = #000); SwanCrystal TFT, gamma "close to 2.2", "very similar to the Game Boy Color"; "there is no canon palette for the system". ares (ISC, `ares/ws/ppu/color.cpp`, commit 58af8cb by asie, 2025-02-16, "First pass"): mono per channel min + (c/15)^(1/2.2) x (max - min), endpoints R 0x2B to 0xE0 (43 to 224), G 0x2B to 0xDB (43 to 219), B 0x26 to 0xCD (38 to 205), comment "similar characteristics to the GBP", no measurement cited. Mesen2 (GPLv3, numbers only): the same, crediting asie's ares work, so not independent. Mednafen (GPLv2): neutral grey (15 - shade) x 17, no tint. near.sh (2019): "no good filters" for the WonderSwan, contrast controls and lighting mean "no one true colour". Conflicts: Mednafen's ramp is linear in the encoded value, ares, Mesen2 and the wiki are linear in light (the agent's reading of the 1/2.2 exponent); endpoints exist only in ares; 16 shades (wiki) against 8 (press). Missing: any measured white and black point, contrast ratio, the dial's effect, response time. Verdict: a 16-shade curve with reflectance linear in the shade index plus two tint endpoints (paper white, black floor), attributed to the wiki (CC0) and ares (ISC) as a derivation by Game Boy Pocket analogy.

**WonderSwan Color and SwanCrystal: no defensible measured target.** ares (ISC): SwanCrystal mixing on 4-bit channels R = 26r + 4g + 2b, G = 24g + 8b, B = 6r + 4g + 22b, clamp 480, normalised to 16 bits, no gamma step, comment "the SC's display has similar characteristics to the GBC" (no evidence). near.sh gives the same coefficients ("I do not know who devised this algorithm") and says the WSC has a software contrast flag "not known how to emulate". Mesen2 (GPL-3.0): the ares matrix halved (13/2/1, 0/12/4, 3/2/11). RetroArch slang-shaders have no WonderSwan colour file in `handheld/shaders/color`. LCDs: WSC reflective FSTN passive matrix with a contrast dial, dark, ghosting; SwanCrystal reflective TFT, no front light, no dial, grey when off (PC Watch 2002); TFT ports $70 to $77 are loaded from EEPROM, function unknown. Conflict: ares justifies the matrix with the SwanCrystal TFT but (as far as the agent could tell) applies it to the WSC's different panel. Missing: primaries and white, per-channel EOTF, black level and contrast under a stated illuminant, the dial and the flag. Verdict: a matrix plus gamma is the right kind with no data to fill it; the only option is the ares and Mesen2 GBC-borrowed matrix labelled unmeasured.

### Group 3: PC Engine, Atari Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, Game Gear, Atari 7800

**PC Engine / TurboGrafx-16 (HuC6260): partial; strong for composite, none for RGB.** furrtek's YUVDUMP gist (no licence stated) reads the HuC6260's RGB-to-YUV ROM on a real console through the test pins (pin 58 grounded: palette-RAM reads return the ROM; pin 59 selects the byte; pins confirmed at pce.nesdev.org/wiki/VCE_pinout): 512 entries, index GGGRRRBBB, value 0UUUUUVVVVVYYYYY (5 bits each); no table in the gist. The table itself is in Kitrinx/TG16_Palette `bfbii.h` as `pbpry_int_lut[512][3]` (the column order U, V, Y is the agent's reading); samples: black {16,15,0}, blue steps 1 to 7 {18,15,0} {20,15,1} {22,15,1} {24,15,2} {26,15,2} {28,15,3} {30,15,3}, R=1 {14,18,1}, white {16,15,31}. Licence is mixed: `tg16color.c` has an MIT header (Jamie Blanks / Kitrinx, 2020), `color_lib.h` copyright only, `bfbii.h` no header, GitHub reports none; to be treated as hardware facts with attribution, not GPL (the MiSTer core that ships the palette is GPL: do not copy from it). Decode method (Kitrinx, fitted to Artemio's analogue vectorscope plots of the 240p Test Suite bars, instrument unnamed): Y/31; chroma is a 1's complement code (0 to 15 = -15 to -0, 16 to 31 = 0 to +15) over 15, times Umax 0.436 and Vmax 0.615 x 0.96; hue rotation -10.4 degrees ("based on a DUO-R belonging to Artemio", varies per system); Rec.601 matrix; desaturation 192/255; no gamma. No measured RGB voltages per level (the raw VCE RGB has a large DC offset and needs an amplifier; no numbers), no composite levels, burst or IRE (Bernard Bygott's scope captures are mentioned by RetroRGB with no numbers). Conflicts: a pure LUT palette is reported "clearly incorrect" and the MiSTer palette leans on vectorscope tuning; the V gain is hand-reduced and the phase varies per console; Kitrinx's comment says 14/15 but the code uses 0.96. Missing: the full table (24 of 512 entries seen through a summariser: read `bfbii.h` directly or re-dump), the analogue Y, U, V DAC voltages, burst, RGB step voltages, SuperGrafx differences. Verdict: a 512-entry lookup (9-bit RGB to 5-bit Y, U, V) with a parametric YUV decode whose phase and saturation are exposed and marked fitted; RGB output only plain linear 3-bit scaling.

**Atari Lynx: no.** No instrument or photographic-method data for Lynx I or II. Handy and libretro-handy `lynx/mikie.cpp` (zlib-style licence): each 4-bit channel duplicated into both nibbles (x17), no gamma or matrix; core options only an "LCD Ghosting Filter" (2, 3 or 4 frame blend). Mednafen (GPL-2): no colour setting, accepts a custom `lynx.pal` (4096 triplets), ships none. Gearlynx (GPL-3) and the MiSTer core (GPL-2): no colour option, only frame blend. No Lynx file in the libretro slang-shaders `handheld/shaders/color` listing (maybe truncated). Panel: 160 x 102, RGB triads, three 160-triad columns, 105 lines a frame (Osman Celimli's notes at atarilynxvault.com, CC BY-NC-SA, reproduced with permission); Lynx I backlight a cold-cathode tube (forum posts); Lynx II backlight type not confirmed (the "electroluminescent" in our brief is unverified); panel maker and technology not found. Palette registers (Epyx spec, App. 3, monlynx.de): GREEN0-F at FDA0 to FDAF (bits 3-0 green), BLUERED0-F at FDB0 to FDBF (bits 7-4 blue, bits 3-0 red); the hardware notes say three 4-bit latches per pixel go to the panel, so the level-to-light curve is inside the LCD module, not a Mikey DAC (conflict with our brief). One AtariAge report that a naive 24-bit to 12-bit conversion looked pinkish on hardware, unmeasured. Verdict: identity (v/15 per channel) only; a real target needs primaries, white, a 16-level tone curve, per model.

**Neo Geo Pocket Color: no.** No colorimeter data (the libretro "Real GBA and DS-Phat colors" thread covers GBA SP-101, DS, GB Micro, PSP, GBC only), no photo calibration, no emulator with NGPC-specific numbers. ares (`ares/ngp/kge/color.cpp`): 4 bits per channel scaled linearly to 16 bits; mono NGP 3 bits inverted (7 - l) as equal RGB. near.sh (2019): "we don't have good filters for approximating the colors of the WonderSwan and Neo Geo Pocket". Mednafen NGP: no colour, gamma or LCD setting (only blur and a CRT shader). Beetle NeoPop and RACE (GPLv2): no colour options (a Recalbox table lists a generic RACE "Dark Filter Level"). No NGP file in slang-shaders. Panel: 2.6-inch reflective TFT, 160 x 152, no backlight, 146 of 4096 colours (Wikipedia-derived; one mirror says 2.7 inch); palette format 0x0BGR (NgpCraft dev reference, licence not stated). Verdict: identity (x17, sRGB assumed) labelled "emulator convention, unmeasured"; the mono NGP is 8 inverted greys; any matrix or gamma would be invented.

**Sega Game Gear: no.** The Handheld Color Space Project (instrumented only) does not list the Game Gear. Genesis Plus GX (custom non-commercial source licence, use numbers only): CRAM 12-bit BBBBGGGGRRRR, each nibble duplicated (n x 17), no correction; the "LCD Ghosting filter" (`genesis_plus_gx_lcd_filter`) is off by default and sets `config.lcd = 0.80 x 256 = 204` (origin undocumented, blend formula unconfirmed). MAME: an "LCD Persistence Hack", on by default for `gamegear` (`sega/sms.cpp`, mametesters 8648; no ratio given). Mednafen (GPL-2): SMS Plus based, accepts a custom `gg.pal`, ships none. No Game Gear shader in slang-shaders. LCD hardware: fluorescent-tube backlight draining 6 AA cells in 3 to 5 hours (Sega Retro, snippet); a RetroSix snippet gives drive pulses "around 8 to 18 V" and VD2 "1.73 V to 2.74 V" (unverified); panel type (STN or TFT) unconfirmed; a generic STN response of 200 to 300 ms is not Game Gear data. No photo calibration; only qualitative (dim, slow, low contrast, viewing-angle dependent). Verdict: identity (n x 17), optionally a persistence stage labelled as a convention (Genesis Plus GX 0.80, attributed); a real target (matrix, tone curve, black level, persistence) needs our own measurements on a recapped unit.

**Atari 7800 (MARIA): partial, parametric.** No instrument measurement. Atari 7800 wiki "RGB" page (anonymous, largely based on a Stella-list discussion, licence unknown, schematic-computed, unverified): SYNC 8.2k, LUM3 10k, LUM2 20k, LUM1 39k, LUM0 82k, pins sink current; NTSC Y = (COLU and 15) x 0.0526 (first attempt 0.0444, brightness -0.081), saturation 40 IRE / 185 IRE = 0.2162 (zero for hue 0), hue = 180 - (hue - 1) x 25.714 degrees; PAL contrast 0.044, burst 42.86 IRE / 200 = 0.2143; the "ideal" step is 24 degrees; calibration usually makes hue 15 match hue 1. MAME `a7800.cpp` (BSD-3-Clause): 2012 note, palette from an "NTSC Color Generator" "based on observation of several systems across multiple displays"; settings contrast 0.05, brightness 0.00, colour 0.22, phase 25.7, colourburst 180 degrees; stored tables for 24.7 to 27.7 degrees in 0.5 steps; hue 1x is "gold" (about -33 degrees) on a CRT, greener on flat panels; the pot trims a delay line and is "extremely sensitive"; warm-up reaches about 28 degrees; clocks NTSC 14,318,180 Hz "from schematics", PAL 14,187,576 Hz "from hardware tests"; PAL a separate table with a different hue order. 7800.8bitdev.org Color Documentation (observed, licence unknown): two historic pot calibrations about 25.7 and 26.7 degrees; drift about 24.7 cold to 27.7 warm over the first 20 minutes, worst on hues $Ex and $Fx. ProSystem palette (GPL, snippets only): hand-tuned by forum users (Underball and Trebor "NTSC Final", 2008 to 2010), its PAL palette never updated. Conflicts: phase step 24, 25.7, 26.2 or 26.7, up to 27.7 to 28 warm (MAME's changelog and its active table disagree: 26.2 against 25.7); the wiki's luma is linear with 39k and 82k rounded to 40k and 80k. Missing: resistor values checked against the schematic (saundby.com images unreadable), load and buffer stage, real voltages, measured chroma to burst ratio, the absolute hue-1 angle, PAL numbers. Verdict: a parametric composite-encode target (4-bit ladder luma, constant chroma equal to the burst, hue = 180 - (h - 1) x step with the step exposed, 24.7 to 27.7, default 25.7 or 26.2), attributed to the wiki and the MAME notes; the same model as the 2600 (the 7800 and the TIA share the scheme), not a fixed table.

### Group 4: Amiga OCS, ECS, AGA, Atari ST, Atari STE

The earlier "no data at all" was wrong for all five: the DACs are documented by component values (a design-level linear ramp, not measured voltages). Nobody has published per-step scope readings for any of them.

**Amiga OCS: partial.** The RGB DAC is not inside Denise: Denise outputs 12 digital lines to the "Video Hybrid" (VIDIOT, Commodore 390229-01, -02, -03), a binary-weighted resistor DAC per channel followed by one NPN stage with a 75 ohm output. Per channel 1k, 2k, 4k, 8k (weights 8 : 4 : 2 : 1) plus 470, 390 and 75 ohm around the transistor; the same in all three revisions, only the sync and mono-composite sections differ in -03 (github.com/SukkoPera/OpenAmigaVideoHybrid, README table; reverse-engineered values, no voltages; "Open Hardware", no licence named: numbers with attribution). The topology of the 470, 390 and 75 ohm parts was not read, so no levels were computed. Measured: black level "around 500 mV", sync 4.8 V peak to peak behind 47 ohm, on an Amiga RGB output (ianstedman.co.uk, an A600 scope reading, method not stated, no white level). Monitor side: 0.7 V peak to peak, 75 ohm RGB and 1 V CVBS (the 2080 SCART pinout, a CU Amiga CD copy, secondary); the 1084S has a 14-inch slot mask, 0.42 mm pitch, no published gamma or primaries (John Novak's blog uses a Philips-tube shader profile, white "closer to 6100K", calibrated by eye). Composite: the A500 and A2000 composite out is monochrome 16-level grey (Technical Reference Manual); colour needs the A520 (A1000 has colour composite); the encoder is the Motorola MC1377 (A1000 U7A, A520 IC1): 10k inputs, 1.0 V peak to peak for 100 percent saturation, 50 ohm output (amiga-stuff.com, datasheet summary); burst, luma and chroma levels not obtained. Emulators: n x 17 only. Conflict: the 500 mV black against the nominal 0.7 V swing (pedestal or AC-coupled swing unknown). Verdict: a linear 16-step ramp (n/15) is defensible from the 8 : 4 : 2 : 1 weights; real deviation (driver resistance, transistor stage) unquantified; composite only a generic MC1377 encode, A1000 and A520 only.

**Amiga ECS: partial; "ECS = OCS".** The same hybrid 390229 is used in the A500, A500+, A1000, A2000 and A3000 (the A3000 carries two, one on the 15 kHz Denise output and one on the 31 kHz Amber output, so the 31 kHz path is the same network, not a VGA RAMDAC; no voltage figure). Super Denise changes modes, not levels (the agent's inference from the shared DAC). A600: no hybrid; an EAB summary says buffers feed binary-weighted resistors and a summing amplifier, and the Sony CXA1145P (U12) supplies the 23-pin RGB (unverified); a retrosix snippet mentions "1 V signals on about 2 V DC offset". CXA1145 composite constants (a snippet of the Sony datasheet, second-hand; Sony copyright): burst 0.26, 0.29, 0.32 V peak to peak (min, typ, max), R/burst ratio 2.84, 3.16, 3.48, B/burst 2.01 to 2.47, B phase 337, 347, 357 degrees, PAL burst ratio 0.9 to 1.1. Emulators: vAmiga (MPL-2.0, `PixelEngine.cpp`) treats OCS and ECS the same, the nibble in the upper half (x16, white 240), optional adjustments linearise with gamma 2.8 and re-encode with 2.2; WinUAE 1.1 changelog (snippet) "OCS/ECS color translation to native colors fixed... slightly brighter", nibble duplication (x17) per an EAB snippet. Conflicts: x16 against x17 (scale only); the A600 RGB route. Missing: any measured voltage per step, the loaded white level, an A3000 31 kHz level, the 8373 DC characteristics, A600 schematic values, whether BRDRBLNK or genlock change levels. Verdict: the same 16-step linear ramp as OCS, no matrix; the A600 composite can use the CXA1145 constants once read from the datasheet itself.

**Amiga AGA: partial.** The DAC is external to Lisa: Lisa outputs 24-bit digital RGB and needs "an industry standard, 30 MHz Triple 8-Bit Video DAC", the A1200 spec names a Bt101 or equivalent (A1200 Functional Spec rev 1.6, files.haxor.fi, a search snippet, the fetch failed). Parts: A1200 U30 ADV101 or VP101, CD32 U30 and A4000T U460 ADV101, A4000 U460 ADV7120KP30; VREF from an LM385, FS ADJUST to ground via R217 (A4000 R509), REF WHITE grounded; the A1200 wiring implies sync on green, the CD32 none (the agent's inference); no resistor values (amiga-stuff.com adv101 and adv7120 pages, schematic transcription, licence unknown). ADV7120 datasheet (Rev. B, read through a reseller mirror with garbled OCR; Analog Devices copyright): current-steering, IOR and IOB (mA) = 8,628 x VREF / RSET, IOG = 12,082 x VREF / RSET, ISYNC = 3,455 x VREF / RSET; at VREF 1.235 V, RSET 560 ohm, RL 37.5 ohm: red and blue white 19.05 mA / 0.714 V, black (00h) 1.44 mA / 0.054 V (a 7.5 IRE pedestal), blank 0; green with sync white 26.67 mA / 1.000 V, black 9.05 mA / 0.340 V, blank 7.62 mA / 0.286 V, sync 0; white relative to black 17.62 mA typical. Composite: Sony CXA1145 (CXA1145M at U12 per an AmigaKit note) in the A600, A1200 and CD32 (a weak article source). Emulators: FS-UAE and libretro-uae (GPL, cite only) have per-channel AGA lookup tables and a `direct_rgb` flag, no stated gamma. Conflicts: which DAC is in which model (ADV101, VP101, ADV7120: pin-equivalent, datasheets of the first two unread); the 500 mV A600 black against the datasheet's 0.054 V (a different machine). Verdict: a per-channel linear 8-bit code to voltage, V = 0.054 + (code/255) x 0.660 V into 75 ohm if the Amiga uses RSET near 560 ohm and BLANK is driven; after a monitor's black clamp that is the identity scaling emulators already use. Missing: the Amiga-side resistor values, INL and DNL, any measurement, all composite numbers.

**Atari ST: partial; a linear ladder by design.** The 520ST/1040ST service manual (schematicsforfree.com, OCR text layer; Atari copyright, facts only): "The value of the resistor at R0 is twice that of R1, which is twice that of R2. This allows us to get 8 equal steps on the summed outputs"; one emitter follower per colour (Q3 blue, Q4 green, Q5 red); the scope check is "a staircase pattern (eight levels)". Monitor port: "R,G,B: analog 0-1.0V P-P, 75 ohm", monochrome "digital 1.0V P-P, 75 ohm", syncs TTL negative, 3.3k; so full scale is 1.0 V nominal, not 0.7 V (info-coach.fr: 1 V peak to peak, 27 ohm series on the STF and 75 ohm on the STE; a SCART guide calls the levels "a little high", suggests 150 ohm in series). Ladder ohm values not established (the parts list has 1.2k, 2.4k, 4.7k, 3.6k, 3k, 12k, 27 ohm, none tied to the ladder; Atari-Forum snippets give 12k / 6.2k / 3k, possibly STE, and a "colors.32k" hack text three 3.6k from Shifter pins 21, 24, 27). SC1224 (ManualsLib service manual): 12-inch in-line gun tube, 90 degree deflection, "R.G.B Analog Negative" input, TTL sync, 15.75 kHz, 18 MHz bandwidth; no input level, gamma, chromaticities or pitch. SM124: "Phosphor: Paper White", TTL input (snippet); tubes 310KAB4 and relatives at 71.25 Hz (the P4-family reading is the agent's inference). Composite and RF: the emitter-follower outputs feed the modulator box, a PLL locks the burst (added April 1986), the STE uses an MC1377; no constants. Conflicts: 1.0 V against an assumed 0.7 V; "equal steps" against 12k / 6.2k (5 percent parts allow a few percent of step error); n x 36 against n x 255/7 is rounding. Verdict: a linear 8-step ramp (n/7, 1.0 V full scale into 75 ohm) is defensible from Atari's own text, so the transform is the identity apart from the level. Missing: the ladder values from the schematic sheet, any scope measurement, SC1224, SC1435 and SM124 chromaticities and gamma, composite levels.

**Atari STE: partial, leaning yes; the best of the five.** KiCad conversion of the STE motherboard schematic C300780-001 (github.com/sporniket/kicad-conversions--atari-ste-motherboard--c300780-001, **CC0 1.0**; net assignments were traced from file coordinates by the fetch tool, so to be checked against the PDF in the repository releases): per channel four Shifter pins feed one summing node, 12k (R3), 1k5 (R2), 3k (R1), 6k2 (R0), the same for green and blue, mono 1k; output stage a 2N3904 emitter follower per colour, 2k to ground at the node, 75 ohm on the output (1k resistors near the bases unexplained); composite an MC1377 (U403) fed from the same R, G, B, R456 2k2 on the sheet with a note giving 1k8 (PAL-I) and 2k7 (PAL-B). Levels per step, **computed by the agent** (ideal 0 V and 5 V pin drivers, a linear buffer, sum of switched conductances over the total): 0, 0.0670, 0.1296, 0.1965, 0.2678, 0.3348, 0.3974, 0.4644, 0.5356, 0.6026, 0.6652, 0.7322, 0.8035, 0.8704, 0.9330, 1 (binary-weighted, linear to about 0.4 percent, 6k2 instead of an ideal 6k). ST colours on an STE: an ST value n lands on STE level 2n: 0, 0.1296, 0.2678, 0.3974, 0.5356, 0.6652, 0.8035, 0.9330; ST white $777 is about 93 percent of $FFF (computed); corroboration: exxos forum, poster sandord, non-STE programs "a bit less bright (1/16th)" and "an average of 2.88 V" on a stock STE (measurement point unstated). info-coach.fr: 75 ohm series on the STE against 27 ohm on the STF, "Output Level 1 Volt Peak to Peak", 15.75 kHz; bit order "0 3 2 1" encoding "1 8 4 2" (alive.atari.org). Conflicts: an Atari-Forum snippet gives the STE as 12k, 9k, 1.5k and the STFM as 3.9k, 2k, 1k (disagrees with the schematic); a GrafX2 snippet maps ST levels to STE 0, 2, 4, 6, 9, 11, 13, 15 (a tool convention; the hardware gives 0, 2, ..., 14). Missing: scope readings per step, the Shifter pin drive levels, the exact bias topology (so no absolute volts), SC1224 and SC1435 data, the MC1377 detail, the TT030 and Mega STE. Verdict: a per-channel 16-entry DAC curve from the resistor values, nearly identical to n/15; the defensible difference from emulators is that ST-only software peaks at 14/15.

### What the third pass changes

- Of the 20 platforms researched, none gained an instrument measurement that the agents could read. By what they gained:
  - **Documented component values or a documented decode, buildable as a labelled target:** Atari STE (CC0 schematic, ladder computed), Atari ST, Amiga OCS, ECS and AGA (linear ramps by design, voltages missing), PC Engine (silicon ROM and a fitted decode), Amstrad CPC (half level 0.40, green-screen weights), MSX2 (V9958 mapping and YJK decode).
  - **Parametric composite model with an exposed unknown (the phase step):** Atari 2600, Atari 7800; Apple II (logic-derived, bit-pattern decode); ZX Spectrum (RGB models of the 128K, +2A and +3 only).
  - **Convention-only palettes, nothing measured:** Game Boy, Game Boy Pocket, WonderSwan (16-shade curve by Pocket analogy), Virtual Boy (documented register arithmetic, LED hue unknown).
  - **Nothing defensible, identity only:** WonderSwan Color, Atari Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, Game Gear.
- The two blocked-source classes that recur: scanned PDFs without a text layer (no renderer is installed here) and sites that refuse automated fetches (the Wayback Machine, AtariAge, Atari-Forum, EAB, consolemods, the CPCWiki main site, retrosix). The owner can read these in a browser; the list is in the TODO.

## Fourth research pass (2026-11-26): the seven "partial data" platforms, two agents each

Method (owner request): seven platforms (ZX Spectrum, Apple II, Game Boy, Game Boy Pocket and Light, Virtual Boy, WonderSwan, WonderSwan Color), two agents each, one Opus at high effort and one Fable at medium effort, 15 minutes, given what the third pass found and what was missing; no new platform added. **Process failure, to know before trusting the result:** the session's web-search budget (200 calls, shared by every agent) was spent by the 14 agents running at once. Most agents ran zero to four searches and worked by fetching known addresses only, so the community angle (Reddit, forums, repair and mod sites, social posts, Japanese blogs) was **not searched** for any of the seven. What follows is a primary-document pass, not a complete one. One agent also reported that a fetched page (a blog) carried text addressed to the reader asking it to change its standing instructions; it ignored it, and it is not a finding.

### Game Boy (DMG-01): the BGB "reality" values are now numeric

- **BGB "reality" swatches, read exactly** (one agent read the palette chunk of the four-bit PNG files; nothing was colour-picked). DMG (`dmg-reality-colorscheme.png`), left to right: 948A04, 759833, 588F51, 3B7560, 2E615A (background 404040). MGB (`mgb-reality-colorscheme.png`): C2CFA8, ADBF92, 96A67C, 727E64, 5A635C. **The slot order is an inference** (the page speaks of 4 shades and an "LCD disabled" colour, five swatches, so leftmost = LCD off, then shade 0 to 3; the images carry no labels). Method (BGB page, licence unknown, BGB is closed freeware): ColorChecker-corrected photographs under colour-accurate light, sRGB gamma 2.2, 1/60 s shutter, defocused, several photos averaged; camera, unit, revision and contrast setting not stated; the BGB manual says only the GBC "reality" was "measured from a real GBC". Not an instrument measurement. Relative luminance computed from the DMG values: about 0.245 (LCD off), 0.265 (shade 0), 0.098 (shade 3), so lightest to darkest is only about 2.7 : 1, and LCD-off and shade 0 differ in hue (yellow-olive against green) rather than brightness. BGB models the contrast wheel: the shades are points on one continuous gradient, at lower contrast the darkest shade goes below its normal value; the MGB panel responds "a bit faster" and has thinner pixel-matrix edges (key `MgbMatrixIntensity`); no numbers for either.
- **SameBoy's DMG palette is not a measurement:** the author (issue #269, 2020-06-21) calls it "not faithful, but it's the same palette used by the SDL GUI and the CGB boot ROM". Its MGB and Light sets came with commit 163a5ea (2020-01-29, "Add DMG color palettes (Cocoa)", menu names Lime, Olive, Teal), no derivation. The issue's reporter offered DMG, Light and Pocket palettes from "over five years" of replication (images only); the maintainer "liked the GBP one".
- **Gambatte's DMG palette** (GPLv2, numbers only) probably comes from that reporter, who wrote that their palettes were built from "2 busted DMG Game Boys that have unhealthy screens… a lot of guesses and estimates" (the link is the agent's inference from the dates; the file credits no author); the values may be inputs to companion shaders, not screen colours.
- Other: mGBA's default is plain grey (0x7FFF, 0x56B5, 0x294A, 0x0000); the Switch Online DMG look (Lospec, Retronika, extraction method unstated) 8CAD28, 6C9421, 426B29, 214231; HerrZatacke gb-palettes (MIT) holds the BGB default with an empty origin field. The Handheld-Color-Space-Project has no DMG, MGB or Pocket data (checked again; its tree covers GBC, GBA, GBA SP, Micro, DS, 3DS, Switch, PSP). Not found: any instrument measurement, the contrast-dial curve, revision differences, defaults of Emulicious, Mesen2 and Gearboy.
- Verdict: a photo-derived 4-shade target with the LCD-off colour as the background and pixel-gap colour (BGB reality), SameBoy and Switch Online as stylised alternatives; the dial only as a slide along one gradient. Ask BGB's author before shipping the numbers (licence unknown).

### Game Boy Pocket (MGB-001) and Light (MGB-101): where each emulator's values come from

- **Gambatte (GPLv2, numbers only), a documented history** in `gbcpalettes.h`: 2018 (jdgleaver, 738bca5) Pocket C6CFA5, 8C966B, 4A5139, 181C18, Light 00B581, 009A71, 00694A, 004F3B; 2020-06-25 (RokkumanX, 8bfe76b, PR 152, "Authentic palettes derived from original hardware screens (pt. 2)") Pocket 949B8B, 7C8870, 525F4B, 394038, Light 01CBDF, 01B6D5, 269BAD, 00778D (the Light value still current); 2020-08-17 (jdgleaver, 8911bba, PR 156) Pocket A7B19A, 86927C, 535F49, 2A3325 (current). Method (PR 156): a real Pocket held under fluorescent light against the monitor, RGB raised step by step until it matched a reference photo, brightness set for "a uniformly well-lit screen"; the earlier palette had the wrong hue and reduced contrast. Eye-matched, not measured.
- **mGBA** (MPL-2.0, `overrides.c`, commit a713d51, 2021-05-01, "GB: Presets for Game Boy palettes", no derivation): the channel order is settled from `image.h` (red in the low five bits, the 5-to-8-bit shift without high-bit replication): Pocket 0x52D4, 0x4270, 0x2989, 0x10A3 renders A0B0A0, 809880, 486050, 182820 (A5B5A5, 849C84, 4A6352, 182921 with full expansion), a neutral grey with a slight green cast; Light 0x7FCF, 0x738B, 0x56C3, 0x39E0 renders 78F0F8, 58E0E0, 18B0A8, 007870 (cyan).
- **HerrZatacke gb-palettes (MIT):** Pocket C4CFA1, 8B956D, 4D533C, 1F1F1F (origin empty); Light 1DDECE, 19C7B3, 16A596, 0B7A6D ("by HerrZatacke", hand-made). **Analogue Pocket:** display modes 0x22 "Original GBP" and 0x23 "Original GBP Light" exist, but the core sends pure grey and the console applies the tint; the values are not published.
- Physical (Wikipedia citing EGM and Next Generation, 1996 to 1998): the Pocket is FSTN, the film gives a "true black-and-white" screen instead of the DMG's green, faster response and "mostly eliminated" ghosting (no figure); the Light is FSTN with an electroluminescent backlight "blue-green glow", 12 h with and 20 h without it. BGB has an "MGB reality" swatch (above). Conflicts: Pocket white olive (SameBoy, Switch Online, HerrZatacke) against grey-green (Gambatte, mGBA) against warm beige (Lospec photo): consistent with a reflective screen's dependence on the light and the camera white balance (the agent's inference); Light mint (SameBoy) against cyan (Gambatte, mGBA) against teal (HerrZatacke), none says backlight on or off.
- Still missing: any colorimeter data, the Light's EL spectrum, the MGB LCD datasheet, how SameBoy and mGBA derived theirs. Verdict: palette-only "community estimate"; with the BGB MGB reality values above it is now photo-derived as well; the Light a separate approximate palette, hue uncertain.

### Virtual Boy: the arithmetic confirmed, the LED still unknown

- **Patent US5682171A** (Nintendo, Yokoi, public text): grey levels from LED pulse widths of two clock pulses (T1, T2) and their total (T3), the dot data 01, 10, 11 selecting T1, T2, T3; a 200 ns down-counter period; 224 LEDs an array; luminance "defined by the pulse widths" (no statement that light is proportional to on-time); no wavelength. Reflection Technology's Private Eye patent US4934773A: LEDs about 40 um square with 20 um gaps, a "Telefunken TPMP8160", a 256-LED printing module, grey by pulse width (the Private Eye, not confirmed as the Virtual Boy part). iFixit teardown: "32 levels of intensity", red chosen as "cheapest, most efficient, and most visible"; Japanese Wikipedia "32 steps" (uncited); conflict with a register that saturates near 0x80 unresolved.
- **Sacred Tech Scroll and forum (Guy Perfect, dasi, blitter; licence unknown):** a column-table entry is Repeat (high byte) and Column Length (low byte plus 1, 200 ns units); brightness is level duration x (Repeat + 1), saturating at "about 128"; Nintendo's recommended table, "used by all commercial software", has Repeat 0 and Column Length 0xFE at the edges down to 0x3C in the centre (61 x 200 ns = 12.2 us); each eye is lit 5 ms of the 20 ms frame; games typically set C = 0 and B = 2 x A, giving 0, A, 2A, 3A ("3 shades of red at a time, plus black, out of 128 possible"); constraint (Repeat + 1) x (BRTA + BRTB + BRTC + REST + 5) x 50 ns below the column period; after DPRST and DPEN the registers read 0x03, after DPRST alone 0x01 (hardware tests, the manual says "indeterminate"). Unit conflict: the scroll as fetched says 5 ns, others say 50 ns; 50 ns fits the timing arithmetic. The cause of the 0x80 ceiling is unresolved: a VIP counter window (Mednafen) or an LED limit (Guy Perfect's hypothesis).
- **Emulators (numbers only):** Mednafen and Beetle VB (GPLv2, `vip.c`): accumulated LED on-time over Repeat + 1 cycles, each shade window clipped to a 128-unit cycle, scaled by 255/128, encoded with a 1/2.2 power (the code marks "TODO: use correct gamma"), shade 3 = BRTA + BRTB + BRTC, REST only timing; `vb.ledonscale` 1.75 (range 1.0 to 2.0, "1.0 is near ideal but dark", higher crushes white in one game) looks like a tuning value; default colour 0xF0F0F0. Red Viper (MIT plus a non-commercial notice): default tint 0x0000FF (read as pure red, the channel order is inferred), a brightness table (v/255 x 1.75)^0.9 (licence of that file unknown). No emulator cites a measured LED chromaticity.
- Still missing: the LED wavelength, chromaticity and supplier, the luminance, a measured on-time-to-light curve, per-game register values, the source of "32 levels". The agents' assumption (not from any Virtual Boy source): a 1990s GaAsP or AlGaAs red at about 630 to 660 nm. Verdict: three linear-light shades proportional to BRTA, BRTB, BRTA + BRTB + BRTC, saturating at 128, on an assumed red primary labelled as such, a user scale like Mednafen's.

### WonderSwan (mono): the numbers trace back to the Game Boy Pocket

- **ares PR 1834** (asie, merged 2025-02-16, ISC): the endpoints R 0x2B to 0xE0, G 0x2B to 0xDB, B 0x26 to 0xCD are the darkest and lightest entries of Near's "Game Boy Pocket" palette in `ares/gb/ppu/color.cpp` (E0DBCD, A89F94, 706B66, 2B2B26), reused "for consistency" because the WS FSTN gives "the same display hue"; the linear gamma 1.0 is "at the optimal contrast wheel setting" citing lidnariq (attributed, no link); asie notes games may assume other dial settings and that a second pass "would probably need photographic documentation", pointing to BGB's reality page. The formula `min + (c/15)^(1/2.2) x (max - min)` works on encoded values, which is linear luminance re-encoded (not a contradiction of the wiki's "~1.0"). So **nothing is a WonderSwan measurement**, and nothing shows Near's Pocket numbers were measured either. The WSdev "Color reproduction" section is by the same author on the same basis (all revisions asie, 2025-02-23).
- **Beetle-WonderSwan** (GPL-2.0, numbers only; commit 114218a, jdgleaver, 2020-10-25; option `wswan_mono_palette`), dark to light: WonderSwan 3E3D20 to 9B9D66; WonderSwan Color 1B201E to D7D49D; SwanCrystal 151108 to FFFCCA; Game Boy Pocket 2A3325 to A7B19A; default 000000 to FFFFFF; linear per channel over 16 steps; "mimic the display of original WonderSwan hardware", no provenance. Conflict: the paper colour E0DBCD (ares, warm grey) against 9B9D66 (Beetle, olive, much darker); neither measured.
- **Panel:** WSdev "Monochrome STN display pinout" (CC0): a 4-bit grey bus Y0 to Y3 (16 levels at the panel), drive rails about +24 V and -20 V, "amplitude contrast dependent" (the wheel changes the rails), no maker. Japanese Wikipedia (CC BY-SA): FSTN reflective, 2.49 inch, 224 x 144, "8 shades chosen from 16", STN "dark with heavy ghosting"; no contrast ratio, no maker. StoicGoose, the MiSTer core and the Analogue Pocket port have no LCD model.
- Verdict: derived and unmeasured: 16 levels linear in light between a Pocket-like white (E0DBCD) and black (2B2B26) at the best dial setting, a user contrast dial; Mono mode on the WSC and SwanCrystal separately (below). Missing: any photograph or colorimeter data of a WS-001, the panel maker, response figures, lidnariq's statement, Oswan and Cygne constants.

### WonderSwan Color and SwanCrystal

- **Origin of the matrix:** `ares/ws/ppu/color.cpp` has three commits before the mono one (Near, 2020-03-24 and 2021-02-08; asie 2025-02-16, mono only), so the coefficients date from Near's era with no derivation. The matrix is higan's Game Boy Color matrix carried over (higan GB: 26/4/2, 24/8, 6/4/22 with a clamp of 960 for 5-bit input; the WonderSwan clamp of 480 is the 4-bit version; every row sums to 32, white kept); higan (GPL) has no model branch at all. Mesen2 (GPL-3): 13/2/1, 0/12/4, 3/2/11, never divided by 16, so white is 240 of 255, for every non-mono model. **ares applies the SwanCrystal matrix to the WSC too** (confirmed from its model table: WSC on SPHINX, SwanCrystal on SPHINX2). Who devised the matrix: unknown (Near's article, as one agent read it, says there are "no good filters"; the two agents disagree on whether it gives the coefficients).
- **Panels:** SwanCrystal 2.8-inch reflective TFT made by Citizen Watch (Japanese Wikipedia, no footnote; no datasheet found), no contrast dial, less afterimage, brighter and more saturated than the WSC, grey when off (the WSC black) (PC Watch, 2002). Japanese Wikipedia says the Game Boy Color and Neo Geo Pocket panels are Sharp: a different maker, which weakens the "similar to the GBC" claim (it rests only on both being reflective TFTs). WSC: Bandai's announcement (PC Watch, 2000-08-30) "reflective FSTN colour LCD", 4,096 colours, 241 simultaneous, 224 x 144, 2.8 inch; ITmedia (2002-05-08) calls it FSTN; the WSdev wiki says CSTN (colour STN against film-compensated: probably both). WSC contrast: a physical dial plus a High/Low setting (EEPROM byte $83 bit 6 the boot default, port $14 bit 1 at run time; High drives two lines with data from the SoC, which changes the drive scheme; no optical number). WSC panel signal: RGB444 sent time-multiplexed as three 4-bit values per pixel, about 75 Hz, the driver does no colour translation ("three panels in a trenchcoat"); no vendor or grey-level data. Ports $70 to $77 (SwanCrystal TFT): "From EEPROM, SC only", TODO on the wiki; EEPROM offsets $70 to $77 hold owner data, so the TFT bytes are stored elsewhere (not found).
- Others: MiSTer WonderSwan and the Analogue Pocket port (flicker blend only), Beetle Cygne (mono palettes only), StoicGoose (generic shaders): no correction. WSdev: SwanCrystal gamma "close to 2.2", IPS mods and TV-out kits use standard video gamma (no source).
- Verdict: a heuristic only: SwanCrystal = the GBC-derived matrix plus a gamma of about 2.2 (asie's statement), labelled unmeasured; WSC = the same matrix as an explicit guess, or a darker, low-contrast, desaturated STN look with a High/Low variant. Missing: any measurement for either, the Citizen datasheet, the meaning of $70 to $77, the matrix's origin.

### Apple II: the output levels are documented, the colours still derived

- **Apple II Reference Manual (January 1978, the "Red Book", archive.org OCR, Apple copyright):** the main video jack is "NTSC compatible, EIA standard, positive composite video", a level control sets the output "to 1 Volt (peak) into an external 75 OHM load" (the lower bound is missing in the OCR), a 200 ohm trim pot sets the level, an "adjustable trimmer capacitor" gives tint range; the auxiliary video pin is a "DC coupled emitter follower", "black level is about .75 Volts, and white level is about 2.0 Volts into 470 Ohms" (the sync value is missing in the OCR). **Apple IIe Technical Reference, chapter 7, pp. 185 to 186:** a linear summing circuit around Q1 mixes video, sync and burst, R3, R5, R7, R10, R13, R15 set the amplitudes (values not in the text), the burst is conditioned by an L3/C32 tank at 3.58 MHz; Table 7-15 (J13 pin 2): white about 2.0 V, black about 0.75 V, sync 0.0 V (loaded or not not stated); the RCA jack goes through a resistor network to "about 1 volt" into 75 ohm.
- **Sather, "Understanding the Apple II", chapter 8 (archive.org OCR, copyright book):** R6, the "COLOR BURST summing resistor", is 2.7k and sets the burst relative to the picture (he suggests 4.7k to weaken it for some monitors); the burst is a 14-cycle sample; the Revision 1 colour killer Q6 leaves a small residual burst in text; the hi-res delay is exactly one 14M period (90 degrees of the colour reference); the picture flip-flop high-to-low delay is 25 ns typical, so a white dot is about 24 ns wider than a black one (a candidate physical source of a small hue offset, not stated in degrees); a typical TV's chroma amplifier band is 3.1 to 4.1 MHz. Figure 8.2's levels are garbled in every OCR (Apple II and IIe): unusable.
- **Emulator constants (numbers only):** OpenEmulator (via Zellyn Hunter's port, upstream GPLv3): 14,318,182 Hz sampling, decode coefficients 1.139883, -0.394642, -0.580622, 2.032062, luma bandwidth 2.0 MHz, chroma 600 kHz, a 17-tap Chebyshev (50 dB) with a Lanczos window, black 0, white 1, a burst phase offset of -33/360 cycle; AppleWin (GPLv2+): cites Munafo and Sather p. 3-11, cycle start 45 degrees ("'Mostly' correct _if_"), burst window cycles 12 to 16, 2-pole Butterworth with the comment "What filter is this ??", the FCC YIQ matrix, empirical saturation, brightness and hue constants of undocumented origin; MAME `apple2video.cpp` (BSD-3-Clause): the palette comes from a spreadsheet, "Apple II Video Display Theory" (could not be opened; examples dark red A70B40, purple E628FF, orange E66F00, light green 19D700, medium blue 1990FF); colorinspector (MIT) is Munafo's table rescaled. None cites a measurement. Munafo's text calls the hi-res hues both "12 degrees higher" and "12 degrees lower" than the YIQ axes; the numbers (45 against 33) say higher.
- Conflicts: dark and light chroma 0.60 (Munafo) against 0.707 (Ticmanis, "no longer convinced", assumes a perfectly rectangular output); the aux black 0.75 V (the IIe manual) against "36V" in the OCR of Sather's IIe (garbled); "1 V into 75 ohm" (the manual) against the 0.9 to 1.64 V measured on two boards (the pot range explains it). Still missing: R3 to R15 and Q1 (only R6), burst amplitude in IRE, measured chroma ratio and hue positions, rise time, vectorscope or colorimeter data, Gayler's circuit description (over the fetch limit).
- Verdict: a derived-signal target: square-wave 14M bit patterns on the 45-degree grid decoded with the burst as the phase reference (every emulator does this), levels sync 0, black 0.75, white 2.0 V (the aux pin, 470 ohm) scaled to about 1 V into 75 ohm, hue and chroma as user trims (the hue capacitor and the level pot vary per unit).

### ZX Spectrum: the original documents exist, nobody could read them

- **Amstrad patent EP0107687B1** ("Display for a computer", Richard F. Altwasser, priority 1982-04-22): the ULA circuits that turn digital R, G, B into analogue Y, U, V (figure 2 makes U, figure 3 V, figure 4 Y; in figure 4 the bright bit gates a transistor that changes the base current of the Y control transistor; burst, sync and blank are combined with R, G, B as phase digital signals); the resistor values are on the drawings, which exist only as an image PDF (patent text is public). **emu-russia/ula** (CC0-1.0, die reverse-engineering of the 6C001): the video DAC pad has 15 digital inputs (RedD, nRedDD, RedS, BurstS, nBurstS, nBurstDD, nHL for bright, nBLACKS for black) and analogue U, V, /Y outputs, so U and V are switched among a few levels; no analogue levels or transistor weights in the repository. **archive.org item `ferranti-sinclair-docs`** (licence unknown, watermarked "for educational and archival use only, strictly non-commercial"): the Ferranti ULA 6C001 specification (260882), the 5C102 specification, the 7K010 specifications and pencil notes, a TEA1002 datasheet (the index says TEA2000), the MC1376 datasheet, the 128 analogue schematic, the Issue 2 schematic, a clamp-circuit proposal letter: all image-only PDFs with no usable text layer; the likeliest place for the Y/U/V levels and the issue differences, to be read by eye.
- **48K service manual (HTML transcription, k1.spdns.de, licence unknown):** Issue 2 chroma bias relative to LM1889 pin 3: VR1 (pin 4) factory 130 +- 20 mV, optimum +50 mV, range +45 to +150 mV; VR2 (pin 2) factory 75 +- 20 mV, optimum -50 mV, range -45 to -100 mV; Issue 2 level shifters passive (VR1, VR2), Issue 3 onwards active (TR8, TR9); subcarrier 4.433619 MHz +- 50 Hz at pin 17, the 90-degree lead and lag network between pins 1 and 18; no signal amplitudes. Forum (Spectrum Computing): a composite-modded 48K "1V from the bottom of the sync pulse to the white level" (one machine); LM1889 DC readings on a faulty board (not reference values); scope photos of U, V, Y exist, images only.
