- **World of Spectrum pinouts:** the 128K composite pin is "75 Ohms, 1.2 Volts pk-pk" (confirmed), Bright, Red, Green, Blue are TTL; the +2A and +3 RGB socket gives R, G, B as "Analogue 1.67V p-p" (open circuit or into 75 ohm not stated; compatible with the 0.68 V bright of the third pass if the cable resistor divides it, unverified). **Wikipedia's levels are not a source:** "85% voltage (0.55 V)" and "100% (0.65 V)" cite zxdesign.info ddrivedesign (a clone-design blog), the page itself says the real colours are "currently unknown" and its sRGB values assume PAL gamma 2.8 and maximum saturation.
- Still missing: the per-colour Y, U, V levels, the burst amplitude and phase, the measured normal-to-bright ratio, the issue differences, TEA2000 constants, Chris Smith's numbers, the sinclairzxworld thread. Verdict: derived and low-confidence: an ideal PAL encoding of the 3-bit RGB with a normal-to-bright ratio of about 0.85 (flagged indicative), saturation a user parameter, the Issue 2 bias offsets as an optional tint tolerance; the +2A and +3 as RGB from the schematic.

### What the fourth pass changes

- **New and usable:** the BGB DMG and MGB reality colours (numbers, with the slot order inferred); the Apple II output levels (black 0.75 V, white 2.0 V, about 1 V into 75 ohm) and the burst resistor; the provenance of every Game Boy Pocket and WonderSwan palette (all eye-matched, the WonderSwan one derived from the Pocket); the Virtual Boy pulse-width model with the saturation at 128.
- **Still not measured by anyone, anywhere the agents could read:** all seven.
- **To redo:** the community angle, with the search budget restored and fewer agents at a time (TODO).

## Fifth research pass (2026-11-26): the community angle, ten platforms

Method (owner request): the search budget was restored, so the community angle that the fourth pass could not search was run again, with the budget capped: six agents in two waves of three, at most 25 searches each, one agent for the Game Boy family, one for the Virtual Boy, one for the WonderSwan family, one for the Apple II, one for the ZX Spectrum, one for the Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color and Game Gear together. What follows is what they read; the decisions (what is built, what is closed) are in the TODO.

- **Game Boy (DMG), Pocket, Light:** no instrument measurement of any kind (English, Japanese, colorimeter brands, mod-site wording, Reddit wording). New: the BGB slot order is now supported (not proven) by BGB's own images: in `dmgblend-shades.png` the five swatches run from yellow-olive (948A04) to dark teal with a gradient strip under them that starts at the same yellow-olive, the page text calls the set "4 + the LCD disabled color (top left)", and in `contrastwheel.png` (BGB-rendered frames, not photographs) the lowest-contrast frame drifts to yellow-olive and the highest goes dark blue-teal with the darkest shade falling below its normal value; BGB history: "DMG reality" in 1.6, "MGB reality" in 1.6.3. Oddity: the page says the lightest shade equals the LCD-off colour, yet 948A04 and 759833 differ in hue (their luminances are close, about 0.245 and 0.265). The libretro post on the Gambatte palettes (J.D.G. Leaver) says they are for a standard TV and must not be colour-corrected; the SameBoy maintainer found the other palettes of issue 269 "too low contrast" and liked the GBP one; the libretro DMG shader's author matched by eye and noted the real grey nearest black was darker than his and the palette "very yellow"; the Pocket's repair schematic shows a bias supply at about -18 V with the contrast pot feeding the IR3E02. Nothing on the voltage-to-luminance curve, the response time, the Light's spectrum, or a panel datasheet.
- **Virtual Boy:** the first sourced LED colour, from a manufacturer datasheet of the sibling product: "Private Eye Display, Model P4, Product Specification" (Reflection Technology Inc., Rev 2.93, 1991-04-15, page 1, read from the scan): "COLOR: Red pixels (660 +/- 20 nm wavelength) on black background", "BRIGHTNESS: 2 foot-lamberts nominal" (about 6.9 cd/m2, a conversion), "CONTRAST: 70:1 nominal", 50 Hz refresh. It is an unofficial archive of RTI documents (no licence stated, copied without the manufacturer's permission) and it is the Private Eye, not the Virtual Boy: Nintendo licensed RTI's Scanned Linear Array technology, but no source says the Virtual Boy's LED or brightness is the P4's; no chromaticity. "32 levels" comes from David Tucker's `vbprog.pdf` as quoted by Guy Perfect (Planet Virtual Boy, 2014-07-01): "Four shades of red, at one time, from a pallet of 32 shades", which Perfect calls inaccurate (effectively 128 settings, 7 bits). The Nintendo manual's "directly proportional to how long the LED stays active" is quoted second-hand (a design statement, not a measurement). Emulator conventions with an origin: RunnerPack's "RGB Hex Codes" thread (Planet VB, 2015) #000000, #550000, #AA0000, #FF0000 (the 2-bit value repeated, not sampled), Guy Perfect rgb(round(i x 255/127), 0, 0) for brightness 0 to 127. No chromaticity, no Virtual-Boy-specific luminance, no commercial BRT values.
- **WonderSwan family:** nothing measured. The lidnariq statement ("with the optimal contrast wheel setting, they follow a linear gamma of 1.0") exists only second-hand in the ares PR 1834 comment by asiekierka (no link; the PR has no photographs or numbers; "not every game expects the same contrast wheel setting"; a second pass "would require photographic documentation"); WSdev Display/Palette (CC0, no sources): a WSC in mono-compatibility mode maps shade 0 to #FFF, shade 1 to #EEE, shade 15 to #000 (a digital mapping); the WSC contrast bit (port $14 bit 1, WSC only, not the SwanCrystal) drives two SoC lines per LCD line, no optical number; the WSdev pinout pages (mono STN rails 24 V and -20 V "amplitude contrast dependent", colour CSTN 20 V and -16 V with 50 percent PWM) give no maker or optical data; PC Watch 2002 has only qualitative remarks. Dead ends: Hackaday's FSTN project (a different panel), NitroSwan (credits "some BnW palettes", no values), the MiSTer core, Mesen2 docs.
- **Apple II (the one real find):** **Winston Gayler, "The Apple II Circuit Description"** (Howard W. Sams, the Applefritter PDF with a text layer; Fig. 4-4 and p. 40; copyright, numbers only): composite out, **open circuit, level pot R11 at maximum: sync 0 V, black 0.5 V, white 2.0 V, burst 0.7 V peak to peak centred about 0.45 V**; into 75 ohm the output "drops about 50%, mostly across R10"; the burst is 14 cycles; its tank is L1, C2 and C3 (about 3.6 MHz), C3 sets the hue by delaying the burst through R6; Q3 is an emitter follower with summing inputs R6, R7, R8 (relative sync, black, white and burst levels), R10 protects Q3, R11 is the level adjust; a white worked example: about 3.5 V into R7 and R8 gives 2.6 V at the base, less Vbe 2.0 V; phases relative to the burst: violet 135, blue 225, green 315 degrees (the 45-degree grid). Derived by the agent from these figures: with black to white 100 IRE (1.5 V), 1 IRE is about 15 mV, so sync is about -33 IRE (standard -40) and the burst about 47 IRE peak to peak (standard 40), black is the blanking level (no 7.5 IRE setup). **Resistor values** (baldengineer/bit-preserve, KiCad captures, **CC BY 4.0**): Apple II+ (Rev 7): Q3 2N3904, R6 2.7k (burst, matches Sather), R7 1.5k (video), R8 2k (sync), R5 1k, R9 10R, R10 27R, R11 200R (level pot), R12 12k, R13 3.3M, L1 27 uH, CT3 5 to 50 pF, R27 4.7k to the colour killer Q6; with these values white gives 2.66 V at the base (Gayler 2.6 V) and black about 0.49 V at the emitter (Gayler 0.5 V), sync 0 V: roles and values consistent with the measurement. Apple IIe (NTSC, 050-0051-D, rjhelms' capture): R3 6.8k, R5 330R, R7 2.7k, R10 5.6k, R13 6.8k, R15 1k, R8 75R, R11 200R, R9 100R, R17 100R, R19 1k, R25 1k, L3 27 uH, C32 47 pF, C25 100 pF, Q1 2N3906 (mixer), Q2 2N3904 (output); the role of each IIe resistor is not determined (no netlist). The Reference Manual's aux pin black 0.75 V conflicts with Gayler's 0.5 V (probably the IIe, or a revision or load). "Apple II Video Display Theory" (the sheet MAME cites; the CSV export works through a redirect): Y 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1 and "Actual" I and Q magnitudes 0, 0.104, 0.193, 0.297, 0.489 against "Naive" 0, 0.25, 0.5: derived, not measured, and flattened so the colour mapping was not recoverable. Still not found: per-colour chroma amplitudes and hue offsets, vectorscope numbers, colorimeter data.
- **ZX Spectrum:** two primary documents read by eye (image-only PDFs rendered with a PDF library installed in the scratch folder only): **Philips TEA2000 datasheet** (September 1985; the 128K's encoder): 2 bits per primary, TTL, "equally spaced intensity levels", 64 colours, gamma applied internally, no per-colour Y, U, V table and no gamma number; crystal 8.867238 MHz for PAL, burst 5.7 us after line sync and 2.5 us wide, COVO 2 V sync tip to white, sync tip at 5 V, output impedance about 15 ohm, luminance amplitude and chroma amplitude tolerance 10 percent, chroma phase tolerance "tbf". **Ferranti 6C001 specification** (260882): two pages (cover and digital DC table), the U, V and /Y analogue outputs listed with no analogue levels, Vcc1 5 V, Vcc2 2 V; **5C102** pages 1 to 5 of 17: pins 15, 16, 17 "ANALOGUE o/p", Vcc2 (pin 13) 2 V, pin 28 "3 LEVEL o/p"; pages 6 to 17 unread. **Patent EP0107687B1:** the description says the circuits of figures 2 to 4 are "omitted since resistance values are given on the drawings"; the family has no US member (WO1983003916A1, DE3377306D1, JPS59500929A, ATE35586T1), so that route is closed; structure: U (fig. 2) from transistors 31 to 33 gating B, G, R plus 36 gated by the burst, Y (fig. 4) from 51 to 53 plus 56 gated by the HL bit and 57 by sync. Cable page (128K SCART): composite 1.2 V p-p, 68 ohm in series on each colour line into 75 ohm, normal to bright 0.63 to 0.65; a Harlequin video-mod thread (search snippet only): bright and normal across 75 ohm 0.700 V and 0.510 V (0.729), 0.680 and 0.502 (0.738), 0.685 and 0.459 (0.670): 128K and Harlequin-type RGB through resistors, not the 48K ULA. The origin of the emulator values 0xD7, 0xCD, 0xD8: nothing authoritative (one preset site claims 255 x 0.85). Still missing: the 48K per-colour Y, U, V, the burst, the patent drawings' resistors, the 5C102 pages 6 to 17, the Issue 2 and 128 analogue schematics (26 MB, image-only), Chris Smith's book.
- **Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color, Game Gear:** nothing for any of the three (about 17 searches and 12 fetches, English). New facts only: the Lynx II screen is labelled "C104160 REV A" (k-retro.com; one forum post says the Lynx I and II share the LCD, unverified); k-retro.com lists the original NGPC screen as "LQ026B2DD01" (a 2.6-inch TFT per another listing; the "LQ" prefix suggests Sharp, nothing confirms it; no datasheet); the Game Gear service manual (iFixit copy, Sega copyright): "3.2" Color LCD with Back-Light", active area 65.27 x 48.90 mm, 160 x 146 dots, 32 colours from 4096 on screen; the CCFL is a Wikipedia snippet. The Analogue Pocket's "Original Lynx", "Original NGPC" and "Original GG" modes are named and documented with no values. The libretro "Real GBA and DS-Phat colors" thread (Calibrite Display Plus HL, ColorMunki Display, about 80 nits) covers only the Game Boy family, the GBA and the DS: a method template if a real unit is ever measured. The agent could not prove a negative on the forums that refuse automated fetches (Reddit, AtariAge, Atari-Forum, consolemods, the Wayback Machine).

### What the fifth pass decides

- **Closed and removed from the TODO:** the **WonderSwan** (mono): every number is a Game Boy Pocket analogy by one author on the same basis (ares' endpoints are Near's Pocket palette), so the Game Boy Pocket variant is the honest equivalent; the **WonderSwan Color and SwanCrystal**: the matrix is the Game Boy Color's (higan's), so `game_boy_color` is the same transform, and the WSC's own panel has no data; the **Atari Lynx, Neo Geo Pocket Color and Game Gear**: the community angle gave only part numbers and sizes, and the emulators agree on the identity (n x 17); the gap needs a colorimeter on a real unit (the method of the libretro "Real GBA and DS-Phat colors" thread). Not proven impossible on the forums that refuse automated fetches, but nothing reachable from here.
- **To build:** the Game Boy family (BGB reality, SameBoy), the Virtual Boy (660 nm red, pulse-width model), the Apple II lo-res colours (measured level set), the ZX Spectrum 128K, +2 and +3 RGB output (ratio variants). The 48K Spectrum's PAL encode stays derived and unbuilt (its documents are image-only).
- **Python:** one agent installed a PDF library (Python) into the scratch folder only, to render image-only PDFs; the owner has objected to Python use, so this is recorded here and in the TODO rather than done again without asking.

## White-scale policy of the colour targets (2026-11-26)

Question (the TODO line "One white-scale policy"): the Game Boy Color keeps 240 of 255, the Game Boy Advance has a tinted white, the Mega Drive has full white at 255 against an open 238. Which rule?

Decision (by the owner's delegate): **a target never rescales white to hide or invent a level: its white is whatever its sourced model gives for the emulator's full white (255, 255, 255 in, or the emulator's own full white where the data file says the emulator's white is lower, e.g. Mega Drive Genesis Plus GX 238). Where a source states the device's white (the Game Boy Color's 240 of 255 from the emulator-correction clip, the AGS-101's measured tinted white, the CRT-like set white of a composite model), the target keeps it. Where the sources leave the scale open (the Mega Drive's 255 against 238), the file says which it uses and why. Every file states its white (the level and the tint, per variant when they differ) in a sentence of its notes that starts with "White scale:".** The policy changes no number.

What was done: the white of every variant of the 25 targets was computed with the oracle (`crt_model::target`, through `Target::transform_for` and `Transform::apply`, for 255, 255, 255 and, for the `dac` and `signal` kinds, for the emulator's own top level or brightest reference colour: the same white every time), each file got its `White scale:` sentence, `REFERENCE.md` got the table "White of each target", and two tests in `targets.rs` check the sentence and pin the white of variant 1. Findings: no white is above the input's (the AGS-101's blue reaches 1.00000x from its six-digit matrices); the 255-in white of every identity-like target (levels, signal, lookup kinds, the SNES table) is 255, 255, 255; the tinted or lowered whites are the Game Boy Color (240), the Game Boy Advance (252, 238, 242), the Game Boy Color colorimetric (248, or 244 for mGBA), the AGS-101 (243, 236, 255 and 248, 238, 255 as measured, neutral once adapted to D65), the Neo Geo's dark bit and shadow variants (251, 142, 141), the Super Game Boy and Game Boy LCD palettes (their own lightest shades) and the Virtual Boy (red only); each has a source or is derived from one. The Mega Drive keeps 255: its readings are published scaled to the 951 mV top level and no source gives the encoder's gain, so the 238 reading (4.7 V taken as 0.9 V) stays an argument, not a number; the Genesis Plus GX top level of 238 and a plain 255 both land on that 255.

## Headless look at every target, 2026-11-26

What was run: the debug build of `phoxium render --image in.png --preset pvm-20m4u --source-colour <none | target[:variant]> --size 4x --validation`, for the 25 shipped targets, variant 1 and a second variant where one exists (variant 2, and 9 = 2-A for the Super Game Boy), each on and off with the same tube preset: 46 on/off pairs. No emulator was available, so the inputs were built for the purpose by a scratch program outside the repository (flat 8x8 patches): the target's own reference colours in index order for the recognising kinds (signal, remap, lut: the references of the data file, the 512 codes of the PC Engine), a row of colours that no reference takes (found with the oracle's `classify` and `lut_index`) as the pass-through test, the DAC's own input levels as grey, red, green and blue ramps plus mixed combinations for the `dac` kind, four grey shades and 16 greys for the palette kind, and for every target 5-bit-expanded grey, red, green and blue ramps, 100 % and 75 % colour bars, a hue sweep and a 0 to 255 grey gradient. The PNGs stayed in the scratch directory; nothing in the repository was changed by the check.

Checks that passed: the validation layer printed no message in any of the 92 renders; no output is black, NaN-like or broken (the share of pure black pixels moves by a few points at most; the Game Boy LCD and Super Game Boy outputs have no black at all because their darkest shade is a colour); in the recognising targets the pass-through row is bit-identical on and off in the middle of its patches (0 of 8192 pixels differ over the two centre rows for the C64, NES, Atari 8-bit, PC Engine, CGA composite and Apple II; the 1 to 4 % of differing pixels over the whole row are the beam spot and halation of the recognised patches above and below it).

One line per target (what the images show, on against off; the numbers are the largest 8-bit code difference between the two renders unless stated):
- `game_boy_color`: yellow turns orange, cyan turns greenish, saturation and white drop (mean RGB 89.6, 87.6, 79.3 to 79.4, 77.9, 73.3; max 111); no band.
- `game_boy_advance`: darker, steeper curve and desaturated primaries (red turns raspberry, blue dim), pure colours tinted; pure black share 4.8 % to 8.2 % from the gamma (max 153).
- `game_boy_color_colorimetric` (v1, v2): lifted and pastel colours (yellow turns sand, blue turns azure), the dark end slightly lifted; v1 is brighter than v2 (mean 110 against 99; max 175).
- `game_boy_advance_sp_ags_101` (v1 the screen's own white, v2 D65 white): mild desaturation, a cool cast in v1 (mean 85.4, 82.9, 78.5) and a more neutral white in v2 (89.8, 90.2, 78.0), no black, no band (max 59 and 69).
- `super_nintendo_black_boost`: the lower half of the 32 codes darker, the upper half unchanged, hues kept (max 45).
- `snk_neo_geo` (v1 normal, v2 dark bit): the low half of the ramps drops and the upper half is unchanged to a few codes, hues kept; v2 slightly darker (max 9 and 11).
- `super_game_boy` (v1 1-A, v9 2-A): the four grey shades and the ramps become the palette's colours (cream, orange, brick, navy for 1-A), no black at all; 2-A is a visibly different set (max 211 and 253).
- `game_boy_lcd` (v1 BGB DMG, v2 BGB Pocket): the four shades and the greys become olive to dark teal (DMG) and a greyer olive (Pocket); no black; ramps blend between the four shades (max 204 and 181).
- `nintendo_virtual_boy` (v1 grey picture, v2 red picture): everything is red light on black, 499 distinct colours, mean RGB 93.1, 15.7, 0.0 (v1); with v2 the green and blue ramps disappear as the notes say (max 254).
- `sega_mega_drive` (v1 Genesis Plus GX 15 levels, v2 Kega Fusion 8 levels): the ramps move onto the DAC's steps (low steps lower, top equal), the combination patches shift level between the two emulators, no band beyond the steps themselves (max 31 and 37).
- `sega_master_system` (v1 first VDP bench, v2 second VDP): four levels per channel with the two middle ones shifted, combination patches keep their hues (max 36 and 27).
- `msx2_v9958` (v1, v2): the 5-bit DAC against 255/7, at most 7 codes of change as the notes promise, nothing else moves.
- `amstrad_cpc` (v1 emulator half level 128, v2 127): the half level goes to 0.40 (the mid patches darker), black and full unchanged, combination patches keep their hues (max 36 and 34).
- `amstrad_cpc_green_screen` (v1, v2): on a P22 tube all channels become one grey with red weighing three times green (full red 207.9 to 143.9, full green 208.2 to 37.0 in the end patches), the 27 steps show in the bars, mean RGB 76.7, 76.6, 76.4; on a monochrome phosphor it does nothing (see the finding).
- `ibm_cga_rgbi` (one variant): the 16 colours become the measured 5153 ones, slightly different in tint (max 54); the pass-through row keeps its colours; the ramps are snapped by the references within the threshold of 0.1.
- `ibm_cga_composite` (v1 old card, v2 new card): the 16 colours as an NTSC decoder shows them (the blue, green and magenta tints move, max 133 and 86), pass-through unchanged; v2 differs from v1.
- `nintendo_entertainment_system` (v1 2C02G blank, v2 2C02E setup): the 64 colours shift in tint and level (max 107), the smooth ramps and bars are snapped onto the palette (the first dark steps go black), pass-through unchanged.
- `commodore_64` (v1 6569R3, v2 R4): the 16 colours go to the PAL decoder's colours (max 78 and 89), the hue sweep and ramps are snapped onto the 16 colours because the threshold (0.25) is wide, pass-through unchanged, v2 slightly darker (mean 84.2 against 90.1).
- `atari_8bit_gtia` (v1 800XL, v2 130XE): the 256 colours change in tint and level (max 50), the grey ramp keeps its 16 greys, pass-through unchanged.
- `atari_7800_maria` (v1 25.7 degrees, v2 26.7): the 256 colours shift in tint (max 73 and 63), the sweep stays mostly smooth (threshold 0.05), pass-through unchanged.
- `atari_2600_tia` (v1 25.7, v2 24 degrees): the 128 colours shift in tint (max 126 and 140, in a few patches), the sweep stays smooth, pass-through unchanged.
- `texas_instruments_tms9928` (v1 data manual, v2 SC-3000 scope): the 15 colours (two reference sets) change a little in hue and level (max 82 and 85), the transparent entry stays black, pass-through unchanged.
- `apple_ii_lores` (v1, v2 Munafo ratio): the 16 colours move toward the NTSC decoder's, small changes (mean 0.5, max 28 and 30), pass-through unchanged.
- `sinclair_zx_spectrum_rgb` (v1 205 and ratio 0.68, v2 205 and 0.63): the normal level drops against bright (mean RGB 79.6, 91.6, 83.9 to 71.4, 82.5, 75.7 in v1, 68.3, 79.0, 72.4 in v2), bright colours unchanged (max 40 and 54).
- `nec_pc_engine_composite` (v1 kitrinx, v2 V gain 14/15): the 512 codes are replaced by the silicon table's colours, a visible change over the whole grid (max 105), pass-through (non 3-bit levels) unchanged.

Anything odd: only the wide thresholds of the C64 (0.25) and NES (0.2) snap a smooth ramp or sweep onto the palette, because every pixel within the threshold of a reference is taken for it; that is the specified behaviour for a picture that is not an emulator's flat palette (the pass-through row of colours beyond the threshold shows it is a threshold, not a fault). The dark first steps of the ramps of the signal targets go black because they lie within the threshold of the black reference. Nothing else looked wrong.

### The finding: a monochrome phosphor makes every colour-dependent target see grey (a real bug)

Question: `decode.comp` runs before the `target` slot (the chain order is `decode`, then `target`, `signal`, `amp`, ..., `gun`; `crates/phoxium/src/gpu/stages/mod.rs`) and, when `phosphor_type` is not P22 (the parameter of `params/phosphor.toml`: p22, p4, p31, p1, p39, philips_yellow_data_display_blend), replaces the picture by its BT.601 luma in all three channels (`code = vec3(dot(code, vec3(0.299, 0.587, 0.114)))`). Does the target act on the original colours?

Experiment (the scratch inputs above, `--set phosphor_type=p31`, same tube preset), the colour picture and the same picture converted to its BT.601 luma by a scratch program, each with the target off and on:
- `amstrad_cpc_green_screen`, P31: target on against target off on the colour picture: max difference 0 codes, 0 of 262144 pixels differ (bit-identical). Target on, colour picture against target on, luma picture: max 3 codes, the same 3 codes that separate colour-off from luma-off (the luma's own 8-bit rounding). On a P22 tube the same target changes 68 % of the pixels (max 249). End patches of the red and green ramps: P31 off 58.5 and 135.8, P31 on 58.5 and 135.8 (the BT.601 order, red darker than green), P22 on 143.9 and 37.0 (the 9 : 3 order). The target is a no-op in the setup its own label recommends ("use a green phosphor type").
- `commodore_64`, P31: target on, colour picture against target on, luma picture: 6282 of 327680 pixels differ (1.9 %, mean 0.29 code), against 84 % of the pixels that differ between target on and off; the few differing pixels are references that the luma's 8-bit rounding moved across a classification boundary. The patch centres show it: red, blue, orange, brown and dark grey all come out at 73.0 on the green channel of the output with the target on (the dark grey reference), where on P22 they are five different colours; light blue and medium grey both at 126.0, light red and light green both at 125.9.

Verdict: identical; on a monochrome phosphor the target sees only grey. For the CPC green screen this is a bug (the 9 : 3 : 1 mix never sees a colour, the target does nothing, and its notes claim that decode takes the luma of the grey, as if it came after the target). The recognising and matrix targets have the same fault (they only ever see the greys of the picture). `super_nintendo_black_boost`'s note ("acts on the grey the phosphor shows") describes the actual order and is the one text that is right.

Clean fix, not made (this task changes no source): physically the device's colour comes first and the one-gun tube takes the luma of what the device outputs. `decode.comp` would keep the luma only while `source_colour_target` is none (every no-target user bit for bit unchanged) and skip it when a target is on; `target.comp` would then take the BT.601 luma of its own output when `phosphor_type` is not P22, so that the downstream stages still receive three equal channels as `phosphor_type.rs` assumes. Risks: the `target` slot gets a second job; the GPU tests of the monochrome chain and the oracle (`crt_model::phosphor_types::mono_drive`) need a case with a target on; the SNES table and the Neo Geo curves would act on the colour and then the luma instead of on the grey (a few codes) and the SNES note changes. The alternative with no shader change is to say in the notes and REFERENCE that a target does nothing useful on a monochrome phosphor and to remove the advice from the CPC green screen's label (the target then loses its purpose). Recorded in `docs/TODO.md`.

## A colour target on a monochrome phosphor, fixed 2026-11-26

Question: the verdict above (a monochrome phosphor makes every colour-dependent target see grey) was a bug; fix it with the order agreed there (the device's colour first, then the one-gun tube).

Decision and change: `decode.comp` takes the BT.601 luma for a `phosphor_type` other than P22 only while `source_colour_target` is `none` (it reads `params.source_colour_target`, already in the `Params` block: no push constant and no frame constant added; every value after `none` has a data file, a test of `targets.rs` keeps it so, so "not none" is exactly "the `target` slot runs"). `target.comp` writes the BT.601 luma of its own output to the three channels when `phosphor_type` is not P22, so `signal`, `amp`, `gun` and the rest still receive three equal channels as `phosphor_type.rs` assumes. The oracle gets `crt_model::target::Transform::apply_mono` (the luma of `apply`). The SNES black boost and Neo Geo curves now act on the colour and then the luma instead of on the grey (a few codes on a monochrome phosphor); the description of `source_colour_target` ("acts on the grey the phosphor shows"; the SNES file itself never said it) and the notes and provenance of the CPC green screen ("decode takes the BT.601 luma of the grey") were rewritten to the order the chain now has.

Evidence (the earlier render check, same inputs and `--preset pvm-20m4u`, debug build, `phoxium render ... --size 1024x256 | 1024x320 --validation`, compared with the renders of the earlier look):
- No change without a target: P31 target off, P22 target off and P22 target on are bit-identical to the renders before the fix (max difference 0 codes, 0 differing pixels, for both inputs). The GPU test `mono_without_a_target_is_unchanged` prints the hash of the output and decode raster of the five monochrome types with no target, `81b32dc75de29dd5`, the same on the commit before the change; `p22_sequence_hash` is `dd3a781343fcf649` on both.
- `amstrad_cpc_green_screen`, P31, colour picture: target on against off now differs in 65.4 % of the pixels (max 139 codes; it was 0). Green channel at the end patches of the red and green ramps: off 62.8 and 145.4 (the BT.601 order, as before), on 172.1 and 46.4 (the 9 : 3 order, red the heavier; P22 on: 153.5 and 39.4 on all channels).
- `commodore_64`, P31, colour picture, green channel at the patch centres of the references: red, blue, orange, brown and dark grey were all 73.0; now 73.0, 57.7, 94.4, 57.7 and 73.0 (light red 136.7, cyan 168.7 as before). Red and dark grey share one grey and so do blue and brown: that is the VIC-II (they have the same hardware luma, 0.339 and 0.278 in the oracle), not a defect of the order; five colours of five different luma stay apart (test `mono_c64_target_keeps_five_colours_apart`).
- GPU tests `mono_cpc_green_target_acts_on_the_colour` and `mono_c64_target_keeps_five_colours_apart` (`tests/phosphor_types.rs`) fail on the shaders before the change and pass now.


## Image-only documents read, 2026-11-26

How: a scratch Rust tool outside the repository (no Python, no installed software, no prebuilt binary). `hayro` 0.9 (MIT or Apache-2.0, a pure-Rust PDF rasteriser with pure-Rust JBIG2 and CCITT decoders) renders the pages to PNG, a few lines of the `image` crate crop and enlarge them, and the pages were read by eye (each digit quoted below was read on an enlarged crop unless marked otherwise). `lopdf` plus `fax` also decoded the CCITT tiles of the V9958 booklet; `lopdf` alone cannot decode JBIG2 (the patent), `hayro` does. All documents are third-party, mostly unlicensed, and the Ferranti copies are marked "For Educational and Archival Use Only, Strictly Non-Commercial": the numbers are quoted with attribution, nothing is copied. Confidence: H = digits checked on an enlarged crop, M = read at normal size, legible, not enlarged, U = unclear or inferred.

### 1. Yamaha V9938 data book and V9958 booklet

V9938 data book (vgamuseum.info copy, 170 scanned pages; printed pages 126 to 128 = PDF pages 139 to 141) and V9958 booklet (December 1988, 35 pages, section 7.3.7 to 7.3.10 on printed pages 23 and 24).

- V9938 "RGB output level" (RL = 470 ohm, VCC = 5.00 V, 25 C), min / typ / max: VRGB7 (maximum output voltage) 2.5 / 2.8 / 3.2 V; VRGB0 (minimum, black level) 1.7 / 2.0 / 2.4 V; VP-P (VRGB7 - VRGB0) 0.65 / 0.8 / 1.00 V; DRGB (VP-P deviation) 5.0 % max. H. The timing drawing shows 8 levels VRGB0 to VRGB7 (3-bit palette entries). No per-step table: a linear step would be VP-P / 7 = 0.114 V typical (derived, U on linearity). Rise and fall VRGB0 to VRGB7: 60 ns max (RL 470 ohm, CL 150 pF, 10 % to 90 %).
- V9938 sync output levels: BLEO tri-level (RL 1 kohm) high 4.5 V min to VCC, intermediate 2.5 to 3.5 V, low 0.4 V max; HSYNC and CSYNC (no load) high 4.5 V min to VCC, intermediate 2.7 to 3.7 V, low 0.8 V max. H.
- V9938 composite video output level (RL = 470 ohm), min / typ / max. Versions A and B: VWHITE 2.20 / 2.55 / 2.90 V (B/W use 2.30 / 2.65 / 3.00); VBLACK 2.00 / 2.30 / 2.70; VSYNC 1.90 / 2.20 / 2.60; VCB (colour burst; the table labels it "pulse width" but the unit is volts) 0.14 / 0.19 / 0.24; VP-P white to sync 0.24 / 0.35 / 0.44 (B/W 0.34 / 0.45 / 0.54). Version C: VWHITE 2.20 / 2.60 / 3.00 (B/W 2.50 / 2.80 / 3.20); VBLACK 1.80 / 2.20 / 2.50; VSYNC 1.60 / 2.00 / 2.30; VCB 0.16 / 0.22 / 0.28; VP-P 0.40 / 0.60 / 0.75 (B/W 0.60 / 0.80 / 0.95). H. Whether VCB is peak or peak-to-peak is not stated (U); typical VCB over VP-P is 0.19 / 0.35 = 54 % (A, B) and 0.22 / 0.60 = 37 % (C) as the ratio of the printed numbers.
- V9938 composite timing (RL 470 ohm, CL 150 pF): HSYNC width 4.50 to 4.70 us; burst delay after HSYNC 0.40 to 0.60 us; burst width 2.60 to 3.30 us; burst to left border 1.10 to 1.50 us; active display 47.00 to 48.00 us (typ 47.68); left border 2.4 to 2.7 us and right border 2.50 to 2.80 us (at Display Adjust 0); right border to HSYNC 1.20 to 1.50 us; edge times 90 to 110 ns. H.
- V9938 palette: 9 bits per entry, 3 bits of each of R, G, B (register #16 selects the palette entry, then two bytes at port #2: byte 1 = 0 R2 R1 R0 0 B2 B1 B0, byte 2 = 0000 0 G2 G1 G0). Mode register 2 bit BW = 1 gives "black and white in 32 tones" (composite encoder only). H. The book has no palette-code-to-volt table beyond VP-P above, and no colour-difference matrix or burst phase: not documented there.
- V9958: 5-bit linear DAC ("DAC (LINEAR)" in the block diagram, levels VRGND0 to VRGND31 on the timing drawing). Section 7.3.7 "R.G.B. Output Level" (RL = 470 ohm, Vdd 5.00 V, 25 C), min / typ / max: VRGB31 (maximum) 2.8 / 3.1 / 3.5 V; VRGB0 (black) 1.9 / 2.2 / 2.5 V; VP-P 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.1 V; DRGB 5.0 % max. Sync output (RL 1 kohm): BLEO high 4.5 V to Vdd, intermediate 2.5 to 3.5 V, low 0.4 V max. RGB rise and fall 60 ns max. H. The booklet carries only the changes from the V9938 and no composite table, so the composite figures above are the only ones.

### 2. Ferranti and Sinclair documents (archive.org item `ferranti-sinclair-docs`)

The 5C102 specification pages 6 to 17 hold the first analogue figures of the Spectrum ULA found so far: hand-drawn probe-test circuits of the die with the output voltage of each state. Pages 1 to 5 and the 6C001 specification (DC characteristics of the logic pins only) have no U, V or Y level.

- **/Y output (5C102 page 7, H)**, 5 V supply: 7K from 5 V to the level-shift base node; 3K collector load of the transistor feeding an emitter follower (Vout); bias leg: diode then 2K to ground; a 5K switched by /HL in parallel to that 2K; switched emitter branches R 4K, G 2K, B 8K to ground; sync branch: diode and 4K switched by /SYNC. Vout (probe load 1 kohm to 0 V): reference (R=G=B=0, /SYNC=1, HL don't care) 2.40 V; /HL=1 with R only 2.10 V; G only 1.79 V; B only 2.25 V; /HL=0 with R=G=B=1 1.00 V; sync (R=G=B=0, /SYNC=0) about 4.1 V. The dim (non-bright) white R=G=B=1 with /HL=1 is not in the table. The voltage falls as light increases (the pin is /Y).
- **U output (page 9, M for the digits, clearly written)**: 2.22K from 5 V to the base node; diode and 1K to ground; 3K collector load; emitter node with a permanent 11K to ground and switched branches B' 4K, /G' 6K, /R' 12K, /BURST 4.4K. Vout (columns B', /G', /R', /BURST): 0 0 0 1 (reference) 2.96 V; 1 1 1 1 1.00 V; 1 0 0 1 1.98 V; 0 1 0 1 2.30 V; 0 0 1 1 2.63 V; burst (1 1 1 0) 1.89 V. Comparator thresholds of the probe test (page 10): 2.7K / 2.7K and 3.6K / 1.5K dividers, 1K probe load.
- **V output (page 11, M)**: 7K from 5 V; diode and 2K to ground; 3K collector load; emitter node branches R* 2K, /G* 2.4K, /B* 11.8K, BURST* 3K, /BURST* 3K. Vout (columns R*, /G*, /B*, BURST*, /BURST*): 0 0 0 0 1 (reference) 3.27 V; R* only 1.87 V; /G* only 2.10 V; /B* only 3.03 V; BURST* only 2.33 V; the last row (R* 1, /G* 0, /B* 1, both bursts 0) is circled and its number is struck out with a "?" (the struck value looks like 1.62, U). Probe thresholds (page 12): 2.4K / 2.7K and 3K / 2.0K.
- Other pages: probe-test comparators for TAPE/LS (1.1K / 3.9K, 100K / 50K / 62K, 330 R, 4.7K, 3.3K, 0.01 uF), the crystal-oscillator buffer (180 R and 470 pF to 5 V, 220 R, 1500 pF), a tri-state tester (470 R, 2.2K, 8 x 560 R) and the package-test pin allocation (page 16). Pages 13, 15 and 17 were not opened.
- **Sinclair to Ferranti letter of 28 January 1983 and the clamp proposal** (5-page scan; handwritten pages 4 and 5 and two Polaroid pages): the clamp replaces the tuning pots VR1 and VR2 (2 x 2K2) in the LM1889 interface: U and V each through 0.1 uF with 220 R to ground, a BC184 clamp transistor with 10K, then 1K in series and 150 pF to ground into B-Y (pin 4) and R-Y (pin 2); 470 R from LM1889 pin 3 to +5 V; Y through two 0.1 uF with 220 R. Parts removed: VR1, VR2, R38, R39, R40, R41, C36; C52 and C53 become 150 pF. Notes: during the blue bar the collector-base of the clamp transistor is forward biased by 200 to 300 mV; a redesign could raise the Y sync amplitude by 500 mV to 1 V. Alternative: replace a 47K by 15K ("desaturates the picture slightly, but acceptable"), V13 = 5 V - 15 x 0.207 = 1.9 V (pin 13 is the chroma subcarrier input; the first digits of "47K" are M). The letter also mentions PAL (6C001) and NTSC ULA versions and a requested move of the colour burst. The scope photographs have no legible numeric value (U: "50 mV, 10 us" and "1 V, 5 mV, 10 us" scales only).
- **Issue 2 schematic** (16 August 1982, SRC 102): the video section shows designators only, no component values anywhere on the sheet. Topology (M): ULA pins U (15), V (16) and /Y (17) go to IC14 (LM1889): U through VR1 (with R56 to 0 V and R38 to +12VA, C52) to B-Y (pin 4), V through VR2 (R63, R39, C53) to R-Y (pin 2); /Y towards Video Input (pin 12) via the R42 / R47 area and the TR1, TR2 modulator stage with R48 to R53, R64, R72, C25, C65, a 4.433619 MHz crystal X2 and TC2. The Y path routing is U. The only values known for these parts come from the letter (VR1 and VR2 are 2K2).
- **Spectrum 128 analogue circuit** (24 May 1985): TEA2000 encoder (IC36) driven by R, G, B and BRIGHT through six 1N4148 diodes (D20 to D25) with 3 x 10K (R96 to R98) to the supply, 6 x 68R on the DIN socket (R92 to R95), R105 1K on LUMO, 470 R on the crystal inputs (R99, R104). H. A different encoder, so the 48K levels above do not apply to it.
- TEA1002 datasheet: not opened, no time.

### 3. Amstrad patent EP0107687B1 (JBIG2 scans, 15 pages; figures 2 to 4 are on the drawing sheets 2 to 4, PDF pages 8 to 10, read at 2.6x, H)

All three circuits are the same structure as the 5C102 test circuits: a diode-connected bias leg set by the Vs current source ("R = 1K7", base resistor R/2), a collector load into an emitter follower, and one switched sink per colour on a common emitter node. Reading column by column, the patent matches the 5C102 sheet (in series sums):
- **Fig. 2 U**: Vcc1 to 5K parallel 4K (= 2.22K) to the bias node; 2K parallel 2K (= 1K) below the diode; 3K collector load (transistor 35, follower 34, output U). Emitter node 35: a permanent 5K + 5K + 1K (= 11K) to ground; BLUE'' (31) 4K; GREEN'' (32) 5K + 1K (= 6K); RED'' (33) 5K + 5K + 1K + 1K (= 12K); BURST (36) 4K + 400R (= 4.4K).
- **Fig. 3 V**: Vcc1 to 5K + 2K (= 7K); 3K collector load (45, follower 44, output V); bias leg 2K; RED* (43) 2K; GREEN* (42) 2K + 400R (= 2.4K); BLUE* (41) 5K + 4K + 1K + 1K + 400R + 400R (= 11.8K); BURST* (46) 3K; BURST* (48) 3K.
- **Fig. 4 Y**: Vcc1 to 3K + 4K (= 7K); 3K collector load (55, follower 54); bias leg 2K; /HL (56) 5K; RED' (53) 4K; GREEN' (52) 2K; BLUE' (51) 5K plus a second resistor whose value is blank on the drawing (the 5C102 sheet gives 8K, so 3K is inferred, U); /SYNC (57) diode and 4K.
- So the U, V, Y levels per colour can be computed from either source, and the voltages of the 5C102 sheet (above) serve as the check. The column-to-transistor assignment of the two middle columns of Fig. 4 is by position and label (M).

### Still unread or unresolved

TEA1002 datasheet, 5C102 specification pages 13, 15, 17, the V9938 palette-code-to-DAC mapping (not in the book), a composite burst phase and colour-difference matrix of the V9938 (not in the book), the value struck out on page 11, the Y routing of the Issue 2 schematic, Sony CXA1145 and the Amiga Hardware Reference Manual (not tried: no time).
